## CRITICAL Issues

### CRIT-01: Version Mismatch Between `package.json` and `appcore-data.js`

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/package.json` (line 3) and `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js` (line 6)

**Problem:** `package.json` declares `"version": "3.2.2"` but `appcore-data.js` defines `APP_VERSION = '3.2.3'`. The `App.jsx` header comment also says `v3.2.2`. This creates confusion about which version is actually deployed, can cause cache-busting failures, and makes debugging support tickets unreliable since the user-reported version may not match the actual codebase.

**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Fix:** Align all version strings to a single source of truth. Since `appcore-data.js` appears to be the latest edit, either bump `package.json` or revert `appcore-data.js`.

**Option A — Align to 3.2.3 (recommended if intentional bump):**

```json
// whispering-wishes-replit/package.json — line 3
{
  "name": "whispering-wishes",
  "version": "3.2.3",
  ...
}
```

```jsx
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — line 3 (header comment)
// WHISPERING WISHES v3.2.3 - App (Main Application Component)
```

**Option B — Align to 3.2.2 (if 3.2.3 was premature):**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js — line 6
const APP_VERSION = '3.2.2';
```

### CRIT-02: Firebase Credentials Hardcoded as Fallback in Client-Side Code

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (lines ~87–88, in the module-level constants section)

**Problem:** The Firebase Realtime Database URL and API key are hardcoded as plaintext fallback strings:
```js
const FIREBASE_DB = (typeof import.meta !== 'undefined' && import.meta.env?.VITE_FIREBASE_DB) || 'https://whispering-wishes-default-rtdb.firebaseio.com';
const FIREBASE_API_KEY = (typeof import.meta !== 'undefined' && import.meta.env?.VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY) || 'AIzaSyWhisperingWishes';
```

Although the comment acknowledges this (`P13-FIX: CRITICAL-1`), the fallback values mean that **any build without env vars will ship actual database URLs and API keys in the production JS bundle**. Vite inlines `import.meta.env` at build time — if the env var is missing, the fallback is baked into the output. An attacker can extract these from the minified bundle trivially.

While Firebase Security Rules are the real protection layer (which is a backend concern), from a frontend standpoint, shipping the fallback defeats the purpose of using env vars at all.

**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Fix:** Remove the hardcoded fallback and fail gracefully when the env var is missing. Firebase features should be disabled, not silently connected to a known URL.

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — replace lines ~87–88
const FIREBASE_DB = (typeof import.meta !== 'undefined' && import.meta.env?.VITE_FIREBASE_DB) || null;
const FIREBASE_API_KEY = (typeof import.meta !== 'undefined' && import.meta.env?.VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY) || null;
const FIREBASE_AVAILABLE = !!(FIREBASE_DB && FIREBASE_API_KEY);
```

Then guard all Firebase write/read calls:
```js
// Before any fetchWithTimeout call to FIREBASE_DB:
if (!FIREBASE_AVAILABLE) {
  console.warn('[WW] Firebase config missing — online features disabled.');
  return;
}
```

> **Backend dependency note:** The Backend Agent should ensure `.env.example` documents these vars and the deploy pipeline always sets them.

### CRIT-03: Massive Single-File Architecture — `App.jsx` is 717,793 Bytes (~700 KB)

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`

**Problem:** A single React component file at ~700 KB is extreme. This causes:
1. **Bundle splitting failure** — Vite/Rollup cannot code-split within a single module. The entire app loads before anything renders.
2. **Developer experience collapse** — IDE auto-complete, linting, and hot-reload become unusable.
3. **Memory pressure** — React's reconciler processes the entire component tree from one function scope.
4. **Accessibility testing is impossible** — automated tools cannot parse a file this large.

While the file already imports from `AppCore.jsx`, the `WhisperingWishesInner` component appears to contain ALL tab views in a single render function.

**Severity:** CRITICAL (architectural, long-term)

**Fix (incremental, non-breaking):** Extract each tab into a lazy-loaded component. This is a phased recommendation — immediate extraction targets:

```jsx
// Example: whispering-wishes-replit/src/tabs/TrackerTab.jsx (new file)
import React from 'react';
// import necessary shared components from AppCore

export default function TrackerTab({ state, dispatch, activeBanners, bannerEndDate, ...props }) {
  // Move [TAB-TRACKER] section content here
  return (
    // ... existing tracker JSX
  );
}
```

```jsx
// In App.jsx, replace inline tab content with:
import { lazy, Suspense } from 'react';
const TrackerTab = lazy(() => import('./tabs/TrackerTab'));
const EventsTab = lazy(() => import('./tabs/EventsTab'));
// ... etc.

// In render:
<Suspense fallback={<div className="flex items-center justify-center h-64"><Sparkles className="animate-spin" /></div>}>
  {activeTab === 'tracker' && <TrackerTab state={state} dispatch={dispatch} ... />}
</Suspense>
```

> This is a structural recommendation. Full implementation spans multiple passes. Flagging here for the audit record.

## HIGH Issues

### HIGH-01: `fetchWithTimeout` Has No Error Handling for `AbortError` Type

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (lines ~46–50)

**Problem:**
```js
const fetchWithTimeout = (url, options = {}) => {
  const controller = new AbortController();
  const timeoutId = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS);
  return fetch(url, { ...options, signal: controller.signal }).finally(() => clearTimeout(timeoutId));
};
```

The function returns a raw `Promise<Response>`. When the timeout fires, `controller.abort()` rejects with a `DOMException` of type `AbortError`. Callers receive this as an opaque error with no indication that it was a timeout vs. a network failure. This makes debugging user-reported "connection failed" issues extremely difficult. Additionally, the `.finally()` only clears the timeout — it doesn't catch or transform the error.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — replace fetchWithTimeout
const fetchWithTimeout = (url, options = {}) => {
  const controller = new AbortController();
  const timeoutId = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS);
  return fetch(url, { ...options, signal: controller.signal })
    .catch((err) => {
      if (err.name === 'AbortError') {
        throw new Error(`[WW] Request to ${new URL(url).hostname} timed out after ${FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS}ms`);
      }
      throw err;
    })
    .finally(() => clearTimeout(timeoutId));
};
```

### HIGH-02: Image URL Allowlist (`ALLOWED_IMAGE_HOSTS`) is Checked Nowhere in Visible Code

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (line ~83)

**Problem:** The constant `ALLOWED_IMAGE_HOSTS` is defined but the `App.jsx` excerpt (first 40,000 chars of 715,589) does not show any validation function that actually uses it. If the validation exists deeper in the file, it may be bypassed in admin panel custom image URL inputs (`bannerForm.charImages`, `bannerForm.weapImages`, `bannerForm.standardCharImg`, etc.), since the form state is set via `updateBannerForm` which does no sanitization.

If a user (or admin) enters an arbitrary URL like `https://evil.com/tracker.gif`, it would be rendered as an `<img>` src, enabling:
- IP tracking via image requests
- CSRF-like attacks if the URL points to an API endpoint
- XSS via SVG images (if rendered in certain contexts)

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:** Create and apply a validation utility:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — add after ALLOWED_IMAGE_HOSTS definition
const isAllowedImageUrl = (url) => {
  if (!url || typeof url !== 'string') return false;
  try {
    const parsed = new URL(url);
    if (parsed.protocol !== 'https:') return false;
    return ALLOWED_IMAGE_HOSTS.some(host => 
      parsed.hostname === host || parsed.hostname.endsWith('.' + host)
    );
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
};

// Sanitize image URLs before rendering — use in any <img> src or background-image
const sanitizeImageUrl = (url, fallback = '') => {
  return isAllowedImageUrl(url) ? url : fallback;
};
```

Then apply in the `updateBannerForm` callback for image fields:

```js
const updateBannerForm = useCallback((field, value) => {
  // Validate image URL fields
  const imageFields = ['standardCharImg', 'standardWeapImg', 'wwImg', 'dpImg', 'toaImg', 'irImg', 'drImg'];
  if (imageFields.includes(field) || field.startsWith('charImages.') || field.startsWith('weapImages.')) {
    if (value && !isAllowedImageUrl(value)) {
      toast?.('Invalid image URL — must be from an allowed host', 'error');
      return;
    }
  }
  setBannerForm(prev => ({ ...prev, [field]: value }));
}, [toast]);
```

### HIGH-03: Admin Panel Has No Rate Limiting on Password Attempts (Client-Side)

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (lines ~107–117)

**Problem:** The admin lockout mechanism reads `ww-admin-lockout` from `localStorage`, but:
1. A user can clear `localStorage` to bypass the lockout entirely.
2. The lockout constants (`MAX_ADMIN_ATTEMPTS = 5`, `ADMIN_LOCKOUT_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000`) are generous.
3. There's no server-side rate limiting mentioned.
4. The admin password hash (`ADMIN_HASH`) is imported from `AppCore` and lives in the client bundle — an attacker can extract it and brute-force offline.

While server-side enforcement is a backend concern, the frontend should make the UX more robust.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix (frontend hardening):**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — enhance admin lockout init
const [adminLockedUntil, setAdminLockedUntil] = useState(() => {
  try {
    const lockoutUntil = localStorage.getItem('ww-admin-lockout');
    const failCount = parseInt(localStorage.getItem('ww-admin-fails') || '0', 10);
    if (lockoutUntil && Date.now() < parseInt(lockoutUntil, 10)) {
      return parseInt(lockoutUntil, 10);
    }
    if (lockoutUntil) {
      localStorage.removeItem('ww-admin-lockout');
      localStorage.removeItem('ww-admin-fails');
    }
    // Progressive lockout: double the lockout time for each set of 5 failures
    if (failCount >= MAX_ADMIN_ATTEMPTS * 3) {
      const extendedLockout = Date.now() + ADMIN_LOCKOUT_MS * 4;
      localStorage.setItem('ww-admin-lockout', String(extendedLockout));
      return extendedLockout;
    }
  } catch {}
  return false;
});
```

> **Backend dependency note:** The Backend Agent should implement server-side admin authentication rather than relying on a client-side hash comparison.

### HIGH-04: `useRef` for `stateRef` is Declared but Never Updated in Visible Code

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (line ~127)

**Problem:**
```js
const stateRef = useRef(state);
```

This ref is initialized with `state` but is never updated with `stateRef.current = state` in a visible `useEffect`. If the ref is used deeper in the file (in event handlers, intervals, or timeouts), it would always hold the **initial** state, causing stale closure bugs. This is a common React anti-pattern when the synchronizing effect is forgotten.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:** Add a synchronization effect immediately after the ref declaration:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — after line ~127
const stateRef = useRef(state);
useEffect(() => {
  stateRef.current = state;
}, [state]);
```

If this effect already exists deeper in the file (beyond the truncated region), this issue can be downgraded. But since the truncation is at 40K chars and state management is typically near the top, it's likely missing.

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js` — `CURRENT_BANNERS.characters` (lines ~78–81), `CURRENT_BANNERS.weapons` (lines ~82–85)

**Problem:** Characters and weapons in `CURRENT_BANNERS` have `id` fields (`'luuk-herssen'`, `'galbrena'`, etc.), but in `BANNER_HISTORY` entries, characters are referenced by name strings only (e.g., `characters: ['Luuk Herssen', 'Galbrena']`). If any list rendering iterates over `BANNER_HISTORY` and uses `index` as a key (which is the default anti-pattern), reordering or filtering will cause React reconciliation bugs — wrong data attached to wrong DOM nodes, ghost state in controlled inputs, etc.

### HIGH-06: Excessive Unused Lucide Imports in `App.jsx`

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (line ~16)

**Problem:** The import line pulls in **47 named icons** from `lucide-react`:
```js
import { AlertCircle, AlertTriangle, Archive, Award, BarChart3, BookmarkPlus, Calculator, Calendar, Check, ChevronDown, ClipboardList, Clover, Crown, Diamond, Download, Fish, Flame, Gamepad2, Gift, Heart, Info, Minus, Monitor, Plus, RefreshCcw, Search, Settings, Shield, Smartphone, Sparkles, Star, Sword, Swords, Target, TrendingDown, TrendingUp, Trophy, Upload, User, Users, X, Zap } from 'lucide-react';
```

Even though Vite tree-shakes unused exports, each named import forces the bundler to resolve and include the icon's SVG path data in the initial parse. With 47 icons, many of which may only be used in one tab, this adds unnecessary weight to the main chunk.

Additionally, icons like `Fish`, `Clover`, `Crown`, `Diamond`, `Gamepad2`, `Shield`, `Smartphone`, `Monitor` are unlikely to all be used — several may be dead imports from removed features.

**Severity:** HIGH (performance)

**Fix:** Audit usage of each icon. Remove any that are not referenced in the JSX. For the remaining ones, consider dynamic imports for tab-specific icons:

```js
// Only import icons actually used in the top-level shell
import { BarChart3, Calculator, Calendar, Settings, Star, X } from 'lucide-react';

// Tab-specific icons can be imported within the tab component files
// (once tab extraction per CRIT-03 is done)
```

Without access to the full 700KB file, I cannot list which are dead. The fix action is: **grep each icon name in the full `App.jsx`; remove any with zero JSX usage.**

### HIGH-07: `getServerOffset` Silently Falls Back on Unknown Server — Should Surface Warning to User

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js` (lines in `getServerOffset` function)

**Problem:**
```js
if (!serverData) {
  console.warn(`[WW] Unknown server "${server}", defaulting to Europe (UTC+1)`);
  return 1;
}
```

This silently defaults to Europe UTC+1, which means:
- An Asia player whose server value is corrupted sees wrong banner end times (off by 7 hours).
- Timer countdowns display incorrect values.
- Event schedules are wrong.

A `console.warn` is invisible to the user. This should surface a toast notification.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:** Return a structured result that callers can use to show a warning:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js — getServerOffset
const getServerOffset = (server, atDate) => {
  const serverData = SERVERS[server];
  if (!serverData) {
    console.warn(`[WW] Unknown server "${server}", defaulting to Europe (UTC+1)`);
    // Return default offset; caller should validate server before calling
    return 1;
  }
  // ... rest unchanged
};
```

And in the consumer (`App.jsx`), validate server on use:

```jsx
// In WhisperingWishesInner, after state is loaded:
useEffect(() => {
  if (state.server && !SERVERS[state.server]) {
    toast?.(`Unknown server "${state.server}" — defaulting to Europe. Please update in Settings.`, 'warning');
    dispatch({ type: 'SET_SERVER', payload: 'Europe' });
  }
}, [state.server]);
```

## MEDIUM Issues

### MED-01: `DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS` Reads `window.matchMedia` at Module Scope

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (lines ~96–97)

**Problem:**
```js
const DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS = {
  // ...
  animationsEnabled: typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.matchMedia ? !window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches : true
};
```

This runs at **module evaluation time**, not at component mount. In SSR or prerendering environments (which Vite supports), `window` may exist but `matchMedia` may not behave correctly. More importantly, this value is computed once and never re-evaluated — if the user changes their OS reduced-motion preference while the app is open, `DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS.animationsEnabled` won't update.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:** Use a lazy initializer that respects runtime changes:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS
const DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS = {
  // ... other fields unchanged ...
  animationsEnabled: true // default; will be overridden at mount
};

// Inside WhisperingWishesInner:
useEffect(() => {
  if (typeof window === 'undefined' || !window.matchMedia) return;
  const mql = window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)');
  
  const handler = (e) => {
    setVisualSettings(prev => ({ ...prev, animationsEnabled: !e.matches }));
  };
  
  // Set initial value
  setVisualSettings(prev => ({ ...prev, animationsEnabled: !mql.matches }));
  
  // Listen for changes
  mql.addEventListener('change', handler);
  return () => mql.removeEventListener('change', handler);
}, []);
```

---

### MED-02: `firebaseWriteTimestamps` Map Grows Without Bound

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (lines ~94–101)

**Problem:**
```js
const firebaseWriteTimestamps = new Map();
```

Every unique path key written to Firebase creates an entry in this `Map` that is never cleaned up. In a long-running session where the user interacts with many features (leaderboard, presence, profile), this Map grows indefinitely. While the values are just numbers (8 bytes each), the keys are strings of arbitrary length.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:** Add a cleanup mechanism:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — replace firebaseWriteTimestamps section
const firebaseWriteTimestamps = new Map();
const FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS = 5000;
const FIREBASE_RATE_LIMIT_MAX_ENTRIES = 100;

const checkFirebaseRateLimit = (pathKey) => {
  const now = Date.now();
  const lastWrite = firebaseWriteTimestamps.get(pathKey) || 0;
  if (now - lastWrite < FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS) return false;
  
  firebaseWriteTimestamps.set(pathKey, now);
  
  // Prune old entries if map grows too large
  if (firebaseWriteTimestamps.size > FIREBASE_RATE_LIMIT_MAX_ENTRIES) {
    const staleThreshold = now - FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS * 10;
    for (const [key, ts] of firebaseWriteTimestamps) {
      if (ts < staleThreshold) firebaseWriteTimestamps.delete(key);
    }
  }
  
  return true;
};
```

---

### MED-03: `hashUidForStorage` Uses `crypto.subtle` Which is Unavailable on HTTP

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (lines ~86–91)

**Problem:**
```js
const hashUidForStorage = async (uid) => {
  if (!uid) return null;
  try {
    const buf = await crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', new TextEncoder().encode('ww-uid-' + uid));
    return Array.from(new Uint8Array(buf)).map(b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('').slice(0, 32);
  } catch { return uid; } // fallback if crypto unavailable (HTTP)
};
```

The fallback returns the **raw UID** — the exact thing hashing was supposed to prevent. On HTTP origins (local dev, some deployed environments), `crypto.subtle` is undefined, so every UID goes to Firebase in cleartext.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:** Implement a software SHA-256 fallback or use a simpler hash:

```js
const hashUidForStorage = async (uid) => {
  if (!uid) return null;
  
  // Try Web Crypto first (HTTPS only)
  if (typeof crypto !== 'undefined' && crypto.subtle) {
    try {
      const buf = await crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', new TextEncoder().encode('ww-uid-' + uid));
      return Array.from(new Uint8Array(buf)).map(b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('').slice(0, 32);
    } catch {}
  }
  
  // Fallback: simple non-reversible hash (not cryptographic, but better than raw UID)
  let hash = 0x811c9dc5; // FNV-1a offset basis
  const str = 'ww-uid-' + uid;
  for (let i = 0; i < str.length; i++) {
    hash ^= str.charCodeAt(i);
    hash = Math.imul(hash, 0x01000193); // FNV prime
  }
  // Convert to hex and pad to 32 chars
  const h1 = (hash >>> 0).toString(16).padStart(8, '0');
  // Run again with different seed for more bits
  let hash2 = 0x1a2b3c4d;
  for (let i = 0; i < str.length; i++) {
    hash2 ^= str.charCodeAt(i);
    hash2 = Math.imul(hash2, 0x01000193);
  }
  const h2 = (hash2 >>> 0).toString(16).padStart(8, '0');
  return (h1 + h2 + h1.split('').reverse().join('') + h2.split('').reverse().join('')).slice(0, 32);
};
```

---

### MED-04: `presenceSessionId` Uses `generateUniqueId` Which May Produce Predictable IDs

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (line ~154)

**Problem:**
```js
const presenceSessionId = useRef('s_' + generateUniqueId().replace(/-/g, '').slice(0, 12));
```

The `generateUniqueId` function (in `appcore-data.js`) has three tiers:
1. `crypto.randomUUID()` — good
2. `crypto.getRandomValues()` — good
3. `Math.random()` + `Date.now()` — predictable

If tier 3 is used, session IDs are guessable. For presence tracking, this means an attacker could forge session IDs and inflate/deflate active player counts.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:** The fix is already partially in place (the tiered approach). Add a length check to ensure sufficient entropy:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — line ~154
const presenceSessionId = useRef(() => {
  // Ensure we get at least 12 chars of cryptographic randomness
  try {
    const arr = new Uint8Array(9); // 9 bytes = 12 base64 chars
    crypto.getRandomValues(arr);
    return 's_' + Array.from(arr, b => b.toString(36)).join('').slice(0, 12);
  } catch {
    return 's_' + generateUniqueId().replace(/-/g, '').slice(0, 12);
  }
});
// Note: useRef with a function initializer — need to call it:
// Actually useRef doesn't support lazy init like useState. Fix:
const [presenceSessionIdValue] = useState(() => {
  try {
    const arr = new Uint8Array(9);
    crypto.getRandomValues(arr);
    return 's_' + Array.from(arr, b => b.toString(36)).join('').slice(0, 12);
  } catch {
    return 's_' + generateUniqueId().replace(/-/g, '').slice(0, 12);
  }
});
const presenceSessionId = useRef(presenceSessionIdValue);
```

### MED-05: Banner Form Initialization Doesn't Handle Missing Banner Data Gracefully

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (lines ~158–174, `buildBannerForm`)

**Problem:**
```js
const buildBannerForm = useCallback((banners) => ({
  version: banners.version || '1.0',
  phase: String(banners.phase ?? 1),
  // ...
  charsJson: JSON.stringify(banners.characters, null, 2),
  weapsJson: JSON.stringify(banners.weapons, null, 2),
  charImages: Object.fromEntries((banners.characters || []).map((c, i) => [i, c.imageUrl || ''])),
  // ...
}), []);
```

If `banners` is `null` or `undefined` (e.g., `getActiveBanners()` returns falsy due to a data error), every property access throws. The `|| []` guards on `characters` are inconsistent — `charsJson` uses `banners.characters` directly (would stringify `undefined`), while `charImages` uses `banners.characters || []`.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```js
const buildBannerForm = useCallback((banners) => {
  const b = banners || {};
  const chars = b.characters || [];
  const weaps = b.weapons || [];
  return {
    version: b.version || '1.0',
    phase: String(b.phase ?? 1),
    startDate: b.startDate?.slice(0, 16) || '',
    endDate: b.endDate?.slice(0, 16) || '',
    charsJson: JSON.stringify(chars, null, 2),
    weapsJson: JSON.stringify(weaps, null, 2),
    charImages: Object.fromEntries(chars.map((c, i) => [i, c.imageUrl || ''])),
    charImagePositions: Object.fromEntries(chars.map((c, i) => [i, c.imagePosition || ''])),
    weapImages: Object.fromEntries(weaps.map((w, i) => [i, w.imageUrl || ''])),
    weapImagePositions: Object.fromEntries(weaps.map((w, i) => [i, w.imagePosition || ''])),
    standardCharImg: b.standardCharBannerImage || '',
    standardWeapImg: b.standardWeapBannerImage || '',
    wwImg: b.whimperingWastesImage || '',
    dpImg: b.doubledPawnsImage || '',
    toaImg: b.towerOfAdversityImage || '',
    irImg: b.illusiveRealmImage || '',
    drImg: b.dailyResetImage || '',
  };
}, []);
```

### MED-06: `TRACKER_CATEGORIES` Uses Magic Strings for Colors

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (line ~102)

**Problem:**
```js
const TRACKER_CATEGORIES = [['character', 'Resonators', 'yellow'], ['weapon', 'Weapons', 'pink'], ['standard', 'Standard', 'cyan']];
```

These are nested arrays with positional meaning — `[0]` is the key, `[1]` is the label, `[2]` is the color. This is fragile; any misindexing silently returns the wrong value. Destructuring `[type, label, color]` everywhere is error-prone.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```js
const TRACKER_CATEGORIES = [
  { key: 'character', label: 'Resonators', color: 'yellow' },
  { key: 'weapon', label: 'Weapons', color: 'pink' },
  { key: 'standard', label: 'Standard', color: 'cyan' },
];
```

And update all consumers from `cat[0]` / `cat[1]` / `cat[2]` to `cat.key` / `cat.label` / `cat.color`.

### MED-07: `HEADER_ICON` Base64 Image (~2.6 KB) Inlined in JS Module

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js` (line ~8)

**Problem:** The app icon is a ~2.6 KB base64 PNG embedded directly in JavaScript. This:
1. Cannot be cached independently by the browser (it's part of the JS bundle)
2. Is ~33% larger than the raw binary (base64 overhead)
3. Must be parsed by the JS engine, adding to startup time

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:** Move to a static asset:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js — line 8
// Replace base64 with an import (Vite handles this natively)
// In the consuming component:
// import headerIcon from '../assets/header-icon.png';
// Then use: <img src={headerIcon} ... />

// For now, export a reference path instead:
const HEADER_ICON = '/assets/header-icon.png';
```

Place the decoded PNG file at `whispering-wishes-replit/public/assets/header-icon.png`.

### MED-08: `getServerAdjustedEnd` Called with Possibly Undefined `activeBanners.endDate`

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (line ~134)

**Problem:**
```js
const bannerEndDate = useMemo(() => getServerAdjustedEnd(activeBanners.endDate, state.server), [activeBanners.endDate, state.server]);
```

If `getActiveBanners()` returns an object without `endDate` (e.g., between banner cycles when no banner is active), `activeBanners.endDate` is `undefined`. The behavior of `getServerAdjustedEnd(undefined, ...)` depends on its implementation (not in this chunk), but likely produces `NaN` or an invalid date, which would then propagate to `CountdownTimer`.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```js
const bannerEndDate = useMemo(() => {
  if (!activeBanners?.endDate) return null;
  return getServerAdjustedEnd(activeBanners.endDate, state.server);
}, [activeBanners?.endDate, state.server]);
```

And ensure `CountdownTimer` handles a `null` end date gracefully (display "No active banner" or similar).

### MED-09: `CHARACTER_DATA` Has Inconsistent `element` Values — "Electro" vs Game's "Electro"

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js` — CHARACTER_DATA section

**Problem:** Wuthering Waves uses the element name **"Electro"** in-game, and this is consistent in the data file. However, the `Rover` entry has `element: 'Spectro'` only, despite the description saying "multiple Resonance forms (Spectro, Havoc, Aero)." If the Collection tab shows element icons/colors based on this field, Rover will always show as Spectro regardless of the user's chosen form. Also, there is no "Electro" element in WuWa — the game uses **"Electro"** (which is correct as-is). *(Scratch that — WuWa indeed uses "Electro".)* 

Actually, upon re-reading: the element names used (`Spectro`, `Aero`, `Electro`, `Fusion`, `Glacio`, `Havoc`) are all correct WuWa element names. No inconsistency here. **Retracting this issue.**

```js
  return {
```

### MED-10: `calculateLuckRating` Returns `null` for Edge Case `avgPity === 0`

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js` — `calculateLuckRating` function

**Problem:**
```js
if (isNaN(avg) || avg <= 0) return null;
```

An average pity of exactly 0 would mean the user got a 5★ on their very first pull. While extremely unlikely, it's not impossible. The function returns `null` for this case, which means the luckiest possible outcome shows "No luck rating" in the UI.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```js
if (isNaN(avg) || avg < 0) return null; // Allow avg === 0 (pity 1 = 0 pulls wasted)
// Clamp to minimum of 1 for the calculation
const avg = Math.max(1, parseFloat(avgPity));
```

Wait — pity count starts at 1, not 0. A 5★ on pull 1 means avgPity = 1, not 0. So `avg <= 0` correctly filters invalid data. **Adjusting severity to LOW — the guard is correct; the edge case is theoretical.**

## LOW Issues

### LOW-01: `currentYear` Computed at Module Scope — Stale Across Midnight on New Year's Eve

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (line ~75)

**Problem:**
```js
const currentYear = new Date().getFullYear();
```

If a user has the app open at 11:59 PM on December 31st, the copyright year in the footer will be wrong until they refresh. Minor but technically a bug.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:** Compute in render or use a memo:

```js
// In the component body:
const currentYear = useMemo(() => new Date().getFullYear(), []);
// Or simply inline: © {new Date().getFullYear()}
```

### LOW-02: `VISUAL_SETTINGS_KEY` and `IMAGE_FRAMING_KEY` Use Different Version Suffixes

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (lines ~89–91)

**Problem:**
```js
const VISUAL_SETTINGS_KEY = 'whispering-wishes-visual-settings-v3';
const IMAGE_FRAMING_KEY = 'whispering-wishes-image-framing-v1';
const TROPHY_OVERRIDES_KEY = 'whispering-wishes-trophy-overrides-v1';
```

The visual settings key is at `v3` while the others are at `v1`. If a future migration changes the data format, the inconsistent versioning makes it unclear which keys need bumping. This isn't a bug but a maintenance risk.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:** Document the versioning strategy in a comment:

```js
// localStorage keys — bump the suffix when the schema changes
// v3 = visual settings were restructured in app v3.0; others haven't changed since v1
const VISUAL_SETTINGS_KEY = 'whispering-wishes-visual-settings-v3';
const IMAGE_FRAMING_KEY = 'whispering-wishes-image-framing-v1';
const TROPHY_OVERRIDES_KEY = 'whispering-wishes-trophy-overrides-v1';
```

---

### LOW-03: Recharts Imports May Include Unused Components

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (line ~17)

**Problem:**
```js
import { XAxis, YAxis, Tooltip as RechartsTooltip, ResponsiveContainer, AreaChart, Area, BarChart, Bar, Cell } from 'recharts';
```

Recharts is a large library (~200 KB gzipped). If any of these components are unused (e.g., `AreaChart` + `Area` if only bar charts are displayed), they still get bundled. Without seeing the full file, I cannot confirm which are unused, but `Cell` is only needed for custom-colored bar segments.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:** Audit usage in the full file. If `AreaChart`/`Area` are unused, remove them. Consider:

```js
// Only import what's actually rendered
import { XAxis, YAxis, Tooltip as RechartsTooltip, ResponsiveContainer, BarChart, Bar, Cell } from 'recharts';
```

### LOW-04: `ADMIN_SALT` is Defined but Its Usage Is Not Visible

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (line ~77)

**Problem:**
```js
const ADMIN_SALT = 'whispering-wishes-v3-admin';
```

This constant is defined at module scope but no visible code in the first 40K chars of `App.jsx` uses it. It may be used deeper in the file for the admin password hash comparison, but if it's not used at all, it's dead code that reveals implementation details.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:** Verify usage in full file. If unused, remove it. If used, move it adjacent to its usage site for readability.

### LOW-05: `MIN_ZOOM` / `MAX_ZOOM` Defined but Context-Free

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (lines ~78–79)

**Problem:**
```js
const MIN_ZOOM = 100;
const MAX_ZOOM = 300;
```

These appear to relate to the collection card image zoom feature (`collectionZoom: 120` in `DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS`). However, `DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS.collectionZoom` defaults to 120, and `MIN_ZOOM` is 100 — so the relationship isn't enforced. If a slider uses `MIN_ZOOM`/`MAX_ZOOM` as bounds but the default starts at 120, there's no validation that the stored value from localStorage falls within `[100, 300]`.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:** Add validation in the localStorage load:

```js
// In the useEffect that loads visual settings from localStorage:
useEffect(() => {
  if (!storageAvailable) return;
  try {
    const saved = localStorage.getItem(VISUAL_SETTINGS_KEY);
    if (saved) {
      const parsed = JSON.parse(saved);
      setVisualSettings(prev => {
        const merged = { ...prev };
        for (const key of Object.keys(prev)) {
          if (parsed[key] !== undefined && parsed[key] !== null) {
            merged[key] = parsed[key];
          }
        }
        // Clamp numeric values to valid ranges
        merged.collectionZoom = Math.min(MAX_ZOOM, Math.max(MIN_ZOOM, merged.collectionZoom || 120));
        // Clamp all percentage values to [0, 100]
        for (const key of ['fadePosition', 'fadeIntensity', 'pictureOpacity',
          'standardFadePosition', 'standardFadeIntensity', 'standardOpacity',
          'shadowFadePosition', 'shadowFadeIntensity', 'shadowOpacity',
          'collectionFadePosition', 'collectionFadeIntensity', 'collectionOpacity']) {
          if (typeof merged[key] === 'number') {
            merged[key] = Math.min(100, Math.max(0, merged[key]));
          }
        }
        return merged;
      });
    }
  } catch {}
}, []);
```

### LOW-06: `LEADERBOARD_DISPLAY_LIMIT = 20` — No Pagination or "Show More" UX Pattern Implied

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (line ~73)

**Problem:** The leaderboard limit is hardcoded at 20. For a companion app with potentially hundreds of active players, showing only 20 entries without a "show more" or pagination mechanism means users ranked 21+ can never see themselves. This is a UX limitation, not a bug.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix (UX recommendation):**

```js
// Consider adding a "Show my rank" button that scrolls to the user's position
// Or increase the limit with virtual scrolling for performance
const LEADERBOARD_DISPLAY_LIMIT = 50; // Show more by default
// Add a "Your rank: #X" indicator at the top of the leaderboard
```

### LOW-07: Empty `catch` Blocks Swallow Errors Silently

**File:** Multiple locations in `App.jsx` and `appcore-data.js`

**Problem:** Patterns like:
```js
try { ... } catch {} // line ~109 in App.jsx (admin lockout)
try { ... } catch {} // line ~141 in App.jsx (trophy overrides)
try { ... } catch { return uid; } // line ~91 in App.jsx (hashUidForStorage)
try { ... } catch {} // in generateUniqueId
```

Empty `catch` blocks make debugging impossible. If localStorage is full, if JSON is malformed, or if crypto APIs fail, there's zero indication of what went wrong.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:** At minimum, log in development:

```js
// Utility for silent-in-prod, logged-in-dev error handling
const silentCatch = (err, context = '') => {
  if (import.meta.env?.DEV) {
    console.warn(`[WW] Silent catch${context ? ` in ${context}` : ''}:`, err);
  }
};

// Usage:
try { ... } catch (err) { silentCatch(err, 'admin lockout init'); }
```

---

### LOW-08: `haptic` Utility Has Redundant `try/catch` Wrapping

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js` (lines ~12–19)

**Problem:**
```js
const haptic = {
  light: () => { try { navigator?.vibrate?.(10); } catch {} },
  // ...
};
```

The optional chaining (`?.`) already prevents `TypeError` if `navigator` or `vibrate` is undefined. The `try/catch` is redundant since `vibrate()` doesn't throw — it returns `true`/`false`. The only scenario where it could throw is if `navigator` exists but `vibrate` is a getter that throws, which doesn't happen in any browser.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:**
```js
const haptic = {
  light: () => { navigator?.vibrate?.(10); },
  medium: () => { navigator?.vibrate?.(25); },
  heavy: () => { navigator?.vibrate?.(50); },
  success: () => { navigator?.vibrate?.([15, 50, 15]); },
  warning: () => { navigator?.vibrate?.([30, 30, 30]); },
  error: () => { navigator?.vibrate?.([50, 50, 80]); },
};
```

---

### LOW-09: `Rover` Character Data Has Multiple Possible Elements Not Reflected in Data Model

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js` — `CHARACTER_DATA['Rover']`

**Problem:**
```js
'Rover': { rarity: 5, element: 'Spectro', weapon: 'Sword', role: 'Sub DPS',
  desc: 'The protagonist with multiple Resonance forms (Spectro, Havoc, Aero).',
```

The description mentions 3 element forms, but `element` is a single string `'Spectro'`. If the Collection tab filters or groups by element, Rover will only appear under Spectro. Players using Havoc Rover or Aero Rover won't find their character in the correct element filter.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:** Add an `elements` array field:

```js
'Rover': { 
  rarity: 5, 
  element: 'Spectro', // Primary/default
  elements: ['Spectro', 'Havoc', 'Aero'], // All available forms
  weapon: 'Sword', 
  role: 'Sub DPS',
  desc: 'The protagonist with multiple Resonance forms (Spectro, Havoc, Aero).',
  // ...
},
```

Then update any element filter logic to check `elements` array if present, falling back to `element`.

### LOW-10: `agent/package.json` — `@anthropic-ai/sdk` Dependency Exposed in Repository

**File:** `agent/package.json` (line 10)

**Problem:**
```json
"dependencies": {
  "@anthropic-ai/sdk": "^0.39.0"
}
```

This is the update agent's dependency, not the frontend's. However, if the repository is public, this reveals that the update pipeline uses Anthropic's API, which could be leveraged for social engineering attacks (e.g., "I'm the Anthropic agent, grant me access").

**Severity:** LOW (frontend scope — noting for completeness)

**Fix:** Not a frontend concern, but recommend the Backend Agent add `agent/` to `.gitignore` for public repos, or document it clearly.

## CRITICAL Issues

### C-01: `handleFocus` function body is truncated/incomplete in CountdownTimer
**File:** `appcore-components.jsx` — near line ~460 (truncated boundary)
**Problem:** The `CountdownTimer` component's `useEffect` registers a `handleFocus` event listener, but the code is cut off mid-assignment: `const handleFocus = () => { updateTim`. This means the function body is syntactically incomplete. If the actual source file has this truncation, the entire app will fail to parse and crash on load. Even if this is a display artifact, the cleanup return for this effect is invisible, meaning the `focus` and `visibilitychange` listeners may never be removed, causing memory leaks.

**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Analysis:** Based on the pattern established above (visibility handler has `stopInterval()` and `startInterval()`), the `handleFocus` handler should call `updateTimer()` as an immediate catch-up. The cleanup function must remove all three listeners and stop the interval.

**Corrected code (complete useEffect block for CountdownTimer):**

```jsx
useEffect(() => {
  let intervalId = null;

  const updateTimer = () => {
    const end = currentEndRef.current;
    const t = getTimeRemaining(end);
    if (t.expired && recalcFnRef.current) {
      const newEnd = recalcFnRef.current();
      setCurrentEnd(newEnd);
      setTime(getTimeRemaining(newEnd));
      expiredRef.current = false;
    } else {
      setTime(t);
      if (t.expired && !expiredRef.current) {
        expiredRef.current = true;
        if (onExpireRef.current) setTimeout(onExpireRef.current, 500);
      }
    }
  };

  const startInterval = () => {
    if (intervalId) return;
    updateTimer();
    intervalId = setInterval(updateTimer, 1000);
  };

  const stopInterval = () => {
    if (intervalId) { clearInterval(intervalId); intervalId = null; }
  };

  startInterval();

  const handleVisibilityChange = () => {
    if (document.visibilityState === 'visible') {
      updateTimer();
      startInterval();
    } else {
      stopInterval();
    }
  };
  document.addEventListener('visibilitychange', handleVisibilityChange);

  const handleFocus = () => { updateTimer(); };
  window.addEventListener('focus', handleFocus);

  return () => {
    stopInterval();
    document.removeEventListener('visibilitychange', handleVisibilityChange);
    window.removeEventListener('focus', handleFocus);
  };
}, []); // stable deps — all values accessed via refs
```

## HIGH Issues

### H-01: `TabErrorBoundary` does not reset error state on tab change
**File:** `appcore-components.jsx` — `TabErrorBoundary` class (~line 340–375)
**Problem:** When a tab crashes, the error boundary catches it and shows "Try Again." However, if the user navigates to a different tab (changing `children` prop), the error boundary still shows the crash screen because `hasError` is never reset on prop change. React class component error boundaries do not auto-reset when children change.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Corrected code:**

```jsx
class TabErrorBoundary extends React.Component {
  constructor(props) {
    super(props);
    this.state = { hasError: false, error: null };
  }
  static getDerivedStateFromError(error) {
    return { hasError: true, error };
  }
  static getDerivedStateFromProps(nextProps, prevState) {
    // Reset error when tab changes (tabName prop changes)
    if (prevState.prevTabName !== undefined && prevState.prevTabName !== nextProps.tabName) {
      return { hasError: false, error: null, prevTabName: nextProps.tabName };
    }
    return { prevTabName: nextProps.tabName };
  }
  componentDidCatch(error, info) {
    console.error(`[${this.props.tabName || 'Tab'}] Crash:`, error, info?.componentStack);
  }
  render() {
    if (this.state.hasError) {
      return (
        <div className="kuro-calc space-y-3 tab-content">
          <div className="kuro-card">
            <div className="kuro-card-inner">
              <div className="kuro-body text-center py-8">
                <AlertCircle size={32} className="mx-auto mb-3 text-red-400" />
                <div className="text-white font-bold text-sm mb-1">Something went wrong</div>
                <p className="text-gray-400 text-xs mb-4">The {this.props.tabName || 'tab'} tab encountered an error.</p>
                <button 
                  onClick={() => this.setState({ hasError: false, error: null })}
                  className="kuro-btn active-cyan text-xs px-4 py-2"
                  aria-label={`Retry loading the ${this.props.tabName || 'tab'} tab`}
                >
                  Try Again
                </button>
                {this.state.error && (
                  <details className="mt-3 text-left">
                    <summary className="text-gray-400 text-[9px] cursor-pointer">Error details</summary>
                    <pre className="mt-1 p-2 bg-black/50 rounded text-red-400 text-[10px] overflow-x-auto whitespace-pre-wrap">{this.state.error.message}</pre>
                  </details>
                )}
              </div>
            </div>
          </div>
        </div>
      );
    }
    return this.props.children;
  }
}
```

### H-02: CharacterDetailModal missing keyboard accessibility on close-overlay click
**File:** `appcore-components.jsx` — `CharacterDetailModal` (~line 100)
**Problem:** The modal backdrop `<div>` has an `onClick={onClose}` handler but no `role`, `tabIndex`, or `onKeyDown` handler. Screen reader users and keyboard-only users cannot dismiss the modal by activating the backdrop. While `useEscapeKey` handles Escape, the backdrop itself is not an interactive element per WCAG.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Corrected code (backdrop div):**

```jsx
<div 
  className="fixed inset-0 z-[100] flex items-center justify-center p-4 bg-black/80 backdrop-blur-sm"
  onClick={onClose}
  onKeyDown={(e) => { if (e.key === 'Enter' || e.key === ' ') { e.preventDefault(); onClose(); } }}
  role="button"
  tabIndex={-1}
  aria-label="Close dialog"
  ref={focusTrapRef}
>
```

Apply the same fix to `WeaponDetailModal`'s backdrop div (~line 270):

```jsx
<div 
  className="fixed inset-0 z-[100] flex items-center justify-center p-4 bg-black/80 backdrop-blur-sm"
  onClick={onClose}
  onKeyDown={(e) => { if (e.key === 'Enter' || e.key === ' ') { e.preventDefault(); onClose(); } }}
  role="button"
  tabIndex={-1}
  aria-label="Close dialog"
  ref={focusTrapRef}
>
```

**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` — `OnboardingModal` (~line 220)
**Problem:** The onboarding modal uses `fixed inset-0` overlay but does not disable body scroll. On mobile, users can scroll the page behind the modal, which is both a UX issue and confusing for screen reader users navigating behind the modal.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Corrected code (add body scroll lock effect):**

```jsx
const OnboardingModal = ({ onComplete }) => {
  const [step, setStep] = useState(0);
  const focusTrapRef = useFocusTrap(true);
  useEscapeKey(true, onComplete);

  // Prevent body scroll while onboarding modal is open
  useEffect(() => {
    const originalOverflow = document.body.style.overflow;
    document.body.style.overflow = 'hidden';
    return () => { document.body.style.overflow = originalOverflow; };
  }, []);

  const steps = [
    // ... (unchanged)
  ];
  // ... rest unchanged
};
```

This same pattern should be applied inside `CharacterDetailModal` and `WeaponDetailModal` in `appcore-components.jsx`:

```jsx
// Add at top of each modal component, after hooks:
useEffect(() => {
  const originalOverflow = document.body.style.overflow;
  document.body.style.overflow = 'hidden';
  return () => { document.body.style.overflow = originalOverflow; };
}, []);
```

### H-04: Toast system has no max-toast limit — potential DOM flooding
**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` — `ToastProvider` (~line 155)
**Problem:** The `addToast` callback appends to the array without limit. If code rapidly fires toasts (e.g., in a loop or from an event storm), the DOM could accumulate hundreds of toast elements before timeouts clear them, causing layout thrashing and potential freezing.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Corrected code:**

```jsx
const MAX_TOASTS = 5;

const addToast = useCallback((message, type = 'info', duration = 3000) => {
  const id = generateUniqueId();
  setToasts(prev => {
    const next = [...prev, { id, message, type }];
    // Keep only the most recent MAX_TOASTS
    return next.length > MAX_TOASTS ? next.slice(-MAX_TOASTS) : next;
  });
  setTimeout(() => setToasts(prev => prev.filter(t => t.id !== id)), duration);
  if (type === 'success') haptic.success();
  else if (type === 'error') haptic.error();
  else if (type === 'warning') haptic.warning();
  else haptic.light();
}, []);
```

---

### H-05: PWAProvider injects meta tags without checking existing non-WW tags
**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` — `PWAProvider` (~line 55–70)
**Problem:** The check `document.querySelector(`meta[name="${name}"]`)` prevents duplicate insertion but does NOT check for already existing meta tags set by the HTML template or framework (e.g., Vite injects `viewport` meta). If the HTML already has `<meta name="viewport">`, the code skips insertion — which is correct. But the cleanup only removes tags with `data-ww="true"`, which is safe. However, the `viewport` meta is critical: if the HTML template does NOT have it, the dynamically injected one gets removed on unmount (e.g., HMR), leaving the page without a viewport tag.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Corrected code (don't remove viewport on cleanup):**

```jsx
return () => {
  window.removeEventListener('beforeinstallprompt', handleBeforeInstall);
  window.removeEventListener('appinstalled', handleAppInstalled);
  window.removeEventListener('online', handleOnline);
  window.removeEventListener('offline', handleOffline);
  // Clean up only our injected DOM elements — except viewport which is critical
  metaTags.forEach(({ name }) => {
    if (name === 'viewport') return; // Never remove viewport meta
    const el = document.querySelector(`meta[name="${name}"][data-ww="true"]`);
    if (el) el.remove();
  });
};
```

## MEDIUM Issues

### M-01: `TabButton` indicator animation runs on every render cycle due to missing dep guard
**File:** `appcore-components.jsx` — `TabButton` useEffect (~line 395)
**Problem:** The useEffect for the tab indicator depends on `[active, tabRef]`. Since `tabRef` is a ref object (stable reference), this is fine. However, `requestAnimationFrame` is called without cancellation in the cleanup, meaning if the component re-renders rapidly (e.g., during fast tab switching), multiple rAF callbacks can queue up and race.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code:**

```jsx
useEffect(() => {
  let rafId = null;
  try {
    if (active && btnRef.current && tabRef?.current) {
      rafId = requestAnimationFrame(() => {
        const btn = btnRef.current;
        const nav = tabRef?.current;
        if (!btn || !nav) return;
        const indicator = nav.querySelector('.tab-indicator');
        if (indicator) {
          indicator.style.left = `${btn.offsetLeft + btn.offsetWidth * 0.2}px`;
          indicator.style.width = `${btn.offsetWidth * 0.6}px`;
          indicator.style.background = `linear-gradient(90deg, rgba(237,175,24,0.6), rgba(237,175,24,1), rgba(237,175,24,0.6))`;
          indicator.style.boxShadow = `0 0 12px rgba(237,175,24,0.5)`;
        }
      });
    }
  } catch (e) { /* ignore indicator errors */ }
  return () => { if (rafId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(rafId); };
}, [active, tabRef]);
```

---

### M-02: `generateMaskGradient` and `generateVerticalMaskGradient` are not memoized
**File:** `appcore-components.jsx` — lines ~30–65
**Problem:** These functions are called from render paths (likely in `CollectionGrid` or banner components). Each call generates a new string, which forces style recalculation. While the functions themselves are pure, the result strings are recreated every render. For frequently-rendered items (e.g., collection grid with 50+ items), this creates unnecessary garbage.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code (add simple memoization cache):**

```jsx
// Simple memo cache for mask gradients — avoids recreating identical strings
const _maskCache = new Map();
const generateMaskGradient = (fadePos, fadeIntensity) => {
  const key = `h-${fadePos}-${fadeIntensity}`;
  if (_maskCache.has(key)) return _maskCache.get(key);
  
  let result;
  if (fadePos === undefined || fadeIntensity === undefined) {
    result = 'linear-gradient(to right, rgba(0,0,0,0) 0%, rgba(0,0,0,0) 10%, rgba(0,0,0,0.15) 20%, rgba(0,0,0,0.35) 30%, rgba(0,0,0,0.6) 40%, rgba(0,0,0,0.9) 50%, rgba(0,0,0,0.9) 100%)';
  } else {
    const maxOpacity = fadeIntensity / 100;
    const endPos = fadePos;
    if (endPos <= 10) {
      result = `linear-gradient(to right, rgba(0,0,0,0) 0%, rgba(0,0,0,${maxOpacity}) ${endPos}%, rgba(0,0,0,${maxOpacity}) 100%)`;
    } else {
      const steps = [`rgba(0,0,0,0) 0%`];
      const fadeStart = Math.max(0, endPos - 40);
      if (fadeStart > 0) steps.push(`rgba(0,0,0,0) ${fadeStart}%`);
      for (let i = 1; i <= 5; i++) {
        const pos = fadeStart + (endPos - fadeStart) * (i / 5);
        const opacity = maxOpacity * (i / 5);
        steps.push(`rgba(0,0,0,${opacity.toFixed(2)}) ${pos.toFixed(0)}%`);
      }
      steps.push(`rgba(0,0,0,${maxOpacity}) 100%`);
      result = `linear-gradient(to right, ${steps.join(', ')})`;
    }
  }
  
  // Cap cache size to prevent memory leak
  if (_maskCache.size > 200) _maskCache.clear();
  _maskCache.set(key, result);
  return result;
};

const _vertMaskCache = new Map();
const generateVerticalMaskGradient = (fadePos, fadeIntensity, direction = 'bottom') => {
  const key = `v-${fadePos}-${fadeIntensity}-${direction}`;
  if (_vertMaskCache.has(key)) return _vertMaskCache.get(key);
  
  const maxOpacity = fadeIntensity / 100;
  const endPos = fadePos;
  const dir = direction === 'top' ? 'to top' : 'to bottom';
  let result;
  if (endPos <= 10) {
    result = `linear-gradient(${dir}, rgba(0,0,0,0) 0%, rgba(0,0,0,${maxOpacity}) ${endPos}%, rgba(0,0,0,${maxOpacity}) 100%)`;
  } else {
    const steps = [`rgba(0,0,0,0) 0%`];
    const fadeStart = Math.max(0, endPos - 40);
    if (fadeStart > 0) steps.push(`rgba(0,0,0,0) ${fadeStart}%`);
    for (let i = 1; i <= 5; i++) {
      const pos = fadeStart + (endPos - fadeStart) * (i / 5);
      const opacity = maxOpacity * (i / 5);
      steps.push(`rgba(0,0,0,${opacity.toFixed(2)}) ${pos.toFixed(0)}%`);
    }
    steps.push(`rgba(0,0,0,${maxOpacity}) 100%`);
    result = `linear-gradient(${dir}, ${steps.join(', ')})`;
  }
  
  if (_vertMaskCache.size > 200) _vertMaskCache.clear();
  _vertMaskCache.set(key, result);
  return result;
};
```

Better approach — extract to a computed block before JSX return:

```jsx
const WeaponDetailModal = ({ name, onClose, imageUrl }) => {
  const data = WEAPON_DATA[name];
  if (!data) return null;
  
  const focusTrapRef = useFocusTrap(true);
  useEscapeKey(true, onClose);
  const colors = WEAPON_RARITY_COLORS[data.rarity] ?? WEAPON_RARITY_COLORS[4];
  
  // Pre-compute ascension material data
  const ascensionInfo = useMemo(() => {
    if (!data.ascensionMaterials) return null;
    return {
      costs: data.rarity === 5 ? WEAPON_ASCENSION_COSTS_5 : WEAPON_ASCENSION_COSTS_4,
      forgeryTiers: FORGERY_MAT_TIERS[data.ascensionMaterials.forgery],
      commonTiers: COMMON_MAT_TIERS[data.ascensionMaterials.common],
    };
  }, [data]);

  const expCosts = useMemo(() => {
    return Object.entries(data.rarity === 5 ? WEAPON_EXP_COSTS_5 : WEAPON_EXP_COSTS_4)
      .filter(([, qty]) => qty > 0);
  }, [data.rarity]);

  // Body scroll lock
  useEffect(() => {
    const originalOverflow = document.body.style.overflow;
    document.body.style.overflow = 'hidden';
    return () => { document.body.style.overflow = originalOverflow; };
  }, []);
  
  return (
    // ... JSX unchanged except replace the IIFE:
    // Replace the ascension section with:
    // {ascensionInfo && (
    //   <div>
    //     <h3 className="text-white font-bold text-sm mb-2 flex items-center gap-2">

    //     </h3>

    //         <MaterialItem name={ascensionInfo.forgeryTiers[0]} qty={ascensionInfo.costs.forgeryT3} />
    //         <MaterialItem name={ascensionInfo.forgeryTiers[1]} qty={ascensionInfo.costs.forgeryT4} />

    //       {ascensionInfo.commonTiers && <>

    //         <MaterialItem name={ascensionInfo.commonTiers[1]} qty={ascensionInfo.costs.commonT4} />

  );
};
```

### M-05: `FocusTrapModal` component accepts `onClick` but passes it to the wrong element
**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` — `FocusTrapModal` (~line 200)
**Problem:** The `onClick` prop is passed to the outermost overlay div, but this is the same element that has `role="dialog"`. Dialogs should not be clickable — the click-to-dismiss should be on a separate backdrop element behind the content. Currently, clicking anywhere inside the dialog content also triggers the onClick (unless consumers call `e.stopPropagation()` themselves, which is fragile).

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code:**

```jsx
const FocusTrapModal = ({ isOpen, onClose, ariaLabel, children, className = '', onClick }) => {
  const focusTrapRef = useFocusTrap(isOpen);
  useEscapeKey(isOpen, onClose);
  if (!isOpen) return null;
  return (
    <div 
      ref={focusTrapRef}
      className={`fixed inset-0 z-[100] flex items-center justify-center p-4 ${className}`}
      role="dialog"
      aria-modal="true"
      aria-label={ariaLabel}
    >
      {/* Backdrop layer — handles click-to-dismiss */}
      {onClick && (
        <div
          className="absolute inset-0"
          onClick={onClick}
          aria-hidden="true"
        />
      )}
      {/* Content layer — above backdrop */}
      <div className="relative z-10 w-full flex items-center justify-center">
        {children}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
};
```

### M-06: CountdownTimer creates a new `TIMER_COLOR_MAP` lookup on every render
**File:** `appcore-components.jsx` — `CountdownTimer` component (near truncation point)
**Problem:** `TIMER_COLOR_MAP` is correctly defined at module scope (good). However, the component itself is `memo`'d with no custom comparator. Since `recalcFn` and `onExpire` are inline functions in most call sites, `memo` is defeated every render because shallow comparison sees new function references. The refs pattern partially mitigates the effect issue but `memo` still causes a wasted shallow compare pass.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Recommendation:** Add a custom comparator to CountdownTimer's memo:

```jsx
const CountdownTimer = memo(({ endDate, color = 'yellow', compact = false, alwaysShow = false, onExpire, recalcFn }) => {
  // ... component body unchanged
}, (prev, next) => {
  return prev.endDate === next.endDate &&
         prev.color === next.color &&
         prev.compact === next.compact &&
         prev.alwaysShow === next.alwaysShow;
  // Intentionally omit onExpire and recalcFn — they are stored in refs
});
CountdownTimer.displayName = 'CountdownTimer';
```

**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` — line 7

Actually checking: `useMemo` is used for `contextValue` in ToastProvider. All imports appear used. No issue here — false positive on review. **Retracted.**

### M-08: `KuroStyles` CSS animation `badgeRotate` runs infinitely with no `prefers-reduced-motion` guard
**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` — `KuroStyles` component, `.luck-badge::before` animation (~line 480 in CSS)
**Problem:** The `badgeRotate` animation spins continuously at 8s per cycle. Multiple animations (`shimmer`, `borderGlow`, `pulseScale`, `trophyShine`, `badgeRotate`) run infinitely with no reduced motion media query. Users with vestibular disorders or motion sensitivity will be affected. WCAG 2.1 SC 2.3.3 requires respecting `prefers-reduced-motion`.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code (add at end of KuroStyles CSS):**

```css
/* ═══ REDUCED MOTION ═══ */
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  *, *::before, *::after {
    animation-duration: 0.01ms !important;
    animation-iteration-count: 1 !important;
    transition-duration: 0.01ms !important;
  }
  .tab-content,
  .tab-content > .kuro-card,
  .tab-content > div > .kuro-card {
    animation: none !important;
  }
  .luck-badge::before {
    animation: none !important;
  }
  .pulse-subtle {
    animation: none !important;
  }
  .trophy-badge {
    animation: none !important;
  }
  .pity-ring-fill {
    transition: none !important;
  }
}
```

```jsx
      <div className="min-w-0 flex-1">
        <div className="text-[9px] text-gray-300 truncate leading-tight">{name}</div>
```

### M-10: `hideOnError` sets `visibility: hidden` but parent container may still occupy space for layout
**File:** `appcore-components.jsx` — line 15
**Problem:** The comment says "Use visibility:hidden instead of display:none to prevent layout shift (CLS)." However, `visibility: hidden` still occupies space — this is actually *desired* to prevent CLS. The issue is that broken images leave invisible boxes that confuse screen readers. The `alt` text becomes unreadable because the image is hidden but still in the accessibility tree.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code:**

```jsx
const hideOnError = (e) => {
  e.target.style.visibility = 'hidden';
  e.target.setAttribute('aria-hidden', 'true');
  e.target.alt = ''; // Clear alt to remove from accessibility tree
};
```

### M-11: AppErrorBoundary uses inline styles but hardcoded color values that don't match the design system
**File:** `appcore-components.jsx` — `AppErrorBoundary` (~line 380)
**Problem:** The component uses inline styles (necessary since CSS may not have loaded if the app crashed), but the background color `#080c12` should match the CSS variable for consistency. The `rgba(6,182,212,0.2)` for the "Try Again" button uses cyan but the app's primary brand color is gold. This inconsistency could confuse users about the app identity.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code (align to gold brand color):**

```jsx
render() {
  if (this.state.hasError) {
    return (
      <div style={{ minHeight: '100vh', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', background: '#080c14', color: '#fff', fontFamily: 'system-ui, sans-serif', padding: '2rem' }}>
        <div style={{ textAlign: 'center', maxWidth: 420 }}>
          <div style={{ fontSize: 48, marginBottom: 16 }}>⚠️</div>
          <h1 style={{ fontSize: 20, fontWeight: 700, marginBottom: 8 }}>Whispering Wishes crashed</h1>
          <p style={{ color: '#9ca3af', fontSize: 14, marginBottom: 24 }}>Something unexpected went wrong. Your data is safe in local storage.</p>
          <button
            onClick={() => this.setState({ hasError: false, error: null })}
            style={{ padding: '10px 24px', background: 'rgba(237,175,24,0.2)', border: '1px solid rgba(237,175,24,0.4)', color: '#fef08a', borderRadius: 8, cursor: 'pointer', fontSize: 14, marginRight: 8 }}
          >
            Try Again
          </button>
          <button
            onClick={() => window.location.reload()}
            style={{ padding: '10px 24px', background: 'rgba(255,255,255,0.1)', border: '1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.2)', color: '#d1d5db', borderRadius: 8, cursor: 'pointer', fontSize: 14 }}
          >
            Reload Page
          </button>
          {this.state.error && (
            <details style={{ marginTop: 16, textAlign: 'left' }}>
              <summary style={{ color: '#6b7280', fontSize: 11, cursor: 'pointer' }}>Error details</summary>
              <pre style={{ marginTop: 8, padding: 12, background: 'rgba(0,0,0,0.5)', borderRadius: 8, color: '#f87171', fontSize: 10, overflow: 'auto', whiteSpace: 'pre-wrap' }}>{this.state.error.message}</pre>
            </details>
          )}
        </div>
      </div>
    );
  }
  return this.props.children;
}
```

### M-12: `TabButton` creates inline arrow function for `onClick` defeating `memo`
**File:** `appcore-components.jsx` — `TabButton` (~line 390)
**Problem:** The component does `onClick={() => { haptic.light(); onClick(); }}` inside the render. Since `TabButton` is `memo`'d, but the parent likely passes a new `onClick` each render (e.g., `() => setTab('calc')`), memo is defeated anyway. The internal wrapper also creates an additional closure. However, the real issue is that parent call sites pass inline callbacks which break memo.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Recommendation:** Document that parent must memoize `onClick` for `TabButton`'s `memo` to work, OR switch to a custom comparator:

```jsx
const TabButton = memo(({ active, onClick, children, tabRef, tabId }) => {
  // ... body unchanged
}, (prev, next) => {
  return prev.active === next.active && prev.tabId === next.tabId;
  // onClick and children are stable enough in this app — tabs don't change text
});
TabButton.displayName = 'TabButton';
```

### M-13: CSS `.tab-content` negative margins assume parent padding of 0.75rem
**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` — KuroStyles CSS (~line 300)
**Problem:** The comment says "Negative margins must match parent's horizontal padding (0.75rem / 12px). If parent padding changes, update these values together." This is a maintenance hazard. If the parent padding is updated in one place (likely in main app layout), the tab-content breaks with visible gutters or overflows.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Recommendation:** Use CSS custom property to link them:

```css
:root {
  /* ... existing vars ... */
  --tab-content-inset: 0.75rem;
}

.tab-content {
  animation: tabFadeIn 0.35s cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1);
  margin-left: calc(-1 * var(--tab-content-inset));
  margin-right: calc(-1 * var(--tab-content-inset));
  padding: var(--tab-content-inset);
}
```

### M-14: `appcore-engine.js` — `reducer` IMPORT_HISTORY doesn't validate `action.history` is an array
**File:** `appcore-engine.js` — `reducer` case `IMPORT_HISTORY` (~line 370)
**Problem:** The `deduplicateMerge` function checks `if (!incoming || incoming.length === 0)` which handles null/undefined, but if `action.history` is a non-array truthy value (e.g., a string or object from malformed import data), `.filter()` and `.map()` will throw. This is a frontend concern because the import is triggered from the Profile tab UI.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code (add array validation):**

```jsx
case ACTION.IMPORT_HISTORY: {
  const newProfile = { ...state.profile, importedAt: new Date().toISOString(), uid: action.uid || state.profile.uid };
  
  const deduplicateMerge = (existing, incoming) => {
    // Validate incoming is actually an array
    if (!Array.isArray(incoming) || incoming.length === 0) return existing || [];
    if (!Array.isArray(existing) || existing.length === 0) return incoming;
    const makeKey = (p) => `${p.timestamp || ''}|${p.name || ''}|${p.rarity || ''}|${p.id || ''}`;
    const existingKeys = new Set(existing.map(makeKey));
    const newEntries = incoming.filter(p => !existingKeys.has(makeKey(p)));
    if (newEntries.length === 0) return existing;
    return [...existing, ...newEntries].sort((a, b) => new Date(a.timestamp || 0) - new Date(b.timestamp || 0));
  };
  
  // ... rest unchanged
}
```

---

### M-15: `appcore-engine.js` — `calcStats` returns string-typed numeric values
**File:** `appcore-engine.js` — `calcStats` return object (~line 490)
**Problem:** Values like `successRate`, `p1`–`p7`, `expectedCopies`, `stddev` are returned as strings via `.toFixed()`. This forces every consumer to parse them back to numbers for comparisons or math. The UI components will need to do `parseFloat(stats.successRate)` or risk string concatenation bugs (e.g., `stats.p1 > 50` comparing string to number).

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Recommendation:** Return numbers and let the UI format:

```jsx
return {
  successRate: parseFloat(successRate.toFixed(1)),
  p1: parseFloat(pGe(1).toFixed(1)),
  p2: parseFloat(pGe(2).toFixed(1)),
  p3: parseFloat(pGe(3).toFixed(1)),
  p4: parseFloat(pGe(4).toFixed(1)),
  p5: parseFloat(pGe(5).toFixed(1)),
  p6: parseFloat(pGe(6).toFixed(1)),
  p7: parseFloat(pGe(7).toFixed(1)),
  missingPulls,
  missingAstrite: missingPulls * ASTRITE_PER_PULL,
  fourStarCount,
  featuredFourStarCount,
  pity4,
  expectedCopies: parseFloat(stats.expected.toFixed(2)),
  stddev: parseFloat(stats.stddev.toFixed(2)),
  expectedPullsToTarget: Math.ceil(expectedToTarget),
  worstCase,
};
```

**Note:** This change requires auditing all UI consumers to ensure they format with `.toFixed()` at display time instead. Flagging as a dependency for Pass 3.

### L-08: `saveToStorage` returns `true` when storage is unavailable
**File:** `appcore-engine.js` — `saveToStorage` (~line 300)
**Problem:** `if (!storageAvailable) return true;` — returning `true` (success) when storage is actually unavailable is misleading. The caller may think the save succeeded.

**Severity:** LOW

**Corrected code:**

```jsx
const saveToStorage = (state) => {
  if (!storageAvailable) return false; // Storage unavailable — save did not happen
  try {
    const data = JSON.stringify(state);
    if (data.length > 4 * 1024 * 1024) {
      console.warn('Storage approaching limit:', (data.length / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(1) + 'MB');
    }
    localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, data);
    return true;
  } catch (e) {
    console.error('Save failed (storage full?):', e);
    return false;
  }
};
```

```jsx
const ONBOARDING_STEPS = [
  { title: "Welcome to Whispering Wishes!", icon: 'Sparkles', desc: "Your companion for Wuthering Waves Convene planning.", gradient: 'from-neutral-900/30 via-neutral-900/20 to-yellow-900/30', border: 'border-yellow-500/30', bg: 'bg-yellow-500/20', color: '#edaf18' },
  { title: "Import Your History", icon: 'Upload', desc: "Go to the Profile tab and import data from wuwatracker.com.", gradient: 'from-neutral-900/30 via-neutral-900/20 to-cyan-900/30', border: 'border-cyan-500/30', bg: 'bg-cyan-500/20', color: '#22d3ee' },
  { title: "Track Your Banners", icon: 'Target', desc: "View current banners, pity progress, and time remaining.", gradient: 'from-neutral-900/30 via-neutral-900/20 to-orange-900/30', border: 'border-orange-500/30', bg: 'bg-orange-500/20', color: '#fb923c' },
  { title: "Build Your Collection", icon: 'LayoutGrid', desc: "Track all your Resonators and weapons.", gradient: 'from-neutral-900/30 via-neutral-900/20 to-purple-900/30', border: 'border-purple-500/30', bg: 'bg-purple-500/20', color: '#a855f7' },
  { title: "Calculate Your Odds", icon: 'Calculator', desc: "See your chances based on pity and resources.", gradient: 'from-neutral-900/30 via-neutral-900/20 to-emerald-900/30', border: 'border-emerald-500/30', bg: 'bg-emerald-500/20', color: '#34d399' },
  { title: "View Analytics", icon: 'BarChart3', desc: "Check your luck rating, charts, and Convene history.", gradient: 'from-neutral-900/30 via-neutral-900/20 to-pink-900/30', border: 'border-pink-500/30', bg: 'bg-pink-500/20', color: '#f472b6' },
  { title: "You're Ready!", icon: 'CheckCircle', desc: "Good luck on your Convenes, Rover!", gradient: 'from-neutral-900/30 via-neutral-900/20 to-yellow-900/30', border: 'border-yellow-500/30', bg: 'bg-yellow-500/20', color: '#edaf18' }
];

const ONBOARDING_ICONS = { Sparkles: <Sparkles size={32} />, Upload: <Upload size={32} />, Target: <Target size={32} />, LayoutGrid: <LayoutGrid size={32} />, Calculator: <Calculator size={32} />, BarChart3: <BarChart3 size={32} />, CheckCircle: <CheckCircle size={32} /> };

const OnboardingModal = ({ onComplete }) => {
  const [step, setStep] = useState(0);
  const focusTrapRef = useFocusTrap(true);
  useEscapeKey(true, onComplete);
  
  useEffect(() => {
    const originalOverflow = document.body.style.overflow;
    document.body.style.overflow = 'hidden';
    return () => { document.body.style.overflow = originalOverflow; };
  }, []);

  const s = ONBOARDING_STEPS[step];
  const icon = ONBOARDING_ICONS[s.icon];
  
  return (
    <div className="fixed inset-0 flex items-center justify-center z-[100] p-4 bg-black/90" role="dialog" aria-modal="true" aria-label="Welcome to Whispering Wishes" ref={focusTrapRef}>
      <div className={`relative overflow-hidden rounded-2xl border ${s.border} bg-gradient-to-r ${s.gradient} w-full max-w-xs`} style={{ backgroundColor: 'rgba(12, 16, 24, 0.12)', backdropFilter: 'blur(6px)', zIndex: 5 }}>
        <div className="absolute right-0 top-0 bottom-0 w-1/2 pointer-events-none" aria-hidden="true">
          <div className={`absolute right-4 top-1/2 -translate-y-1/2 w-20 h-20 rounded-full ${s.bg} blur-2xl opacity-40`} />
          <div className={`absolute right-12 top-1/4 w-10 h-10 rounded-full ${s.bg} blur-xl opacity-25`} />
        </div>
        <button onClick={onComplete} className="absolute top-3 right-3 z-20 text-[11px] min-h-[44px] min-w-[44px] px-3 py-2 rounded text-gray-400 hover:text-gray-300 transition-colors flex items-center justify-center" style={{background:'rgba(255,255,255,0.05)'}}>Skip</button>
        <div className="relative z-10 p-5 pt-8 text-center" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true">
          <div className={`inline-flex items-center justify-center w-12 h-12 rounded-xl ${s.bg} border ${s.border} mb-3`} style={{color: s.color}}>
            {icon}
          </div>
          <h4 className="font-bold text-sm text-gray-200 mb-1">{s.title}</h4>
          <p className="text-gray-400 text-[10px]">{s.desc}</p>
        </div>
        <div className="flex justify-center gap-1.5 pb-3" role="group" aria-label={`Step ${step + 1} of ${ONBOARDING_STEPS.length}`}>
          {ONBOARDING_STEPS.map((_, i) => (
            <div key={i} className={`h-1 rounded-full transition-all ${i === step ? s.bg : 'bg-white/10'}`} style={{ width: i === step ? '14px' : '5px' }} aria-hidden="true" />
          ))}
        </div>
        <div className="p-3 flex justify-between items-center" style={{borderTop:'1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.05)'}}>
          <div className="w-12">
            {step > 0 && (
              <button onClick={() => setStep(step - 1)} className="text-[11px] min-h-[44px] px-4 py-2 rounded text-gray-400 hover:text-gray-300 transition-colors" style={{background:'rgba(255,255,255,0.05)'}}>Back</button>
            )}
          </div>
          <div>
            {step < ONBOARDING_STEPS.length - 1 ? (
              <button onClick={() => setStep(step + 1)} className="text-[11px] min-h-[44px] px-4 py-2 rounded text-gray-400 hover:text-gray-300 transition-colors" style={{background:'rgba(255,255,255,0.05)'}}>Next</button>
            ) : (
              <button onClick={onComplete} className="text-[11px] min-h-[44px] px-4 py-2 rounded border border-emerald-500/30 bg-emerald-500/20 text-emerald-400 font-medium">Get Started</button>
            )}
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
};
```

### L-13: `WeaponDetailModal` is not memoized while `CharacterDetailModal` also isn't
**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`
**Problem:** Neither detail modal is wrapped in `memo`. Since they're conditionally rendered (when a user clicks an item), this is not a performance concern during normal rendering. However, if a parent re-renders while a modal is open (e.g., timer tick), the entire modal tree re-renders unnecessarily.

**Severity:** LOW

**Recommendation:** Consider wrapping both modals in `memo` with name-based comparison:

```jsx
const CharacterDetailModal = memo(({ name, onClose, imageUrl, framing, infoFraming, getImageFraming }) => {
  // ... body unchanged
}, (prev, next) => prev.name === next.name);

const WeaponDetailModal = memo(({ name, onClose, imageUrl }) => {
  // ... body unchanged
}, (prev, next) => prev.name === next.name);
```

## CRITICAL Issues

### ISSUE C-01: Service Worker Version Hardcoded — Cache Will Never Properly Bust for Users

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js` — Lines 8–11
**Problem:** `APP_VERSION` is hardcoded to `'3.2.3'` in the service worker file. This means the SW cache names (`ww-app-v3.2.3`, `ww-images-v3.2.3`, `ww-cdn-v3.2.3`) will NOT change when the app version is bumped in `appcore-data.js`. Users will be served stale cached content indefinitely because the activate event only purges caches that don't match the hardcoded version. This is the single most impactful frontend bug: **users may never see updates**.

**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Corrected Code:**
The version must be injected at build time. Since this is a Vite project (evidenced by `main.jsx` + module script), use a build-time replacement strategy:

**Option A — Vite plugin (recommended):**

Create `whispering-wishes-replit/vite-sw-version.js`:
```js
// vite-sw-version.js — Injects APP_VERSION into sw.js at build time
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from 'fs';
import { resolve } from 'path';

export function swVersionPlugin() {
  return {
    name: 'sw-version-inject',
    closeBundle() {
      // Read the current version from appcore-data.js
      const dataFile = readFileSync(resolve('src/appcore-data.js'), 'utf-8');
      const match = dataFile.match(/APP_VERSION\s*=\s*['"]([^'"]+)['"]/);
      if (!match) {
        console.warn('[sw-version] Could not extract APP_VERSION');
        return;
      }
      const version = match[1];

      // Patch sw.js in the dist/public output
      const swPath = resolve('dist/sw.js');
      try {
        let sw = readFileSync(swPath, 'utf-8');
        sw = sw.replace(
          /const APP_VERSION = '[^']+'/,
          `const APP_VERSION = '${version}'`
        );
        writeFileSync(swPath, sw, 'utf-8');
        console.log(`[sw-version] Injected APP_VERSION = '${version}' into sw.js`);
      } catch {
        console.warn('[sw-version] dist/sw.js not found — skipping');
      }
    },
  };
}
```

Then in `vite.config.js`, add:
```js
import { swVersionPlugin } from './vite-sw-version.js';

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [
    react(),
    swVersionPlugin(),
  ],
});
```

**Option B — Immediate guard (no build step change):**

If build config changes are out of scope, add a message-based version sync to the SW:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js — Lines 8-11 REPLACE with:
let APP_VERSION = '3.2.3'; // Fallback — overridden by app on load
let APP_CACHE = `ww-app-v${APP_VERSION}`;
let IMG_CACHE = `ww-images-v${APP_VERSION}`;
let CDN_CACHE = `ww-cdn-v${APP_VERSION}`;
const MAX_IMG_ENTRIES = 250;
```

And update the message handler at the bottom:
```js
// Handle messages — Lines 115-122 REPLACE with:
self.addEventListener('message', (event) => {
  if (event.data === 'skipWaiting') self.skipWaiting();
  if (event.data === 'clearImageCache') {
    caches.delete(IMG_CACHE).then(() => {
      event.source?.postMessage('imageCacheCleared');
    });
  }
  if (event.data?.type === 'SET_VERSION' && event.data.version) {
    APP_VERSION = event.data.version;
    APP_CACHE = `ww-app-v${APP_VERSION}`;
    IMG_CACHE = `ww-images-v${APP_VERSION}`;
    CDN_CACHE = `ww-cdn-v${APP_VERSION}`;
  }
});
```

### ISSUE C-02: Service Worker Registration Missing from Frontend Entry Point

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/main.jsx` — Lines 1–10
**Problem:** There is no service worker registration anywhere in the frontend entry point. The `sw.js` file exists in `public/` but nothing ever calls `navigator.serviceWorker.register('/sw.js')`. This means the entire caching infrastructure (offline support, CDN caching, image caching) is **completely non-functional**. The PWA features advertised by `<meta name="apple-mobile-web-app-capable">` in `index.html` do not work.

**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Corrected Code — `whispering-wishes-replit/src/main.jsx`:**
```jsx
import React from "react";
import ReactDOM from "react-dom/client";
import App from "./App.jsx";
import "./index.css";

// Register service worker for offline support and caching
if ('serviceWorker' in navigator && import.meta.env.PROD) {
  window.addEventListener('load', () => {
    navigator.serviceWorker.register('/sw.js')
      .then((registration) => {
        // Check for updates periodically
        setInterval(() => registration.update(), 60 * 60 * 1000); // hourly

        registration.addEventListener('updatefound', () => {
          const newWorker = registration.installing;
          if (newWorker) {
            newWorker.addEventListener('statechange', () => {
              if (newWorker.state === 'installed' && navigator.serviceWorker.controller) {
                // New version available — notify user via custom event
                window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('sw-update-available', {
                  detail: { registration }
                }));
              }
            });
          }
        });
      })
      .catch((err) => {
        console.warn('SW registration failed:', err);
      });
  });
}

ReactDOM.createRoot(document.getElementById("root")).render(
  <React.StrictMode>
    <App />
  </React.StrictMode>,
);
```

**Note:** The `PWAProvider` exported from `appcore-providers.jsx` (referenced in `AppCore.jsx` line 39) MAY handle SW registration internally. **Backend Agent request:** Confirm whether `PWAProvider` in `appcore-providers.jsx` registers the service worker. If it does, this issue is downgraded to MEDIUM (the `main.jsx` approach above is still more robust and standard). If it does NOT, this is confirmed CRITICAL.
