## HIGH Issues

### HIGH-1: IMGBB API Key Exposed in Request Body Without HTTPS Enforcement

**File:** `agent/lib/images.js`, lines 30-41
**Problem:** The imgbb API key is sent via `URLSearchParams` in the POST body. While imgbb's API endpoint uses HTTPS, there's no explicit validation that `IMGBB_API` is actually HTTPS. If someone changes the constant to HTTP (or a proxy intercepts), the API key leaks in plaintext. Additionally, the API key is logged indirectly through error messages that might contain the full URL.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/images.js — corrected uploadToImgbb function (lines 24-57)

const IMGBB_API = 'https://api.imgbb.com/1/upload';

/**
 * Upload an image to imgbb from a URL or base64 data.
 * Returns the imgbb URL or null on failure.
 */
export async function uploadToImgbb(imageSource, name, { isUrl = true } = {}) {
  const apiKey = process.env.IMGBB_API_KEY;
  if (!apiKey) {
    log.warn('IMGBB_API_KEY not set — cannot upload images');
    return null;
  }

  // SECURITY: Enforce HTTPS for API endpoint
  if (!IMGBB_API.startsWith('https://')) {
    log.error('IMGBB_API must use HTTPS — refusing to send API key over insecure connection');
    return null;
  }

  // SECURITY: Validate API key format (imgbb keys are 32 hex chars)
  if (!/^[a-f0-9]{32}$/i.test(apiKey)) {
    log.error('IMGBB_API_KEY has unexpected format — refusing to proceed');
    return null;
  }

  // SECURITY: Validate image source to prevent SSRF
  if (isUrl) {
    try {
      const parsed = new URL(imageSource);
      if (!['http:', 'https:'].includes(parsed.protocol)) {
        log.error(`Invalid image source protocol: ${parsed.protocol}`);
        return null;
      }
      // Block internal/private IPs
      const hostname = parsed.hostname;
      if (hostname === 'localhost' || hostname === '127.0.0.1' || hostname.startsWith('192.168.') || hostname.startsWith('10.') || hostname.startsWith('172.')) {
        log.error(`Blocked internal image URL: ${hostname}`);
        return null;
      }
    } catch {
      log.error(`Invalid image source URL: ${imageSource.slice(0, 100)}`);
      return null;
    }
  }

  try {
    const formData = new URLSearchParams();
    formData.append('key', apiKey);
    formData.append('name', sanitizeImgName(name));

    if (isUrl) {
      formData.append('image', imageSource);
    } else {
      // base64 data
      formData.append('image', imageSource);
    }

    const res = await fetch(IMGBB_API, {
      method: 'POST',
      body: formData,
      headers: { 'User-Agent': SCRAPER.userAgent },
    });

    const data = await res.json();

    if (data.success && data.data?.url) {
      const url = data.data.display_url || data.data.url;
      log.ok(`Uploaded to imgbb: ${name} → ${url}`);
      return url;
    } else {
      // SECURITY: Don't log the full error which might contain the API key
      const errMsg = data.error?.message || 'Unknown error';
      log.error(`imgbb upload failed for "${name}": ${errMsg.slice(0, 200)}`);
      return null;
    }
  } catch (err) {
    // SECURITY: Sanitize error message to prevent API key leakage
    const safeMsg = err.message?.replace(apiKey, '[REDACTED]') || 'Unknown error';
    log.error(`imgbb upload error for "${name}": ${safeMsg}`);
    return null;
  }
}
```

### HIGH-2: ReDoS Vulnerability in HTML Image URL Extraction

**File:** `agent/lib/images.js`, lines 141-144
**Problem:** The regex for extracting image URLs from HTML content is applied against potentially massive (40KB+) scraped HTML. The pattern `[^"]+` within a group followed by another character class can cause catastrophic backtracking on malformed HTML with unclosed quotes.

```javascript
const imgUrls = [...page.content.matchAll(/(?:src|data-src)="(https?:\/\/[^"]+\.(?:webp|png|jpg|jpeg)[^"]*)"/gi)]
```

Similarly in `banner-images.js` line 77:
```javascript
const imgUrls = [...p.content.matchAll(/(?:src|data-src|href)="(https?:\/\/[^"]+\.(?:webp|png|jpg|jpeg|avif)[^"]*)"/gi)]
```

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/images.js — lines 141-144, corrected regex with bounded quantifier
// Replace the matchAll regex with a safer version that limits backtracking

const imgUrls = [...page.content.matchAll(/(?:src|data-src)="(https?:\/\/[^"\s]{1,500}\.(?:webp|png|jpg|jpeg))"/gi)]
  .map(m => m[1])
  .filter(url => !url.includes('thumbnail') && !url.includes('/scale-to-width-down/'));
```

```javascript
// agent/lib/banner-images.js — line 77, same fix
const imgUrls = [...p.content.matchAll(/(?:src|data-src|href)="(https?:\/\/[^"\s]{1,500}\.(?:webp|png|jpg|jpeg|avif))"/gi)]
  .map(m => m[1])
  .filter(url => !url.includes('thumbnail') && !url.includes('icon') && !url.includes('20px'))
  .slice(0, 50);
```

### HIGH-3: Unvalidated JSON Parsing of AI Responses — No Schema Enforcement

**File:** `agent/lib/images.js` lines 84-87, `agent/lib/banner-images.js` lines 107-121, `agent/lib/meta-refresh.js` lines 74-78, `agent/lib/new-events.js` lines 68-71, `agent/lib/standard-pool.js` lines 57-60, `agent/lib/cross-verify.js` lines 65-68

**Problem:** Every Claude API response is parsed with `JSON.parse()` after a simple regex strip of markdown code fences. The parsed result is used without schema validation. If Claude returns unexpected fields, wrong types, or injected content, the downstream code trusts it blindly. This is a systemic issue across all modules.

Example from `meta-refresh.js`:
```javascript
const parsed = JSON.parse(response.replace(/```json?\n?/g, '').replace(/```/g, '').trim());
return parsed.updates || [];
```

If `parsed.updates` contains objects with `name: "'; process.exit(); //"`, this flows into regex pattern construction and `loadBufferFn` calls that write to files.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix — Create a shared validation utility:**

```javascript
// NEW FILE: agent/lib/ai-response.js
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// WW Update Agent — AI Response Parser & Validator
// Centralizes Claude response parsing with schema validation.
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

import { log } from './log.js';

/**
 * Safely parse a Claude response that should contain JSON.
 * Strips markdown code fences and validates against a schema.
 *
 * @param {string} response — raw Claude response
 * @param {Object} schema — validation schema: { field: 'type' | validator_fn }
 * @param {string} context — description for error messages
 * @returns {Object|null} — parsed and validated object, or null on failure
 */
export function parseAIResponse(response, schema = null, context = 'AI response') {
  if (!response || typeof response !== 'string') {
    log.warn(`${context}: empty or invalid response`);
    return null;
  }

  // Strip markdown code fences
  let cleaned = response
    .replace(/```json?\s*\n?/gi, '')
    .replace(/```\s*/g, '')
    .trim();

  // Try to extract JSON object/array from surrounding text
  const jsonStart = cleaned.indexOf('{');
  const jsonArrayStart = cleaned.indexOf('[');
  if (jsonStart === -1 && jsonArrayStart === -1) {
    log.warn(`${context}: no JSON structure found in response`);
    return null;
  }

  // Use whichever comes first
  const start = jsonStart >= 0 && (jsonArrayStart < 0 || jsonStart < jsonArrayStart)
    ? jsonStart : jsonArrayStart;
  cleaned = cleaned.slice(start);

  let parsed;
  try {
    parsed = JSON.parse(cleaned);
  } catch (err) {
    log.warn(`${context}: JSON parse failed — ${err.message}`);
    return null;
  }

  // Validate against schema if provided
  if (schema && typeof parsed === 'object' && parsed !== null) {
    for (const [field, validator] of Object.entries(schema)) {
      const value = parsed[field];
      if (typeof validator === 'function') {
        if (!validator(value)) {
          log.warn(`${context}: field "${field}" failed validation`);
          return null;
        }
      } else if (typeof validator === 'string') {
        // Type check
        if (validator === 'string' && typeof value !== 'string' && value !== null) {
          log.warn(`${context}: field "${field}" expected string, got ${typeof value}`);
          return null;
        }
        if (validator === 'number' && typeof value !== 'number' && value !== null) {
          log.warn(`${context}: field "${field}" expected number, got ${typeof value}`);
          return null;
        }
        if (validator === 'array' && !Array.isArray(value) && value !== null && value !== undefined) {
          log.warn(`${context}: field "${field}" expected array, got ${typeof value}`);
          return null;
        }
      }
    }
  }

  return parsed;
}

/**
 * Sanitize a string value from AI output before using in regex patterns or code.
 * Strips characters that could break regex or JS string interpolation.
 */
export function sanitizeAIString(value, maxLength = 200) {
  if (typeof value !== 'string') return '';
  return value
    .replace(/[\\`${}]/g, '')     // Remove shell/template dangerous chars
    .replace(/[\x00-\x1f]/g, '')  // Remove control characters
    .slice(0, maxLength)
    .trim();
}

/**
 * Validate a character/weapon name from AI output.
 * Names should only contain letters, numbers, spaces, hyphens, apostrophes, and periods.
 */
export function isValidGameName(name) {
  if (typeof name !== 'string') return false;
  if (name.length < 1 || name.length > 100) return false;
  return /^[a-zA-Z0-9\s\-'.,:()]+$/.test(name);
}

/**
 * Validate a URL from AI output.
 */
export function isValidUrl(url) {
  if (typeof url !== 'string') return false;
  try {
    const parsed = new URL(url);
    return ['http:', 'https:'].includes(parsed.protocol);
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
}

/**
 * Validate an ISO date string from AI output.
 */
export function isValidISODate(dateStr) {
  if (typeof dateStr !== 'string') return false;
  const d = new Date(dateStr);
  return !isNaN(d.getTime()) && d.getFullYear() >= 2024 && d.getFullYear() <= 2030;
}
```

Then update one representative consumer — `meta-refresh.js` — to use it:

```javascript
// agent/lib/meta-refresh.js — line 74-78, corrected
import { parseAIResponse, sanitizeAIString, isValidGameName } from './ai-response.js';

// Inside refreshCharacterBuilds, replace the JSON.parse block:
  try {
    const response = await askClaudeFn(
      'You are a Wuthering Waves meta analyst. Compare current build data against latest sources. Only flag genuine meta shifts. Return valid JSON only.',
      prompt,
      { maxTokens: 4096 }
    );

    const parsed = parseAIResponse(response, {
      updates: 'array',
    }, 'meta-refresh');

    if (!parsed) return [];

    // Validate each update entry
    const validUpdates = (parsed.updates || []).filter(u => {
      if (!isValidGameName(u.name)) {
        log.warn(`meta-refresh: invalid character name "${String(u.name).slice(0, 50)}"`);
        return false;
      }
      if (!['bestWeapon', 'bestEchoes', 'teams'].includes(u.field)) return false;
      if (typeof u.confidence !== 'number' || u.confidence < 0 || u.confidence > 1) return false;
      return true;
    });

    return validUpdates;
  } catch (err) {
    log.warn(`Meta refresh analysis failed: ${err.message}`);
    return [];
  }
```

### HIGH-4: Regex Pattern Injection via Unsanitized Character/Weapon Names

**File:** `agent/lib/meta-refresh.js` lines 92-93, `agent/lib/banner-images.js` lines 142-143, `agent/lib/standard-pool.js` lines 78-80, `agent/lib/images.js` lines 171-172

**Problem:** Character and weapon names from AI responses are escaped with a standard regex escape, BUT the names are first used as-is in string templates that get passed to `loadBufferFn`, which writes directly to the source file. If a name contains a single quote `'`, the replacement string `'${update.newValue}'` produces broken JS syntax in the data file.

Example from `meta-refresh.js`:
```javascript
if (update.field === 'bestWeapon' && typeof update.newValue === 'string') {
  const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?bestWeapon:\\s*)'[^']*'`);
  // ...
  loadBufferFn(buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}'${update.newValue}'`));
```

If `update.newValue` is `Daybreaker's Spine`, this produces: `bestWeapon: 'Daybreaker's Spine'` — broken JS.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/meta-refresh.js — corrected applyMetaUpdates function

export function applyMetaUpdates(updates, getBufferFn, loadBufferFn, memory, minConfidence = 0.85) {
  let changed = false;

  for (const update of updates) {
    if (update.confidence < minConfidence) continue;

    // SECURITY: Validate the name before using in regex
    if (typeof update.name !== 'string' || update.name.length > 100) continue;

    const buf = getBufferFn();
    const escaped = update.name.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');

    if (update.field === 'bestWeapon' && typeof update.newValue === 'string') {
      // FIXED: Escape single quotes in the replacement value
      const safeValue = update.newValue.replace(/'/g, "\\'");
      if (safeValue.length > 200) continue; // Sanity check

      const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?bestWeapon:\\s*)'[^']*'`);
      const match = buf.match(pattern);
      if (match) {
        loadBufferFn(buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}'${safeValue}'`));
        addChange('meta-refresh', `${update.name} bestWeapon → ${update.newValue}`);
        changed = true;
      }
    }

    if (update.field === 'bestEchoes' && Array.isArray(update.newValue)) {
      // FIXED: Validate and escape each array element
      const safeValues = update.newValue
        .filter(e => typeof e === 'string' && e.length <= 200)
        .map(e => e.replace(/'/g, "\\'"));
      if (safeValues.length === 0 || safeValues.length > 10) continue;

      const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?bestEchoes:\\s*)\\[[^\\]]*\\]`);
      const match = buf.match(pattern);
      if (match) {
        const newArr = `[${safeValues.map(e => `'${e}'`).join(', ')}]`;
        loadBufferFn(buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}${newArr}`));
        addChange('meta-refresh', `${update.name} bestEchoes updated`);
        changed = true;
      }
    }

    if (update.field === 'teams' && Array.isArray(update.newValue)) {
      // FIXED: Validate and escape each array element
      const safeValues = update.newValue
        .filter(t => typeof t === 'string' && t.length <= 300)
        .map(t => t.replace(/'/g, "\\'"));
      if (safeValues.length === 0 || safeValues.length > 10) continue;

      const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?teams:\\s*)\\[[^\\]]*\\]`);
      const match = buf.match(pattern);
      if (match) {
        const newArr = `[${safeValues.map(t => `'${t}'`).join(', ')}]`;
        loadBufferFn(buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}${newArr}`));
        addChange('meta-refresh', `${update.name} teams updated`);
        changed = true;
      }
    }
  }

  return changed;
}
```

### HIGH-5: Uncontrolled Resource Consumption in `fetchPage` — No Response Size Limit

**File:** `agent/lib/scraper.js`, lines 40-55
**Problem:** The `fetchPage` function calls `await res.text()` without limiting the response body size. A malicious or misconfigured source could return a multi-gigabyte response, causing an out-of-memory crash. The `SCRAPER.maxContentLength` trim only applies AFTER the entire body is loaded into memory.

```javascript
let content = await res.text(); // Could be gigabytes

if (extractText) {
  content = htmlToText(content); // Processing a huge string
}

// This trim only happens AFTER full body is in memory
if (content.length > SCRAPER.maxContentLength) {
  content = content.slice(0, SCRAPER.maxContentLength) + '\n\n[...TRUNCATED...]';
}
```

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/scraper.js — corrected fetchPage function (lines 18-65)

const MAX_BODY_BYTES = 5 * 1024 * 1024; // 5MB hard limit for any response

export async function fetchPage(url, { retries = 2, extractText = true } = {}) {
  const domain = getDomain(url);

  // Politeness delay per domain
  const lastFetch = lastFetchByDomain.get(domain) || 0;
  const elapsed = Date.now() - lastFetch;
  if (elapsed < SCRAPER.politenessDelay) {
    await sleep(SCRAPER.politenessDelay - elapsed);
  }

  for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= retries; attempt++) {
    try {
      log.dim(`Fetching ${url}${attempt > 0 ? ` (retry ${attempt})` : ''}`);
      lastFetchByDomain.set(domain, Date.now());

      const controller = new AbortController();
      const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), SCRAPER.timeout);

      const res = await fetch(url, {
        headers: {
          'User-Agent': SCRAPER.userAgent,
          'Accept': 'text/html,application/xhtml+xml,application/xml;q=0.9,*/*;q=0.8',
          'Accept-Language': 'en-US,en;q=0.5',
        },
        signal: controller.signal,
      });

      clearTimeout(timeout);

      if (!res.ok) {
        throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status} ${res.statusText}`);
      }

      // FIXED: Check Content-Length header before downloading body
      const contentLength = parseInt(res.headers.get('content-length') || '0', 10);
      if (contentLength > MAX_BODY_BYTES) {
        throw new Error(`Response too large: ${(contentLength / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(1)}MB exceeds ${MAX_BODY_BYTES / 1024 / 1024}MB limit`);
      }

      // FIXED: Read body with size limit using streaming
      const reader = res.body?.getReader();
      if (!reader) {
        throw new Error('Response body is not readable');
      }

      const chunks = [];
      let totalBytes = 0;

      while (true) {
        const { done, value } = await reader.read();
        if (done) break;

        totalBytes += value.length;
        if (totalBytes > MAX_BODY_BYTES) {
          reader.cancel();
          throw new Error(`Response body exceeded ${MAX_BODY_BYTES / 1024 / 1024}MB limit mid-stream`);
        }
        chunks.push(value);
      }

      const decoder = new TextDecoder();
      let content = chunks.map(c => decoder.decode(c, { stream: true })).join('') + decoder.decode();

      if (extractText) {
        content = htmlToText(content);
      }

      // Trim to max length for Claude consumption
      if (content.length > SCRAPER.maxContentLength) {
        content = content.slice(0, SCRAPER.maxContentLength) + '\n\n[...TRUNCATED...]';
      }

      log.ok(`Fetched ${domain} — ${(content.length / 1024).toFixed(1)}KB`);
      return { ok: true, content, url };

    } catch (err) {
      if (attempt === retries) {
        log.error(`Failed to fetch ${url}: ${err.message}`);
        return { ok: false, content: '', url, error: err.message };
      }
      log.warn(`Fetch attempt ${attempt + 1} failed for ${domain}: ${err.message}`);
      await sleep(2000 * (attempt + 1));
    }
  }
}
```

### HIGH-6: Race Condition in Memory File Read/Write

**File:** `agent/lib/memory.js`, lines 28-38 and 43-52
**Problem:** `loadMemory` and `saveMemory` use `readFileSync`/`writeFileSync` without any file locking. If two agent instances run concurrently (e.g., a scheduled micro run overlaps with a manual full run), they can corrupt the memory file — one reads stale data, the other overwrites, losing the first's changes.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/memory.js — corrected with file locking mechanism

import { readFileSync, writeFileSync, existsSync, mkdirSync } from 'fs';
import { resolve, dirname } from 'path';
import { PATHS } from './config.js';
import { log } from './log.js';

const MEMORY_FILE = resolve(PATHS.repoRoot, 'agent/.memory.json');
const LOCK_FILE = resolve(PATHS.repoRoot, 'agent/.memory.lock');
const MAX_ENTRIES = 200;
const LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS = 30000; // 30 second lock timeout
const LOCK_STALE_MS = 120000; // Locks older than 2 min are considered stale

/**
 * Acquire a simple file lock. Returns true if lock acquired.
 */
function acquireLock() {
  const lockDir = dirname(LOCK_FILE);
  if (!existsSync(lockDir)) mkdirSync(lockDir, { recursive: true });

  // Check for stale lock
  if (existsSync(LOCK_FILE)) {
    try {
      const lockData = JSON.parse(readFileSync(LOCK_FILE, 'utf-8'));
      const lockAge = Date.now() - lockData.timestamp;
      if (lockAge > LOCK_STALE_MS) {
        log.warn(`Removing stale memory lock (${(lockAge / 1000).toFixed(0)}s old)`);
        // Fall through to create new lock
      } else {
        return false;
      }
    } catch {
      // Corrupt lock file — remove it
    }
  }

  try {
    writeFileSync(LOCK_FILE, JSON.stringify({
      pid: process.pid,
      timestamp: Date.now(),
    }), { flag: 'wx' }); // 'wx' = exclusive create, fails if file exists
    return true;
  } catch {
    // Another process created the lock between our check and write
    return false;
  }
}

/**
 * Release the file lock.
 */
function releaseLock() {
  try {
    if (existsSync(LOCK_FILE)) {
      const lockData = JSON.parse(readFileSync(LOCK_FILE, 'utf-8'));
      // Only release our own lock
      if (lockData.pid === process.pid) {
        const { unlinkSync } = require('fs');
        unlinkSync(LOCK_FILE);
      }
    }
  } catch {
    // Best effort
  }
}

/**
 * Load memory from disk with lock protection.
 * Returns { runs: [], patches: [], failures: [] }
 */
export function loadMemory() {
  if (!existsSync(MEMORY_FILE)) {
    return { runs: [], patches: [], failures: [], checkedUrls: {} };
  }
  try {
    const raw = readFileSync(MEMORY_FILE, 'utf-8');
    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);

    // Validate structure — prevent corrupted data from propagating
    if (!parsed || typeof parsed !== 'object') {
      throw new Error('Memory file root is not an object');
    }
    if (!Array.isArray(parsed.runs)) parsed.runs = [];
    if (!Array.isArray(parsed.patches)) parsed.patches = [];
    if (!Array.isArray(parsed.failures)) parsed.failures = [];
    if (!parsed.checkedUrls || typeof parsed.checkedUrls !== 'object') parsed.checkedUrls = {};

    return parsed;
  } catch (err) {
    log.warn(`Memory file corrupted — starting fresh: ${err.message}`);
    return { runs: [], patches: [], failures: [], checkedUrls: {} };
  }
}

/**
 * Save memory to disk with lock protection.
 */
export function saveMemory(memory) {
  // Trim old entries
  if (memory.runs?.length > MAX_ENTRIES) memory.runs = memory.runs.slice(-MAX_ENTRIES);
  if (memory.patches?.length > MAX_ENTRIES) memory.patches = memory.patches.slice(-MAX_ENTRIES);
  if (memory.failures?.length > MAX_ENTRIES) memory.failures = memory.failures.slice(-MAX_ENTRIES);

  // Wait for lock with timeout
  const start = Date.now();
  while (!acquireLock()) {
    if (Date.now() - start > LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS) {
      log.warn('Could not acquire memory lock within timeout — saving anyway');
      break;
    }
    // Busy wait with small delay (sync context)
    const waitUntil = Date.now() + 100;
    while (Date.now() < waitUntil) { /* spin */ }
  }

  try {
    writeFileSync(MEMORY_FILE, JSON.stringify(memory, null, 2));
    log.dim('Memory saved');
  } catch (err) {
    log.warn(`Could not save memory: ${err.message}`);
  } finally {
    releaseLock();
  }
}

// ... rest of the file (recordRun, recordPatch, etc.) stays unchanged
```

### HIGH-7: Prototype Pollution via Unvalidated JSON in Memory/Checkpoint Files

**File:** `agent/lib/memory.js` line 18, `agent/lib/checkpoint.js` line 82
**Problem:** Both modules parse JSON files from disk with `JSON.parse()` without sanitizing against prototype pollution. If an attacker can write to `.memory.json` or `.checkpoint.json` (e.g., via a compromised dependency or a tampered repo clone), they can inject `__proto__` or `constructor` keys that pollute the Object prototype, affecting all subsequent operations.

```javascript
// memory.js
return JSON.parse(readFileSync(MEMORY_FILE, 'utf-8'));

// checkpoint.js
return JSON.parse(readFileSync(CHECKPOINT_FILE, 'utf-8'));
```

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```javascript
// Add this helper to agent/lib/memory.js and import in checkpoint.js

/**
 * Safely parse JSON, removing prototype pollution vectors.
 */
function safeJsonParse(str) {
  const parsed = JSON.parse(str);
  return sanitizeObject(parsed);
}

function sanitizeObject(obj) {
  if (obj === null || typeof obj !== 'object') return obj;
  if (Array.isArray(obj)) return obj.map(sanitizeObject);

  const clean = Object.create(null);
  for (const key of Object.keys(obj)) {
    // Block prototype pollution keys
    if (key === '__proto__' || key === 'constructor' || key === 'prototype') continue;
    clean[key] = sanitizeObject(obj[key]);
  }
  return clean;
}
```

Then replace `JSON.parse(...)` with `safeJsonParse(...)` in both `loadMemory()` and `loadRawCheckpoint()`.

### HIGH-8: Missing Timeout Cleanup (Memory Leak) in Health Check

**File:** `agent/lib/health.js`, lines 27-35
**Problem:** The `AbortController` timeout is created but if the fetch throws an error before `clearTimeout` is called, the timeout is never cleared, creating a timer leak. Over many URL checks (267+ images), this accumulates.

```javascript
async function checkUrl(url) {
  try {
    const controller = new AbortController();
    const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 8000);

    const res = await fetch(url, {
      method: 'HEAD',
      signal: controller.signal,
      // ...
    });

    clearTimeout(timeout); // Only cleared on success path
    return { url, alive: res.ok, status: res.status };
  } catch (err) {
    // TIMEOUT NEVER CLEARED ON ERROR PATH
    return { url, alive: false, status: 0, error: err.message };
  }
}
```

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/health.js — corrected checkUrl function

async function checkUrl(url) {
  const controller = new AbortController();
  const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 8000);

  try {
    const res = await fetch(url, {
      method: 'HEAD',
      signal: controller.signal,
      headers: { 'User-Agent': 'WhisperingWishes-HealthCheck/1.0' },
      redirect: 'follow',
    });

    return { url, alive: res.ok, status: res.status };
  } catch (err) {
    return { url, alive: false, status: 0, error: err.message };
  } finally {
    // FIXED: Always clear timeout, regardless of success or failure
    clearTimeout(timeout);
  }
}
```

Same pattern exists in `deploy-check.js` lines 39-49 — apply the same `finally` fix:

```javascript
// agent/lib/deploy-check.js — lines 39-52, corrected
for (const url of [productionUrl, fallbackUrl]) {
  const controller = new AbortController();
  const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 10000);

  try {
    const res = await fetch(url, {
      method: 'HEAD',
      signal: controller.signal,
      headers: { 'User-Agent': 'WhisperingWishes-DeployCheck/1.0' },
    });

    if (res.ok) {
      log.ok(`Deployment healthy: ${url} (${res.status})`);
      return { status: 'success', url, statusCode: res.status };
    } else {
      log.warn(`Deployment issue: ${url} returned ${res.status}`);
      return { status: 'failure', url, statusCode: res.status };
    }
  } catch (err) {
    log.dim(`Could not reach ${url}: ${err.message}`);
    continue;
  } finally {
    clearTimeout(timeout);
  }
}
```

### HIGH-9: Single-Quote Injection in Changelog Writing to Source File

**File:** `agent/lib/changelog.js`, lines 89-91
**Problem:** The `updateAppChangelog` function writes entries into JS source code with only a simple `.replace(/'/g, "\\'")` escape. This doesn't handle backslashes themselves. An AI-generated description containing `\' ` already would double-escape incorrectly, and a description containing `` `${...}` `` template literals would execute if the file were ever eval'd.

```javascript
const newEntries = entries.map(e =>
  `  { date: '${e.date}', category: '${e.category}', text: '${e.description.replace(/'/g, "\\'")}' }`
);
```

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/changelog.js — lines 89-91, corrected

/**
 * Safely escape a string for embedding in JS single-quoted string literal.
 */
function escapeForJsString(str) {
  return str
    .replace(/\\/g, '\\\\')     // Escape backslashes FIRST
    .replace(/'/g, "\\'")       // Then single quotes
    .replace(/\n/g, '\\n')      // Newlines
    .replace(/\r/g, '\\r')      // Carriage returns
    .replace(/\t/g, '\\t')      // Tabs
    .replace(/[\x00-\x1f]/g, '') // Remove control chars
    .slice(0, 300);             // Hard length limit
}

// Then in updateAppChangelog:
const newEntries = entries.map(e =>
  `  { date: '${escapeForJsString(e.date)}', category: '${escapeForJsString(e.category)}', text: '${escapeForJsString(e.description)}' }`
);
```

### HIGH-10: Unbounded Content Sent to Claude API — Token Cost Explosion

**File:** `agent/lib/images.js` lines 66-67, `agent/lib/banner-images.js` lines 89-101, `agent/lib/new-events.js` lines 30-38

**Problem:** Multiple modules slice content at arbitrary large sizes (30000, 40000, 12000 chars) before sending to Claude. These are per-source. When multiple sources are concatenated, the total prompt can exceed 100K+ characters (25K+ tokens input), significantly increasing costs. The `AI.maxTokens` config (8192) only caps OUTPUT, not input.

Example from `banner-images.js`:

// agent/lib/config.js — add input budget constants (add after line 81)

export const AI = {
  model: process.env.ANTHROPIC_MODEL || 'claude-sonnet-4-20250514',
  maxTokens: 8192,
  rateLimitMs: 1500,
  maxRetries: 3,
  // NEW: Input content budgets (characters) per prompt type

    perSource: 10000,      // Max chars from any single web source
    totalPrompt: 40000,    // Max total chars in any single prompt
    imageSearch: 15000,    // Max for image-finding prompts
    bannerAnalysis: 30000, // Max for banner analysis prompts

};
```

// agent/lib/banner-images.js — line 89, corrected

  const imgUrls = [...p.content.matchAll(/(?:src|data-src|href)="(https?:\/\/[^"\s]{1,500}\.(?:webp|png|jpg|jpeg|avif))"/gi)]
    .map(m => m[1])

## MEDIUM Issues

### MED-1: Inadequate Syntax Validation in `validate.js` — Misses Template Literals

**File:** `agent/lib/validate.js`, lines 104-126
**Problem:** The `checkSyntax` function tracks balanced braces/brackets/parens with a simple state machine, but it only handles single-quote, double-quote, and backtick strings. It doesn't handle template literal nesting (`${...}` inside backticks), which means template literals containing braces will cause false positives for "unbalanced braces."

Also, the function doesn't handle regex literals (`/regex/g`), so a regex like `/}/g` would decrement the brace count.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/validate.js — corrected checkSyntax function

function checkSyntax(source, errors) {
  let braces = 0, brackets = 0, parens = 0;
  let inString = false, stringChar = '';
  let templateDepth = 0; // Track nested template literal expressions

  for (let i = 0; i < source.length; i++) {
    const c = source[i];
    const prev = i > 0 ? source[i - 1] : '';

    if (inString) {
      if (stringChar === '`') {
        // Template literal — handle ${...} nesting
        if (c === '$' && i + 1 < source.length && source[i + 1] === '{') {
          templateDepth++;
          inString = false; // Exit string mode to count the expression's braces
          i++; // skip the '{'
          braces++; // count this opening brace
          continue;
        }
        if (c === '`' && prev !== '\\') {
          inString = false;
        }
      } else {
        if (c === stringChar && prev !== '\\') {
          inString = false;
        }
      }
      continue;
    }

    // Handle closing brace that ends a template expression
    if (templateDepth > 0 && c === '}') {
      braces--;
      // Check if we should re-enter template literal mode
      // (simplified: just decrement depth)
      templateDepth--;
      inString = true;
      stringChar = '`';
      continue;
    }

    if (c === "'" || c === '"' || c === '`') {
      inString = true;
      stringChar = c;
    } else if (c === '/' && i + 1 < source.length) {
      // Skip line comments
      if (source[i + 1] === '/') {
        const lineEnd = source.indexOf('\n', i);
        i = lineEnd === -1 ? source.length : lineEnd;
        continue;
      }
      // Skip block comments
      if (source[i + 1] === '*') {
        const blockEnd = source.indexOf('*/', i + 2);
        i = blockEnd === -1 ? source.length : blockEnd + 1;
        continue;
      }
    } else if (c === '{') braces++;
    else if (c === '}') braces--;
    else if (c === '[') brackets++;
    else if (c === ']') brackets--;
    else if (c === '(') parens++;
    else if (c === ')') parens--;
  }

  if (braces !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced braces: ${braces > 0 ? braces + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(braces) + ' extra closing'}`);
  if (brackets !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced brackets: ${brackets > 0 ? brackets + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(brackets) + ' extra closing'}`);
  if (parens !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced parentheses: ${parens > 0 ? parens + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(parens) + ' extra closing'}`);
}
```

### MED-2: `extractEvents` Regex Fails on Nested Braces

**File:** `agent/lib/reader.js`, lines 100-115
**Problem:** The event extraction regex uses a non-greedy `[\s\S]*?` pattern that will fail when an event body contains nested objects. The lookahead `(?=\n\s*\w+:|$)` doesn't handle the closing brace of the EVENTS object correctly, causing the last event to either not match or capture garbage.

```javascript
const eventMatches = content.matchAll(/(\w+):\s*\{([\s\S]*?)(?=\n\s*\w+:|$)/g);
```

If the last event is followed by `};` instead of another `\w+:`, the `$` anchor in the alternation may match at the wrong position.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/reader.js — corrected extractEvents function (lines 100-120)

export function extractEvents(source) {
  const events = {};
  const eventsBlock = source.match(/const EVENTS\s*=\s*\{([\s\S]*?)\n\};\s*\n/);
  if (!eventsBlock) return events;

  const content = eventsBlock[1];

  // FIXED: Match each event key followed by { ... } using a more robust approach
  // Look for top-level keys (indented with 2 spaces) followed by their block
  const eventMatches = content.matchAll(/^  (\w+):\s*\{([\s\S]*?)\},?\s*$/gm);
  for (const m of eventMatches) {
    const key = m[1];
    const body = m[2];
    const name = body.match(/name:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
    const currentEnd = body.match(/currentEnd:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || null;
    const resetType = body.match(/resetType:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
    const rewards = body.match(/rewards:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
    events[key] = { name, currentEnd, resetType, rewards };
  }

  // Fallback: if no matches with the strict pattern, try the original approach
  if (Object.keys(events).length === 0) {
    const fallbackMatches = content.matchAll(/(\w+):\s*\{([\s\S]*?)(?=\n\s{2}\w+:\s*\{|\s*$)/g);
    for (const m of fallbackMatches) {
      const key = m[1];
      const body = m[2];
      const name = body.match(/name:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
      const currentEnd = body.match(/currentEnd:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || null;
      const resetType = body.match(/resetType:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
      const rewards = body.match(/rewards:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
      events[key] = { name, currentEnd, resetType, rewards };
    }
  }

  return events;
}
```

### MED-3: `addNewEvent` Unreliable Insertion Point — Fragile Regex

**File:** `agent/lib/new-events.js`, lines 101-114
**Problem:** The function tries to find the end of the EVENTS object with two different regex patterns, neither of which is guaranteed to match. If the file structure changes slightly (e.g., a comment is added), the new event won't be inserted. Worse, it silently fails and returns `false`.

```javascript
const eventsEnd = buf.match(/(\n\};\s*\n\s*\/\/ \[SECTION:)/);
if (!eventsEnd) {
  const alt = buf.match(/(\n\};\s*\n\s*const (?:BANNER_HISTORY|SUBSCRIPTION))/);
  // ...
}
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/new-events.js — corrected addNewEvent function

export function addNewEvent(eventData, getBufferFn, loadBufferFn) {
  const buf = getBufferFn();

  // Validate eventData before inserting
  if (!eventData.key || !/^[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9]*$/.test(eventData.key)) {
    log.warn(`Invalid event key: "${eventData.key}" — must be camelCase alphanumeric`);
    return false;
  }
  if (!eventData.name || eventData.name.length > 200) {
    log.warn(`Invalid event name: "${eventData.name}"`);
    return false;
  }

  // Check if key already exists (belt-and-suspenders)
  if (buf.includes(`  ${eventData.key}: {`)) {
    log.warn(`Event key "${eventData.key}" already exists — skipping`);
    return false;
  }

  // Strategy: Find the EVENTS object's closing brace.
  // Look for the pattern: EVENTS = { ... }; followed by something
  const eventsBlockMatch = buf.match(/(const EVENTS\s*=\s*\{[\s\S]*?)(\n\};\s*\n)/);
  if (!eventsBlockMatch) {
    log.warn(`Could not find EVENTS object boundary for new event "${eventData.name}"`);
    return false;
  }

  const eventsContent = eventsBlockMatch[1];
  const closingBrace = eventsBlockMatch[2];

  // Safely escape all string values
  const esc = (s) => String(s || '').replace(/\\/g, '\\\\').replace(/'/g, "\\'").slice(0, 300);
  const currentEnd = eventData.currentEnd ? `'${esc(eventData.currentEnd)}'` : 'null';

  const entry = `  ${eventData.key}: {\n    name: '${esc(eventData.name)}', subtitle: '${esc(eventData.subtitle)}',\n    description: '${esc(eventData.description)}',\n    resetType: '${esc(eventData.resetType)}', color: '${esc(eventData.color || 'cyan')}',\n    currentEnd: ${currentEnd},\n    rewards: '${esc(eventData.rewards)}',\n    imageUrl: '' },\n`;

  // Insert before the closing brace
  const newBuf = buf.replace(eventsBlockMatch[0], eventsContent + '\n' + entry + closingBrace);
  loadBufferFn(newBuf);

  log.ok(`Added new event: ${eventData.key} ("${eventData.name}")`);
  addChange('new-event', `New event: ${eventData.name}`);
  return true;
}
```

### MED-4: Skill Files Read Without Size Limits

**File:** `agent/lib/skills.js`, lines 43-50
**Problem:** `loadSkillFile` reads files with `readFileSync` without any size validation. A corrupted or extremely large skill file would be loaded entirely into memory and then passed into Claude prompts, potentially exceeding API limits and inflating costs.

```javascript
function loadSkillFile(paths) {
  for (const p of paths) {
    if (existsSync(p)) {
      try {
        const content = readFileSync(p, 'utf-8'); // No size limit
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/skills.js — corrected loadSkillFile (lines 43-53)

const MAX_SKILL_FILE_SIZE = 512 * 1024; // 512KB max per skill file

function loadSkillFile(paths) {
  for (const p of paths) {
    if (existsSync(p)) {
      try {
        const stats = statSync(p);
        if (stats.size > MAX_SKILL_FILE_SIZE) {
          log.warn(`Skill file too large (${(stats.size / 1024).toFixed(0)}KB): ${p.split('/').pop()}`);
          continue;
        }
        const content = readFileSync(p, 'utf-8');
        log.dim(`Loaded skill: ${p.split('/').pop()} (${(content.length / 1024).toFixed(0)}KB)`);
        return content;
      } catch { continue; }
    }
  }
  return null;
}
```

Add `statSync` to the import:
```javascript
import { readFileSync, existsSync, statSync } from 'fs';
```

## 1. Dependency Health & Configuration

### ISSUE 1.1 — Vite v7.3.1 does not exist (phantom dependency)
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/package.json`, line ~15  
**Severity:** CRITICAL

The `devDependencies` lists `"vite": "^7.3.1"`. As of the latest stable releases, Vite's latest major version is **6.x** (with 5.x being the most widely used). Version 7.3.1 does not exist on npm. This will cause `npm install` to fail in a clean environment.

**Problem:** Build system is unbuildable in any fresh checkout. This likely means the lockfile was manually edited or the version was mistyped, and builds are only succeeding because a `node_modules` cache exists.

**Corrected code:**
```json
{
  "name": "whispering-wishes",

### ISSUE 1.2 — Outdated lucide-react pinned to 0.263.1
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/package.json`, line ~9  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

`lucide-react` at `^0.263.1` is significantly behind the current 0.4xx+ range. This version predates many icon additions and bug fixes. Given the large number of icons imported in `App.jsx` (line ~2: `AlertCircle, AlertTriangle, Archive, Award, BarChart3...`), there is risk that some icons referenced in newer code may not exist in this version, causing silent undefined imports.

**Corrected code:**
```json
"lucide-react": "^0.446.0",
```

**Note:** Before upgrading, verify all icon names in the import block on `App.jsx` line 2 are present in the target version's export list.

---

### ISSUE 1.3 — Agent package has no lockfile or version pinning strategy
**File:** `agent/package.json`  
**Severity:** LOW

The agent package uses `"@anthropic-ai/sdk": "^0.39.0"` with caret versioning and no lockfile mentioned. For an autonomous update agent that modifies production app data, dependency drift can introduce breaking behavioral changes silently.

**Recommendation:** Add a `package-lock.json` to the agent directory and consider using exact version pinning (`"0.39.0"` without caret) for the AI SDK dependency.

---

### ISSUE 1.4 — No shared dependency contract between agent and app
**File:** `agent/package.json` and `whispering-wishes-replit/package.json`  
**Severity:** LOW

The agent and the app are two separate packages with no workspace configuration, no shared types, and no shared constants. The agent writes data that the app reads (banners, events, characters), but there is no schema contract between them. If the agent produces a field the app doesn't expect (or vice versa), there's no compile-time or runtime validation to catch it.

**Recommendation:** Create a shared `schema/` directory or JSON Schema files that both the agent and app validate against at build/test time.

## 2. Environment & Config Consistency

### ISSUE 2.1 — Firebase credentials hardcoded as fallbacks with misleading API key
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, lines near `const FIREBASE_DB` and `const FIREBASE_API_KEY`  
**Severity:** HIGH

```javascript
const FIREBASE_DB = (typeof import.meta !== 'undefined' && import.meta.env?.VITE_FIREBASE_DB) || 'https://whispering-wishes-default-rtdb.firebaseio.com';
const FIREBASE_API_KEY = (typeof import.meta !== 'undefined' && import.meta.env?.VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY) || 'AIzaSyWhisperingWishes';
```

**Problems:**
1. The fallback `FIREBASE_API_KEY` value `'AIzaSyWhisperingWishes'` is clearly a placeholder — it will fail silently at runtime when env vars are missing, producing opaque Firebase 403 errors instead of a clear "missing configuration" message.
2. The `typeof import.meta !== 'undefined'` guard is unnecessary in a Vite project (import.meta is always defined in ESM). It suggests copy-pasted SSR code.
3. The comment says "production should always use env vars" but there is no runtime check that enforces this — a production build without `.env` will silently use broken fallbacks.

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
// Firebase configuration — loaded from environment variables.
// Fallbacks provided for local development only; production builds MUST set these.
const FIREBASE_DB = import.meta.env.VITE_FIREBASE_DB || 'https://whispering-wishes-default-rtdb.firebaseio.com';
const FIREBASE_API_KEY = import.meta.env.VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY || '';

if (!FIREBASE_API_KEY && import.meta.env.PROD) {
  console.error('[Whispering Wishes] CRITICAL: VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY is not set in production. Firebase features will not work.');
}
```

---

### ISSUE 2.2 — No `.env.example` or environment documentation visible
**File:** Project root (missing)  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

The app references two environment variables (`VITE_FIREBASE_DB`, `VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY`) but there's no `.env.example` file in the provided chunk. This means:
- New contributors won't know which env vars are needed
- CI/CD pipelines have no reference for required secrets
- The agent package may also need env vars (e.g., `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) that are undocumented

**Recommendation:** Create a `.env.example`:
```env
# Firebase Realtime Database
VITE_FIREBASE_DB=https://whispering-wishes-default-rtdb.firebaseio.com
VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY=

# Agent (if running update agent)
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=
```

## 3. Frontend-Backend Contract Issues

### ISSUE 3.1 — Banner data shape implicitly coupled between agent and app with no validation
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx` (buildBannerForm function) and `agent/package.json`  
**Severity:** HIGH

The `buildBannerForm` callback in `App.jsx` reveals the expected banner data structure:
```javascript
const buildBannerForm = useCallback((banners) => ({
  version: banners.version || '1.0',
  phase: String(banners.phase ?? 1),
  startDate: banners.startDate?.slice(0, 16) || '',
  endDate: banners.endDate?.slice(0, 16) || '',
  charsJson: JSON.stringify(banners.characters, null, 2),
  weapsJson: JSON.stringify(banners.weapons, null, 2),
  charImages: Object.fromEntries((banners.characters || []).map((c, i) => [i, c.imageUrl || ''])),
  charImagePositions: Object.fromEntries((banners.characters || []).map((c, i) => [i, c.imagePosition || ''])),
  weapImages: Object.fromEntries((banners.weapons || []).map((w, i) => [i, w.imageUrl || ''])),
  weapImagePositions: Object.fromEntries((banners.weapons || []).map((w, i) => [i, w.imagePosition || ''])),
  standardCharImg: banners.standardCharBannerImage || '',
  standardWeapImg: banners.standardWeapBannerImage || '',
  wwImg: banners.whimperingWastesImage || '',
  dpImg: banners.doubledPawnsImage || '',
  toaImg: banners.towerOfAdversityImage || '',
  irImg: banners.illusiveRealmImage || '',
  drImg: banners.dailyResetImage || '',
}), []);
```

This reveals that the app expects banners to have:
- `version`, `phase`, `startDate`, `endDate` (string)
- `characters[]` each with `imageUrl`, `imagePosition`
- `weapons[]` each with `imageUrl`, `imagePosition`
- Multiple named image fields (`standardCharBannerImage`, `whimperingWastesImage`, etc.)

**But the agent is a separate package with zero type-safety connection to this shape.** If the agent outputs `image_url` instead of `imageUrl`, or `start_date` instead of `startDate`, nothing catches it.

**Recommendation:** Create a shared schema definition:
```javascript
// shared/bannerSchema.js
export const BANNER_REQUIRED_FIELDS = [
  'version', 'phase', 'startDate', 'endDate', 'characters', 'weapons'
];

export const CHARACTER_REQUIRED_FIELDS = ['imageUrl'];
export const WEAPON_REQUIRED_FIELDS = ['imageUrl'];

export function validateBannerData(data) {
  const errors = [];
  for (const field of BANNER_REQUIRED_FIELDS) {
    if (data[field] === undefined) errors.push(`Missing required field: ${field}`);
  }
  if (Array.isArray(data.characters)) {
    data.characters.forEach((c, i) => {
      for (const f of CHARACTER_REQUIRED_FIELDS) {
        if (c[f] === undefined) errors.push(`characters[${i}] missing: ${f}`);
      }
    });
  }
  return errors;
}
```

---

### ISSUE 3.2 — Firebase data written with hashed UIDs but read path is not visible
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, `hashUidForStorage` function  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

```javascript
const hashUidForStorage = async (uid) => {
  if (!uid) return null;
  try {
    const buf = await crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', new TextEncoder().encode('ww-uid-' + uid));
    return Array.from(new Uint8Array(buf)).map(b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('').slice(0, 32);
  } catch { return uid; } // fallback if crypto unavailable (HTTP)
};
```

**Problems:**
1. The `catch` block swallows the error and falls back to using the **raw UID** as the storage key. This silently defeats the privacy protection on HTTP origins or environments where `crypto.subtle` isn't available (e.g., older WebViews, non-secure contexts).
2. The hash is truncated to 32 hex chars (128 bits) from a SHA-256 (256 bits). While 128 bits is sufficient for collision resistance in this context, the truncation should be documented.
3. There's no corresponding read-side validation visible in this chunk — we can't confirm that the same hash function is used to read data back.

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
const hashUidForStorage = async (uid) => {
  if (!uid) return null;
  try {
    // SHA-256 hash with app-specific salt, truncated to 128 bits (32 hex chars)
    // Sufficient for pseudonymous storage — not cryptographic key material
    const buf = await crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', new TextEncoder().encode('ww-uid-' + uid));
    return Array.from(new Uint8Array(buf)).map(b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('').slice(0, 32);
  } catch (e) {
    // crypto.subtle unavailable (non-HTTPS context) — log and return null to prevent
    // raw UID from being written to Firebase
    console.warn('[Whispering Wishes] crypto.subtle unavailable — UID hashing disabled. Firebase presence tracking will not work on HTTP.', e);
    return null;
  }
};
```

---

### ISSUE 3.3 — Firebase rate limiter uses in-memory Map, resets on page reload
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, `firebaseWriteTimestamps` and `checkFirebaseRateLimit`  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

```javascript
const firebaseWriteTimestamps = new Map(); // key → last write timestamp
const FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS = 5000;
const checkFirebaseRateLimit = (pathKey) => {
  const now = Date.now();
  const lastWrite = firebaseWriteTimestamps.get(pathKey) || 0;
  if (now - lastWrite < FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS) return false;
  firebaseWriteTimestamps.set(pathKey, now);
  return true;
};
```

**Problems:**
1. This Map is module-scoped (not in a component), which is correct for singleton behavior, but it resets on every page reload. A malicious user can bypass this by simply refreshing the page.
2. No mention of corresponding server-side (Firebase Security Rules) rate limiting visible in this chunk. The comment says "Firebase Security Rules handle server-side enforcement" but we can't verify that without seeing `database.rules.json`.
3. The Map will grow unbounded if many different path keys are written — no cleanup/eviction.

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
const firebaseWriteTimestamps = new Map();
const FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS = 5000;
const FIREBASE_RATE_LIMIT_MAP_MAX = 100; // prevent unbounded growth

const checkFirebaseRateLimit = (pathKey) => {
  const now = Date.now();
  const lastWrite = firebaseWriteTimestamps.get(pathKey) || 0;
  if (now - lastWrite < FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS) return false;
  firebaseWriteTimestamps.set(pathKey, now);
  // Evict stale entries if map grows too large
  if (firebaseWriteTimestamps.size > FIREBASE_RATE_LIMIT_MAP_MAX) {
    for (const [key, ts] of firebaseWriteTimestamps) {
      if (now - ts > FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS * 10) firebaseWriteTimestamps.delete(key);
    }
  }
  return true;
};
```

## 4. Data Flow Consistency

### ISSUE 4.1 — `getActiveBanners()` called both in initializer and as initial state, creating dual source of truth
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, `WhisperingWishesInner` function  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

```javascript
const [activeBanners, setActiveBanners] = useState(() => getActiveBanners());
```

The active banners are derived from `getActiveBanners()` (imported from `AppCore`), which presumably reads `CURRENT_BANNERS` data. But `activeBanners` is stored in **component state**, meaning it becomes stale if the underlying data changes (e.g., admin updates banners). The `bannerEndDate` is then derived from this potentially stale state:

```javascript
const bannerEndDate = useMemo(() => getServerAdjustedEnd(activeBanners.endDate, state.server), [activeBanners.endDate, state.server]);
```

**Problem:** If admin changes banners via the admin panel, `activeBanners` state must be explicitly updated. There's no subscription/effect that re-fetches active banners when the underlying data changes.

**Recommendation:** Either:
1. Add an effect that re-calls `getActiveBanners()` when relevant state changes, or
2. Derive `activeBanners` via `useMemo` instead of `useState` (if `getActiveBanners` is pure and deterministic based on current time + stored banner data).

---

### ISSUE 4.2 — Visual settings loaded in two phases, creating potential flash of default content
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, visual settings initialization  
**Severity:** LOW

```javascript
const [visualSettings, setVisualSettings] = useState(() => {
  return { ...DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS };
});

useEffect(() => {
  if (!storageAvailable) return;
  try {
    const saved = localStorage.getItem(VISUAL_SETTINGS_KEY);
    if (saved) {
      const parsed = JSON.parse(saved);
      setVisualSettings(prev => {
        const merged = { ...prev };
        for (const key of Object.keys(prev)) {
          if (parsed[key] !== undefined && parsed[key] !== null) merged[key] = parsed[key];
        }
        return merged;
      });
    }
  } catch {}
}, []);
```

**Problem:** The state initializes with defaults, then an effect runs post-mount to merge saved settings. This causes a render cycle where the UI briefly shows default settings before jumping to saved ones. For OLED mode toggle (`oledMode`), this can cause a visible flash from light to dark.

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
const [visualSettings, setVisualSettings] = useState(() => {
  // Attempt synchronous load from localStorage during initialization
  // to prevent flash of default visual settings
  const defaults = { ...DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS };
  if (!storageAvailable) return defaults;
  try {
    const saved = localStorage.getItem(VISUAL_SETTINGS_KEY);
    if (saved) {
      const parsed = JSON.parse(saved);
      for (const key of Object.keys(defaults)) {
        if (parsed[key] !== undefined && parsed[key] !== null) {
          defaults[key] = parsed[key];
        }
      }
    }
  } catch { /* use defaults */ }
  return defaults;
});
// Remove the post-mount useEffect for visual settings — synchronous init handles it
```

**Note:** The original code comment says "don't load from localStorage on initial load" for SSR/preview. However, this is a Vite SPA — there is no SSR. `localStorage` is always available during state initialization in this architecture. The `storageAvailable` guard is sufficient.

---

### ISSUE 4.3 — `fetchWithTimeout` AbortController signal isn't propagated to consumers for cleanup
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, `fetchWithTimeout`  
**Severity:** LOW

```javascript
const FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000;
const fetchWithTimeout = (url, options = {}) => {
  const controller = new AbortController();
  const timeoutId = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS);
  return fetch(url, { ...options, signal: controller.signal }).finally(() => clearTimeout(timeoutId));
};
```

**Problem:** If the caller passes its own `signal` in `options`, it gets overwritten by `{ ...options, signal: controller.signal }`. This means component unmount cleanup signals are silently dropped. Any component using this during an effect that cleans up on unmount will have the fetch orphaned.

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
const FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000;
const fetchWithTimeout = (url, options = {}) => {
  const controller = new AbortController();
  const timeoutId = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS);
  
  // If caller provided a signal, chain it so either signal can abort
  if (options.signal) {
    options.signal.addEventListener('abort', () => controller.abort());
  }
  
  return fetch(url, { ...options, signal: controller.signal }).finally(() => clearTimeout(timeoutId));
};
```

## 5. Architectural & Structural Issues

### ISSUE 5.1 — Monolithic App.jsx at 717,793 bytes (~718 KB)
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`  
**Severity:** HIGH

The file `App.jsx` is **717,793 bytes**. This is approximately 18,000–20,000 lines of code in a single React component file. Even though the file header mentions it imports from `AppCore.jsx`, the sheer size indicates that `App.jsx` itself contains the vast majority of the application logic, rendering, and state management.

**Problems:**
1. **Bundle splitting impossible:** Vite can't tree-shake or code-split within a single file. The entire 718KB is loaded upfront.
2. **Developer experience:** Any edit triggers re-parsing of 718KB by the dev server, HMR, and IDE.
3. **Memory pressure:** React's reconciler processes the entire component tree returned by this single function on every render.
4. **Testing impossible:** No unit-testable boundaries exist within a single function.
5. **Merge conflicts:** Any two developers touching this file simultaneously will face intractable merge conflicts.

**Recommendation (structural, not a code fix):**
Split `WhisperingWishesInner` into route-level or tab-level components:
```
src/
  App.jsx               (~100 lines: providers + router)
  tabs/
    TrackerTab.jsx
    EventsTab.jsx
    CalculatorTab.jsx
    PlannerTab.jsx
    StatsTab.jsx
    CollectionTab.jsx
    ProfileTab.jsx
  features/
    admin/AdminPanel.jsx
    presence/PresenceTracker.jsx
  components/            (already partially extracted to AppCore)
```

---

### ISSUE 5.2 — Massive import list from AppCore creates tight coupling
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, lines 22–100 (import block)  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

The import from `AppCore.jsx` contains **~90 named exports**:
```javascript
import {
  APP_VERSION, MAX_IMPORT_SIZE_MB, HEADER_ICON, haptic, PWAProvider, ToastProvider,
  useToast, useFocusTrap, useEscapeKey, FocusTrapModal, OnboardingModal,
  calculateLuckRating, KuroStyles, SERVERS, getServerOffset, getServerAdjustedEnd,
  CURRENT_BANNERS, getActiveBanners, BANNER_HISTORY, CHARACTER_DATA, WEAPON_DATA,
  // ... ~70 more exports
} from './AppCore';
```

**Problem:** `AppCore.jsx` appears to be a "god module" exporting everything — data constants, utility functions, React components, hooks, configuration, and UI primitives. This defeats module boundary benefits:
- Everything is re-evaluated when any import changes
- No clear separation between data layer, business logic, and presentation
- Vite's HMR boundary can't granularly update — any change to AppCore invalidates all dependents

**Recommendation:** Decompose AppCore into domain-specific modules:
```
src/
  data/banners.js       (CURRENT_BANNERS, BANNER_HISTORY, getActiveBanners)
  data/characters.js    (CHARACTER_DATA, ALL_5STAR_RESONATORS, etc.)
  data/weapons.js       (WEAPON_DATA, ALL_5STAR_WEAPONS, etc.)
  data/events.js        (EVENTS, SUBSCRIPTIONS)
  data/constants.js     (HARD_PITY, ASTRITE_PER_PULL, etc.)
  state/reducer.js      (initialState, reducer, STORAGE_KEY, etc.)
  state/storage.js      (loadFromStorage, saveToStorage, sanitizeStateObj)
  hooks/useToast.js
  hooks/useFocusTrap.js
  components/Card.jsx
  components/BannerCard.jsx
  // etc.
```

---

### ISSUE 5.3 — Admin panel logic embedded in main component instead of isolated
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, admin state variables  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

The `WhisperingWishesInner` component contains ~15 admin-specific state variables:
```javascript
const [showAdminPanel, setShowAdminPanel] = useState(false);
const [adminUnlocked, setAdminUnlocked] = useState(false);
const [adminPassword, setAdminPassword] = useState('');
const [adminTapCount, setAdminTapCount] = useState(0);
const adminTapTimerRef = useRef(null);
const adminTapCountRef = useRef(0);
const [adminTab, setAdminTab] = useState('banners');
const [adminMiniMode, setAdminMiniMode] = useState(false);
const [trophyOverrides, setTrophyOverrides] = useState(/* ... */);
const [trophyJsonInput, setTrophyJsonInput] = useState('');
const [activePlayersCount, setActivePlayersCount] = useState(null);
const [activePlayersHistory, setActivePlayersHistory] = useState([]);
const [presenceError, setPresenceError] = useState(null);
const [adminPlayerList, setAdminPlayerList] = useState(null);
```

**Problem:** Every regular user pays the cost of initializing, storing, and reconciling ~15 state variables and their associated refs that only an admin will ever use. This is wasted memory and render overhead for 99.9% of users.

**Recommendation:** Extract admin into a lazy-loaded component:
```javascript
const AdminPanel = React.lazy(() => import('./features/admin/AdminPanel'));

// In render:
{showAdminPanel && (
  <Suspense fallback={<div>Loading admin...</div>}>
    <AdminPanel
      activeBanners={activeBanners}
      onBannersChange={setActiveBanners}
      state={state}
      dispatch={dispatch}
    />
  </Suspense>
)}
```

## 6. Security — System-Wide Concerns

### ISSUE 6.1 — Admin authentication uses client-side hash comparison
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, `ADMIN_HASH` import and admin lockout logic  
**Severity:** HIGH

The import `ADMIN_HASH` from `AppCore` and the presence of `ADMIN_SALT`, `MAX_ADMIN_ATTEMPTS`, `ADMIN_LOCKOUT_MS` reveals a client-side admin authentication system:

```javascript
const ADMIN_SALT = 'whispering-wishes-v3-admin';
const MAX_ADMIN_ATTEMPTS = 5;
const ADMIN_LOCKOUT_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000;
```

**Problems:**
1. The admin hash is embedded in the client bundle — any user can extract it from DevTools or the built JS.
2. The lockout is stored in `localStorage` — it can be cleared by the user.
3. There's no server-side admin verification — the admin panel presumably modifies Firebase data, meaning the "admin check" is purely cosmetic.
4. `ADMIN_BANNER_KEY` (also imported) suggests banners can be modified from the admin panel — if this writes to Firebase without server-side auth, any user who finds the hash (or bypasses the check) can modify banner data for all users.

**Corrected approach (requires architectural change):**
The admin panel should:
1. Authenticate via Firebase Authentication (not a client-side hash)
2. Firebase Security Rules should enforce admin-only write paths
3. The admin hash should be removed from the client bundle entirely

**Minimum viable fix (if server-side auth is not feasible):**
```javascript
// At minimum, add a warning and ensure Firebase rules are the true enforcement layer
// database.rules.json should contain:
// "banners": { ".write": "auth !== null && auth.uid === 'ADMIN_UID'" }
// The client-side check is only a UX convenience, not a security boundary.
```

---

### ISSUE 6.2 — Image URL allowlist is client-side only
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, `ALLOWED_IMAGE_HOSTS`  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

```javascript
const ALLOWED_IMAGE_HOSTS = ['i.ibb.co', 'ibb.co', 'i.imgur.com', 'imgur.com', 'cdn.discordapp.com', 'media.discordapp.net', 'pbs.twimg.com', 'raw.githubusercontent.com', 'i.postimg.cc', 'wuwa.gg', 'wuwatracker.com'];
```

**Problem:** This allowlist is only enforced client-side. If banner data is written to Firebase (which it appears to be via the admin panel), a malicious admin or API call could write arbitrary image URLs that bypass this allowlist. Other clients would then load those URLs without the allowlist check.

**Recommendation:** The allowlist must also be enforced in Firebase Security Rules via a regex validation rule on any field that stores image URLs.

---

### ISSUE 6.3 — `presenceSessionId` uses weak entropy
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, `presenceSessionId` ref  
**Severity:** LOW

```javascript
const presenceSessionId = useRef('s_' + generateUniqueId().replace(/-/g, '').slice(0, 12));
```

The session ID depends on `generateUniqueId()` (imported from AppCore). Without seeing its implementation, if it uses `Math.random()` or `Date.now()`, the session IDs are guessable/colliding. For a presence system, collisions could cause one user to overwrite another's presence data.

**Recommendation:** Ensure `generateUniqueId` uses `crypto.randomUUID()` or `crypto.getRandomValues()`.

## 7. Dead Code, Orphaned References & Unused Exports

### ISSUE 7.1 — Large number of imports that may be unused in the truncated view
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, lines 2–3  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

The Lucide icon import block:
```javascript
import { AlertCircle, AlertTriangle, Archive, Award, BarChart3, BookmarkPlus, Calculator, Calendar, Check, ChevronDown, ClipboardList, Clover, Crown, Diamond, Download, Fish, Flame, Gamepad2, Gift, Heart, Info, Minus, Monitor, Plus, RefreshCcw, Search, Settings, Shield, Smartphone, Sparkles, Star, Sword, Swords, Target, TrendingDown, TrendingUp, Trophy, Upload, User, Users, X, Zap } from 'lucide-react';
```

This imports **44 icons**. In a 718KB file, it's likely that some of these are no longer used (especially domain-specific ones like `Fish`, `Clover`, `Gamepad2`). Each unused icon adds ~1-3KB to the bundle.

**Recommendation:** Run `npx knip` or a custom grep to verify all 44 icons are actually referenced in JSX. Remove any that aren't.

---

### ISSUE 7.2 — `currentYear` computed at module level, never refreshes
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, `const currentYear = new Date().getFullYear();`  
**Severity:** LOW

If the app is left open across a year boundary (New Year's Eve), `currentYear` will be stale. This is a minor issue but relevant for a long-running PWA that users may keep open.

**Recommendation:** Move into a `useMemo` or compute inline where needed:
```javascript
// Instead of module-level:
// const currentYear = new Date().getFullYear();
// Use inline where needed:
<span>{new Date().getFullYear()}</span>
```

---

### ISSUE 7.3 — `MIN_ZOOM` and `MAX_ZOOM` defined but context unclear
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`  
**Severity:** LOW

```javascript
const MIN_ZOOM = 100;
const MAX_ZOOM = 300;
```

These constants are defined at module scope but without clear documentation of what they control. They appear to relate to `collectionZoom` in visual settings (which defaults to 120), but the naming doesn't specify "collection" — they could be confused with page zoom or map zoom in future development.

**Recommendation:** Rename for clarity:
```javascript
const COLLECTION_MIN_ZOOM = 100;
const COLLECTION_MAX_ZOOM = 300;
```

---

## 8. Code Quality Patterns

### ISSUE 8.1 — Inconsistent error handling pattern — empty catch blocks
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, multiple locations  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

Throughout the visible code, there are multiple empty `catch` blocks:
```javascript
try { const s = localStorage.getItem(TROPHY_OVERRIDES_KEY); return s ? JSON.parse(s) : {}; } catch { return {}; }
```
```javascript
} catch {}
return false;
```
```javascript
} catch {}
}, []);
```

**Problem:** Silent error swallowing makes debugging impossible. When localStorage is corrupted, quota exceeded, or JSON is malformed, the user and developer get zero signal about what went wrong.

**Corrected pattern:**
```javascript
try {
  const s = localStorage.getItem(TROPHY_OVERRIDES_KEY);
  return s ? JSON.parse(s) : {};
} catch (e) {
  console.warn('[Whispering Wishes] Failed to load trophy overrides from storage:', e);
  return {};
}
```

Apply this pattern consistently across all catch blocks.

---

### ISSUE 8.2 — Magic numbers scattered through module constants
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, module-level constants  
**Severity:** LOW

```javascript
const STORAGE_WARNING_THRESHOLD = 3.5 * 1024 * 1024;  // What is 3.5MB? Why 3.5?
const MAX_USERNAME_LENGTH = 24;  // Why 24? Firebase limit? Display limit?
const MAX_BOOKMARK_NAME_LENGTH = 30;  // Why 30?
const LEADERBOARD_DISPLAY_LIMIT = 20;  // Why 20?
```

**Problem:** No comments explain the rationale behind these numbers. When someone needs to change them, they don't know what constraint they're relaxing.

**Recommendation:** Add rationale comments:
```javascript
// localStorage quota is typically 5-10MB; warn at 3.5MB to leave room for other apps
const STORAGE_WARNING_THRESHOLD = 3.5 * 1024 * 1024;
// Fits comfortably in the profile header without wrapping on 320px screens
const MAX_USERNAME_LENGTH = 24;
// Fits in banner bookmark dropdown without horizontal scroll
const MAX_BOOKMARK_NAME_LENGTH = 30;
// Balances scroll performance with meaningful ranking depth
const LEADERBOARD_DISPLAY_LIMIT = 20;
```

---

### ISSUE 8.3 — `stateRef` pattern without visible sync effect
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

```javascript
const stateRef = useRef(state);
```

A `stateRef` is declared to hold a mutable reference to the current state (common pattern for accessing current state in callbacks/intervals), but within the visible code chunk, there is no `useEffect` that syncs it:
```javascript
// Expected pattern (not visible in truncated code):
useEffect(() => { stateRef.current = state; });
```

**Problem:** If the sync effect exists deeper in the file (likely given the 718KB size), it's fragile — if it's accidentally removed or reordered, any callback reading `stateRef.current` will get stale data silently.

**Recommendation:** Place the sync immediately after declaration and add a guard comment:
```javascript
const stateRef = useRef(state);
// CRITICAL: Must stay in sync — callbacks and intervals read stateRef.current
useEffect(() => { stateRef.current = state; });
```

---

### ISSUE 8.4 — Inconsistent localStorage key naming convention
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, various constants  
**Severity:** LOW

```javascript
const VISUAL_SETTINGS_KEY = 'whispering-wishes-visual-settings-v3';
const IMAGE_FRAMING_KEY = 'whispering-wishes-image-framing-v1';
const TROPHY_OVERRIDES_KEY = 'whispering-wishes-trophy-overrides-v1';
// From the lockout logic:
localStorage.getItem('ww-admin-lockout');
localStorage.removeItem('ww-admin-fails');
```

**Problem:** Two naming conventions coexist:
- Long form: `whispering-wishes-visual-settings-v3`
- Short form: `ww-admin-lockout`, `ww-admin-fails`

And `STORAGE_KEY` (imported from AppCore) is likely yet another format.

**Recommendation:** Standardize all keys under one prefix convention:
```javascript
const STORAGE_PREFIX = 'ww-';
const VISUAL_SETTINGS_KEY = `${STORAGE_PREFIX}visual-settings-v3`;
const IMAGE_FRAMING_KEY = `${STORAGE_PREFIX}image-framing-v1`;
const TROPHY_OVERRIDES_KEY = `${STORAGE_PREFIX}trophy-overrides-v1`;
const ADMIN_LOCKOUT_KEY = `${STORAGE_PREFIX}admin-lockout`;
const ADMIN_FAILS_KEY = `${STORAGE_PREFIX}admin-fails`;
```

## 9. Build & Deploy Configuration

### ISSUE 9.1 — No build validation or type checking configured
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/package.json`  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

The build script is simply `"build": "vite build"`. There is:
- No TypeScript (no `tsconfig.json`, no `.ts` files)
- No ESLint configuration
- No pre-build validation step
- No test runner

For a 718KB+ application handling user data, financial-adjacent calculations (gacha pity/probability), and Firebase writes, the complete absence of any static analysis or testing is a significant risk.

**Recommendation (minimum viable):**
```json
{
  "scripts": {
    "dev": "vite",
    "lint": "eslint src/ --ext .js,.jsx",
    "build": "eslint src/ --ext .js,.jsx --max-warnings 0 && vite build",
    "preview": "vite preview",
    "test": "vitest run"
  },
  "devDependencies": {
    "eslint": "^8.56.0",
    "eslint-plugin-react": "^7.33.2",
    "eslint-plugin-react-hooks": "^4.6.0",
    "vitest": "^1.2.0"
  }
}
```

---

### ISSUE 9.2 — Agent has no integration test or dry-run validation
**File:** `agent/package.json`  
**Severity:** MEDIUM

The agent has a `"dry-run": "node index.js --dry-run"` script, but there's no test suite validating that:
- The output matches the schema the app expects
- API responses are correctly transformed
- Edge cases (empty responses, malformed data, rate limiting) are handled

Given that the agent modifies data consumed by all app users, an unvalidated agent run could corrupt banner/event/character data.

**Recommendation:** Add a `validate` script that checks agent output against the shared schema (from Issue 3.1).
