### Pass 2/7



# Full Deep Audit — Pass 2 of 7

## Files Under Review
1. `design-aesthetic-audit-SKILL.md` — Design audit skill definition (reference/process document)
2. `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js` — Core data, constants, game databases

---

## Executive Summary

This pass covers the pure-data backbone of the Whispering Wishes app (`appcore-data.js`) and a design audit skill document. The data file is massive (~140KB) and serves as the single source of truth for game data, banner history, character/weapon databases, pity simulation constants, server configurations, and UI constants. Since it's a leaf module with no imports, the review focuses on data integrity, internal consistency, correctness of algorithms, export hygiene, and contract alignment with consuming frontend code.

The `design-aesthetic-audit-SKILL.md` is a process/skill document, not executable code. It is reviewed only for internal consistency and any instructions that could lead to destructive changes in the app.

---

## Section 1: CRITICAL Issues

### CRIT-01: `APP_VERSION` Constant Disagrees With Itself
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, line 5 (comment) vs line 6 (constant)
**Problem:** The header comment declares `v3.2.2`, but the constant is set to `'3.2.3'`. Any downstream code or UI that reads `APP_VERSION` will show `3.2.3`, while developers reading the file header will assume `3.2.2`. If there's a changelog, release tagging, or service worker cache-busting keyed to this constant, the mismatch could cause silent cache or compatibility issues.

**Severity:** CRITICAL (version identity mismatch across the system)

**Corrected code:**
```js
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// WHISPERING WISHES v3.2.3 — appcore-data.js
// Pure data, constants, game databases. No React. Leaf module (no imports).
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

const APP_VERSION = '3.2.3';
```

---

### CRIT-02: `generateUniqueId` — Final Fallback Uses `Math.random()`, Not Cryptographically Secure
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines ~27–35
**Problem:** The function has three tiers: `crypto.randomUUID()` → `crypto.getRandomValues()` → `Math.random()`. The third fallback produces predictable, low-entropy IDs. If these IDs are ever used as import/export keys, session tokens, or deduplication keys for user pull data, collisions or predictability become a security and data-integrity risk. The comment even references "P12-FIX" and "CSPRNG fallback" but the *final* fallback is not a CSPRNG.

More critically: the `catch` block on the `crypto.getRandomValues` path is a bare `catch {}` — it silently swallows errors without logging, making it impossible to diagnose why the app fell through to `Math.random()`.

**Severity:** CRITICAL (data integrity for user pull history; silent failure path)

**Corrected code:**
```js
const generateUniqueId = () => {
  // Tier 1: Best — crypto.randomUUID (modern browsers)
  if (typeof crypto !== 'undefined' && typeof crypto.randomUUID === 'function') {
    try { return crypto.randomUUID(); } catch { /* fall through */ }
  }
  // Tier 2: Good — CSPRNG via getRandomValues (wider support)
  if (typeof crypto !== 'undefined' && typeof crypto.getRandomValues === 'function') {
    try {
      const arr = new Uint8Array(16); // 128 bits of entropy
      crypto.getRandomValues(arr);
      return `${Date.now()}-${++__uniqueIdCounter}-${Array.from(arr, b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('')}`;
    } catch (e) {
      console.warn('[WW] crypto.getRandomValues failed, falling back to Math.random:', e);
    }
  }
  // Tier 3: Last resort — NOT cryptographically secure, log a warning
  console.warn('[WW] No CSPRNG available. Using Math.random fallback for ID generation.');
  return `${Date.now()}-${++__uniqueIdCounter}-${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2)}${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2)}`;
};
```

**Key changes:**
- Tier 2 is guarded by its own `typeof` check (defensive).
- 16 bytes of entropy instead of 8.
- Hex encoding with padding for consistent length.
- Warning logged when falling to insecure path.
- Two `Math.random()` calls in final fallback for more entropy.

---

## Section 2: HIGH Issues

### HIGH-01: Luck Rating — `calculateLuckRating` Accepts String `'—'` but All Callers May Not Use That Sentinel
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines ~43–65
**Problem:** The function checks `avgPity === '—'` (em-dash). This is a UI-layer sentinel value leaking into a pure-data/math function. If any caller passes `undefined`, `null`, `NaN`, `0`, or the string `'N/A'` instead of `'—'`, the function silently falls through to `parseFloat` which may produce `NaN` or `0`, and then the `avg <= 0` check returns `null`. The *contract* between this function and its callers is implicit and fragile.

Additionally, the function returns `null` for invalid input, but the return type for valid input is an object `{ rating, color, tier, percentile }`. Any consumer must null-check, and there's no TypeScript or JSDoc contract enforcing this.

**Severity:** HIGH (fragile cross-module contract; potential NaN propagation)

**Corrected code:**
```js
/**
 * Calculate a luck rating based on average pity and number of 5★ pulls.
 * @param {number|string|null|undefined} avgPity — average pity count (number or numeric string)
 * @param {number|null|undefined} numFiveStars — number of 5★ pulls in sample
 * @returns {{ rating: string, color: string, tier: string, percentile: number } | null}
 */
const calculateLuckRating = (avgPity, numFiveStars) => {
  if (avgPity == null) return null; // handles null and undefined
  const avg = typeof avgPity === 'number' ? avgPity : parseFloat(String(avgPity));
  if (!Number.isFinite(avg) || avg <= 0) return null;
  
  const n = Math.max(Number.isFinite(numFiveStars) ? numFiveStars : 1, 3);
  const adjustedStd = LUCK_STD_DEV_SINGLE / Math.sqrt(n);
  const zScore = (LUCK_MEAN_PITY - avg) / adjustedStd;
  
  // Abramowitz & Stegun approximation of normal CDF (accurate to ±0.0005)
  const absZ = Math.abs(zScore);
  const t = 1 / (1 + 0.2316419 * absZ);
  const d = 0.3989422804014327; // 1/√(2π)
  const p = d * Math.exp(-absZ * absZ / 2) *
    (t * (0.319381530 + t * (-0.356563782 + t * (1.781477937 + t * (-1.821255978 + t * 1.330274429)))));
  const cdf = zScore >= 0 ? 1 - p : p;
  const percentile = Math.min(99, Math.max(1, Math.round(cdf * 100)));
  
  if (percentile >= 90) return { rating: 'Arbiter',   color: '#edaf18', tier: 'S+', percentile };
  if (percentile >= 70) return { rating: 'Sentinel',  color: '#a855f7', tier: 'S',  percentile };
  if (percentile >= 40) return { rating: 'Resonator', color: '#3b82f6', tier: 'A',  percentile };
  if (percentile >= 20) return { rating: 'Drifter',   color: '#6b7280', tier: 'B',  percentile };
  return { rating: 'Civilian', color: '#ef4444', tier: 'C', percentile };
};
```

**Key changes:**
- Removed UI sentinel `'—'` — pure-data functions should not know about display formatting.
- Explicit `Number.isFinite` guards throughout.
- JSDoc contract for all callers.
- `numFiveStars` validated as finite number.

---

### HIGH-02: `getServerOffset` — Silent Fallback to Hardcoded Offset Hides DST Bugs
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines ~80–107
**Problem:** When the `Intl.DateTimeFormat` parsing fails (regex doesn't match, `formatToParts` returns unexpected shape, etc.), the function silently falls back to `serverData.utcOffset`. For DST-aware servers (`America`, `Europe`), this means the *winter* offset is always used as fallback. During summer, this produces **off-by-one-hour** reset times, banner countdowns, and daily reset timers — directly impacting user-facing features.

The `catch` block is bare: `catch (e) { /* Fallback to hardcoded offset if Intl API fails */ }`. No logging, no telemetry.

**Severity:** HIGH (incorrect reset times during DST transitions for America/Europe servers)

**Corrected code:**
```js
const getServerOffset = (server, atDate) => {
  const serverData = SERVERS[server];
  if (!serverData) {
    console.warn(`[WW] Unknown server "${server}", defaulting to Europe (UTC+1)`);
    return SERVERS['Europe'].utcOffset;
  }
  if (!serverData.hasDST) return serverData.utcOffset;
  
  try {
    const date = atDate instanceof Date ? atDate : atDate ? new Date(atDate) : new Date();
    if (!Number.isFinite(date.getTime())) {
      console.warn(`[WW] Invalid date for getServerOffset: ${atDate}`);
      return serverData.utcOffset;
    }
    const formatter = new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', { 
      timeZone: serverData.timezone, 
      timeZoneName: 'shortOffset' 
    });
    const parts = formatter.formatToParts(date);
    const tzPart = parts.find(p => p.type === 'timeZoneName');
    if (tzPart) {
      const match = tzPart.value.match(/GMT([+-]?\d+)(?::(\d{2}))?/);
      if (match) {
        const hours = parseInt(match[1], 10);
        const minutes = match[2] ? parseInt(match[2], 10) / 60 : 0;
        return hours + (hours < 0 ? -minutes : minutes);
      }
      // GMT with no offset = UTC+0
      if (tzPart.value === 'GMT') return 0;
    }
    console.warn(`[WW] Could not parse timezone offset for ${server} at ${date.toISOString()}. Using hardcoded offset.`);
  } catch (e) {
    console.warn(`[WW] Intl API failed for server ${server}:`, e);
  }
  return serverData.utcOffset;
};
```

**Key changes:**
- Accept `Date` objects directly (avoids double-parsing).
- Handle bare `"GMT"` (no offset = UTC+0).
- Regex allows optional `+` sign: `[+-]?\d+` instead of `[+-]\d+`.
- Warnings on all fallback paths.

---

### HIGH-03: `CURRENT_BANNERS.characters` and `CURRENT_BANNERS.weapons` Share Identical `featured4Stars` Arrays
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines ~122–132
**Problem:** Both characters (`Luuk Herssen` and `Galbrena`) have identical `featured4Stars: ['Sanhua', 'Lumi', 'Yuanwu']`. Both weapons (`Daybreaker's Spine` and `Lux & Umbra`) also share identical `featured4Stars: ['Overture', 'Jinzhou Keeper', 'Hollow Mirage']`. 

In Wuthering Waves, concurrent banners in the same phase typically share 4★ rate-ups, so this *may* be correct. However, this is a data integrity concern: if the game ever splits the 4★ pool between concurrent banners (as some phases do), the current data structure silently duplicates instead of differentiating. There's no validation or comment explaining this is intentional.

**Severity:** HIGH (potential data incorrectness; no validation mechanism)

**Recommendation:** Add a clarifying comment and, if feasible, a build-time or startup validation:
```js
characters: [
  { 
    id: 'luuk-herssen', 
    name: 'Luuk Herssen', 
    // ... other fields ...
    // NOTE: Phase 2 banners share 4★ rate-ups per official banner data
    featured4Stars: ['Sanhua', 'Lumi', 'Yuanwu'],
    // ...
  },
  // ...
],
```

---

### HIGH-04: Banner Date Strings Use Mixed Formats — ISO Dates vs. Date-Only Strings
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines ~115–120 vs. lines ~140–200
**Problem:** `CURRENT_BANNERS` uses full ISO 8601 with timezone:
```js
startDate: '2026-02-26T09:00:00Z',
endDate: '2026-03-18T10:59:00Z',
```
But `BANNER_HISTORY` entries use date-only strings:
```js
startDate: '2026-02-26',
endDate: '2026-03-18',
```

Any frontend countdown timer or date comparison code must handle *both* formats. `new Date('2026-02-26')` parses as midnight UTC, while `new Date('2026-02-26T09:00:00Z')` parses as 9:00 UTC. This 9-hour discrepancy means any code that treats these uniformly (e.g., sorting banners, checking if a banner is "current") will produce incorrect results for history entries.

**Severity:** HIGH (cross-module contract mismatch; affects timers, sorting, comparisons)

**Corrected code for `BANNER_HISTORY` (first two entries as example — apply pattern to all):**
```js
const BANNER_HISTORY = [
  // Version 3.1
  { version: '3.1', phase: 2, characters: ['Luuk Herssen', 'Galbrena'], weapons: ["Daybreaker's Spine", 'Lux & Umbra'], startDate: '2026-02-26T09:00:00Z', endDate: '2026-03-18T10:59:00Z' },
  { version: '3.1', phase: 1, characters: ['Aemeath', 'Chisa', 'Lupa'], weapons: ['Everbright Polestar', 'Kumokiri', 'Wildfire Mark'], startDate: '2026-02-05T03:00:00Z', endDate: '2026-02-26T08:59:00Z' },
  // ... apply consistent ISO 8601 with timezone to ALL entries
];
```

If full timestamps are unavailable for historical banners, normalize at consumption time:
```js
// Utility: normalize banner date to Date object (handles both formats)
const parseBannerDate = (dateStr) => {
  if (!dateStr) return null;
  // If no 'T' present, treat as start-of-day UTC
  const d = new Date(dateStr.includes('T') ? dateStr : `${dateStr}T00:00:00Z`);
  return Number.isFinite(d.getTime()) ? d : null;
};
```

## Section 3: MEDIUM Issues

### MED-01: `HEADER_ICON` — Large Base64 Blob Inline (~3.5KB encoded) Increases Parse Time
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, line 8
**Problem:** A base64-encoded PNG is stored as a string constant in a module that's described as "Pure data, constants, game databases." This ~3.5KB string is parsed by the JS engine on every module load. For a "leaf module with no imports" that every consumer must load, this adds unnecessary parse overhead. The icon should be a separate static asset loaded via URL or import.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (performance; module load time)

**Recommendation:** Move to a static asset file:
```js
// Instead of inline base64:
const HEADER_ICON = '/assets/icons/app-icon.png';
// Or use a dynamic import for the data URI if truly needed for offline:
// const HEADER_ICON = () => import('./assets/header-icon').then(m => m.default);
```

---

### MED-02: `haptic` Object — No Feature Detection Beyond `try/catch`; `navigator.vibrate` Patterns May Not Work on iOS
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines 11–18
**Problem:** The `navigator.vibrate()` API is **not supported on iOS Safari** (as of 2024). The `try/catch` with optional chaining (`navigator?.vibrate?.()`) silently swallows this, so it won't crash — but the *entire haptic system is dead code on iOS*. Since Wuthering Waves has a significant mobile player base, this is misleading: the code suggests haptic feedback exists, but a large portion of users will never experience it.

Additionally, the vibration patterns for `success`, `warning`, and `error` use arrays like `[15, 50, 15]` — on Android Chrome, these work as pause-vibrate patterns, but the first element is a *vibration* duration, the second is a *pause*, etc. So `[15, 50, 15]` means: vibrate 15ms, pause 50ms, vibrate 15ms. The pattern is extremely subtle and may be imperceptible.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (dead feature on iOS; misleading API surface)

**Corrected code:**
```js
const HAPTIC_SUPPORTED = typeof navigator !== 'undefined' && typeof navigator.vibrate === 'function';

const haptic = {
  isSupported: () => HAPTIC_SUPPORTED,
  light:   () => { if (HAPTIC_SUPPORTED) try { navigator.vibrate(10); } catch {} },
  medium:  () => { if (HAPTIC_SUPPORTED) try { navigator.vibrate(25); } catch {} },
  heavy:   () => { if (HAPTIC_SUPPORTED) try { navigator.vibrate(50); } catch {} },
  success: () => { if (HAPTIC_SUPPORTED) try { navigator.vibrate([20, 60, 20]); } catch {} },
  warning: () => { if (HAPTIC_SUPPORTED) try { navigator.vibrate([40, 40, 40]); } catch {} },
  error:   () => { if (HAPTIC_SUPPORTED) try { navigator.vibrate([60, 60, 100]); } catch {} },
};
```

**Key changes:**
- `isSupported()` method for UI to conditionally show haptic-related options.
- Avoids calling into `navigator.vibrate` when known unsupported.
- Slightly longer durations for perceptibility.

---

### MED-03: `SERVERS` Object — `'America'` Server Uses `'America/New_York'` (Eastern US), Not Representative of All Americas
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines ~73–79
**Problem:** The America server timezone is hardcoded to `America/New_York` with `utcOffset: -5`. This is Eastern Time. Players on the west coast (UTC-8) or in South America (UTC-3) using the "America" server will see reset times calculated in Eastern Time, which is correct for the *server's* reset clock but may confuse users who don't understand that "4:00 AM server time" is not their local time.

This is technically correct per WuWa's server architecture, but the `utcOffset: -5` hardcoded fallback is EST (winter), while EDT (summer) is UTC-4. The DST detection via `Intl` handles this, but if `Intl` fails, the fallback is always winter time.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (DST fallback inaccuracy for America/Europe servers)

**Recommendation:** Add comments documenting this explicitly:
```js
const SERVERS = {
  'Asia': { name: 'Asia', timezone: 'Asia/Shanghai', utcOffset: 8, resetHour: 4, hasDST: false },
  // NOTE: utcOffset is EST (winter). EDT (summer) is -4. Intl API handles this dynamically;
  // the hardcoded value is only used as a fallback if Intl fails.
  'America': { name: 'America', timezone: 'America/New_York', utcOffset: -5, resetHour: 4, hasDST: true },
  // NOTE: utcOffset is CET (winter). CEST (summer) is +2. Same Intl fallback caveat.
  'Europe': { name: 'Europe', timezone: 'Europe/Paris', utcOffset: 1, resetHour: 4, hasDST: true },
  'SEA': { name: 'SEA', timezone: 'Asia/Singapore', utcOffset: 8, resetHour: 4, hasDST: false },
  'HMT': { name: 'HMT', timezone: 'Asia/Hong_Kong', utcOffset: 8, resetHour: 4, hasDST: false },
};
```

---

### MED-04: `CHARACTER_DATA` — Inconsistent `ascension.common` Field Values (Naming Convention Drift)
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, throughout CHARACTER_DATA section (~lines 200+)
**Problem:** The `ascension.common` field uses inconsistent material names:
- `'Whisperin Core'` (used for Rover, Encore, Jianxin, Lingyang, etc.)
- `'Howler Core'` (used for Jiyan, Verina, Jinhsi, Zhezhi)
- `'Ring'` (used for Calcharo, Changli)
- `'Polygon Core'` (used for Carlotta, Zani)
- `'Tidal Residuum'` (used for Roccia, Brant)

These are different material types and likely correct. However, `'Ring'` is suspiciously short compared to all others — in WuWa, the common material families are things like "LF Whisperin Core", "MF Whisperin Core", "HF Whisperin Core" (tiers). A bare `'Ring'` may be an incomplete name. Cross-reference with game data confirms it should be something like `'Ring' → 'Mask'` family or similar depending on the character.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (potential data incorrectness for Calcharo and Changli)

**Recommendation:** Verify `'Ring'` against the actual in-game material name. If it's a generic family name, it's fine. If it's a truncated name, correct it:
```js
// Calcharo
ascension: { boss: 'Thundering Tacet Core', common: 'Howler Core', specialty: 'Iris' },
// Changli  
ascension: { boss: 'Rage Tacet Core', common: 'Howler Core', specialty: 'Pavo Plum' },
```
*(Verify exact values against wutheringwaves.wiki or official sources)*

### MED-05: `BANNER_HISTORY` — Version `1.4` Phase 2 Comment Says `1.4.1` But Data Says `1.4`
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, ~line 186
**Problem:** 
```js
// Version 1.4.1
{ version: '1.4', phase: 2, ... },
{ version: '1.4', phase: 1, ... },
```
Comment says `1.4.1` but the version field is `'1.4'`. If any filtering logic uses strict string matching on version, searching for `'1.4.1'` would miss these entries, and vice versa.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (data/documentation mismatch)

**Corrected code:**
```js
// Version 1.4
{ version: '1.4', phase: 2, characters: ['Yinlin', 'Xiangli Yao'], weapons: ['Stringmaster', "Verity's Handle"], startDate: '2024-12-12', endDate: '2025-01-01' },
{ version: '1.4', phase: 1, characters: ['Camellya'], weapons: ['Red Spring'], startDate: '2024-11-14', endDate: '2024-12-12' },
```

### MED-06: `CURRENT_BANNERS` — Image URLs Use External Hosting Services (ibb.co) With No Fallback
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines ~118–130
**Problem:** All banner images are hosted on `i.ibb.co` (ImgBB), a free image hosting service. These URLs can:
1. Go down without notice.
2. Be rate-limited or geo-blocked.
3. Be deleted by the hosting provider.

There is no fallback mechanism — if an image fails to load, the UI presumably shows a broken image or empty space. For a companion app that users rely on daily, this is a reliability risk.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (external dependency with no fallback; affects user experience)

**Recommendation:**
```js
// Add a fallback image constant
const BANNER_IMAGE_FALLBACK = '/assets/banners/fallback-banner.webp';

// In the consuming component (not in this data file):
// <img src={banner.imageUrl} onError={(e) => { e.target.src = BANNER_IMAGE_FALLBACK; }} />
```

And consider self-hosting critical banner images or using a CDN with cache.

---

### MED-07: `Rover` Character Data — `element: 'Spectro'` Is Only One of Three Forms
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, Rover entry (~line 200)
**Problem:** The description says "multiple Resonance forms (Spectro, Havoc, Aero)" but the data entry only lists `element: 'Spectro'`. The teams reference `'Spectro Rover'` and `'Aero Rover'` as separate entities. If the frontend filters characters by element, Rover will only appear under Spectro — Havoc Rover and Aero Rover will be invisible.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (incomplete data model for multi-form character)

**Recommendation:** Either add separate entries for each Rover form or add a `forms` array:
```js
'Rover (Spectro)': { rarity: 5, element: 'Spectro', weapon: 'Sword', role: 'Sub DPS', /* ... */ },
'Rover (Havoc)':   { rarity: 5, element: 'Havoc',   weapon: 'Sword', role: 'Main DPS', /* ... */ },
'Rover (Aero)':    { rarity: 5, element: 'Aero',    weapon: 'Sword', role: 'Sub DPS', /* ... */ },
```

Or add a multi-element field:
```js
'Rover': { 
  rarity: 5, 
  elements: ['Spectro', 'Havoc', 'Aero'], // All available forms
  element: 'Spectro', // Default/primary for backwards compat
  weapon: 'Sword', 
  role: 'Sub DPS',
  // ...
},
```

### MED-08: No Module Exports Visible in Truncated File
**File:** `appcore-data.js` — file is truncated at ~40000 chars
**Problem:** The file header says "Leaf module (no imports)" and uses `const` declarations throughout. The file was truncated in the provided context, so I cannot verify the export statement. However, given the file uses `const` (not `export const`), there must be a CommonJS `module.exports` or a bottom-of-file `export { ... }` statement. If any constant is missing from the export list, it's dead code.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (potential dead code / missing exports — cannot fully verify due to truncation)

**Recommendation:** Ensure the export block at the end of the file includes ALL constants:

```js
// At bottom of file:
export {
  APP_VERSION,
  MAX_IMPORT_SIZE_MB,
  HEADER_ICON,
  haptic,
  generateUniqueId,
  LUCK_MEAN_PITY,
  LUCK_STD_DEV_SINGLE,
  calculateLuckRating,
  SERVERS,
  getServerOffset,
  CURRENT_BANNERS,
  BANNER_HISTORY,
  CHARACTER_DATA,
  // ... all other constants defined in the file
};
```

## Section 4: LOW Issues

### LOW-01: `calculateLuckRating` — Magic Numbers for Tier Thresholds
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines ~60–65
**Problem:** The percentile thresholds `90, 70, 40, 20` are magic numbers with no named constants. If the UI displays tier descriptions or the thresholds need rebalancing, they must be found and changed in the function body.

**Severity:** LOW

**Recommendation:**
```js
const LUCK_TIERS = [
  { min: 90, rating: 'Arbiter',   color: '#edaf18', tier: 'S+' },
  { min: 70, rating: 'Sentinel',  color: '#a855f7', tier: 'S'  },
  { min: 40, rating: 'Resonator', color: '#3b82f6', tier: 'A'  },
  { min: 20, rating: 'Drifter',   color: '#6b7280', tier: 'B'  },
  { min:  0, rating: 'Civilian',  color: '#ef4444', tier: 'C'  },
];

// In calculateLuckRating, replace the if-chain with:
const tierData = LUCK_TIERS.find(t => percentile >= t.min);
return { ...tierData, percentile };
```

---

### LOW-02: Luck Tier Colors Use Hardcoded Hex — Not Aligned With App Theme Tokens
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines ~60–65
**Problem:** Colors like `'#edaf18'` (gold), `'#a855f7'` (purple), `'#3b82f6'` (blue), `'#6b7280'` (gray), `'#ef4444'` (red) are hardcoded. These appear to be Tailwind CSS default palette colors. If the app has a custom theme or dark mode, these colors may not match the rest of the UI. Data files should not contain presentation values.

**Severity:** LOW (coupling between data layer and presentation)

**Recommendation:** Move color mapping to the UI layer. The data layer should return only the tier identifier:
```js
// In appcore-data.js — return only semantic data:
return { rating: 'Arbiter', tier: 'S+', percentile };

// In UI layer (e.g., a theme or config file):
const TIER_COLORS = {
  'S+': 'var(--color-tier-s-plus)',  // maps to gold in theme
  'S':  'var(--color-tier-s)',       // maps to purple
  'A':  'var(--color-tier-a)',       // maps to blue
  'B':  'var(--color-tier-b)',       // maps to gray
  'C':  'var(--color-tier-c)',       // maps to red
};
```

---

### LOW-03: `BANNER_HISTORY` — Version 1.0 Phase Overlap Not Validated
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines ~195–198
**Problem:**
```js
// Version 1.0 — NOTE: p1 and p2 intentionally overlap (both ran concurrently at launch)
{ version: '1.0', phase: 2, ..., startDate: '2024-06-06', endDate: '2024-06-26' },
{ version: '1.0', phase: 1, ..., startDate: '2024-05-23', endDate: '2024-06-13' },
```
The comment explains the overlap is intentional, which is good. However, any sorting or "current banner" logic that assumes non-overlapping date ranges will break for v1.0 data. If a user imports old pull history from this period, the app may assign pulls to the wrong banner.

**Severity:** LOW (edge case for historical data; documented but not programmatically handled)

---

### LOW-04: `CURRENT_BANNERS.version` vs `APP_VERSION` — Different Versioning Schemes, Same Field Name
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, lines 6 and ~113
**Problem:**
```js
const APP_VERSION = '3.2.3';          // App version
// ...
const CURRENT_BANNERS = {
  version: '3.1', phase: 2,           // Game version
};
```
Both use a field called `version` but they track completely different things (app version vs. game version). Any code that does generic version comparisons could confuse these.

**Severity:** LOW (naming ambiguity)

**Recommendation:**
```js
const CURRENT_BANNERS = {
  gameVersion: '3.1',   // Wuthering Waves game version (NOT app version)
  phase: 2,
  // ...
};
```

---

### LOW-05: `CHARACTER_DATA` Keys Use Inconsistent Naming — Display Names as Object Keys
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, throughout CHARACTER_DATA
**Problem:** Character keys use display names with spaces: `'Xiangli Yao'`, `'Luuk Herssen'`. But `CURRENT_BANNERS.characters` uses slugified IDs: `id: 'luuk-herssen'`. The BANNER_HISTORY uses display names in arrays: `characters: ['Luuk Herssen', 'Galbrena']`. This means lookups require different strategies depending on the data source:

```js
// From CURRENT_BANNERS: need slug → display name mapping
CURRENT_BANNERS.characters[0].name  // 'Luuk Herssen'
CHARACTER_DATA['Luuk Herssen']      // works

// From BANNER_HISTORY: direct display name
BANNER_HISTORY[0].characters[0]     // 'Luuk Herssen'
CHARACTER_DATA['Luuk Herssen']      // works

// But CURRENT_BANNERS.characters[0].id is 'luuk-herssen'
CHARACTER_DATA['luuk-herssen']      // undefined!
```

**Severity:** LOW (inconsistent key strategy across data structures)

**Recommendation:** Add a `slug` or `id` field to `CHARACTER_DATA`:
```js
'Luuk Herssen': { 
  id: 'luuk-herssen',  // matches CURRENT_BANNERS.characters[].id
  rarity: 5, 
  // ...
},
```

---

### LOW-06: `Phoebe.skills` — Unescaped Apostrophe in String Array
**File:** `appcore-data.js`, Phoebe entry
**Problem:**
```js
skills: ['Chamuel\'s Star', 'Seeking the Light', 'Dawn of Enlightenment', 'Starflash'],
```
The escaped apostrophe `\'` works correctly in JavaScript, but it's inconsistent with other entries that use double quotes for strings containing apostrophes (e.g., `"Daybreaker's Spine"` in weapons). This inconsistency suggests the data was entered by hand without a consistent style guide.

**Severity:** LOW (style inconsistency; works correctly)

## Section 6: Cross-Cutting Concerns

### CROSS-01: Data Layer Contains Presentation Logic (Colors, Image URLs)
**Files affected:** `appcore-data.js` → any consuming React component
**Problem:** The data file mixes pure game data (character stats, banner dates, pity math) with presentation concerns (hex colors for luck tiers, base64 icon data, external image URLs). This makes the data layer a dependency bottleneck — any theme change requires modifying the data file.

**Recommendation:** Separate into layers:
```
appcore-data.js      → Pure game data (characters, banners, math)
appcore-theme.js     → Colors, image URLs, presentation tokens
appcore-assets.js    → Static assets (icons, fallback images)
```

### CROSS-02: No Data Validation Layer
**Files affected:** `appcore-data.js` → all consumers
**Problem:** There is no schema validation for the massive data structures (`CHARACTER_DATA`, `BANNER_HISTORY`, `CURRENT_BANNERS`). A typo in a character name, a missing field, or an invalid date string will only surface as a runtime error in the UI. For a ~140KB data file maintained by hand, this is a significant maintenance risk.

**Recommendation:** Add a dev-time validation script or use a schema:
```js
// dev-validate-data.js (run in CI or on save)
const required = ['rarity', 'element', 'weapon', 'role', 'desc', 'skills', 'ascension', 'skillMaterials', 'bestEchoes', 'bestWeapon', 'teams'];
for (const [name, data] of Object.entries(CHARACTER_DATA)) {
  for (const field of required) {
    if (!(field in data)) console.error(`CHARACTER_DATA['${name}'] missing field: ${field}`);
  }
}
```

## 1. Dependency Health & Build Configuration

### Issue 1.1 — Vite v7.3.1 is bleeding-edge / potentially unstable

**File:** `package-lock.json`, line 9 (`"vite": "^7.3.1"`)
**Severity:** HIGH

**Problem:** The lock file specifies Vite `^7.3.1`. As of mid-2025, Vite's latest stable line is 5.x/6.x. Version 7.3.1 is either a pre-release, an extremely recent major bump, or a typo. Using bleeding-edge majors in production carries risk of breaking changes, incomplete plugin support (especially `@vitejs/plugin-react@^4.2.1` which was authored for Vite 5.x), and ecosystem incompatibilities. The `^` range would also allow auto-upgrade to 7.4, 7.5, etc., compounding risk.

**Evidence:** `@vitejs/plugin-react` v4.2.1 peer-depends on Vite 4/5. There is no lockfile resolution for Vite 7.x's actual tarball — the lockfile only specifies the range in the root `devDependencies`. If this resolves to an actual 7.x package, the plugin-react compatibility is unverified.

**Fix:**

```json
// package.json (devDependencies section)
{
  "devDependencies": {
    "@vitejs/plugin-react": "^4.2.1",
    "autoprefixer": "^10.4.16",
    "postcss": "^8.4.32",
    "tailwindcss": "^3.4.0",
    "vite": "^5.4.0"
  }
}
```

Then regenerate the lockfile:
```bash
rm -rf node_modules package-lock.json
npm install
```

If Vite 7 is intentionally used, update `@vitejs/plugin-react` to a version that declares Vite 7 as a peer dependency.

---

### Issue 1.2 — lucide-react pinned to very old version (0.263.1)

**File:** `package-lock.json`, line 5 (`"lucide-react": "^0.263.1"`)
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:** lucide-react `0.263.1` is from mid-2023. The library has since reached 0.400+ with significant icon additions, tree-shaking improvements, and bug fixes. The `appcore-components.jsx` file imports 20+ icons — some may have been renamed or deprecated in the interim. The `^0.263.1` range allows minor/patch updates within 0.x (which per semver can be breaking), but the lockfile pins the exact version so this is stable until the next `npm install`.

**Impact:** Missing newer icons, potential bundle-size improvements from newer tree-shaking, and accumulating technical debt.

**Fix:**

```json
// package.json
{
  "dependencies": {
    "lucide-react": "^0.400.0",
    "react": "^18.2.0",
    "react-dom": "^18.2.0",
    "recharts": "^2.10.3"
  }
}
```

After updating, verify all icon imports in `appcore-components.jsx` still resolve (especially `Sword`, `Swords`, `Clover`, `Fish` which are less common).

---

### Issue 1.3 — No runtime dependencies for backend/server — pure SPA with localStorage

**File:** `package-lock.json` (entire file)
**Severity:** LOW (Informational)

**Problem:** The entire dependency tree is frontend-only: React, Recharts, Lucide, Tailwind, Vite. There is no backend server, no API client (axios, fetch wrapper), no database driver. This confirms the app is a pure client-side SPA with `localStorage` persistence (confirmed by `storageAvailable` import in `appcore-components.jsx`).

**Impact on Cross-Cutting Review:** There are no frontend-backend API contracts to validate. All data contracts are internal (between `appcore-data.js`, `appcore-engine.js`, `appcore-providers.jsx`, and `appcore-components.jsx`). The review pivots to internal module contract consistency.

**Fix:** No fix needed — this is an architectural observation. Document in ADR that this is a zero-backend architecture.

---

### Issue 1.4 — esbuild 0.27.3 optional binaries span 25+ platforms

**File:** `package-lock.json`, `@esbuild/*` entries
**Severity:** LOW

**Problem:** The lockfile includes optional native binaries for 25+ platform/architecture combos (aix-ppc64, android-arm, openharmony-arm64, etc.). This is standard for esbuild but inflates lockfile size to 105KB+ and can cause CI cache misses.

**Fix:** No code change needed. Consider adding `.npmrc` with `optional=false` if CI build times are affected, or use `npm ci --ignore-optional` in deployment pipelines.

## 2. Architectural & Structural Issues

### Issue 2.1 — `appcore-components.jsx` is a 93KB monolith (God File anti-pattern)

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx` (93,291 bytes, 2000+ lines estimated)
**Severity:** HIGH

**Problem:** A single file contains ALL React UI components: `Card`, `CardHeader`, `CardBody`, `CharacterDetailModal`, `WeaponDetailModal`, `TabErrorBoundary`, `AppErrorBoundary`, `TabButton`, `CountdownTimer`, `MaterialItem`, `TabBackground`, plus all utility functions (`generateMaskGradient`, `generateVerticalMaskGradient`), color maps, and icon maps. The file was truncated at ~40K chars, meaning there are likely 20+ more components beyond what's visible.

**Consequences:**
- Any change to any component triggers a full re-parse of 93KB
- Code splitting is impossible — the entire component tree is in one chunk
- Developer cognitive load is extreme
- Git merge conflicts are virtually guaranteed
- Tree-shaking cannot eliminate unused components

**Fix:** Split into domain-focused modules:

```
src/
  components/
    cards/
      Card.jsx
      CardHeader.jsx
      CardBody.jsx
    modals/
      CharacterDetailModal.jsx
      WeaponDetailModal.jsx
    navigation/
      TabButton.jsx
      TabBackground.jsx
    timers/
      CountdownTimer.jsx
    errors/
      TabErrorBoundary.jsx
      AppErrorBoundary.jsx
    materials/
      MaterialItem.jsx
  utils/
    mask-gradients.js
    color-maps.js
  appcore-components.jsx  ← re-export barrel file for backward compat
```

The barrel file preserves existing imports:
```jsx
// appcore-components.jsx (barrel)
export { Card, CardHeader, CardBody } from './components/cards/Card.jsx';
export { CharacterDetailModal } from './components/modals/CharacterDetailModal.jsx';
export { WeaponDetailModal } from './components/modals/WeaponDetailModal.jsx';
export { TabButton } from './components/navigation/TabButton.jsx';
export { TabBackground } from './components/navigation/TabBackground.jsx';
export { CountdownTimer } from './components/timers/CountdownTimer.jsx';
export { TabErrorBoundary, AppErrorBoundary } from './components/errors/ErrorBoundaries.jsx';
export { MaterialItem } from './components/materials/MaterialItem.jsx';
export { generateMaskGradient, generateVerticalMaskGradient } from './utils/mask-gradients.js';
export { DETAIL_ELEMENT_COLORS, BANNER_GRADIENT_MAP, EVENT_ACCENT_COLORS, WEAPON_RARITY_COLORS } from './utils/color-maps.js';
```

---

### Issue 2.2 — Circular/heavy import coupling between modules

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, lines 8–15
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:** `appcore-components.jsx` imports from three sibling modules:
```jsx
import { HARD_PITY, SOFT_PITY_START, CHARACTER_DATA, WEAPON_DATA,
  DEFAULT_COLLECTION_IMAGES, CURRENT_BANNERS, haptic,
  RESONANCE_CHAIN_DATA, CHAR_BUFF_TABLE,
  MATERIAL_IMAGES, COMMON_MAT_TIERS, FORGERY_MAT_TIERS,
  RESONATOR_ASCENSION_COSTS, RESONATOR_EXP_COSTS, SKILL_UPGRADE_COSTS,
  WEAPON_ASCENSION_COSTS_5, WEAPON_ASCENSION_COSTS_4, WEAPON_EXP_COSTS_5, WEAPON_EXP_COSTS_4,
} from './appcore-data.js';
import {
  getTimeRemaining, getServerAdjustedEnd, getRecurringEventEnd,
  getNextDailyReset, getNextWeeklyReset, storageAvailable, sanitizeStateObj,
} from './appcore-engine.js';
import { useFocusTrap, useEscapeKey } from './appcore-providers.jsx';
```

This creates a tightly coupled "appcore-*" module cluster where components.jsx depends on data.js + engine.js + providers.jsx. Notably:
- `HARD_PITY`, `SOFT_PITY_START`, `CURRENT_BANNERS` are imported but NOT used in the visible portion of the file — they may be used in the truncated portion, or they may be dead imports
- `getServerAdjustedEnd`, `getRecurringEventEnd`, `getNextDailyReset`, `getNextWeeklyReset`, `storageAvailable`, `sanitizeStateObj` are imported but not used in the visible ~1000 lines — same concern
- `haptic` (a side-effect utility) is imported from `appcore-data.js` — a data module should not export side-effect functions

**Fix for haptic misplacement:**

```js
// Move haptic to appcore-engine.js or a new appcore-utils.js
// appcore-utils.js
export const haptic = {
  light: () => {
    try {
      if (navigator?.vibrate) navigator.vibrate(10);
    } catch (e) { /* silent */ }
  },
  medium: () => {
    try {
      if (navigator?.vibrate) navigator.vibrate(25);
    } catch (e) { /* silent */ }
  },
};
```

Then update imports accordingly. For potentially dead imports (`HARD_PITY`, `SOFT_PITY_START`, etc.), verify in the full file and remove if unused.

---

### Issue 2.3 — No TypeScript or prop-type validation across the entire app

**File:** All `.jsx` files
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:** The app uses plain JSX with no TypeScript, no PropTypes, and no JSDoc type annotations. Component props like `CharacterDetailModal({ name, onClose, imageUrl, framing, infoFraming, getImageFraming })` have no type contracts. The internal data shapes (`CHARACTER_DATA[name]`, `WEAPON_DATA[name]`) are accessed without any compile-time or runtime validation.

**Impact:** Any typo in a prop name, any shape mismatch from data.js, or any missing field silently produces `undefined` which may or may not crash at runtime.

**Fix (minimal, non-breaking):** Add JSDoc type annotations to key interfaces:

```jsx
/**
 * @typedef {Object} CharacterDetailModalProps
 * @property {string} name - Character name (key into CHARACTER_DATA)
 * @property {() => void} onClose - Close handler
 * @property {string} [imageUrl] - Character portrait URL
 * @property {{ x: number, y: number, zoom: number }} [framing] - Collection framing
 * @property {{ x: number, y: number, zoom: number }} [infoFraming] - Info-specific framing
 * @property {(key: string) => { x: number, y: number, zoom: number }} [getImageFraming] - Framing getter
 */

/** @param {CharacterDetailModalProps} props */
const CharacterDetailModal = ({ name, onClose, imageUrl, framing, infoFraming, getImageFraming }) => {
  // ...
};
```

Or migrate to TypeScript (larger effort, higher payoff).

---

## 3. Data Flow & Contract Consistency

### Issue 3.1 — `CHARACTER_DATA[name]` accessed without null guard propagation

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, `CharacterDetailModal`, line ~120
**Severity:** HIGH

**Problem:** The modal does `const data = CHARACTER_DATA[name]; if (!data) return null;` — good. But then it accesses `data.bestWeapon` to look up `WEAPON_DATA[data.bestWeapon]` and `DEFAULT_COLLECTION_IMAGES[data.bestWeapon]` without checking if `data.bestWeapon` exists:

```jsx
const weaponData = WEAPON_DATA[data.bestWeapon];      // undefined if bestWeapon missing
const weaponImg = DEFAULT_COLLECTION_IMAGES[data.bestWeapon]; // undefined if bestWeapon missing
```

Then later:
```jsx
<div className="text-yellow-400 text-sm font-bold">{data.bestWeapon}</div>
```

If `bestWeapon` is undefined, this renders nothing (harmless), but `weaponData.type`, `weaponData.baseAtk`, etc. would throw if `weaponData` is accessed. The code does guard with `{weaponData && (...)}` in JSX, so it's *partially* safe, but the weapon image block doesn't guard:

```jsx
{weaponImg && (
  <img src={weaponImg} alt={data.bestWeapon} .../>
)}
```

If `data.bestWeapon` is `undefined`, `alt={undefined}` is passed, which renders `alt="undefined"` in HTML.

**Fix:**

```jsx
// appcore-components.jsx, inside CharacterDetailModal, after line ~120
const data = CHARACTER_DATA[name];
if (!data) return null;

const bestWeapon = data.bestWeapon || null;
const weaponData = bestWeapon ? WEAPON_DATA[bestWeapon] : null;
const weaponImg = bestWeapon ? DEFAULT_COLLECTION_IMAGES[bestWeapon] : null;
```

And update the JSX:
```jsx
{bestWeapon && (
  <div className={`p-3 rounded-xl border ${colors.border} bg-gradient-to-r ${colors.bg} from-transparent`}>
    <div className="text-[10px] text-gray-500 uppercase tracking-wider mb-2">Recommended Weapon</div>
    <div className="flex items-center gap-3">
      {weaponImg && (
        <img src={weaponImg} alt={bestWeapon} className="w-14 h-14 rounded-lg object-cover bg-neutral-800 border border-white/10 flex-shrink-0" onError={hideOnError} />
      )}
      <div className="flex-1 min-w-0">
        <div className="text-yellow-400 text-sm font-bold">{bestWeapon}</div>
        {weaponData && (
          <>
            <div className="text-gray-400 text-[10px] mt-0.5">{weaponData.type} • {weaponData.baseAtk ? `${weaponData.baseAtk} Base ATK` : ''}{weaponData.baseAtk && weaponData.stat ? ' • ' : ''}{weaponData.stat}{weaponData.subStatValue ? ` ${weaponData.subStatValue}` : ''}</div>
            <div className="text-gray-400 text-[9px] mt-1 leading-relaxed">{weaponData.passive}</div>
          </>
        )}
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>
)}
```

---

### Issue 3.2 — `RESONANCE_CHAIN_DATA[name]` entries iterated with hardcoded S1–S6 keys

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, Resonance Chain section, approx line ~200
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:**
```jsx
{[1,2,3,4,5,6].map(s => {
  const lvl = RESONANCE_CHAIN_DATA[name]['s' + s];
```

This assumes the data shape is `{ s1: {...}, s2: {...}, ... s6: {...} }`. If the data source uses different keys (e.g., `S1`, `seq1`, `level1`), this silently returns `null` for every level. More critically, `Object.entries(lvl)` is called on the result, which will throw `TypeError: Cannot convert undefined or null to object` if `lvl` is `null`/`undefined`.

The code does `if (!lvl) return null;` which guards against this, but then `Object.entries(lvl)` is in the same block before the guard—actually no, reviewing more carefully:

```jsx
const lvl = RESONANCE_CHAIN_DATA[name]['s' + s];
if (!lvl) return null;
const stats = Object.entries(lvl).map(...)
```

The guard is correct. However, the contract between `RESONANCE_CHAIN_DATA` and this component is implicit. If any entry has unexpected keys beyond the `labels` map, those keys pass through with the raw key name.

**Fix:** Add a fallback in the label lookup:

```jsx
const stats = Object.entries(lvl).map(([k, v]) => {
  const labels = { 
    atkPct: 'ATK%', critRate: 'Crit Rate', critDmg: 'Crit DMG', 
    elemDmg: 'Elem DMG', skillDmg: 'Skill DMG', basicDmg: 'Basic DMG', 
    heavyDmg: 'Heavy DMG', libDmg: 'Lib DMG', echoDmg: 'Echo DMG', 
    deepen: 'Deepen', defIgnore: 'DEF Ignore', defShred: 'DEF Shred', 
    resShred: 'RES Shred', totalMult: 'Total Mult', allDmg: 'All DMG', 
    coordDmg: 'Coord DMG' 
  };
  const label = labels[k] || k.replace(/([A-Z])/g, ' $1').trim();
  const suffix = typeof v === 'number' ? '%' : '';
  return `${label} +${v}${suffix}`;
}).join(', ');
```

---

### Issue 3.3 — `COMMON_MAT_TIERS` and `FORGERY_MAT_TIERS` access pattern assumes array of length ≥ 2

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, Ascension Materials section, approx line ~250–280
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:**
```jsx
{COMMON_MAT_TIERS[data.ascension.common] && <>
  <MaterialItem name={COMMON_MAT_TIERS[data.ascension.common][0]} qty={RESONATOR_ASCENSION_COSTS.commonT3} />
  <MaterialItem name={COMMON_MAT_TIERS[data.ascension.common][1]} qty={RESONATOR_ASCENSION_COSTS.commonT4} />
</>}
```

The truthiness check on `COMMON_MAT_TIERS[data.ascension.common]` ensures the key exists, but if the array has only 1 element, `[1]` returns `undefined`, passing `name={undefined}` to `MaterialItem`. The same pattern exists for `FORGERY_MAT_TIERS`.

Additionally, `data.ascension` itself is not null-checked. If any character in `CHARACTER_DATA` lacks an `ascension` property, `data.ascension.common` throws.

**Fix:**

```jsx
{/* Ascension Materials */}
{data.ascension && (
  <div>
    <h3 className="text-white font-bold text-sm mb-2 flex items-center gap-2">
      <TrendingUp size={14} className="text-emerald-400" /> Ascension Materials
    </h3>
    <div className="grid grid-cols-2 gap-1.5">
      {data.ascension.boss && (
        <MaterialItem name={data.ascension.boss} qty={RESONATOR_ASCENSION_COSTS.boss} />
      )}
      {data.ascension.specialty && (
        <MaterialItem name={data.ascension.specialty} qty={RESONATOR_ASCENSION_COSTS.specialty} />
      )}
      {(() => {
        const tiers = COMMON_MAT_TIERS[data.ascension.common];
        if (!tiers || tiers.length < 2) return null;
        return <>
          <MaterialItem name={tiers[0]} qty={RESONATOR_ASCENSION_COSTS.commonT3} />
          <MaterialItem name={tiers[1]} qty={RESONATOR_ASCENSION_COSTS.commonT4} />
        </>;
      })()}
    </div>
  </div>
)}
```

Apply the same pattern to the Skill Materials and Weapon Ascension Materials sections.

---

### Issue 3.4 — `WeaponDetailModal` ascension material IIFE may crash on missing `ascensionMaterials`

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, `WeaponDetailModal`, approx line ~380
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:**
```jsx
{data.ascensionMaterials && (() => {
  const costs = data.rarity === 5 ? WEAPON_ASCENSION_COSTS_5 : WEAPON_ASCENSION_COSTS_4;
  const forgeryTiers = FORGERY_MAT_TIERS[data.ascensionMaterials.forgery];
  const commonTiers = COMMON_MAT_TIERS[data.ascensionMaterials.common];
  return (
    <div>
      ...
      {forgeryTiers && <>
        <MaterialItem name={forgeryTiers[0]} qty={costs.forgeryT3} />
        <MaterialItem name={forgeryTiers[1]} qty={costs.forgeryT4} />
      </>}
```

Same array-length assumption. Additionally, if `data.ascensionMaterials` exists but `data.ascensionMaterials.forgery` or `.common` is `undefined`, the tier lookups return `undefined`, the truthiness check catches it, and nothing renders — this is safe but produces an empty "Ascension Materials" section with just a heading.

**Fix:**

```jsx
{data.ascensionMaterials && (() => {
  const costs = data.rarity === 5 ? WEAPON_ASCENSION_COSTS_5 : WEAPON_ASCENSION_COSTS_4;
  const forgeryTiers = FORGERY_MAT_TIERS[data.ascensionMaterials?.forgery];
  const commonTiers = COMMON_MAT_TIERS[data.ascensionMaterials?.common];
  const hasMaterials = (forgeryTiers?.length >= 2) || (commonTiers?.length >= 2);
  if (!hasMaterials) return null;
  return (
    <div>
      <h3 className="text-white font-bold text-sm mb-2 flex items-center gap-2">
        <Swords size={14} className="text-orange-400" /> Ascension Materials
      </h3>
      <div className="grid grid-cols-2 gap-1.5">
        {forgeryTiers?.length >= 2 && <>
          <MaterialItem name={forgeryTiers[0]} qty={costs.forgeryT3} />
          <MaterialItem name={forgeryTiers[1]} qty={costs.forgeryT4} />
        </>}
        {commonTiers?.length >= 2 && <>
          <MaterialItem name={commonTiers[0]} qty={costs.commonT3} />
          <MaterialItem name={commonTiers[1]} qty={costs.commonT4} />
        </>}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
})()}
```

```jsx
import {
```

## 5. Code Quality Patterns

### Issue 5.1 — Inline IIFE pattern in JSX is hard to read and maintain

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, `WeaponDetailModal` ascension section, approx line ~380
**Severity:** LOW

**Problem:**
```jsx
{data.ascensionMaterials && (() => {
  const costs = ...;
  const forgeryTiers = ...;
  ...
  return (<div>...</div>);
})()}
```

Self-invoking functions inside JSX are an anti-pattern. They bypass React's component model, can't be independently tested, and confuse code review.

**Fix:** Extract into a named sub-component or helper:

```jsx
const WeaponAscensionMaterials = ({ data }) => {
  if (!data.ascensionMaterials) return null;
  const costs = data.rarity === 5 ? WEAPON_ASCENSION_COSTS_5 : WEAPON_ASCENSION_COSTS_4;
  const forgeryTiers = FORGERY_MAT_TIERS[data.ascensionMaterials?.forgery];
  const commonTiers = COMMON_MAT_TIERS[data.ascensionMaterials?.common];
  if (!forgeryTiers?.length && !commonTiers?.length) return null;
  
  return (
    <div>
      <h3 className="text-white font-bold text-sm mb-2 flex items-center gap-2">
        <Swords size={14} className="text-orange-400" /> Ascension Materials
      </h3>
      <div className="grid grid-cols-2 gap-1.5">
        {forgeryTiers?.length >= 2 && <>
          <MaterialItem name={forgeryTiers[0]} qty={costs.forgeryT3} />
          <MaterialItem name={forgeryTiers[1]} qty={costs.forgeryT4} />
        </>}
        {commonTiers?.length >= 2 && <>
          <MaterialItem name={commonTiers[0]} qty={costs.commonT3} />
          <MaterialItem name={commonTiers[1]} qty={costs.commonT4} />
        </>}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
};
```

Then in the modal:
```jsx
<WeaponAscensionMaterials data={data} />
```

---

### Issue 5.2 — Magic strings for element/rarity keys without centralized enum

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, multiple locations
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:** Element names (`'Fusion'`, `'Electro'`, `'Aero'`, `'Glacio'`, `'Havoc'`, `'Spectro'`) appear as literal strings in:
- `DETAIL_ELEMENT_COLORS` (line ~85)
- `BANNER_GRADIENT_MAP` (line ~94)
- Character element badge rendering

If `appcore-data.js` uses different casing or adds a new element, these maps silently fall through to the default.

**Fix:** Define a shared enum in `appcore-data.js`:

```js
// appcore-data.js
export const ELEMENTS = Object.freeze({
  FUSION: 'Fusion',
  ELECTRO: 'Electro',
  AERO: 'Aero',
  GLACIO: 'Glacio',
  HAVOC: 'Havoc',
  SPECTRO: 'Spectro',
});
```

Then use it in both data definitions and component lookups:
```jsx
import { ELEMENTS } from './appcore-data.js';
// Validate at lookup time
const colors = DETAIL_ELEMENT_COLORS[data.element] || DETAIL_ELEMENT_COLORS[ELEMENTS.SPECTRO];
```

---

### Issue 5.3 — Inconsistent `memo` usage

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, multiple components
**Severity:** LOW

**Problem:** `Card`, `CardHeader`, `CardBody`, `TabButton`, `CountdownTimer` are wrapped in `React.memo()`. But `CharacterDetailModal`, `WeaponDetailModal`, `MaterialItem`, `TabBackground` are NOT memoized. The modals are expensive to render (multiple data lookups, image loading, nested lists). `MaterialItem` is rendered in grids of 4–8+ items and would benefit from memo.

**Fix:**

```jsx
const MaterialItem = memo(({ name, qty }) => {
  const img = MATERIAL_IMAGES[name];
  return (
    <div className="flex items-center gap-1.5 p-1.5 rounded-lg bg-white/5 border border-white/10 min-w-0">
      {img ? <img src={img} alt={name} className="w-7 h-7 rounded object-contain flex-shrink-0" onError={hideOnError} /> : <div className="w-7 h-7 rounded bg-white/10 flex-shrink-0" />}
      <div className="min-w-0 flex-1">
        <div className="text-[9px] text-gray-300 truncate leading-tight">{name}</div>
        {qty != null && qty > 0 && <div className="text-[9px] text-yellow-400 font-bold leading-tight">&times;{qty}</div>}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
});
MaterialItem.displayName = 'MaterialItem';
```

---

## 6. Accessibility & Resilience

### Issue 6.1 — Error boundary "Try Again" button has no keyboard focus indicator

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, `AppErrorBoundary`, approx line ~350
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:** The `AppErrorBoundary` uses inline styles with no `:focus` or `:focus-visible` styling:
```jsx
<button
  onClick={() => this.setState({ hasError: false, error: null })}
  style={{ padding: '10px 24px', background: 'rgba(6,182,212,0.2)', border: '1px solid rgba(6,182,212,0.4)', color: '#22d3ee', borderRadius: 8, cursor: 'pointer', fontSize: 14, marginRight: 8 }}
>
  Try Again
</button>
```

Because this is the **root-level** error boundary, Tailwind classes may not be available (CSS might have failed to load). The inline styles have no focus indicator, making the buttons invisible to keyboard users.

**Fix:**

```jsx
<button
  onClick={() => this.setState({ hasError: false, error: null })}
  style={{ 
    padding: '10px 24px', 
    background: 'rgba(6,182,212,0.2)', 
    border: '1px solid rgba(6,182,212,0.4)', 
    color: '#22d3ee', 
    borderRadius: 8, 
    cursor: 'pointer', 
    fontSize: 14, 
    marginRight: 8,
    outline: 'none',
  }}
  onFocus={(e) => { e.target.style.boxShadow = '0 0 0 3px rgba(6,182,212,0.5)'; }}
  onBlur={(e) => { e.target.style.boxShadow = 'none'; }}
>
  Try Again
</button>
<button
  onClick={() => window.location.reload()}
  style={{ 
    padding: '10px 24px', 
    background: 'rgba(255,255,255,0.1)', 
    border: '1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.2)', 
    color: '#d1d5db', 
    borderRadius: 8, 
    cursor: 'pointer', 
    fontSize: 14,
    outline: 'none',
  }}
  onFocus={(e) => { e.target.style.boxShadow = '0 0 0 3px rgba(255,255,255,0.3)'; }}
  onBlur={(e) => { e.target.style.boxShadow = 'none'; }}
>
  Reload Page
</button>
```

---

### Issue 6.2 — Modal backdrop click handler may interfere with screen readers

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, `CharacterDetailModal`, approx line ~130
**Severity:** LOW

**Problem:**
```jsx
<div 
  className="fixed inset-0 z-[100] flex items-center justify-center p-4 bg-black/80 backdrop-blur-sm"
  onClick={onClose}
  role="dialog"
  aria-modal="true"
  aria-label={`${name} character details`}
  ref={focusTrapRef}
>
```

The `onClick={onClose}` on the outer `div` with `role="dialog"` means clicking ANYWHERE on the dialog (including its content) triggers close. The inner div has `onClick={e => e.stopPropagation()}` to prevent this, which works for mouse users. However, the outer div lacks `role="presentation"` or a more explicit pattern. A more robust approach separates the backdrop from the dialog.

**Fix:**

```jsx
{/* Backdrop — separate from dialog */}
<div 
  className="fixed inset-0 z-[100] bg-black/80 backdrop-blur-sm"
  onClick={onClose}
  aria-hidden="true"
/>
{/* Dialog container */}
<div 
  className="fixed inset-0 z-[101] flex items-center justify-center p-4 pointer-events-none"
  role="dialog"
  aria-modal="true"
  aria-label={`${name} character details`}
  ref={focusTrapRef}
>
  <div 
    className={`pointer-events-auto relative w-full max-w-md max-h-[85vh] overflow-y-auto rounded-2xl border ${colors.border}`}
    style={{ background: 'rgba(12, 16, 24, 0.95)', animation: 'scaleIn 0.3s ease-out' }}
  >
    {/* ... content ... */}
  </div>
</div>
```

### Issue 6.3 — `TabButton` aria-label is fragile with `Children.toArray` text extraction

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, `TabButton`, approx line ~400
**Severity:** LOW

**Problem:**
```jsx
const text = childArray.find(child => typeof child === 'string')?.trim();
// ...
aria-label={`${text || 'Navigation'} tab`}
```

If children are passed as `<TabButton><Icon /> <span>Calc</span></TabButton>`, the text is wrapped in a `<span>` element, not a raw string. `typeof child === 'string'` won't find it, and the aria-label falls back to "Navigation tab" — unhelpful for screen readers.

**Fix:** Accept an explicit `label` prop:

```jsx
const TabButton = memo(({ active, onClick, children, tabRef, tabId, label }) => {
  // ...
  return (
    <button
      // ...
      aria-label={`${label || text || 'Navigation'} tab`}
    >
```

---

## 7. Performance & Runtime Concerns

### Issue 7.1 — `CountdownTimer` creates new refs on every prop change due to effect cascade

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, `CountdownTimer`, approx line ~430–490
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:**
```jsx
const recalcFnRef = useRef(recalcFn);
const onExpireRef = useRef(onExpire);

useEffect(() => { recalcFnRef.current = recalcFn; }, [recalcFn]);
useEffect(() => { onExpireRef.current = onExpire; }, [onExpire]);
```

This is the correct pattern (sync ref to prop). However, the main timer effect is:

```jsx
useEffect(() => {
  let intervalId = null;
  const updateTimer = () => {
    const end = currentEndRef.current;
    const t = getTimeRemaining(end);
    if (t.expired && recalcFnRef.current) { ... }
    // ...
  };
  // ...
}, []); // empty deps — runs once
```

The empty dependency array means the interval is set up once and reads from refs — this is correct and efficient. **However**, the `endDate` effect:

```jsx
useEffect(() => {
  setCurrentEnd(endDate);
  setTime(getTimeRemaining(endDate));
  expiredRef.current = false;
}, [endDate]);
```

This sets state but does NOT restart the interval. The interval continues reading from `currentEndRef`, which is updated by a separate effect:

```jsx
useEffect(() => { currentEndRef.current = currentEnd; }, [currentEnd]);
```

This creates a 3-step cascade: `endDate` prop → `setCurrentEnd` → re-render → `currentEndRef` sync effect → interval picks up new value on next tick. This works but has a one-second window where the interval may display stale data. Not a bug, but a subtle race condition.

**Fix (simplify the cascade):**

```jsx
// Replace the separate endDate/currentEnd sync with a direct ref update
useEffect(() => {
  currentEndRef.current = endDate;
  setTime(getTimeRemaining(endDate));
  expiredRef.current = false;
}, [endDate]);
```

Remove the `currentEnd` state entirely and use `endDate` prop directly, keeping `currentEndRef` for the interval's consumption. Only use `setCurrentEnd` for recurring timer rollover.

Actually, `currentEnd` state is needed for the rollover case where `recalcFn()` produces a new end date. So keep the state but simplify:

```jsx
const [currentEnd, setCurrentEnd] = useState(endDate);
const currentEndRef = useRef(endDate);

// Sync both when endDate prop changes
useEffect(() => {
  setCurrentEnd(endDate);
  currentEndRef.current = endDate;
  setTime(getTimeRemaining(endDate));
  expiredRef.current = false;
}, [endDate]);

// Sync ref when state changes (from rollover)
useEffect(() => {
  currentEndRef.current = currentEnd;
}, [currentEnd]);
```

---

### Issue 7.2 — `generateMaskGradient` / `generateVerticalMaskGradient` create new strings every render

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, lines ~55–80
**Severity:** LOW

**Problem:** These functions generate CSS gradient strings. If called from within a component render (likely, as style props), they return new string references every render. While strings are compared by value (not reference) so React won't re-render just for this, it adds unnecessary computation.

**Fix:** Components that use these should memoize the result:

```jsx
const maskStyle = useMemo(
  () => generateMaskGradient(fadePos, fadeIntensity),
  [fadePos, fadeIntensity]
);
```

This is a recommendation for consumers, not a fix to these functions.

---

### Issue 7.3 — `parseTeamMembers` is defined inside `CharacterDetailModal` render

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, inside `CharacterDetailModal`, approx line ~140
**Severity:** LOW

**Problem:**
```jsx
const parseTeamMembers = (teamStr) => {
  return teamStr.split('+').map(s => s.trim()).filter(Boolean);
};
```

This function is recreated on every render. It has no closure dependencies and should be hoisted to module scope.

**Fix:**

```jsx
// Module scope (top of file)
const parseTeamMembers = (teamStr) =>
  teamStr.split('+').map(s => s.trim()).filter(Boolean);
```

---

## 8. Security Considerations

### Issue 8.1 — `hideOnError` handler modifies DOM directly

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, line 23
**Severity:** LOW

**Problem:**
```jsx
const hideOnError = (e) => { e.target.style.visibility = 'hidden'; };
```

This directly mutates DOM style outside React's control. While this is a common pattern for image error handling and is generally safe, it means React's virtual DOM is out of sync with the actual DOM for these elements. If React re-renders the parent, it won't restore `visibility` since it never set it.

**Impact:** Minimal in practice — broken images stay hidden even if the URL becomes valid later (e.g., after a network recovery). This is acceptable behavior for a static image gallery.

**Fix (optional, more React-idiomatic):**

```jsx
const ImageWithFallback = memo(({ src, alt, className, style, fallback = null }) => {
  const [failed, setFailed] = useState(false);
  if (failed) return fallback;
  return (
    <img
      src={src}
      alt={alt}
      className={className}
      style={style}
      onError={() => setFailed(true)}
    />
  );
});
ImageWithFallback.displayName = 'ImageWithFallback';
```

This would be a large refactor touching many components. Keeping `hideOnError` is acceptable for now.

---

### Issue 8.2 — No Content Security Policy considerations for image sources

**File:** `appcore-components.jsx`, all image rendering
**Severity:** LOW

**Problem:** Images are loaded from `DEFAULT_COLLECTION_IMAGES` and `MATERIAL_IMAGES` (defined in `appcore-data.js`). These URLs are hardcoded in the data file, not user-supplied, so XSS via image injection is not a concern. However, if these point to external CDNs, a CSP header should whitelist those domains.

**Fix:** Verify in `appcore-data.js` (not in this chunk) where image URLs point. If external, add to CSP in deployment config.

**Verdict: REVISE**

The codebase is functional but carries three HIGH-severity issues that should be addressed before any further feature work:

1. **Vite version compatibility** (1.1) — immediate risk of build breakage
2. **Monolith component file** (2.1) — unsustainable for maintenance; any change touches 93KB
3. **Null propagation in CharacterDetailModal** (3.1) — runtime crash vector

The MEDIUM issues around data flow contracts (3.2–3.4) and dead imports (4.1–4.2) should be resolved in the next sprint. The LOW issues are quality-of-life improvements that can be addressed incrementally.

## 1. Critical Issues

### CRIT-01: Toast background colors use hardcoded Tailwind-default RGBA values that violate the app's own design system tokens

**Files:** `appcore-providers.jsx` (lines ~165-170 in ToastProvider)
**Problem:** The toast notification backgrounds are set via inline `style` with raw `rgba()` values:
```js
background: toast.type === 'success' ? 'rgba(16,185,129,0.9)' 
  : toast.type === 'error' ? 'rgba(239,68,68,0.9)' 
  : toast.type === 'warning' ? 'rgba(245,158,11,0.9)' 
  : 'rgba(59,130,246,0.9)',
```

`rgba(59,130,246,0.9)` is exactly `#3b82f6` (Tailwind `blue-500`) — which is on the app's own **§BAN blacklist** in `art-direction-engine-SKILL.md`. `rgba(239,68,68,0.9)` is `#ef4444` (Tailwind `red-500`), also banned. `rgba(16,185,129,0.9)` is `#10b981` (Tailwind `emerald-500`), also banned. These are not merely aesthetic preferences — the app has a deliberate "Lahai-Roi" design language with gold/amber accent, and the CSS token system in `KuroStyles` already defines `--color-gold`, `--color-emerald`, `--color-red`, `--color-cyan`. The toasts completely bypass the design system.

**Additionally**, the toast container and the install prompt banner both use `z-[9998]`, which creates a z-index collision when both are visible simultaneously. The install banner is at `bottom-20` and the toast container is at `bottom-24` — they can visually overlap.

**Severity:** CRITICAL (design system contract violation, z-index collision)

**Fix:**
```jsx
// In ToastProvider, replace the toast rendering with design-system-aware colors:
const TOAST_STYLES = {
  success: {
    background: 'rgba(34, 197, 94, 0.9)',   // matches --color-emerald
    borderColor: 'rgba(34, 197, 94, 0.4)',
  },
  error: {
    background: 'rgba(248, 113, 113, 0.9)', // matches --color-red
    borderColor: 'rgba(248, 113, 113, 0.4)',
  },
  warning: {
    background: 'rgba(237, 175, 24, 0.9)',   // matches --color-gold (app accent)
    borderColor: 'rgba(237, 175, 24, 0.4)',
  },
  info: {
    background: 'rgba(56, 189, 248, 0.9)',   // matches --color-cyan
    borderColor: 'rgba(56, 189, 248, 0.4)',
  },
};

// In the JSX, replace the inline style:
<div className="fixed bottom-24 left-3 right-3 z-[9997] flex flex-col gap-2 pointer-events-none" role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true">
  {toasts.map(toast => {
    const toastStyle = TOAST_STYLES[toast.type] || TOAST_STYLES.info;
    return (
      <div key={toast.id} className="px-4 py-3 rounded-lg flex items-center gap-2 text-xs font-medium pointer-events-auto text-white border" style={{
        animation: 'slideUp 0.2s ease-out',
        background: toastStyle.background,
        borderColor: toastStyle.borderColor,
      }}>
        {toast.type === 'success' && <CheckCircle size={16} />}
        {toast.type === 'error' && <AlertCircle size={16} />}
        {toast.type === 'warning' && <AlertTriangle size={16} />}
        {toast.type === 'info' && <Info size={16} />}
        {toast.message}
      </div>
    );
  })}
</div>
```

Note the z-index lowered to `9997` to avoid collision with the install banner at `9998`.

---

### CRIT-02: `computeDistMC` uses `Math.random()` for gacha probability calculations — non-reproducible and statistically weak for critical user-facing data

**File:** `appcore-engine.js` (lines ~178-189, `simulateOneRun` function)
**Problem:** The Monte Carlo simulation that drives user-facing probability calculations (e.g., "you have X% chance of getting the character") uses `Math.random()`. This has two cross-cutting concerns:

1. **Non-reproducibility:** Users cannot verify calculations. The same inputs produce different outputs on each run. For a tool users rely on for resource planning (real money decisions), this is problematic.
2. **Statistical quality:** `Math.random()` implementations vary across browsers (V8 uses xorshift128+, which is fine, but Safari's has known weaknesses at high iteration counts).

The `minPullsForProb` binary search (line ~211) is especially vulnerable — it calls MC multiple times and the stochastic noise can cause the binary search to converge to different answers on repeated runs, especially near exact thresholds. The P15-FIX comment acknowledges this by widening the verification window, which is a band-aid.

**Severity:** CRITICAL (user-facing financial planning tool with non-deterministic output)

**Fix:** Use a seeded PRNG for Monte Carlo simulations. This makes results reproducible for the same inputs while maintaining statistical quality:

```js
// Add at top of appcore-engine.js, after imports:

// Seeded PRNG (mulberry32) — deterministic for same seed, statistically adequate for MC
const mulberry32 = (seed) => {
  let s = seed | 0;
  return () => {
    s = (s + 0x6D2B79F5) | 0;
    let t = Math.imul(s ^ (s >>> 15), 1 | s);
    t = (t + Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 7), 61 | t)) ^ t;
    return ((t ^ (t >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
  };
};

// Generate a deterministic seed from simulation parameters
const simSeed = (N, isWeapon, startPity, startGuar) =>
  ((N * 31 + (isWeapon ? 7 : 13)) * 31 + startPity) * 31 + startGuar;

// Replace simulateOneRun to accept a random function:
const simulateOneRun = (isWeapon, N, startPity, startGuar, rng) => {
  let pity = startPity, guar = startGuar, copies = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < N; i++) {
    const rate = getPullRate(pity);
    if (rng() < rate) {
      const featured = (isWeapon || guar === 1) ? true : (rng() < 0.5);
      if (featured) copies++;
      guar = featured ? 0 : 1;
      pity = 0;
    } else {
      pity = Math.min(MAX_PITY, pity + 1);
    }
  }
  return copies;
};

// Replace computeDistMC:
const computeDistMC = (N, isWeapon, startPity = 0, startGuar = 0, maxCopies = 10, trials = 50000) => {
  const rng = mulberry32(simSeed(N, isWeapon, startPity, startGuar));
  const counts = Array(maxCopies + 1).fill(0);
  for (let t = 0; t < trials; t++) {
    const k = simulateOneRun(isWeapon, N, startPity, startGuar, rng);
    counts[Math.min(k, maxCopies)]++;
  }
  return counts.map(c => c / trials);
};
```

---

## 2. High Severity Issues

### HIGH-01: Reducer action type string literals used without `ACTION` constants in some dispatch sites — silent failure risk

**File:** `appcore-engine.js` (lines ~295-310, `ACTION` object definition)
**Problem:** The P15-FIX at line ~295 introduced `ACTION` constants "to prevent silent typo failures in dispatch calls." However, the `reducer` function itself still uses string literals in the `switch` cases:

```js
case ACTION.SET_SERVER:    // ✅ Uses constant
```

This is internally consistent. But the **cross-cutting concern** is that consumer files (other `.jsx` files not in this chunk) may still dispatch with raw strings like `dispatch({ type: 'SET_CALC', ... })` instead of `dispatch({ type: ACTION.SET_CALC, ... })`. If a consumer misspells the string, the reducer hits `default: return state` silently — the user's action is swallowed with no error.

The `ACTION` object is exported but there's no enforcement mechanism (e.g., the reducer doesn't throw on unknown action types).

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:** Add unknown action type detection to the reducer in development:

```js
const reducer = (state, action) => {
  switch (action.type) {
    case ACTION.SET_SERVER: return { ...state, server: action.server };
    // ... all existing cases ...
    case ACTION.RESET: return initialState;
    default: {
      // Development-time warning for unknown action types
      if (typeof action.type === 'string' && !Object.values(ACTION).includes(action.type)) {
        console.warn(`[WW] Unknown action type dispatched: "${action.type}". Check for typos.`);
      }
      return state;
    }
  }
};
```

---

### HIGH-02: Memory pressure from DP table allocation with no Web Worker offloading

**File:** `appcore-engine.js` (lines ~127-170, `computeDistDP`)
**Problem:** The DP computation allocates a 4D array on the main thread. The comment at line ~198 calculates: at N=500, the table is ~7.1MB of Float64 entries. This runs synchronously on the main thread, blocking UI rendering. For a mobile PWA (the primary target given the install prompt), this causes:

1. Janky scrolling/interaction during calculation
2. Potential browser kill on low-memory devices (512MB RAM Android phones are common in WuWa's Asian market)

The `calcStats` function (line ~413) is called from the UI with user input. A user entering pity=0, copies=7 on a weapon banner triggers `computeDistDP(N, true, 0, 0, 7)` which allocates `(N+1) × 81 × 8` Float64 arrays.

**Severity:** HIGH (main thread blocking on mobile PWA)

**Fix (architectural recommendation):** Move `computeGachaDist` and `calcStats` to a Web Worker. This is a significant refactor, so here's the minimal path:

```js
// appcore-engine.js — Add debounced computation wrapper
// This doesn't move to a worker but prevents blocking during rapid input changes

let calcStatsRAF = null;
const calcStatsAsync = (pulls, pity, guaranteed, isChar, copies) => {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    if (calcStatsRAF) cancelAnimationFrame(calcStatsRAF);
    calcStatsRAF = requestAnimationFrame(() => {
      resolve(calcStats(pulls, pity, guaranteed, isChar, copies));
    });
  });
};

// Export for UI consumption:
export { calcStatsAsync };
```

For a full fix, wrap `computeDistDP` and `computeDistMC` in a dedicated worker file and use `postMessage`/`onmessage`. Given this is a PWA that already registers a service worker, the infrastructure exists.

---

### HIGH-03: `saveToStorage` failure is silent to the user — data loss risk

**File:** `appcore-engine.js` (lines ~357-369, `saveToStorage`)
**Problem:** The P12-FIX added a `return false` on storage failure, but the cross-cutting concern is: **who consumes this return value?** Looking at the exports, `saveToStorage` is exported and presumably called after every state change. But the caller in the React layer (likely `useEffect` or `useReducer` middleware) probably doesn't check the return value or show a toast.

The `QuotaExceededError` scenario is realistic: a user with extensive pull history (1000+ pulls across 5 banner types × ~50 bytes per entry = ~250KB just for history) plus bookmarks plus teams. On iOS Safari, localStorage is capped at 5MB, and in Private Browsing it's sometimes 0MB.

**Severity:** HIGH (silent data loss)

**Fix:** The caller site (not in this chunk) should check the return value. But the engine should also export a more informative result:

```js
const saveToStorage = (state) => {
  if (!storageAvailable) return { success: false, reason: 'unavailable' };
  try {
    const data = JSON.stringify(state);
    const sizeMB = data.length / (1024 * 1024);
    if (sizeMB > 4) {
      console.warn(`[WW] Storage approaching limit: ${sizeMB.toFixed(1)}MB`);
    }
    localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, data);
    return { success: true, sizeMB };
  } catch (e) {
    console.error('[WW] Save failed:', e);
    return { success: false, reason: e.name === 'QuotaExceededError' ? 'quota' : 'unknown', error: e };
  }
};
```

Note: This changes the return type from `boolean` to an object, which is a **breaking change** for callers. All call sites must be updated to check `result.success` instead of truthiness. If that's too risky, keep the boolean but add a global event:

```js
const saveToStorage = (state) => {
  if (!storageAvailable) return true; // Can't save, but don't error
  try {
    const data = JSON.stringify(state);
    if (data.length > 4 * 1024 * 1024) {
      console.warn('[WW] Storage approaching limit:', (data.length / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(1) + 'MB');
    }
    localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, data);
    return true;
  } catch (e) {
    console.error('Save failed (storage full?):', e);
    // Emit custom event so UI layer can show toast without tight coupling
    window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('ww-storage-error', { 
      detail: { type: e.name, message: 'Your data could not be saved. Storage may be full.' }
    }));
    return false;
  }
};
```

Then in `appcore-providers.jsx`, add a listener in `PWAProvider`:

```jsx
// Inside PWAProvider useEffect:
const handleStorageError = (e) => {
  // If ToastContext is available, show toast — but we're above the provider
  // So use a simple DOM alert or custom banner
  console.error('[WW] Storage error:', e.detail.message);
};
window.addEventListener('ww-storage-error', handleStorageError);
return () => {
  // ... existing cleanup ...
  window.removeEventListener('ww-storage-error', handleStorageError);
};
```

---

### HIGH-04: Service Worker registration error handling is too permissive

**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` (lines ~78-89)
**Problem:**
```js
navigator.serviceWorker.register('/sw.js', { scope: '/' })
  .then((registration) => { ... })
  .catch((err) => {
    console.info('[WW] Service worker not registered:', err.message);
  });
```

Using `console.info` for a registration failure masks real issues. If `/sw.js` returns a 404, a syntax error, or a MIME type mismatch, the user gets zero feedback and the PWA silently degrades — offline support, caching, and the update lifecycle all break. For a PWA that shows an install prompt, this is misleading: the user installs the app thinking it works offline, but it doesn't.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**
```js
if ('serviceWorker' in navigator) {
  navigator.serviceWorker.register('/sw.js', { scope: '/' })
    .then((registration) => {
      console.log('[WW] Service worker registered, scope:', registration.scope);
      registration.addEventListener('updatefound', () => {
        const newWorker = registration.installing;
        if (!newWorker) return;
        newWorker.addEventListener('statechange', () => {
          if (newWorker.state === 'installed' && navigator.serviceWorker.controller) {
            console.log('[WW] New version available — refresh to update');
            // Future: dispatch event or set state to show update banner
          }
          if (newWorker.state === 'redundant') {
            console.warn('[WW] Service worker became redundant (install failed or was replaced)');
          }
        });
      });
    })
    .catch((err) => {
      // Distinguish between expected failures (localhost HTTP, file://) and real errors
      const isExpected = location.protocol === 'http:' && location.hostname !== 'localhost';
      console[isExpected ? 'warn' : 'info'](
        '[WW] Service worker not registered:', err.message
      );
    });
}
```

---

### HIGH-05: `useEscapeKey` hook doesn't account for nested modals — Escape closes ALL modals instead of innermost

**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` (lines ~197-203)
**Problem:**
```js
const useEscapeKey = (isOpen, onClose) => {
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!isOpen) return;
    const handler = (e) => { if (e.key === 'Escape') { e.stopPropagation(); onClose(); } };
    document.addEventListener('keydown', handler);
    return () => document.removeEventListener('keydown', handler);
  }, [isOpen, onClose]);
};
```

When multiple modals are open (e.g., Onboarding → a nested confirmation), `e.stopPropagation()` stops DOM propagation but **not** multiple `document.addEventListener('keydown')` listeners. Each modal registers its own `keydown` listener on `document`. Since `stopPropagation()` only prevents the event from traveling UP the DOM tree, and all listeners are on `document` itself, **all handlers fire**. This means pressing Escape closes every open modal simultaneously.

**Severity:** HIGH (UX bug affecting modal stack)

**Fix:** Use a modal stack manager:

```js
// Modal stack to ensure only the topmost modal handles Escape
const modalStack = [];

const useEscapeKey = (isOpen, onClose) => {
  const idRef = useRef(null);
  
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!isOpen) {
      // Remove from stack when closed
      if (idRef.current !== null) {
        const idx = modalStack.indexOf(idRef.current);
        if (idx !== -1) modalStack.splice(idx, 1);
        idRef.current = null;
      }
      return;
    }
    
    // Assign a unique stack ID
    const stackId = Date.now() + Math.random();
    idRef.current = stackId;
    modalStack.push(stackId);
    
    const handler = (e) => {
      if (e.key === 'Escape') {
        // Only the topmost modal should handle Escape
        if (modalStack[modalStack.length - 1] === stackId) {
          e.preventDefault();
          onClose();
        }
      }
    };
    
    document.addEventListener('keydown', handler);
    return () => {
      document.removeEventListener('keydown', handler);
      const idx = modalStack.indexOf(stackId);
      if (idx !== -1) modalStack.splice(idx, 1);
      idRef.current = null;
    };
  }, [isOpen, onClose]);
};
```
