## 3. Medium Severity Issues

### MED-01: `PWAProvider` injects meta tags that may conflict with `index.html`

**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` (lines ~55-72)
**Problem:** The code checks `if (!document.querySelector(\`meta[name="${name}"]\`))` before injecting. But the `viewport` meta tag is almost certainly already in `index.html`. If it IS there, the injection is skipped — fine. But if `index.html` has a DIFFERENT viewport value (e.g., without `viewport-fit=cover`), the code silently accepts the inferior version.

Additionally, cleanup on unmount removes only tags with `data-ww="true"`, which is correct. But in a React StrictMode dev environment, the double-mount-unmount-mount cycle will:
1. First mount: inject tags with `data-ww`
2. StrictMode unmount: remove them
3. Second mount: inject them again

This is harmless in production but generates confusing DOM mutations in development.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**
```jsx
metaTags.forEach(({ name, content }) => {
  const existing = document.querySelector(`meta[name="${name}"]`);
  if (existing) {
    // Update existing meta tag if our value is more complete
    if (name === 'viewport' && !existing.content.includes('viewport-fit')) {
      existing.content = content;
      existing.setAttribute('data-ww', 'true');
    }
    // Don't duplicate other existing tags
    return;
  }
  const meta = document.createElement('meta');
  meta.name = name;
  meta.content = content;
  meta.setAttribute('data-ww', 'true');
  document.head.appendChild(meta);
});
```

---

### MED-02: `ToastProvider` uses `setTimeout` for auto-dismiss without cleanup on unmount

**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` (lines ~153-157)
**Problem:**
```js
const addToast = useCallback((message, type = 'info', duration = 3000) => {
  const id = generateUniqueId();
  setToasts(prev => [...prev, { id, message, type }]);
  setTimeout(() => setToasts(prev => prev.filter(t => t.id !== id)), duration);
  // ...
}, []);
```

If `ToastProvider` unmounts before the `setTimeout` fires (unlikely in practice since it wraps the whole app, but possible in testing or fast navigation), `setToasts` will be called on an unmounted component. React 18 doesn't warn about this, but it's still a dangling reference.

More practically: there's no way to dismiss a toast manually (no click handler on the toast itself). The toast type `warning` for offline status may need longer display.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**
```jsx
const ToastProvider = ({ children }) => {
  const [toasts, setToasts] = useState([]);
  const timerRefs = useRef(new Map());
  
  // Cleanup all timers on unmount
  useEffect(() => {
    return () => {
      timerRefs.current.forEach(timer => clearTimeout(timer));
      timerRefs.current.clear();
    };
  }, []);
  
  const removeToast = useCallback((id) => {
    setToasts(prev => prev.filter(t => t.id !== id));
    const timer = timerRefs.current.get(id);
    if (timer) {
      clearTimeout(timer);
      timerRefs.current.delete(id);
    }
  }, []);
  
  const addToast = useCallback((message, type = 'info', duration = 3000) => {
    const id = generateUniqueId();
    setToasts(prev => [...prev, { id, message, type }]);
    const timer = setTimeout(() => removeToast(id), duration);
    timerRefs.current.set(id, timer);
    if (type === 'success') haptic.success();
    else if (type === 'error') haptic.error();
    else if (type === 'warning') haptic.warning();
    else haptic.light();
  }, [removeToast]);
  
  const contextValue = useMemo(() => ({ addToast, removeToast }), [addToast, removeToast]);
  
  // In JSX, add click-to-dismiss:
  // <div key={toast.id} onClick={() => removeToast(toast.id)} className="... cursor-pointer" ...>
```

---

### MED-03: `loadFromStorage` always resets `calc` to initial state, breaking bookmark restore flow

**File:** `appcore-engine.js` (lines ~335-336)
**Problem:**
```js
calc: { ...initialState.calc }, // Always start calculator fresh - no sync from saved data
```

The comment says "always start fresh." But `LOAD_BOOKMARK` (line ~395) restores calculator state from a saved bookmark. If the app loads → `loadFromStorage` wipes calc → user expects their last session's calc to be there → it's gone. Then the user loads a bookmark and it works.

However, if the user saves a bookmark, closes the app, reopens it, the bookmark data is preserved (in `bookmarks` array) but the calculator is reset. This is **intentionally designed** (the comment is clear), but there's a cross-cutting inconsistency: `saveToStorage` saves the **entire** state including `calc`, yet `loadFromStorage` discards `calc`. This means:

1. Wasted storage bytes (calc data is written but never read back)
2. User confusion: "I saved my settings and closed the app, why is the calculator blank?"

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:** Either don't save `calc` to storage (save bandwidth), or restore it. The current design intent seems to be "fresh calc on each session," so:

```js
const saveToStorage = (state) => {
  if (!storageAvailable) return true;
  try {
    // Don't persist calc state — it's always reset on load
    const { calc, ...persistedState } = state;
    const data = JSON.stringify(persistedState);
    if (data.length > 4 * 1024 * 1024) {
      console.warn('Storage approaching limit:', (data.length / 1024 / 1024).toFixed(1) + 'MB');
    }
    localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, data);
    return true;
  } catch (e) {
    console.error('Save failed (storage full?):', e);
    return false;
  }
};
```

---

### MED-04: `sanitizeStateObj` is called redundantly 10+ times during `loadFromStorage`

**File:** `appcore-engine.js` (lines ~315-345)
**Problem:** `loadFromStorage` calls `sanitizeStateObj(parsed)` on the entire state, then calls it again on each nested sub-object:
```js
const safeParsed = sanitizeStateObj(parsed);  // ← sanitizes everything recursively
return {
  ...initialState,
  ...sanitizeImportedState(safeParsed),  // ← sanitizeImportedState calls sanitizeStateObj AGAIN
  profile: {
    ...initialState.profile,
    ...(safeParsed.profile ? sanitizeStateObj(safeParsed.profile) : {}),  // ← THIRD time for profile
    featured: { ...initialState.profile.featured, ...(safeParsed.profile?.featured ? sanitizeStateObj(safeParsed.profile.featured) : {}) },  // ← FOURTH time for featured
    // ... same pattern for weapon, standardChar, standardWeap, beginner
  },
```

`sanitizeStateObj` is recursive — it already sanitizes all nested objects. Calling it again on sub-properties does redundant work. With a large pull history (1000+ items), each with ~5 fields, this creates thousands of unnecessary object allocations on app startup.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (performance on startup, especially mobile)

**Fix:**
```js
const loadFromStorage = () => {
  if (!storageAvailable) return null;
  try {
    const saved = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY);
    if (!saved) return null;
    const parsed = JSON.parse(saved);
    // Single deep sanitization pass — covers all nested objects recursively
    const safe = sanitizeStateObj(parsed);
    // Validate top-level keys
    const validated = sanitizeImportedState(safe);
    
    const safeProfile = validated.profile || {};
    
    return {
      ...initialState,
      ...validated,
      server: validated.server || initialState.server,
      profile: {
        ...initialState.profile,
        ...safeProfile,
        featured: { ...initialState.profile.featured, ...(safeProfile.featured || {}) },
        weapon: { ...initialState.profile.weapon, ...(safeProfile.weapon || {}) },
        standardChar: { ...initialState.profile.standardChar, ...(safeProfile.standardChar || {}) },
        standardWeap: { ...initialState.profile.standardWeap, ...(safeProfile.standardWeap || {}) },
        beginner: { ...initialState.profile.beginner, ...(safeProfile.beginner || {}) },
      },
      calc: { ...initialState.calc },
      planner: { ...initialState.planner, ...(validated.planner || {}) },
      settings: { ...initialState.settings, ...(validated.settings || {}) },
      bookmarks: validated.bookmarks || [],
      eventStatus: validated.eventStatus || {},
      teams: Array.isArray(validated.teams) && validated.teams.length === 5
        ? validated.teams.map((t, i) => ({
            name: (t && typeof t.name === 'string') ? t.name : initialState.teams[i].name,
            slots: (t && Array.isArray(t.slots) && t.slots.length === 3) ? t.slots : [null, null, null],
          }))
        : initialState.teams,
      activeTeamIndex: typeof validated.activeTeamIndex === 'number' ? Math.max(0, Math.min(4, validated.activeTeamIndex)) : 0,
    };
  } catch (e) {
    console.error('Load failed:', e);
    return null;
  }
};
```

---

### MED-05: `IMPORT_HISTORY` reducer case has reference equality check that may give false negatives

**File:** `appcore-engine.js` (lines ~377-383)
**Problem:**
```js
const merged = deduplicateMerge(state.profile.featured?.history, action.history);
const hadNewEntries = merged !== state.profile.featured?.history;
```

`deduplicateMerge` returns `existing` (same reference) when all incoming entries are duplicates:
```js
if (newEntries.length === 0) return existing; // All duplicates
```

But if `existing` is empty (`[]`) and `incoming` is also empty, it returns `existing` — correct. However, if `existing` is `undefined` (first import before any history exists), the function returns `incoming` (a new array). Then `hadNewEntries` would be `true` even though no meaningful data changed, causing unnecessary pity overwrites.

More critically: `state.profile.featured?.history` could be `undefined` if the state was loaded from an old schema. The `deduplicateMerge` function handles this with `if (!existing || existing.length === 0) return incoming;` — but this means the reference equality check `merged !== undefined` is always `true`, triggering a pity overwrite even when `action.history` is empty.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**
```js
const deduplicateMerge = (existing, incoming) => {
  if (!incoming || incoming.length === 0) return existing || [];
  if (!existing || existing.length === 0) return incoming;
  const makeKey = (p) => `${p.timestamp || ''}|${p.name || ''}|${p.rarity || ''}|${p.id || ''}`;
  const existingKeys = new Set(existing.map(makeKey));
  const newEntries = incoming.filter(p => !existingKeys.has(makeKey(p)));
  if (newEntries.length === 0) return existing;
  return [...existing, ...newEntries].sort((a, b) => new Date(a.timestamp || 0) - new Date(b.timestamp || 0));
};

// Replace reference equality with length-based check:
const merged = deduplicateMerge(state.profile.featured?.history, action.history);
const hadNewEntries = merged.length > (state.profile.featured?.history?.length || 0);
```

---

### MED-06: `KuroStyles` CSS uses `rgba(0,0,0,...)` shadows despite the design system banning them

**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` (KuroStyles component, CSS custom properties section)
**Problem:**
```css
--shadow-sm: 0 1px 2px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.3);
--shadow-md: 0 4px 12px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.4);
--shadow-lg: 0 8px 24px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.5);
--shadow-xl: 0 12px 40px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.6);
```

The art direction engine's `§FIX` section explicitly bans `rgba(0,0,0,...)` shadows: *"Black shadows don't exist in reality"* and prescribes `oklch(6-10% 0.02 [palette-hue] / 0.15-0.25)`. The app uses a deep blue hue (`--hue-base ≈ 220-240` based on `#080c14` background). These should be hue-tinted.

Additionally, the `.kuro-card` class uses:
```css
box-shadow: 0 4px 24px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.5), ...
```

And `.kuro-card:hover` uses:
```css
box-shadow: 0 8px 32px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.6), ...
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM (design system inconsistency — functional but violates stated standards)

**Fix:**
```css
:root {
  /* Palette-derived shadows — deep navy hue instead of pure black */
  --shadow-sm: 0 1px 2px rgba(6, 10, 24, 0.4);
  --shadow-md: 0 4px 12px rgba(6, 10, 24, 0.5);
  --shadow-lg: 0 8px 24px rgba(6, 10, 24, 0.6);
  --shadow-xl: 0 12px 40px rgba(6, 10, 24, 0.7);
}

/* In .kuro-card: */
.kuro-card {
  box-shadow: 
    0 4px 24px rgba(6, 10, 24, 0.6),
    0 0 0 1px rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.03),
    inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.05);
}

/* In .kuro-card:hover: */
@media (hover: hover) {
  .kuro-card:hover {
    box-shadow: 
      0 8px 32px rgba(6, 10, 24, 0.7),
      0 0 0 1px rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.06),
      0 0 40px rgba(var(--color-gold), 0.03),
      inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.08);
  }
}
```

```css
}
```

### MED-08: `getNextWeeklyReset` has an edge case with `daysToMon` calculation for Sunday

**File:** `appcore-engine.js` (lines ~95-110)
**Problem:**
```js
if (dayOfWeek === 1 && !pastReset) {
  daysToMon = 0;
} else if (dayOfWeek === 1 && pastReset) {
  daysToMon = 7;
} else {
  daysToMon = dayOfWeek === 0 ? 1 : (8 - dayOfWeek);
}
```

For `dayOfWeek === 0` (Sunday), `daysToMon = 1` — next Monday is tomorrow. This is correct.

But for `dayOfWeek === 6` (Saturday): `daysToMon = 8 - 6 = 2` — Monday is 2 days away. Correct.

For `dayOfWeek === 2` (Tuesday): `daysToMon = 8 - 2 = 6` — Monday is 6 days away. Correct.

Actually, this logic is correct for all days. However, the edge case is when the server is in a DST transition and the day-of-week calculation is off by one due to the initial `serverOffset` being wrong for the target date. The two-pass DST correction at the end handles this for the TIME but not for the DATE. If the initial `nowInServerTz` calculation uses a winter offset but the current moment is the first hour of summer time, `day` and `dayOfWeek` could be wrong.

Wait — `getServerOffset(server)` without a date argument uses the current time, which should be correct for "now." The risk is only theoretical. I'll downgrade this.

**Severity:** LOW (theoretical DST edge case, self-corrects within an hour)

### MED-09: `FocusTrapModal` doesn't prevent background scroll

**File:** `appcore-providers.jsx` (lines ~206-222, `FocusTrapModal`)
**Problem:** When the modal is open, the background page is still scrollable on mobile. The modal has `fixed inset-0` which covers the viewport, but touch events on the modal's background pass through to the underlying scroll container on iOS Safari (known iOS bug with `position: fixed`).

**Severity:** MEDIUM (mobile UX — scroll bleed behind modals)

**Fix:**
```jsx
const FocusTrapModal = ({ isOpen, onClose, ariaLabel, children, className = '', onClick }) => {
  const focusTrapRef = useFocusTrap(isOpen);
  useEscapeKey(isOpen, onClose);
  
  // Prevent background scroll when modal is open
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!isOpen) return;
    const scrollY = window.scrollY;
    document.body.style.overflow = 'hidden';
    document.body.style.position = 'fixed';
    document.body.style.top = `-${scrollY}px`;
    document.body.style.width = '100%';
    return () => {
      document.body.style.overflow = '';
      document.body.style.position = '';
      document.body.style.top = '';
      document.body.style.width = '';
      window.scrollTo(0, scrollY);
    };
  }, [isOpen]);
  
  if (!isOpen) return null;
  return (
    <div 
      ref={focusTrapRef}
      className={`fixed inset-0 z-[100] flex items-center justify-center p-4 ${className}`}
      role="dialog"
      aria-modal="true"
      aria-label={ariaLabel}
      onClick={onClick}
    >
      {children}
    </div>
  );
};
```

---


### LOW-04: `minPullsForProb` MC verification loop may never `break`

**File:** `appcore-engine.js` (lines ~277-286)
**Problem:**
```js
if (ans > DP_MAX_PULLS && ans > 1) {
  for (let check = ans - 5; check <= ans + 5; check++) {
    if (check < 1) continue;
    const vDist = computeDistMC(check, isWeapon, startPity, startGuar, targetK, 500000);
    if (getCumulativeProb(vDist, targetK) * 100 >= minProb) {
      ans = Math.min(ans, check);
      break;
    }
  }
}
```

If the MC with 500K trials never reaches `>= minProb` for any value in `[ans-5, ans+5]`, the loop completes without updating `ans`. This is fine — `ans` retains the binary search result. But if MC is consistently lower than the binary search DP result (which shouldn't happen but can due to stochastic variance), the verification effectively does nothing and wastes ~5.5M simulation iterations.

**Severity:** LOW (wasted computation in edge case)

## 5. Architectural Observations

### ARCH-01: Provider Nesting Order Matters but Isn't Documented

`PWAProvider` and `ToastProvider` are separate components. `ToastProvider` provides `addToast` via context. `PWAProvider` might want to show toasts (e.g., "You're offline" as a toast instead of a banner, or "Storage full"). But if `PWAProvider` wraps `ToastProvider`, it can't access `useToast()`. The current architecture has `PWAProvider` using its own inline UI for offline/install notifications, which duplicates the notification pattern.

**Recommendation:** Consider unifying: `ToastProvider` wraps `PWAProvider`, or `PWAProvider` accepts an `onNotify` callback.

### ARCH-02: Engine File Mixes Concerns

`appcore-engine.js` contains:
1. Time utilities (server timezone, DST, reset times)
2. Gacha simulation (DP, Monte Carlo, probability math)
3. State management (reducer, actions, persistence)
4. Statistics calculation

These are four distinct domains. The file is 36KB and growing. Consider splitting into:
- `engine-time.js` — server time utilities
- `engine-gacha.js` — probability engine
- `engine-state.js` — reducer + persistence
- `engine-calc.js` — `calcStats` and helpers

This would improve tree-shaking (components that only need time utilities don't load the gacha engine) and make testing easier.

### ARCH-03: No Error Boundary

`appcore-providers.jsx` provides Toast, PWA, Onboarding, Focus Trap, and Styles — but no React Error Boundary. If any child component throws during render, the entire app crashes with a white screen. A gacha companion app used during time-sensitive banner events (users checking pity before spending real money) needs graceful degradation.

**Recommendation:** Add an error boundary component in `appcore-providers.jsx`:
```jsx
class ErrorBoundary extends React.Component {
  state = { hasError: false, error: null };
  static getDerivedStateFromError(error) { return { hasError: true, error }; }
  componentDidCatch(error, info) { console.error('[WW] Render error:', error, info); }
  render() {
    if (this.state.hasError) {
      return (
        <div style={{ padding: 24, color: '#dfe5ef', background: '#080c14', minHeight: '100vh' }}>
          <h1 style={{ fontFamily: 'Rajdhani, sans-serif', color: '#edaf18' }}>Something went wrong</h1>
          <p>Try refreshing the page. Your data is saved locally.</p>
          <button onClick={() => window.location.reload()} style={{ marginTop: 12, padding: '8px 16px', background: '#edaf18', color: '#000', border: 'none', borderRadius: 8 }}>
            Refresh
          </button>
        </div>
      );
    }
    return this.props.children;
  }
}
```

## 1. Critical Issues

### CRIT-01: `addCombatDataEntry` Receives Object but Treats It as Array — Logic Bug

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js`, lines ~233–255

**Problem:** The function signature is `addCombatDataEntry(name, dmgFocus, buffs, debuffs)`. Inside, it reads `dmgFocus._rarity` (treating it as an object), then deletes that property, then immediately checks `Array.isArray(dmgFocus)`. Since `dmgFocus` was an object with `_rarity`, it will never be an array, meaning `dmgArr` will always be `''` (empty string). The data written is always wrong.

```javascript
// CURRENT (broken):
export function addCombatDataEntry(name, dmgFocus, buffs, debuffs) {
  const marker5Star = "  // 4★\n";
  const marker4Star = "].forEach(";

  const rarity = dmgFocus._rarity || 5;
  delete dmgFocus._rarity;

  const marker = rarity === 5 ? marker5Star : marker4Star;
  const dmgArr = (Array.isArray(dmgFocus) ? dmgFocus : []).map(d => `'${d}'`).join(', ');
  const buffArr = (Array.isArray(buffs) ? buffs : []).map(b => `'${b}'`).join(', ');
  const debArr = (Array.isArray(debuffs) ? debuffs : []).map(d => `'${d}'`).join(', ');
```

**Fix:** The caller (from `ai.js` `generateCombatData`) returns arrays of arrays `["CharName", ["dmg"], ["buff"], ["debuff"]]`. The rarity detection should come from a separate parameter or the combat data entry itself should be an array. The `_rarity` convention is an undocumented side-channel that corrupts the data.

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
export function addCombatDataEntry(name, dmgFocus, buffs, debuffs, rarity = 5) {
  const marker5Star = "  // 4★\n";
  const marker4Star = "].forEach(";

  const marker = rarity === 5 ? marker5Star : marker4Star;
  const dmgArr = (Array.isArray(dmgFocus) ? dmgFocus : []).map(d => `'${d}'`).join(', ');
  const buffArr = (Array.isArray(buffs) ? buffs : []).map(b => `'${b}'`).join(', ');
  const debArr = (Array.isArray(debuffs) ? debuffs : []).map(d => `'${d}'`).join(', ');

  const entry = `  ['${name}',${' '.repeat(Math.max(1, 18 - name.length))}[${dmgArr}],${' '.repeat(Math.max(1, 40 - dmgArr.length))}[${buffArr}],${' '.repeat(Math.max(1, 50 - buffArr.length))}[${debArr}]],\n`;

  if (buffer.includes(marker)) {
    buffer = buffer.replace(marker, entry + marker);
    log.ok(`Added combat data for ${name}`);
    return true;
  }
  return false;
}
```

**Severity:** CRITICAL — Every combat data entry written by the agent will have empty damage focus arrays.

---

### CRIT-02: `safeReplace` Uses `String.split` for Counting but `String.replace` for Replacement — Race Condition on Multi-Match

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js`, lines ~34–43

**Problem:** `buffer.split(oldStr).length - 1` counts occurrences, and if count > 1 it throws. But `String.prototype.replace(string, string)` only replaces the **first** occurrence. So the uniqueness check is correct as a safety guard, but if for any reason the check were removed or bypassed, only the first match would be replaced. More importantly, there's no protection against `oldStr` being an empty string — `''.split('')` behaves unexpectedly and `buffer.includes('')` is always `true`.

```javascript
// CURRENT:
function safeReplace(oldStr, newStr, description) {
  if (!buffer.includes(oldStr)) {
    throw new Error(`WRITE FAILED — could not find target string for: ${description}\nSearching for:\n${oldStr.slice(0, 200)}...`);
  }
  const count = buffer.split(oldStr).length - 1;
  if (count > 1) {
    throw new Error(`WRITE FAILED — target string appears ${count} times (must be unique): ${description}`);
  }
  buffer = buffer.replace(oldStr, newStr);
  log.ok(`Applied: ${description}`);
}
```

**Fix:**

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
function safeReplace(oldStr, newStr, description) {
  if (!oldStr || oldStr.length === 0) {
    throw new Error(`WRITE FAILED — empty search string for: ${description}`);
  }
  if (!buffer.includes(oldStr)) {
    throw new Error(`WRITE FAILED — could not find target string for: ${description}\nSearching for:\n${oldStr.slice(0, 200)}...`);
  }
  const count = buffer.split(oldStr).length - 1;
  if (count > 1) {
    throw new Error(`WRITE FAILED — target string appears ${count} times (must be unique): ${description}`);
  }
  buffer = buffer.replace(oldStr, newStr);
  log.ok(`Applied: ${description}`);
}
```

**Severity:** CRITICAL — An empty-string `oldStr` would silently corrupt the entire buffer.

---

### CRIT-03: `escapeSingleQuote` Used Inconsistently — SQL-Injection-Style Code Injection in Data Writes

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js`, lines ~199–210 (`addCharacterEntry`), ~228 (`addWeaponEntry`)

**Problem:** Character descriptions, skill names, team comp names, and other user-facing strings are embedded in single-quoted JS string literals via template literals. The `escapeSingleQuote` function is only called on `data.desc`, but NOT on `data.role`, `data.element`, `data.weapon`, skill names, ascension boss names, team names, etc. Any of these values coming from Claude's analysis of web content could contain single quotes (e.g., `"Sentinel's Breach"`, `"Rover's Team"`), which would break the JS syntax of `appcore-data.js`.

```javascript
// CURRENT (partial — showing unescaped fields):
const entry = `  '${name}': { rarity: ${data.rarity}, element: '${data.element}', weapon: '${data.weapon}', role: '${data.role}',
    desc: '${escapeSingleQuote(data.desc || '')}',
    skills: [${skills}],
    ascension: { boss: '${data.ascension?.boss || 'TBD'}', common: '${data.ascension?.common || 'TBD'}', specialty: '${data.ascension?.specialty || 'TBD'}' },
    skillMaterials: { weeklyDrop: '${data.skillMaterials?.weeklyDrop || 'TBD'}', forgery: '${data.skillMaterials?.forgery || 'TBD'}' },
    bestEchoes: [${bestEchoes}], bestWeapon: '${data.bestWeapon || 'TBD'}',
    teams: [${teams}] },\n`;
```

**Fix:** Apply `escapeSingleQuote` to every string field interpolated into single-quoted JS strings:

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
const esc = escapeSingleQuote; // alias for readability

const entry = `  '${esc(name)}': { rarity: ${data.rarity}, element: '${esc(data.element)}', weapon: '${esc(data.weapon)}', role: '${esc(data.role)}',
    desc: '${esc(data.desc || '')}',
    skills: [${skills}],
    ascension: { boss: '${esc(data.ascension?.boss || 'TBD')}', common: '${esc(data.ascension?.common || 'TBD')}', specialty: '${esc(data.ascension?.specialty || 'TBD')}' },
    skillMaterials: { weeklyDrop: '${esc(data.skillMaterials?.weeklyDrop || 'TBD')}', forgery: '${esc(data.skillMaterials?.forgery || 'TBD')}' },
    bestEchoes: [${bestEchoes}], bestWeapon: '${esc(data.bestWeapon || 'TBD')}',
    teams: [${teams}] },\n`;
```

Similarly in `addWeaponEntry` — `data.type`, `data.stat`, `data.subStatValue` are all unescaped. The `bestFor` array entries are also unescaped when they come from `data.bestFor?.map(b => `'${b}'`)`.

```javascript
// CORRECTED addWeaponEntry bestFor:
const bestFor = data.bestFor?.map(b => `'${esc(b)}'`).join(', ') || `'${esc(data.signatureFor || 'TBD')}'`;

const entry = `  ${qName}: { rarity: ${data.rarity}, type: '${esc(data.type)}', stat: '${esc(data.stat)}', baseAtk: ${data.baseAtk || 587}, subStatValue: '${esc(data.subStatValue || 'TBD')}',
    desc: '${esc(data.desc || '')}',
    passive: '${esc(data.passive || '')}', bestFor: [${bestFor}],
    ascensionMaterials: { forgery: '${esc(data.ascensionMaterials?.forgery || 'TBD')}', common: '${esc(data.ascensionMaterials?.common || 'TBD')}' } },\n`;
```

**Severity:** CRITICAL — Web-scraped data containing apostrophes will produce invalid JS, breaking the entire app data file.

## 2. High Severity Issues

### HIGH-01: `updateCurrentBanners` Uses Fragile Regex Anchors That May Not Match

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js`, lines ~100–140

**Problem:** The function uses multiple sequential regex matches to find and replace subsections of `CURRENT_BANNERS`. The weapons regex anchors on `// Standard`:

```javascript
const oldWeaps = buffer.match(/weapons:\s*\[\n([\s\S]*?)\n\s*\],\s*\n\s*\/\/ Standard/);
```

If someone changes or removes the `// Standard` comment in `appcore-data.js`, this entire function silently fails to update weapons. The characters regex anchors on `weapons:` — if the data is reformatted (e.g., trailing whitespace changes), it also fails. There's no error reporting when `oldChars` or `oldWeaps` is null.

**Fix:** Add error logging for failed regex matches:

```javascript
// CORRECTED — add error reporting:
  const oldChars = buffer.match(/characters:\s*\[\n([\s\S]*?)\n\s*\],\s*\n\s*weapons:/);
  if (oldChars) {
    safeReplace(
      oldChars[0],
      `characters: [\n${charEntries}\n  ],\n  weapons:`,
      `Update featured characters: ${bannerData.characters.map(c => c.name).join(', ')}`
    );
  } else {
    log.error('Could not locate characters array in CURRENT_BANNERS — regex anchor mismatch');
    return false;
  }

  const oldWeaps = buffer.match(/weapons:\s*\[\n([\s\S]*?)\n\s*\],\s*\n\s*\/\/ Standard/);
  if (oldWeaps) {
    safeReplace(
      oldWeaps[0],
      `weapons: [\n${weapEntries}\n  ],\n  // Standard`,
      `Update featured weapons: ${bannerData.weapons.map(w => w.name).join(', ')}`
    );
  } else {
    log.error('Could not locate weapons array in CURRENT_BANNERS — regex anchor mismatch');
    return false;
  }
```

**Severity:** HIGH — Silent failures leave stale banner data.

---

### HIGH-02: `addCharacterEntry` Insertion Point Logic is Duplicated and Dead

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js`, lines ~185–196

**Problem:** The variable `insertBefore` is assigned identically regardless of the ternary condition:

```javascript
const insertBefore = isNew5Star ? marker : marker;
buffer = buffer.replace(insertBefore, entry + insertBefore);
```

This is dead logic — both branches of the ternary are `marker`. Additionally, the 4-star marker `"\n};\n\n// Structured combat data"` requires that exact comment to exist in `appcore-data.js`. If the comment doesn't exist, new 4-star characters silently fail to insert.

**Fix:**

```javascript
// CORRECTED — remove dead ternary, add validation:
  if (!buffer.includes(marker)) {
    log.error(`Could not find insertion marker for ${name} (looking for ${isNew5Star ? '4★ section header' : 'combat data block'})`);
    return false;
  }

  buffer = buffer.replace(marker, entry + marker);
```

**Severity:** HIGH — Dead code indicates logic error; 4-star character insertions may silently fail.

---

### HIGH-03: `reader.js` Regex for `extractEvents` Fails on Last Event in Block

**File:** `agent/lib/reader.js`, lines ~107–120

**Problem:** The events regex uses a lookahead `(?=\n\s*\w+:|$)` to delimit each event block. But the `$` anchor may not match if the final event block ends with `}` before the closing `};` of the EVENTS object. The regex `(\w+):\s*\{([\s\S]*?)(?=\n\s*\w+:|$)` is greedy for `[\s\S]*?` but its lookahead may consume too much or too little.

More critically, `\w+:` will match ANY word-colon pattern inside the event body (like `name:`, `currentEnd:`, `resetType:`), potentially splitting an event block prematurely.

```javascript
// CURRENT:
const eventMatches = content.matchAll(/(\w+):\s*\{([\s\S]*?)(?=\n\s*\w+:|$)/g);
```

**Fix:** Use a more precise delimiter — match event keys that are at the start of a line with no leading property indentation:

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
const eventMatches = content.matchAll(/^  (\w+):\s*\{([\s\S]*?)\n  \}/gm);
for (const m of eventMatches) {
  const key = m[1];
  const body = m[2];
  const name = body.match(/name:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
  const currentEnd = body.match(/currentEnd:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || null;
  const resetType = body.match(/resetType:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
  const rewards = body.match(/rewards:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
  events[key] = { name, currentEnd, resetType, rewards };
}
```

**Severity:** HIGH — Event parsing may silently return malformed data, leading to stale or incorrect event timers.

---

### HIGH-04: `banner-images.js` `applyBannerImages` Regex Replacement Without `safeReplace` — Risk of Multi-Match Corruption

**File:** `agent/lib/banner-images.js`, lines ~145–180

**Problem:** Image URL insertions use `buf.replace(match[0], ...)` directly instead of the `safeReplace` function from `writer.js`. If the same pattern appears twice in the buffer (e.g., a character appears in both CURRENT_BANNERS and BANNER_HISTORY), `replace` only replaces the first occurrence, which may not be the intended one.

```javascript
// CURRENT:
loadBufferFn(buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}'${imgbbUrl}'`));
```

**Fix:** Since `banner-images.js` can't directly use `safeReplace` (which operates on the internal `writer.js` buffer), it should at minimum verify uniqueness:

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
const occurrences = buf.split(match[0]).length - 1;
if (occurrences > 1) {
  log.warn(`Banner image slot ${slot} matched ${occurrences} times — skipping to avoid corruption`);
  continue;
}
loadBufferFn(buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}'${imgbbUrl}'`));
```

**Severity:** HIGH — Could replace the wrong image URL in a multi-match scenario.

---

### HIGH-05: `meta-refresh.js` Regex Matches Greedily Across Character Boundaries

**File:** `agent/lib/meta-refresh.js`, lines ~120–145

**Problem:** The regex `'${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?bestWeapon:\\s*'[^']*'` uses `[\s\S]*?` which crosses object boundaries. If character "Aalto" has no `bestWeapon` field but "Baizhi" (later in the file) does, the lazy match still works — but `[\s\S]*?` could theoretically match across multiple character entries if the target field is missing for the first character.

More concretely: if `update.name` is "Encore" and Encore's entry doesn't have a `bestWeapon` field (corrupt data), `[\s\S]*?bestWeapon:` will match all the way to the NEXT character's `bestWeapon`, replacing the wrong character's data.

```javascript
// CURRENT:
const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?bestWeapon:\\s*)'[^']*'`);
```

**Fix:** Bound the regex to a single character entry by limiting the distance:

```javascript
// CORRECTED — use a bounded non-greedy match (max ~2000 chars for one entry):
const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':\\s*\\{[^}]{0,2000}?bestWeapon:\\s*)'[^']*'`);
```

Apply the same pattern to `bestEchoes` and `teams` regexes in the same function.

**Severity:** HIGH — Can overwrite the wrong character's build data.

---

### HIGH-06: Missing Import — `isExistingUrl` Imported but Unused in `images.js`

**File:** `agent/lib/images.js`, line 16

```javascript
import { isExistingUrl } from './protected.js';
```

This import is never used anywhere in `images.js`. If `protected.js` doesn't export `isExistingUrl`, this will cause a runtime import error that crashes the agent.

**Fix:**

```javascript
// CORRECTED — remove unused import:
// Remove this line entirely:
// import { isExistingUrl } from './protected.js';
```

**Severity:** HIGH — If `protected.js` doesn't export this symbol, the entire agent crashes on import.

## 3. Medium Severity Issues

### MED-01: `ai.js` `extractJSON` Fallback is Brittle — Mismatched Brace/Bracket Parsing

**File:** `agent/lib/ai.js`, lines ~65–76

**Problem:** The fallback JSON extraction finds the first `{` or `[` and the last `}` or `]` in the entire response. If Claude's response contains commentary text with curly braces (e.g., "You should use {this pattern}"), the extraction grabs from the commentary brace to the last JSON brace, producing invalid JSON.

```javascript
// CURRENT:
const jsonStart = text.search(/[\[{]/);
const jsonEndBrace = text.lastIndexOf('}');
const jsonEndBracket = text.lastIndexOf(']');
const jsonEnd = Math.max(jsonEndBrace, jsonEndBracket);
if (jsonStart >= 0 && jsonEnd > jsonStart) {
  try { return JSON.parse(text.slice(jsonStart, jsonEnd + 1)); } catch {}
}
```

**Fix:** Match starting delimiter and find the correct closing delimiter:

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
  // Try finding JSON by matching opening to closing delimiter
  const firstBrace = text.indexOf('{');
  const firstBracket = text.indexOf('[');
  
  // Pick whichever comes first (and exists)
  let jsonStart = -1;
  let closingChar = '';
  
  if (firstBrace >= 0 && (firstBracket < 0 || firstBrace < firstBracket)) {
    jsonStart = firstBrace;
    closingChar = '}';
  } else if (firstBracket >= 0) {
    jsonStart = firstBracket;
    closingChar = ']';
  }
  
  if (jsonStart >= 0) {
    const jsonEnd = text.lastIndexOf(closingChar);
    if (jsonEnd > jsonStart) {
      try { return JSON.parse(text.slice(jsonStart, jsonEnd + 1)); } catch {}
    }
  }
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — Can cause false extraction failures or corrupt data from Claude responses.

---

### MED-02: `config.js` AI Model Hardcoded with Stale Version

**File:** `agent/lib/config.js`, line ~70

```javascript
model: process.env.ANTHROPIC_MODEL || 'claude-sonnet-4-20250514',
```

**Problem:** The model version `claude-sonnet-4-20250514` is hardcoded. If this model version is deprecated, the agent will fail silently until someone updates this string. The environment variable fallback is good, but the default should be documented.

**Fix:** Add a comment and use the stable alias if available:

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
model: process.env.ANTHROPIC_MODEL || 'claude-sonnet-4-20250514', // Update when Anthropic deprecates. Override via ANTHROPIC_MODEL env var.
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — Agent breaks when model version is retired.

---

### MED-03: `writer.js` `bumpVersion` Doesn't Handle Two-Part Versions

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js`, lines ~59–63

```javascript
const parts = currentVersion.split('.');
parts[2] = String(parseInt(parts[2] || '0') + 1);
const newVersion = parts.join('.');
```

**Problem:** If `APP_VERSION` is `'3.2'` (two-part), `parts[2]` is `undefined`, `parseInt(undefined)` returns `NaN`, `String(NaN + 1)` returns `'NaN'`, and the version becomes `'3.2.NaN'`.

**Fix:**

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
const parts = currentVersion.split('.');
while (parts.length < 3) parts.push('0');
parts[2] = String(parseInt(parts[2], 10) + 1);
const newVersion = parts.join('.');
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — Corrupts version string if it's not already 3-part semver.

---

### MED-04: `reader.js` `extractCurrentBanners` Regex is Overly Broad

**File:** `agent/lib/reader.js`, lines ~46–55

```javascript
const versionMatch = source.match(/CURRENT_BANNERS\s*=\s*\{[^}]*version:\s*'([^']+)'/s);
```

**Problem:** `[^}]*` won't match correctly if there are nested objects before `version:`. The `/s` flag makes `.` match newlines, but `[^}]` still can't cross a `}` character. If the data structure has any nested `}` before the `version` field, this fails.

The `charsBlock` and `weapBlock` regexes are more robust with `[\s\S]*?`, but the version/phase/dates regexes use `[^}]*` which is fragile.

**Fix:**

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
const versionMatch = source.match(/CURRENT_BANNERS\s*=\s*\{[\s\S]*?version:\s*'([^']+)'/);
const phaseMatch = source.match(/CURRENT_BANNERS\s*=\s*\{[\s\S]*?phase:\s*(\d+)/);
const startMatch = source.match(/CURRENT_BANNERS\s*=\s*\{[\s\S]*?startDate:\s*'([^']+)'/);
const endMatch = source.match(/CURRENT_BANNERS\s*=\s*\{[\s\S]*?endDate:\s*'([^']+)'/);
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — Fragile parsing that breaks when data structure grows.

---

### MED-05: `changelog.js` `updateAppChangelog` Entry Trimming is Line-Based but Entries Span Multiple Lines

**File:** `agent/lib/changelog.js`, lines ~94–99

```javascript
const trimmed = allEntries.split('\n')
  .filter(l => l.trim().startsWith('{'))
  .slice(0, MAX_APP_ENTRIES)
  .join(',\n');
```

**Problem:** Each entry is on one line (as generated by `newEntries`), but existing entries might have been formatted differently (e.g., multi-line). The `filter(l => l.trim().startsWith('{'))` drops continuation lines, potentially corrupting multi-line entries. Also, the join uses `,\n` but each filtered line already has a trailing comma from the original join.

**Fix:**

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
// Parse entries more carefully — each entry starts with `  {` and ends with `}`
const entryPattern = /\s*\{[^}]+\}/g;
const allMatches = [...allEntries.matchAll(entryPattern)];
const trimmedEntries = allMatches.slice(0, MAX_APP_ENTRIES).map(m => m[0].trim());
const trimmed = trimmedEntries.map(e => `  ${e}`).join(',\n');
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — Can produce malformed CHANGELOG constant in `appcore-data.js`.

---

### MED-06: `images.js` `processMissingImages` Fetches with `extractText: false` but `findCharacterImage` Expects Text Content

**File:** `agent/lib/images.js`, lines ~135–140

```javascript
const page = await fetchPageFn(wikiUrl, { extractText: false }); // Need raw HTML for image URLs
// ...
const imageInfo = await finder(name, page.content.slice(0, 40000), askClaudeFn);
```

Then `findCharacterImage` sends this content to Claude with the prompt "From the following web page content, find the BEST full-body sprite/portrait image URL". But `page.content` is raw HTML (since `extractText: false`), which is extremely noisy with HTML tags. This wastes Claude tokens and may exceed context limits.

**Fix:** Extract image URLs from HTML first, then send a cleaner payload to Claude:

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
const page = await fetchPageFn(wikiUrl, { extractText: false });

if (!page.ok) {
  log.warn(`Could not fetch wiki page for ${name}`);
  continue;
}

// Pre-extract image URLs to send cleaner content to Claude
const imgUrls = [...page.content.matchAll(/(?:src|data-src)="(https?:\/\/[^"]+\.(?:webp|png|jpg|jpeg)[^"]*)"/gi)]
  .map(m => m[1])
  .filter(url => !url.includes('thumbnail') && !url.includes('/scale-to-width-down/'));

// Send cleaned content: image URLs + stripped text
const cleanedContent = `IMAGE URLS:\n${imgUrls.join('\n')}\n\nPAGE TEXT:\n${page.content.replace(/<[^>]+>/g, ' ').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').slice(0, 30000)}`;

const finder = type === 'character' ? findCharacterImage : findWeaponImage;
const imageInfo = await finder(name, cleanedContent, askClaudeFn);
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — Wastes API tokens and reduces Claude's accuracy.

---

### MED-07: `skills.js` Loads Same File Twice for Different Purposes

**File:** `agent/lib/skills.js`, lines ~61–62

```javascript
const scopeRaw = loadSkillFile(SKILL_PATHS.scopeContext);
const aestheticAuditRaw = loadSkillFile(SKILL_PATHS.scopeContext); // AUDIT-AESTHETIC.md
```

**Problem:** Both `scopeRaw` and `aestheticAuditRaw` load from `SKILL_PATHS.scopeContext`, which points to `AUDIT-AESTHETIC.md`. This means `extractDesignBrief(aestheticAuditRaw || designRaw)` always gets the scope/aesthetic audit file, never the actual design-aesthetic skill file. The `scopeContext` skill path is being misused as a design brief source.

**Fix:**

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
const SKILL_PATHS = {
  appAudit: [
    resolve(PATHS.repoRoot, 'app-audit-SKILL (1).md'),
    resolve(PATHS.repoRoot, 'docs/skills/app-audit/SKILL.md'),
  ],
  designAesthetic: [
    resolve(PATHS.repoRoot, 'design-aesthetic-audit-SKILL (1).md'),
    resolve(PATHS.repoRoot, 'docs/skills/design-aesthetic-audit/SKILL.md'),
  ],
  scopeContext: [
    resolve(PATHS.repoRoot, 'scope-context-SKILL.md'),
    resolve(PATHS.repoRoot, 'docs/skills/scope-context/SKILL.md'),
  ],
  aestheticAudit: [
    resolve(PATHS.repoRoot, 'AUDIT-AESTHETIC.md'),
  ],
};

// ... then in loadSkills():
const scopeRaw = loadSkillFile(SKILL_PATHS.scopeContext);
const aestheticAuditRaw = loadSkillFile(SKILL_PATHS.aestheticAudit);
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — Design audit context is sourced from the wrong file, reducing audit quality.

---

### MED-08: `writer.js` `addToList` Appends After Last Entry but Before `];` — Missing Comma Handling

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js`, lines ~245–265

```javascript
safeReplace(
  match[0],
  `${match[1]}  ${qName},\n${match[2]}`,
  `Add ${name} to ${listName}`
);
```

**Problem:** `match[1]` is everything before `];`, which may or may not end with a comma and newline. If the last entry in the array is `'LastName'\n` (no trailing comma), inserting `  ${qName},\n` after it creates:

```javascript
  'LastName'
  'NewName',
];
```

Missing comma after `'LastName'`. JavaScript arrays are forgiving with trailing commas, but missing commas between elements cause syntax errors.

**Fix:** Ensure there's a comma after the previous last entry:

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
const prefix = match[1].trimEnd();
const needsComma = prefix.length > 0 && !prefix.endsWith(',') && !prefix.endsWith('[');
const separator = needsComma ? ',\n' : '\n';
safeReplace(
  match[0],
  `${prefix}${separator}  ${qName},\n${match[2]}`,
  `Add ${name} to ${listName}`
);
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — Can produce invalid JS syntax in list arrays.

**File:** `trophies-list.txt`, bottom section

```
TOTAL: 114 unique trophy definitions + 33 per-character S6
```

**Problem:** Manual count of the trophy IDs yields approximately 147 unique non-S6 trophies (the actual count differs from the stated 114). The `s6_other` generic trophy exists alongside the 33 per-character S6 trophies, and should be counted separately. Additionally, `s6_Aemeath` (the 33rd per-character S6) has only 32 actual listed character entries since `s6_Aemeath` appears to be the last. However, counting the `s6_*` specific entries yields 32 named characters (Jiyan through Aemeath), not 33 as claimed. There's no `s6_Rover` or equivalent 33rd character.

**Fix:** Audit the trophy count manually. Update the total:

```
TOTAL: [actual count] unique trophy definitions + 32 per-character S6
```

**Severity:** LOW — Documentation inaccuracy; doesn't affect runtime.

### LOW-04: `README.md` Cost Estimate Is Inaccurate

**Fix:**

```markdown
Each full update cycle uses ~5–12 Claude API calls:
- 1 for banner analysis
- 1 for event analysis
- 1 for character detection
- 1 for weapon detection
- 0–1 for combat data generation
- 0–5 for meta-refresh (build updates for ~5 characters)
- 0–3 for image finding (character/weapon/banner art)

```

### LOW-06: `writer.js` `updateEventDate` Uses Dynamic Regex Without Escaping `eventKey`

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js`, lines ~159–169

```javascript
const pattern = new RegExp(
  `(${eventKey}:\\s*\\{[\\s\\S]*?currentEnd:\\s*)'([^']+)'`,
);
```

**Problem:** `eventKey` is interpolated directly into a regex without escaping. If `eventKey` contains regex-special characters (unlikely for camelCase keys, but defensive coding matters), this could throw or match unintended patterns.

**Fix:**

```javascript
// CORRECTED:
const escapedKey = eventKey.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
const pattern = new RegExp(
  `(${escapedKey}:\\s*\\{[\\s\\S]*?currentEnd:\\s*)'([^']+)'`,
);
```

**Severity:** LOW — Unlikely to trigger with current event key naming, but defensive coding best practice.

## 5. Architectural Observations

### ARCH-01: Module Boundary Violation — `banner-images.js` Bypasses Writer Safety

The `banner-images.js` module uses `getBufferFn()` and `loadBufferFn()` to directly manipulate the write buffer, bypassing all safety checks in `writer.js` (`safeReplace`'s uniqueness check, the error-on-not-found behavior). This creates a parallel write path that is less safe.

**Recommendation:** Either expose a proper API from `writer.js` for image URL updates (e.g., `updateImageUrl(field, url, context)`) or have `banner-images.js` return its findings and let the orchestrator apply them through `writer.js`.

---

### ARCH-02: No Input Validation on AI Responses Before Writing

The pipeline is: Claude response → `extractJSON()` → directly to writer functions. There's no schema validation layer between AI output and data writes. Claude could return a `confidence: 0.99` response with an `element: "Pyro"` (Genshin term, not Wuthering Waves), and it would be written.

**Recommendation:** Add a validation layer after `extractJSON()` that checks:
- Elements against `GAME.ELEMENTS`
- Weapon types against `GAME.WEAPON_TYPES`
- Roles against `GAME.ROLES`
- Date formats (ISO 8601)
- Required fields present and non-null

---

### ARCH-03: Memory System Referenced but Not Defined

`meta-refresh.js` references a `memory` object (`memory.metaRefresh`) for tracking refresh timestamps, but no module defines, persists, or loads this memory. The `recordRefresh` function writes to the object, but it's never saved to disk. This means every run refreshes the same characters (sorted by timestamp 0).

**Recommendation:** Create a `lib/memory.js` module that:
- Loads from `agent/.memory.json`
- Persists after each run
- Tracks per-character meta refresh timestamps
- Tracks last banner/event update timestamps

---

### ARCH-04: Trophy System Has No Runtime Implementation

`trophies-list.txt` defines 147+ trophies with IDs and descriptions, but there's no corresponding implementation in the agent or app code (within this chunk's scope). These are purely planning documents. There should be a path from these definitions to runtime trophy evaluation.

---

## 6. Cross-Cutting Contract Analysis

### CONTRACT-01: Reader ↔ Writer Field Naming Mismatch

`reader.js` `extractCurrentBanners()` returns:
```javascript
{ version, phase, startDate, endDate, characters: [{ name }], weapons: [{ name }] }
```

But `writer.js` `updateCurrentBanners()` expects `bannerData` with:
```javascript
{ version, phase, startDate, endDate, characters: [{ id, name, title, element, weaponType, isNew, featured4Stars }], weapons: [...] }
```

The reader returns a minimal structure (just names), but the writer expects the full structure. If the orchestrator passes reader output directly to the writer, it will produce entries with `undefined` for `id`, `title`, `element`, `weaponType`, and other fields.

**Impact:** The orchestrator must ensure AI analysis results (not reader output) are passed to the writer. This dependency is implicit and undocumented.

---

### CONTRACT-02: `config.js` SOURCES.events Hardcoded Version Page

`config.js` line ~55:
```javascript
'https://game8.co/games/Wuthering-Waves/archives/582216', // Game8 v3.2 page
```

This same URL appears in `banner-images.js` line ~15. Both references will go stale simultaneously. There should be a single source of truth for version-specific URLs.

---

### CONTRACT-03: Protected Module Referenced but Not in Chunk

Both `images.js` and `banner-images.js` import from `./protected.js`:
- `images.js`: `import { isExistingUrl } from './protected.js';` (unused)
- `banner-images.js`: `import { isProtectedImageField } from './protected.js';`

This module is not in the current chunk, so its contract can't be verified. The `isExistingUrl` import in `images.js` is unused and should be removed (see HIGH-06).

---

## Summary

| Severity | Count | Categories |
|----------|-------|-----------|
| CRITICAL | 3 | Combat data logic bug, empty-string buffer corruption, JS injection via unescaped quotes |
| HIGH | 6 | Silent regex failures, dead code, event parsing, multi-match corruption, cross-boundary regex, unused import |
| MEDIUM | 8 | JSON extraction, model version, version bumping, regex fragility, changelog corruption, token waste, skill file mismatch, comma handling |
| LOW | 6 | Trophy count, duplicate URLs, deprecated dep, cost docs, hardcoded version, regex escaping |

**Verdict: REVISE**

The three CRITICAL issues must be fixed before any agent run touches production data. CRIT-03 (unescaped quotes in generated JS) is particularly dangerous — any character name, weapon name, or description containing an apostrophe will break `appcore-data.js` and take down the entire app. The agent's architectural separation between AI output and write operations needs a validation layer (ARCH-02) to prevent category errors from propagating.

## 1. CRITICAL Issues

### ISSUE C-01: Version Drift Across Multiple Files — No Single Source of Truth

**Files affected:**
- `whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js` line 7: `APP_VERSION = '3.2.3'`
- `whispering-wishes-replit/src/AppCore.jsx` line 3: comment says `v3.2.2`
- `whispering-wishes-replit/README.md` line 1: `v2.1.0`
- `replit.md` line 8: `3.1.1`

**Problem:** Four different version strings exist across the codebase. The service worker caches are keyed by `APP_VERSION`, so a mismatch means users may get stale caches or cache invalidation failures. The `replit.md` says "Synced APP_VERSION with package.json" but the actual files disagree. `AppCore.jsx` exports `APP_VERSION` from `appcore-data.js` (not in this chunk but referenced), and the service worker hardcodes its own version. If these diverge, the SW will create cache names that never get cleaned up by activate events.

**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Fix:** Establish a single source of truth. The service worker cannot import from JSX/ES modules, so the build pipeline must inject the version. For now, at minimum, align all static references:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js — line 7
// MUST match the version in package.json and appcore-data.js
const APP_VERSION = '3.2.3'; // Keep this as the canonical latest
```

```jsx
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/AppCore.jsx — line 3
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// WHISPERING WISHES v3.2.3 — AppCore.jsx (Barrel)
// Thin re-export layer. App.jsx imports from here — no changes needed.
// Split into: appcore-data.js, appcore-engine.js, appcore-providers.jsx,
//             appcore-components.jsx
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
```

```md
<!-- whispering-wishes-replit/README.md — line 1 -->
# Whispering Wishes v3.2.3
```

```md
<!-- replit.md — line 8 -->
**Current Version**: 3.2.3
```

Additionally, the README.md has stale game constants:

```md
<!-- whispering-wishes-replit/README.md — lines 29-30 (current) -->
- Soft Pity Start: 66 pulls

<!-- Should be (per replit.md): -->
- Soft Pity Start: 65 pulls
```

```md
<!-- whispering-wishes-replit/README.md — line 36 (current) -->
- Storage key: `whispering-wishes-v2.0` (keep for compatibility)

<!-- Should be: -->
- Storage key: `whispering-wishes-v2.2` (legacy v2.0 and v2.1 are auto-migrated)
```

---

### ISSUE C-02: CI/CD Workflow Uses `--no-git` But Then Relies on Git Diff for PR Creation

**File:** `.github/workflows/ww-auto-update.yml` lines 78-92, 96-103

**Problem:** The agent is invoked with `--no-git` flag (line 82: `ARGS="--no-git"`), which presumably tells the agent NOT to do git operations internally. This is correct because the workflow uses `peter-evans/create-pull-request@v6` to handle branching/committing. However, there's a race condition:

1. Line 95-103: The "Check for changes" step runs `git add agent/run-report.md agent/CHANGELOG.md` — but the agent writes to `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js` (see `agent/lib/git.js` line 63). The check step only stages agent report files, not the actual data file changes.
2. `peter-evans/create-pull-request` auto-detects uncommitted changes, so this partially works — but `git status --porcelain` on line 99 will only show changes for files that are either staged or modified. If the agent writes to files not tracked by git (new files), they'd be missed without explicit `git add`.
3. The "Commit evolution log" step (lines 130-135) does a `git commit` AFTER `create-pull-request` has already created its own commit/branch. This creates a commit directly on the PR branch outside the action's control, potentially causing issues.

**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Fix:**

```yaml
# .github/workflows/ww-auto-update.yml — replace lines 94-103
      - name: Check for changes
        id: check
        run: |
          # Stage ALL files the agent might modify, not just reports
          git add -A 2>/dev/null || true
          if [ -n "$(git status --porcelain)" ]; then
            echo "has_changes=true" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
            echo "Changed files:"
            git status --porcelain
          else
            echo "has_changes=false" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
          fi
```

```yaml
# .github/workflows/ww-auto-update.yml — remove or move the "Commit evolution log" step
# REMOVE lines 130-135 entirely. The evolution log should be included in the
# peter-evans/create-pull-request commit, not committed separately.
# If it needs to persist, stage it in the "Check for changes" step (already covered by git add -A).
```

---

### ISSUE C-03: Git Commit Message Shell Injection Vulnerability

**File:** `agent/lib/git.js` lines 72-76

**Problem:** The commit message is constructed from `getChangeLog()` data which originates from scraped web content processed by Claude. The message is interpolated directly into a shell command via `exec()`. If any change description contains double quotes (even escaped) or backticks or `$(...)`, it can break out of the string or execute arbitrary commands.

```js
// Current (vulnerable):
exec(`git commit -m "${message.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"`);
```

The `.replace(/"/g, '\\"')` is insufficient — it doesn't handle:
- Backslashes before quotes: `\"` in input becomes `\\"` which closes the string
- Backticks: `` `command` `` executes in bash
- `$(...)` command substitution
- Newlines in the message (the template has `\n` characters)

**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/git.js — replace lines 59-76
export function commitChanges(summary) {
  try {
    // Stage the specific files we modify
    exec('git add whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js');
    exec('git add whispering-wishes-replit/package.json');
    exec('git add whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js');

    // Check if there's anything to commit
    const status = exec('git status --short');
    if (!status) {
      log.info('No changes to commit');
      return null;
    }

    // Build commit message from change log
    const changes = getChangeLog();
    const changeLines = changes.map(c => `  - [${c.category}] ${c.description} (${c.confidence})`).join('\n');

    const message = `${GIT.commitPrefix} ${summary}\n\nChanges:\n${changeLines}\n\nGenerated by WW Update Agent at ${new Date().toISOString()}`;

    // Write message to a temp file to avoid shell injection
    const msgFile = resolve(PATHS.repoRoot, 'agent/.commit-msg.tmp');
    const { writeFileSync, unlinkSync } = await import('fs');
    writeFileSync(msgFile, message);
    try {
      exec(`git commit -F "${msgFile}"`);
    } finally {
      try { unlinkSync(msgFile); } catch {}
    }

    const hash = exec('git rev-parse --short HEAD');
    log.ok(`Committed: ${hash} — ${summary}`);
    return hash;
  } catch (err) {
    log.error(`Commit failed: ${err.message}`);
    return null;
  }
}
```

Since the module already uses static imports, a synchronous approach is cleaner:

```js
// agent/lib/git.js — add to imports at top
import { execSync } from 'child_process';
import { writeFileSync, unlinkSync } from 'fs';
import { resolve } from 'path';
import { PATHS, GIT } from './config.js';
import { log, getChangeLog } from './log.js';

// ... then in commitChanges, replace the exec line:

    // Safe: write commit message to file, use -F flag
    const msgFile = resolve(PATHS.repoRoot, 'agent/.commit-msg.tmp');
    writeFileSync(msgFile, message, 'utf-8');
    try {
      exec(`git commit -F ${msgFile}`);
    } finally {
      try { unlinkSync(msgFile); } catch {}
    }
```

Also add to `.gitignore`:

```
agent/.commit-msg.tmp
```

---

## 2. HIGH Issues

### ISSUE H-01: Service Worker `staleWhileRevalidate` Has Silent Failure Path

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js` lines 79-93

**Problem:** The `staleWhileRevalidate` function has a logic flaw. When there's no cached response AND the fetch fails, the `.catch()` block returns `cached` (which is `undefined`/`null` at that point) — then falls through to create a 503 Response. However, the OUTER return on line 93 (`return cached || fetchPromise`) will return the `fetchPromise` when `cached` is null. If that promise rejects internally (which it won't because `.catch()` handles it), the fetch event gets an unhandled rejection. More importantly: when `cached` is falsy, the function returns the `fetchPromise` — but the `.catch()` handler checks `if (cached) return cached` which will always be the closure-captured `null`, so it returns a 503. This works, but the logic is confusing and fragile.

The real bug: if `response.ok` is false (e.g., 404), the function doesn't cache it but also doesn't return a fallback — it returns the 404 response directly. The next time, the cache still has nothing, and the user gets another 404. For images, this means a broken image URL will cause a network request on every page load.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js — replace lines 72-93
// Strategy: Stale-while-revalidate (for images)
async function staleWhileRevalidate(request, cacheName) {
  const cache = await caches.open(cacheName);
  const cached = await cache.match(request);

  const fetchPromise = fetch(request)
    .then(response => {
      if (response.ok) {
        cache.put(request, response.clone());
        trimCache(cacheName, MAX_IMG_ENTRIES);
      }
      return response;
    })
    .catch(() => {
      // Network failure — return cached version or 503
      return cached || new Response('', { status: 503, statusText: 'Service Unavailable' });
    });

  // Return cached immediately if available, let fetch update in background
  // If no cache, wait for network
  return cached || fetchPromise;
}
```

---

### ISSUE H-02: `validate.js` Syntax Checker Doesn't Handle Escaped Quotes Inside Strings

**File:** `agent/lib/validate.js` lines 120-145

**Problem:** The string-tracking logic in `checkSyntax` fails for escaped characters:

```js
if (c === stringChar && prev !== '\\') inString = false;
```

This doesn't handle double-escaped backslashes. For example, in the string `'path\\' + rest`, the `\\` is an escaped backslash, and the `'` after it should close the string. But the code sees `prev === '\\'` and thinks the quote is escaped. The correct check needs to count consecutive backslashes.

Also, template literals (backtick strings) can contain `${...}` expressions with nested braces/brackets/strings, but the parser treats them as flat strings — any `{` or `}` inside a template expression would be missed.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/validate.js — replace lines 117-146
function checkSyntax(source, errors) {
  // Check balanced braces/brackets
  let braces = 0, brackets = 0, parens = 0;
  let inString = false, stringChar = '';
  let templateDepth = 0; // Track template literal expression nesting

  for (let i = 0; i < source.length; i++) {
    const c = source[i];

    if (inString) {
      // Count consecutive preceding backslashes
      let backslashes = 0;
      for (let j = i - 1; j >= 0 && source[j] === '\\'; j--) {
        backslashes++;
      }
      const isEscaped = backslashes % 2 === 1;

      if (stringChar === '`' && c === '$' && i + 1 < source.length && source[i + 1] === '{' && !isEscaped) {
        // Template expression — pause string tracking
        templateDepth++;
        inString = false;
        i++; // skip the '{'
        braces++; // count it as a brace (will be closed by '}')
        continue;
      }

      if (c === stringChar && !isEscaped) {
        inString = false;
      }
      continue;
    }

    if (c === "'" || c === '"' || c === '`') {
      inString = true;
      stringChar = c;
    } else if (c === '{') {
      braces++;
    } else if (c === '}') {
      braces--;
      if (templateDepth > 0 && braces >= 0) {
        // Check if this closes a template expression
        // Re-enter string mode for the template literal
        // This is a simplification — full parsing would need a stack
        // For validation purposes, just track brace balance
      }
    } else if (c === '[') brackets++;
    else if (c === ']') brackets--;
    else if (c === '(') parens++;
    else if (c === ')') parens--;
  }

  if (braces !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced braces: ${braces > 0 ? braces + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(braces) + ' extra closing'}`);
  if (brackets !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced brackets: ${brackets > 0 ? brackets + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(brackets) + ' extra closing'}`);
  if (parens !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced parentheses: ${parens > 0 ? parens + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(parens) + ' extra closing'}`);
}
```

---

### ISSUE H-03: `cross-verify.js` JSON.parse on Unvalidated Claude Response

**File:** `agent/lib/cross-verify.js` lines 99-101

**Problem:** The `extractPerSource` function parses Claude's response as JSON with a simple regex cleanup. If Claude returns malformed JSON (which happens), the `JSON.parse` throws, but the catch block creates `{ source, data: {}, url }` — a silent data loss. More importantly, the regex `replace(/```json?\n?/g, '')` only strips the opening fence. If Claude puts text before or after the JSON block, the parse will fail silently for every source, and `crossVerify` will have no data to compare.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/cross-verify.js — replace lines 92-108
export async function extractPerSource(sources, extractionPrompt, askClaudeFn) {
  const results = [];

  for (const source of sources) {
    const domain = getDomain(source.url);
    const prompt = extractionPrompt.replace('{SOURCE_CONTENT}', source.content.slice(0, 20000));

    try {
      const response = await askClaudeFn(
        'Extract game data from this single source. Return only valid JSON. If data is unclear or not found, use null.',
        prompt,
        { maxTokens: 2048 }
      );

      // Robust JSON extraction: find the first { ... } or [ ... ] block
      let jsonStr = response.replace(/```json?\n?/g, '').replace(/```/g, '').trim();

      // If there's text around the JSON, extract just the JSON object
      const jsonMatch = jsonStr.match(/(\{[\s\S]*\})/);
      if (jsonMatch) {
        jsonStr = jsonMatch[1];
      }

      const data = JSON.parse(jsonStr);
      results.push({ source: domain, data, url: source.url });
    } catch (err) {
      log.warn(`Extraction failed for ${domain}: ${err.message}`);
      results.push({ source: domain, data: {}, url: source.url });
    }
  }

  return results;
}
```

### ISSUE H-04: `standard-pool.js` Regex Matches Are Greedy and Fragile

**File:** `agent/lib/standard-pool.js` lines 75-102

**Problem:** Multiple regex patterns use `[\s\S]*?` (lazy match across all characters) which in a 10,000+ line file will match far more than intended:

```js
const setMatch = buf.match(/(const STANDARD_5STAR_CHARACTERS = new Set\(\[[\s\S]*?)(]\))/);
```

If there are multiple `])` in the file (which there certainly are), the lazy `[\s\S]*?` finds the NEAREST `])`, which might not be the one closing the Set. Similarly:

```js
const stdCharsMatch = buf.match(/(standardCharacters:\s*\[[\s\S]*?)(])/);
```

This matches the first `]` after `standardCharacters: [`, which could be inside a nested array.

Also, line 76: the comma insertion `', '${name}'` will produce `['existing', 'new']` on first entry but `['existing' , 'new']` with extra space if there's already a trailing comma/space pattern.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/standard-pool.js — replace applyStandardPoolUpdates function (lines 62-108)
export function applyStandardPoolUpdates(analysis, getBufferFn, loadBufferFn, minConfidence = 0.85) {
  if (analysis.confidence < minConfidence) {
    if (analysis.standardCharsChanged || analysis.standardWeaponsChanged) {
      log.warn(`Standard pool change detected but confidence ${(analysis.confidence * 100).toFixed(0)}% too low`);
    }
    return false;
  }

  let changed = false;
  let buf = getBufferFn();

  // Helper: find a balanced bracket section after a marker
  function findBalancedSection(source, marker) {
    const start = source.indexOf(marker);
    if (start === -1) return null;
    const openBracket = source.indexOf('[', start + marker.length);
    if (openBracket === -1) return null;

    let depth = 1;
    let i = openBracket + 1;
    while (i < source.length && depth > 0) {
      if (source[i] === '[') depth++;
      else if (source[i] === ']') depth--;
      i++;
    }
    if (depth !== 0) return null;

    return {
      fullMatch: source.slice(start, i),
      innerStart: openBracket + 1,
      innerEnd: i - 1,
      inner: source.slice(openBracket + 1, i - 1),
    };
  }

  // Add new standard characters
  if (analysis.newStandardChars?.length) {
    for (const name of analysis.newStandardChars) {
      // Add to STANDARD_5STAR_CHARACTERS Set
      const section = findBalancedSection(buf, 'const STANDARD_5STAR_CHARACTERS = new Set(');
      if (section && !section.inner.includes(`'${name}'`)) {
        const trimmedInner = section.inner.trimEnd();
        const separator = trimmedInner.endsWith(',') || trimmedInner.endsWith('[') ? ' ' : ', ';
        const newInner = section.inner.replace(/(\s*$)/, `${separator}'${name}'$1`);
        buf = buf.replace(section.inner, newInner);
        addChange('standard-pool', `Added ${name} to STANDARD_5STAR_CHARACTERS`);
        changed = true;
      }

      // Add to CURRENT_BANNERS.standardCharacters
      const scSection = findBalancedSection(buf, 'standardCharacters:');
      if (scSection && !scSection.inner.includes(`'${name}'`)) {
        const trimmedInner = scSection.inner.trimEnd();
        const separator = trimmedInner.endsWith(',') || trimmedInner.endsWith('[') ? ' ' : ', ';
        const newInner = scSection.inner.replace(/(\s*$)/, `${separator}'${name}'$1`);
        buf = buf.replace(scSection.inner, newInner);
        changed = true;
      }
    }
  }

  // Add new standard weapons
  if (analysis.newStandardWeapons?.length) {
    for (const weapon of analysis.newStandardWeapons) {
      const wSection = findBalancedSection(buf, 'const STANDARD_5STAR_WEAPONS = new Set(');
      if (wSection && !wSection.inner.includes(`'${weapon.name}'`)) {
        const trimmedInner = wSection.inner.trimEnd();
        const separator = trimmedInner.endsWith(',') || trimmedInner.endsWith('[') ? '\n  ' : ',\n  ';
        const newInner = wSection.inner.replace(/(\s*$)/, `${separator}'${weapon.name}'$1`);
        buf = buf.replace(wSection.inner, newInner);
        addChange('standard-pool', `Added ${weapon.name} to STANDARD_5STAR_WEAPONS`);
        changed = true;
      }

      const swSection = findBalancedSection(buf, 'standardWeapons:');
      if (swSection && !swSection.inner.includes(`'${weapon.name}'`)) {
        const entry = `\n    { name: '${weapon.name}', type: '${weapon.type}' },`;
        const newInner = swSection.inner.replace(/(\s*$)/, `${entry}$1`);
        buf = buf.replace(swSection.inner, newInner);
        changed = true;
      }
    }
  }

  if (changed) loadBufferFn(buf);
  return changed;
}
```

### ISSUE H-05: CSP Missing `fonts.googleapis.com` in `style-src` Directive

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/vercel.json` line 8 (the CSP header value)
**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/index.html` lines 34-35

**Problem:** The `index.html` loads Google Fonts CSS from `fonts.googleapis.com`:
```html
<link href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=..." rel="stylesheet" />
```

But the CSP `style-src` directive only allows `'self' 'unsafe-inline'`. The Google Fonts CSS is an external stylesheet, not inline. It needs `https://fonts.googleapis.com` in `style-src`. Currently, the browser should block this stylesheet load, meaning **the custom fonts (Rajdhani, JetBrains Mono) are not loading in production** on Vercel.

The `font-src` correctly includes `https://fonts.gstatic.com` for the actual font files, but the CSS that references them is blocked.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```json
{
  "headers": [
    {
      "source": "/(.*)",
      "headers": [
        {
          "key": "Content-Security-Policy",
          "value": "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://fonts.googleapis.com; img-src 'self' https://i.ibb.co https://ibb.co https://i.imgur.com https://cdn.discordapp.com https://media.discordapp.net https://pbs.twimg.com https://raw.githubusercontent.com https://i.postimg.cc https://wuwa.gg https://wuwatracker.com https://*.googleapis.com data:; connect-src 'self' https://*.firebaseio.com https://*.googleapis.com https://i.ibb.co https://ibb.co https://i.imgur.com https://cdn.discordapp.com https://media.discordapp.net https://pbs.twimg.com https://raw.githubusercontent.com https://i.postimg.cc https://wuwa.gg https://wuwatracker.com; font-src 'self' https://fonts.gstatic.com; frame-ancestors 'none'"
        },
        {
          "key": "X-Frame-Options",
          "value": "DENY"
        },
        {
          "key": "X-Content-Type-Options",
          "value": "nosniff"
        },
        {
          "key": "Referrer-Policy",
          "value": "strict-origin-when-cross-origin"
        },
        {
          "key": "Permissions-Policy",
          "value": "camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()"
        },
        {
          "key": "Strict-Transport-Security",
          "value": "max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains; preload"
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

### ISSUE H-06: `deploy-check.js` GitHub CLI Command Uses Placeholder `{owner}/{repo}`

**File:** `agent/lib/deploy-check.js` lines 23-26

**Problem:** The `gh api` command uses literal `{owner}/{repo}` instead of actual repository values:

```js
const result = execSync(
  'gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/deployments --jq ".[0] | ..."',
```

The GitHub CLI does NOT interpolate `{owner}/{repo}` — that's a GitHub REST API docs convention. In `gh api`, you need to either use the full path or let `gh` resolve it. The correct syntax for the current repo is:

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/deploy-check.js — replace lines 23-30
  try {
    const result = execSync(
      'gh api repos/:owner/:repo/deployments --jq ".[0] | {state: .statuses_url, env: .environment, created: .created_at}" 2>/dev/null',
      { cwd: PATHS.repoRoot, encoding: 'utf-8', stdio: 'pipe', timeout: 10000 }
    );
    if (result.trim()) {
      const deployment = JSON.parse(result);
      log.dim(`Latest deployment: ${deployment.env} at ${deployment.created}`);
    }
  } catch {
    // GitHub CLI not available or no permissions — try HTTP
  }
```

Note: `gh api` supports `:owner` and `:repo` as placeholders that auto-resolve to the current repository. The `{owner}/{repo}` syntax is only for GitHub REST API documentation, not `gh` CLI.

## 3. MEDIUM Issues

### ISSUE M-01: `checkpoint.js` `withCheckpoint` Loses Actual Return Values

**File:** `agent/lib/checkpoint.js` lines 108-120

**Problem:** The `withCheckpoint` wrapper converts complex results into `{ completed: true, hadChanges: !!result }`:

```js
const cacheable = typeof result === 'boolean' || typeof result === 'number' || result === null
  ? result
  : { completed: true, hadChanges: !!result };

saveCheckpoint(mode, stepName, cacheable);
return result;  // Returns full result on first run
```

On first run, `result` (the actual data) is returned. On resume from checkpoint, `getCheckpoint` returns the *cacheable* version (`{ completed: true, hadChanges: true }`). So the caller gets completely different data shapes depending on whether this is a fresh run or a resume. Any caller that inspects the step's result beyond a boolean will break on resume.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/checkpoint.js — replace lines 102-125
/**
 * Wrap a step function with checkpoint support.
 * If the step was already completed (from a crashed run), returns a sentinel
 * indicating the step was already done. Callers should check for this.
 *
 * @param {string} mode — 'full' | 'micro'
 * @param {string} stepName — unique step identifier
 * @param {Function} stepFn — async function to run (returns any result)
 * @returns {{ fromCheckpoint: boolean, result: * }} — wrapped result
 */
export async function withCheckpoint(mode, stepName, stepFn) {
  // Check if already completed
  const cached = getCheckpoint(mode, stepName);
  if (cached !== null) {
    return { fromCheckpoint: true, result: cached };
  }

  // Run the step
  const result = await stepFn();

  // Save checkpoint — store a completion marker
  // Complex data (source content, full analysis objects) should NOT be cached
  // to avoid stale/incomplete data on resume
  const marker = typeof result === 'boolean' || typeof result === 'number' || result === null
    ? result
    : { completed: true, hadChanges: !!result };

  saveCheckpoint(mode, stepName, marker);
  return { fromCheckpoint: false, result };
}
```

### ISSUE M-02: `new-events.js` EVENTS Object End Marker Detection Is Fragile

**File:** `agent/lib/new-events.js` lines 92-100

**Problem:** The function tries to find the end of the EVENTS object with regex:

```js
const eventsEnd = buf.match(/(\n\};\s*\n\s*\/\/ \[SECTION:)/);
```

If the section comment format changes, or if there's no section comment, it falls back to:

```js
const alt = buf.match(/(\n\};\s*\n\s*const (?:BANNER_HISTORY|SUBSCRIPTION))/);
```

Both patterns assume the EVENTS object ends with `};` followed by a specific next section. If a new `const` is added between EVENTS and BANNER_HISTORY, the regex silently fails and the new event entry is placed in the wrong location — or not at all.

Also, the constructed entry (line 106) doesn't close the object property properly — it ends with `},\n` but the insertion point is before the EVENTS closing `};`, so the format should be without the trailing comma after the closing brace of the last property.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/new-events.js — replace addNewEvent function (lines 88-115)
export function addNewEvent(eventData, getBufferFn, loadBufferFn) {
  const buf = getBufferFn();

  // Find the EVENTS object's closing brace more reliably
  // Look for "const EVENTS = {" then find its balanced closing "}"
  const eventsStart = buf.indexOf('const EVENTS = {');
  if (eventsStart === -1) {
    log.warn(`Could not find EVENTS object for new event "${eventData.name}"`);
    return false;
  }

  // Find the balanced closing brace
  let depth = 0;
  let closingIndex = -1;
  let inStr = false, strCh = '';
  for (let i = buf.indexOf('{', eventsStart); i < buf.length; i++) {
    const c = buf[i];
    if (inStr) {
      let bs = 0;
      for (let j = i - 1; j >= 0 && buf[j] === '\\'; j--) bs++;
      if (c === strCh && bs % 2 === 0) inStr = false;
      continue;
    }
    if (c === "'" || c === '"' || c === '`') { inStr = true; strCh = c; continue; }
    if (c === '{') depth++;
    else if (c === '}') {
      depth--;
      if (depth === 0) { closingIndex = i; break; }
    }
  }

  if (closingIndex === -1) {
    log.warn(`Could not find EVENTS closing brace for new event "${eventData.name}"`);
    return false;
  }

  const currentEnd = eventData.currentEnd ? `'${eventData.currentEnd}'` : 'null';
  const entry = `  ${eventData.key}: {\n    name: '${eventData.name}', subtitle: '${(eventData.subtitle || '').replace(/'/g, "\\'")}',\n    description: '${(eventData.description || '').replace(/'/g, "\\'")}',\n    resetType: '${eventData.resetType}', color: '${eventData.color || 'cyan'}',\n    currentEnd: ${currentEnd},\n    rewards: '${(eventData.rewards || '').replace(/'/g, "\\'")}',\n    imageUrl: '' },\n`;

  // Insert before the closing brace of EVENTS
  const newBuf = buf.slice(0, closingIndex) + entry + buf.slice(closingIndex);
  loadBufferFn(newBuf);

  log.ok(`Added new event: ${eventData.key} ("${eventData.name}")`);
  addChange('new-event', `New event: ${eventData.name}`);
  return true;
}
```

### ISSUE M-04: `memory.js` — `wasRecentlyPatched` Uses Exact String Match

**File:** `agent/lib/memory.js` line 78

**Problem:**

```js
return memory.patches.some(p =>
  p.description === description && new Date(p.timestamp).getTime() > cutoff
);
```

Descriptions from different runs may differ slightly (e.g., "Updated banner dates" vs "Updated banner dates for v2.4"). Strict equality means the dedup check won't catch near-duplicates, potentially allowing the agent to apply essentially the same patch multiple times with slightly different wording.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/memory.js — replace lines 76-80
export function wasRecentlyPatched(memory, description) {
  const cutoff = Date.now() - 48 * 3600000;
  const normalizedDesc = description.toLowerCase().trim();
  return memory.patches.some(p => {
    if (new Date(p.timestamp).getTime() <= cutoff) return false;
    const normalizedPatch = p.description.toLowerCase().trim();
    // Exact match or high similarity (one contains the other)
    return normalizedPatch === normalizedDesc
      || normalizedPatch.includes(normalizedDesc)
      || normalizedDesc.includes(normalizedPatch);
  });
}
```
