### ISSUE M-05: Netlify and Vercel Deploy Configs Both Present — Potential Confusion

**Files:**
- `netlify.toml` — Netlify build config
- `whispering-wishes-replit/vercel.json` — Vercel deploy config
- `replit.md` mentions Vercel deployment
- `whispering-wishes-replit/public/robots.txt` references `whisperingwishes.vercel.app`

**Problem:** Both deployment platform configs exist. The `netlify.toml` points to `whispering-wishes-replit` as the build base, which matches the Vercel project structure. However, the CSP headers, security headers, and SPA redirect are only configured in `vercel.json`. If someone deploys to Netlify using `netlify.toml`, they get none of those security headers. Also, `deploy-check.js` only checks `whisperingwishes.app` and `whispering-wishes.vercel.app` — no Netlify URL.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:** If Netlify is no longer used, remove `netlify.toml` to avoid confusion. If both platforms are supported, add security headers to `netlify.toml`:

```toml
# netlify.toml
[build]
  base = "whispering-wishes-replit"
  command = "npm run build"
  publish = "dist"

[[redirects]]
  from = "/*"
  to = "/index.html"
  status = 200

[[headers]]
  for = "/*"
  [headers.values]
    Content-Security-Policy = "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://fonts.googleapis.com; img-src 'self' https://i.ibb.co https://ibb.co https://i.imgur.com https://cdn.discordapp.com https://media.discordapp.net https://pbs.twimg.com https://raw.githubusercontent.com https://i.postimg.cc https://wuwa.gg https://wuwatracker.com https://*.googleapis.com data:; connect-src 'self' https://*.firebaseio.com https://*.googleapis.com; font-src 'self' https://fonts.gstatic.com; frame-ancestors 'none'"
    X-Frame-Options = "DENY"
    X-Content-Type-Options = "nosniff"
    Referrer-Policy = "strict-origin-when-cross-origin"
    Permissions-Policy = "camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()"
    Strict-Transport-Security = "max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains; preload"
```

### ISSUE M-06: `scraper.js` `htmlToText` Strips Link URLs

**File:** `agent/lib/scraper.js` lines 130-131

**Problem:**

```js
text = text.replace(/<a[^>]*href="([^"]*)"[^>]*>([\s\S]*?)<\/a>/gi, '$2');
```

The comment on line 129 says "Convert links to text with URL hint" but the replacement only keeps `$2` (link text), dropping `$1` (the URL). For the agent's purposes (scraping game data pages), URLs in links might contain useful information like image URLs or page references.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/scraper.js — replace line 131
  // Convert links to text, preserving URL for context
  text = text.replace(/<a[^>]*href="([^"]*)"[^>]*>([\s\S]*?)<\/a>/gi, '$2 ($1)');
```

## 4. LOW Issues

### ISSUE L-01: `validate.js` Element Regex Creates False Warnings

**File:** `agent/lib/validate.js` lines 84-91

**Problem:** The regex `/element:\s*'([^']+)'/g` scans the ENTIRE source file, not just CHARACTER_DATA. It will match any object property named `element` with a string value — including ECHO_SETS, test data, comments in strings, etc. The warning "Unusual element value found" will fire for legitimate non-character uses of the `element` key.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/validate.js — replace lines 83-91
  // 9. Element validation — only warn, don't error (regex may match non-character objects)
  // Note: A more robust check would parse CHARACTER_DATA specifically,
  // but since we only warn, false positives are acceptable.
  const elementRegex = /element:\s*'([^']+)'/g;
  let m;
  const unusualElements = new Set();
  while ((m = elementRegex.exec(updatedSource)) !== null) {
    if (!validElements.has(m[1]) && m[1] !== '' && !unusualElements.has(m[1])) {
      unusualElements.add(m[1]);
    }
  }
  // Only warn once per unusual value to reduce noise
  for (const el of unusualElements) {
    warnings.push(`Unusual element value found: "${el}" (may be from non-character data)`);
  }
```

### ISSUE L-02: `health.js` Image URL Regex Misses Query String URLs

**File:** `agent/lib/health.js` lines 18-22

**Problem:**

```js
const matches = source.matchAll(/https?:\/\/[^'"\s]+\.(?:webp|png|jpg|jpeg|avif|gif)/gi);
```

This regex requires URLs to end with an image extension. But some image hosts use query parameters (e.g., `https://i.ibb.co/abc123?format=webp`), and the regex would not match them. Also, the `[^'"\s]+` will greedily consume everything up to the extension, potentially including trailing punctuation or JS syntax.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/health.js — replace lines 18-22
export function extractImageUrls(source) {
  const urls = new Set();
  // Match URLs ending in image extensions, possibly followed by query params
  const matches = source.matchAll(/https?:\/\/[^'"\s,;)]+\.(?:webp|png|jpg|jpeg|avif|gif)(?:\?[^'"\s)]*)?/gi);
  for (const m of matches) {
    // Clean trailing punctuation that might have been captured
    let url = m[0].replace(/[,;]+$/, '');
    urls.add(url);
  }
  return [...urls];
}
```

### ISSUE L-03: `adaptive-schedule.js` Has Duplicate Code Path for Post-Transition

**File:** `agent/lib/adaptive-schedule.js` lines 38-43 and 45-48

**Problem:** The post-transition check (`hoursLeft <= 0 && hoursLeft > -6`) is unreachable because the first condition (`hoursLeft <= 6 && hoursLeft > -6`) already covers the `hoursLeft <= 0 && hoursLeft > -6` range. The post-transition comment block (lines 45-48) is dead code.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:**

```js
// agent/lib/adaptive-schedule.js — replace lines 32-52
export function calculateUrgency(bannerEndDate, memory) {
  const hoursLeft = (new Date(bannerEndDate).getTime() - Date.now()) / 3600000;

  // Critical: within 6 hours of banner end OR within 6 hours after end
  // (transition imminent or just happened — new banner data should be available)
  if (hoursLeft <= 6 && hoursLeft > -6) {
    return { urgency: 'critical', hoursLeft, shouldRun: true };
  }

  // Elevated: within 48 hours of banner end
  if (hoursLeft <= 48 && hoursLeft > 0) {
    return { urgency: 'elevated', hoursLeft, shouldRun: true };
  }

  // Normal: no urgency
  return { urgency: 'normal', hoursLeft, shouldRun: true };
}
```

### ISSUE L-04: `log.js` `changeLog` Array Uses `.length = 0` for Clearing

**File:** `agent/lib/log.js` line 37

**Problem:**

```js
const clearChangeLog = () => changeLog.length = 0;
```

This works in JavaScript but is an unusual pattern. If any other module holds a reference to the array returned by `getChangeLog()` (which returns a copy via spread), they'd be unaffected. But if someone refactors `getChangeLog` to return the original array, this would cause subtle bugs. The pattern is fine but worth noting.

Also, `clearChangeLog` is exported but never used in any file in this chunk. It may be used elsewhere.

**Severity:** LOW — No action required, just noting the pattern.

### ISSUE L-05: `database.rules.json` Allows Writing to `community-pulls` Without Meaningful Validation

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/database.rules.json` lines 17-22

**Problem:** The `community-pulls/$uid` validate rule only requires `timestamp`:

```json
".validate": "newData.hasChildren(['timestamp'])
              && newData.child('timestamp').isNumber()
              && newData.child('timestamp').val() <= now + 60000"
```

There's no validation on what OTHER fields can be written. A user could write arbitrary data to their `community-pulls` node as long as `timestamp` is present. This wastes database storage and could be abused.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:** Add explicit allowed fields:

```json
"community-pulls": {
  ".read": "auth != null",
  "$uid": {
    ".write": "auth != null && (!data.exists() || data.child('timestamp').val() + 10000 < now)",
    ".validate": "newData.hasChildren(['timestamp'])
                  && newData.child('timestamp').isNumber()
                  && newData.child('timestamp').val() <= now + 60000
                  && newData.children().count() <= 10"
  }

2. **Agent system is well-structured** — Separation of concerns across `scraper.js`, `validate.js`, `cross-verify.js`, `health.js`, `memory.js`, `checkpoint.js` is clean. Each module has a clear responsibility.

3. **Protected content system (`protected.js`) is a smart guardrail** — The `PROTECTION_RULES` string injection into AI prompts is a good pattern for preventing destructive autonomous changes. The `isEmpty`/`isExistingUrl`/`filterImageUpdates` helpers provide defense-in-depth.

4. **Dual deploy platform (Vercel + Netlify)** needs resolution — pick one and remove the other's config, or maintain both properly.

5. **The `run-report.js` cost tracking** uses hardcoded Sonnet pricing (`$3/$15 per M tokens`). These will drift over time. Consider making them configurable via `config.js`.

```
Web Sources → scraper.js → cross-verify.js → standard-pool.js → validate.js → git.js
                                                                      ↓

                                                                      ↓

The flow is sound. The main risks are:

### VERIFY-02: Frontend CRIT-02 (Firebase Credentials Fallback) — FIX IS INCOMPLETE

The Frontend Agent proposed:
```js
const FIREBASE_DB = (typeof import.meta !== 'undefined' && import.meta.env?.VITE_FIREBASE_DB) || null;
const FIREBASE_API_KEY = (typeof import.meta !== 'undefined' && import.meta.env?.VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY) || null;
const FIREBASE_AVAILABLE = !!(FIREBASE_DB && FIREBASE_API_KEY);
```

The Reviewer Agent proposed a slightly different approach with a `console.error` in production.

**Problem with both fixes:** Neither agent traced where `FIREBASE_DB` and `FIREBASE_API_KEY` are actually *used* in `App.jsx`. If these are set to `null`, every `fetchWithTimeout(FIREBASE_DB + '/some/path...')` call will produce `fetchWithTimeout('null/some/path...')` — a malformed URL that throws an uncaught TypeError deep in the fetch stack, potentially crashing the entire component tree (since the error happens inside event handlers/effects, not in render).

**The correct fix must guard at the call site, not just the declaration:**

**File: `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, lines ~87–88 (module-level):**
```js
// Firebase configuration — env vars required for online features
const FIREBASE_DB = import.meta.env?.VITE_FIREBASE_DB || null;
const FIREBASE_API_KEY = import.meta.env?.VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY || null;
const FIREBASE_AVAILABLE = !!(FIREBASE_DB && FIREBASE_API_KEY);

// Safe Firebase fetch — returns null response if Firebase is not configured
const firebaseFetch = (path, options = {}) => {
  if (!FIREBASE_AVAILABLE) {
    if (import.meta.env?.DEV) {
      console.warn('[WW] Firebase not configured — skipping request to:', path);
    }
    return Promise.resolve(null);
  }
  const separator = path.startsWith('/') ? '' : '/';
  const url = `${FIREBASE_DB}${separator}${path}`;
  return fetchWithTimeout(url, options).catch(err => {
    console.warn('[WW] Firebase request failed:', err.message);
    return null;
  });
};
```

Every call in the file that does `fetchWithTimeout(\`${FIREBASE_DB}/...\`)` must be replaced with `firebaseFetch(...)` — or at minimum, wrapped with an `if (!FIREBASE_AVAILABLE) return;` guard.

**Severity:** CRITICAL

### VERIFY-03: Frontend HIGH-02 (Image URL Allowlist Unused) — FIX HAS A BUG

The Frontend Agent proposed an `isAllowedImageUrl` function and an `updateBannerForm` guard. Their `updateBannerForm` fix has a logic error:

```js
if (imageFields.includes(field) || field.startsWith('charImages.') || field.startsWith('weapImages.')) {
```

But `updateBannerForm` is called as `updateBannerForm('charImages', { ...prev.charImages, [idx]: value })` — it receives the **full key** like `'charImages'`, not `'charImages.0'`. The nested object is set as the entire value. The `field.startsWith('charImages.')` check will never match because the field name doesn't contain a dot.

Looking at the actual `bannerForm` structure:
```js
charImages: Object.fromEntries((banners.characters || []).map((c, i) => [i, c.imageUrl || ''])),
```

`charImages` is a flat object keyed by index. The form update likely does:
```js
updateBannerForm('charImages', { ...bannerForm.charImages, [index]: newUrl })
```

So the field name is literally `'charImages'` and the value is the entire object — the Frontend Agent's fix would need to iterate the object values.

**Corrected fix:**

**File: `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, module-level (after `ALLOWED_IMAGE_HOSTS`):**
```js
const isAllowedImageUrl = (url) => {
  if (!url || typeof url !== 'string' || url.trim() === '') return true; // empty is allowed (clearing)
  try {
    const parsed = new URL(url);
    if (parsed.protocol !== 'https:') return false;
    return ALLOWED_IMAGE_HOSTS.some(host =>
      parsed.hostname === host || parsed.hostname.endsWith('.' + host)
    );
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
};
```

**Then in the `updateBannerForm` callback:**
```js
const IMAGE_OBJECT_FIELDS = new Set(['charImages', 'weapImages']);
const IMAGE_STRING_FIELDS = new Set([
  'standardCharImg', 'standardWeapImg', 'wwImg', 'dpImg', 'toaImg', 'irImg', 'drImg'
]);

const updateBannerForm = useCallback((field, value) => {
  // Validate image URL string fields
  if (IMAGE_STRING_FIELDS.has(field)) {
    if (value && !isAllowedImageUrl(value)) {
      toast?.('Invalid image URL — must be HTTPS from an allowed host', 'error');
      return;
    }
  }
  // Validate image URL object fields (keyed by index)
  if (IMAGE_OBJECT_FIELDS.has(field) && typeof value === 'object' && value !== null) {
    for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(value)) {
      if (v && !isAllowedImageUrl(v)) {
        toast?.(`Invalid image URL for slot ${k} — must be HTTPS from an allowed host`, 'error');
        return;
      }
    }
  }
  setBannerForm(prev => ({ ...prev, [field]: value }));
}, [toast]);
```

**Severity:** HIGH

### VERIFY-04: Frontend HIGH-04 (stateRef Never Updated) — CONFIRMED LIKELY BUG

The Frontend Agent flagged `const stateRef = useRef(state);` without a visible sync effect. This is correct — but since the file is truncated in our context, we can't confirm the effect doesn't exist deeper in the file.

**However,** tracing the pattern: `stateRef` is used in callbacks that close over stale state (interval handlers, event listeners, etc.). The standard fix is:

```js
const stateRef = useRef(state);
useEffect(() => { stateRef.current = state; });
```

**I verify this fix is correct.** The effect has no dependency array intentionally — it runs after every render to keep the ref current.

**Severity:** HIGH

## 2. NEW Critical Issues

### NEW-CRIT-01: `getActiveBanners` is Exported from `appcore-components.jsx` but Defined in `appcore-data.js`

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/AppCore.jsx`, line 42 vs line 6

**Problem:** `AppCore.jsx` exports `getActiveBanners` from `appcore-components.jsx`:
```js
export {
  // ...
  getActiveBanners,
} from './appcore-components.jsx';
```

But `App.jsx` imports it alongside data functions:
```js
import {
  // ...
  getActiveBanners,
  // ...
} from './AppCore';
```

Meanwhile, `getActiveBanners` is used to initialize state:
```js
const [activeBanners, setActiveBanners] = useState(() => getActiveBanners());
```

The function depends on `CURRENT_BANNERS` (from `appcore-data.js`) and likely on a date comparison. **If the function is defined in `appcore-components.jsx` but imports `CURRENT_BANNERS` from `appcore-data.js`, there's a circular dependency risk:** `appcore-components.jsx` → `appcore-data.js` for data, and `appcore-data.js` → (nothing, it's leaf). This specific chain is fine.

**BUT:** Looking at the barrel file, `getServerAdjustedEnd` is exported from `appcore-engine.js`:
```js
export {
  getServerAdjustedEnd,
  // ...
} from './appcore-engine.js';
```

While `getActiveBanners` likely needs `getServerAdjustedEnd` for date calculations. If `appcore-components.jsx` imports from `appcore-engine.js` which imports from `appcore-data.js`, AND `appcore-components.jsx` also imports from `appcore-data.js` directly — this is a diamond dependency that can cause initialization order issues in ESM.

**Real bug potential:** If `getActiveBanners()` is called during module initialization (as a `useState` initializer), and any of its dependencies haven't finished loading due to circular import resolution, it would return `undefined` or throw, crashing the entire app on load.

**Diagnostic fix — add a safety guard in `App.jsx`:**
```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — where activeBanners is initialized
const [activeBanners, setActiveBanners] = useState(() => {
  try {
    const banners = getActiveBanners();
    if (!banners || !banners.endDate) {
      console.error('[WW] getActiveBanners() returned invalid data — using CURRENT_BANNERS fallback');
      return CURRENT_BANNERS;
    }
    return banners;
  } catch (err) {
    console.error('[WW] getActiveBanners() threw:', err);
    return CURRENT_BANNERS;
  }
});
```

**Severity:** CRITICAL (app won't render if this fails at init)

### NEW-CRIT-02: Service Worker Precache List is Insufficient — Offline App Shell Fails

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js`, line 13

```js
const PRECACHE = ['/', '/index.html'];
```

The app is a Vite SPA. The actual entry point that loads React is `/src/main.jsx` which gets bundled into a hashed JS file (e.g., `/assets/index-abc123.js`). The service worker only precaches `/` and `/index.html`, but:

1. The index.html references `<script type="module" src="/src/main.jsx"></script>` — in development this works, but in production Vite rewrites this to a hashed asset path.
2. The CSS, JS bundles, and font files are NOT precached.
3. On first visit with service worker installed, going offline will serve `index.html` but the JS bundle request will fail — the user sees a blank white page.

The `networkFirst` strategy for "everything else" will fall back to cache, but the cache is empty for JS/CSS on first install.

**Fix — dynamically generate the precache list at build time, or expand the SW to handle SPA routing properly:**

**File: `whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js`, line 13 — replace:**
```js
// Core app shell to precache — in production, the build tool should inject
// hashed asset URLs. This minimal list ensures the SPA shell is available.
const PRECACHE = ['/', '/index.html'];

// Navigation fallback: for SPA routing, serve index.html for all navigation requests
```

**Then in the `networkFirst` function, ensure navigation requests always fall back to the cached index:**
```js
// Strategy: Network-first with cache fallback (for app/API)
async function networkFirst(request, cacheName) {
  try {
    const response = await fetch(request);
    if (response.ok) {
      const cache = await caches.open(cacheName);
      cache.put(request, response.clone());
    }
    return response;
  } catch {
    const cached = await caches.match(request);
    if (cached) return cached;
    // SPA fallback: any navigation request gets the cached index.html
    if (request.mode === 'navigate') {
      const indexCache = await caches.match('/index.html');
      if (indexCache) return indexCache;
      return caches.match('/');
    }
    return new Response('Offline', { status: 503 });
  }
}
```

This is already partially there but the issue is the navigate fallback tries `'/'` which may not be cached separately from `'/index.html'` depending on the server config. The fix above adds explicit fallback chaining.

**Additionally, add a vite plugin (recommendation):** Use `vite-plugin-pwa` or inject the manifest into the SW at build time. This is architectural guidance for a future pass.

**Severity:** CRITICAL (offline experience is completely broken)

## 3. NEW High Issues

### NEW-HIGH-01: `getServerOffset` Returns Incorrect Offset for Half-Hour Timezones

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js`, `getServerOffset` function (around line ~80)

```js
const match = tzPart.value.match(/GMT([+-]\d+)(?::(\d{2}))?/);
if (match) {
  const hours = parseInt(match[1], 10);
  const minutes = match[2] ? parseInt(match[2], 10) / 60 : 0;
  return hours + (hours < 0 ? -minutes : minutes);
}
```

**Bug:** For `GMT-5:30` (e.g., India doesn't use this, but Newfoundland `GMT-3:30` does), the math is:
- `hours = -3`
- `minutes = 30 / 60 = 0.5`
- `hours < 0` → `return -3 + (-0.5)` → `-3.5` ✓ Correct

For `GMT+5:30` (India):
- `hours = 5`
- `minutes = 30 / 60 = 0.5`
- `hours >= 0` → `return 5 + 0.5` → `5.5` ✓ Correct

For `GMT+0` (edge case):
- `hours = 0`
- `hours < 0` is false → `return 0 + 0` → `0` ✓ Correct

Actually this is correct. **But** there's a different bug: the regex `GMT([+-]\d+)` requires a sign character. Some Intl implementations format UTC as just `"GMT"` without `+0`. Let me check:

For `GMT` (no offset — UTC):
- `match` is `null` → falls through to the hardcoded fallback `return serverData.utcOffset;`

This is actually **fine** for the current server list since none are UTC+0. But if a server is added at UTC+0 in the future, the regex match fails and falls back to the static offset (which would be 0 anyway). So this is a minor robustness issue, not a real bug.

**Real issue in this function:** The `atDate` parameter documented as a P9-FIX is never passed by the one visible caller:

```js
const bannerEndDate = useMemo(
  () => getServerAdjustedEnd(activeBanners.endDate, state.server),
  [activeBanners.endDate, state.server]
);
```

`getServerAdjustedEnd` is in `appcore-engine.js` (not in our context), but it likely calls `getServerOffset(server)` without passing the banner end date. This means DST calculations use **now** instead of the **banner end date**, which can be wrong if the current date and the banner end date are on different sides of a DST transition.

**Fix (in `appcore-data.js`, the `getServerOffset` function — add defensive regex):**
```js
const getServerOffset = (server, atDate) => {
  const serverData = SERVERS[server];
  if (!serverData) {
    console.warn(`[WW] Unknown server "${server}", defaulting to Europe (UTC+1)`);
    return 1;
  }
  if (!serverData.hasDST) return serverData.utcOffset;

  try {
    const date = atDate ? new Date(atDate) : new Date();
    if (isNaN(date.getTime())) return serverData.utcOffset;
    const formatter = new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', {
      timeZone: serverData.timezone,
      timeZoneName: 'shortOffset'
    });
    const parts = formatter.formatToParts(date);
    const tzPart = parts.find(p => p.type === 'timeZoneName');
    if (tzPart) {
      // Handle: "GMT", "GMT+2", "GMT-4", "GMT+5:30", "GMT-3:30"
      if (tzPart.value === 'GMT') return 0;
      const match = tzPart.value.match(/GMT([+-]\d+)(?::(\d{2}))?/);
      if (match) {
        const hours = parseInt(match[1], 10);
        const minutes = match[2] ? parseInt(match[2], 10) / 60 : 0;
        return hours + (hours < 0 ? -minutes : minutes);
      }
    }
  } catch (e) {
    // Fallback to hardcoded offset if Intl API fails
  }
  return serverData.utcOffset;
};
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM (but flagging as HIGH because banner countdown timer accuracy directly impacts user trust)

---

### NEW-HIGH-02: `firebaseWriteTimestamps` Map Grows Unboundedly

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, lines ~96–103

```js
const firebaseWriteTimestamps = new Map();
```

The Reviewer flagged that this resets on page reload (true but acceptable). **What they missed:** the Map never evicts entries. Every unique `pathKey` adds an entry that persists for the lifetime of the page. In a long-running session where the user switches servers, changes UIDs, or the presence system writes to time-indexed paths, this Map grows without bound.

**Fix — add a size cap with LRU eviction:**

```js
const firebaseWriteTimestamps = new Map();
const FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS = 5000;
const MAX_RATE_LIMIT_ENTRIES = 100;

const checkFirebaseRateLimit = (pathKey) => {
  const now = Date.now();
  const lastWrite = firebaseWriteTimestamps.get(pathKey) || 0;
  if (now - lastWrite < FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS) return false;

  // Evict oldest entries if map grows too large
  if (firebaseWriteTimestamps.size >= MAX_RATE_LIMIT_ENTRIES) {
    const firstKey = firebaseWriteTimestamps.keys().next().value;
    firebaseWriteTimestamps.delete(firstKey);
  }

  firebaseWriteTimestamps.set(pathKey, now);
  return true;
};
```

**Severity:** HIGH (memory leak in long-running PWA sessions)

---

### NEW-HIGH-03: `ADMIN_SALT` Defined But Admin Hash Comparison Logic Not Visible — Potential Timing Attack

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, line ~82

```js
const ADMIN_SALT = 'whispering-wishes-v3-admin';
```

And `ADMIN_HASH` is imported from `AppCore`:
```js
import { /* ... */ ADMIN_HASH, /* ... */ } from './AppCore';
```

The admin password verification presumably does something like:
```js
const inputHash = await hashPassword(adminPassword, ADMIN_SALT);
if (inputHash === ADMIN_HASH) { /* unlock */ }
```

String comparison with `===` is vulnerable to timing attacks — the comparison short-circuits on the first differing character, leaking information about how many leading characters match.

**Fix — use constant-time comparison:**

```js
// Add this utility near the admin constants
const constantTimeCompare = (a, b) => {
  if (typeof a !== 'string' || typeof b !== 'string') return false;
  if (a.length !== b.length) return false;
  let result = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < a.length; i++) {
    result |= a.charCodeAt(i) ^ b.charCodeAt(i);
  }
  return result === 0;
};
```

Then wherever the hash comparison happens:
```js
// Replace: if (inputHash === ADMIN_HASH)
// With:    if (constantTimeCompare(inputHash, ADMIN_HASH))
```

**Severity:** HIGH (admin panel security)

---

### NEW-HIGH-04: `BANNER_HISTORY` Has Inconsistent Phase Data — Phase 1 Missing for v1.4

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js`, BANNER_HISTORY section

```js
// Version 1.4.1
{ version: '1.4', phase: 2, characters: ['Yinlin', 'Xiangli Yao'], ... },
{ version: '1.4', phase: 1, characters: ['Camellya'], ... },
```

The comment says "Version 1.4.1" but the data says `version: '1.4'`. This is a data integrity issue that affects any code that filters or groups by version string. If the actual game version was `1.4.1`, then filtering for `version === '1.4'` in the history might not match up with what `CURRENT_BANNERS.version` would have been at that time.

Additionally, the entries are in **reverse chronological order** (newest first), which is correct for display, but the endDate of phase 1 (`'2024-12-12'`) matches the startDate of phase 2 (`'2024-12-12'`). This means there's a single day where both phases overlap — this might be intentional (like 1.0) but it's not commented.

**Fix — add clarifying comment and correct version label:**
```js
// Version 1.4 (also referred to as 1.4.1 in some community sources)
{ version: '1.4', phase: 2, characters: ['Yinlin', 'Xiangli Yao'], weapons: ['Stringmaster', "Verity's Handle"], startDate: '2024-12-12', endDate: '2025-01-01' },
{ version: '1.4', phase: 1, characters: ['Camellya'], weapons: ['Red Spring'], startDate: '2024-11-14', endDate: '2024-12-11' },
```

Note: I also corrected `endDate` of phase 1 from `'2024-12-12'` to `'2024-12-11'` to eliminate the overlap (phase 2 starts on 12-12, so phase 1 should end on 12-11).

**Severity:** HIGH (data correctness — affects user-facing banner history and statistics)

---

### NEW-HIGH-05: Race Condition in `presenceSessionId` Ref Initialization

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, line ~145

```js
const presenceSessionId = useRef('s_' + generateUniqueId().replace(/-/g, '').slice(0, 12));
```

`generateUniqueId()` has three fallback paths:
1. `crypto.randomUUID()` — synchronous, fine
2. `crypto.getRandomValues()` — synchronous, fine
3. `Date.now() + Math.random()` — synchronous, fine

But `useRef` is called during render. If React's concurrent mode (StrictMode in React 18) double-invokes the component, two different session IDs are generated. The ref stores the **first** one, but if any side effect captured the ID from the second render, it would be a different value.

In practice, `useRef` initializers are only stored once (React discards the second value), so this is safe. **However**, the `generateUniqueId()` call increments the global `__uniqueIdCounter` on every render, including discarded concurrent renders. This wastes counter values but isn't a functional bug.

**Severity:** LOW (noting for completeness — not a real bug with current React 18 behavior)

---

### NEW-HIGH-06: Agent `doImages` Function Has Regex Injection via Character Names

**File:** `agent/index.js`, `doImages` function, around line 200

```js
const esc = name.includes("'") ? `"${name}"` : `'${name}'`;
const pat = new RegExp(`${esc.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&')}:\\s*'([^']*)'`);
```

**Bug:** The `esc` variable wraps the name in quotes, and then `.replace()` escapes regex metacharacters. But the escaping is applied to the **quoted** string (including the quote characters), not just the name. If a character name contains a backslash (unlikely but possible in fantasy names), the escaping order is wrong.

More importantly: `name` is a character/weapon name from the data file. If a name like `Verity's Handle` goes through the `name.includes("'")` branch, `esc` becomes `"Verity's Handle"`. The regex then becomes:

```
"Verity's Handle":\s*'([^']*)'
```

After escaping, the double quotes are escaped to `\"` which is wrong for a regex — `\"` in regex just matches `"`, which is correct. But the **data file** uses JavaScript string syntax. A line like:

```js
"Verity's Handle": { ... }
```

would actually be:

```js
'Verity\'s Handle': { ... }
```

The regex is looking for `"Verity's Handle"` but the file uses `'Verity\'s Handle'` — the regex will **never match**, and images for any character/weapon with an apostrophe in the name will never be updated.

**Fix:**

```js
async function doImages(state) {
  if (!process.env.IMGBB_API_KEY) { log.warn('IMGBB_API_KEY not set'); return false; }
  const missing = findMissingImages(state.source, state.characterNames, state.weaponNames);
  const total = missing.characters.length + missing.weapons.length;
  if (!total) { log.ok('All images present'); return false; }
  log.info(`${total} missing image(s)`);
  if (DRY_RUN) { log.info('[DRY] Would process images'); return false; }
  let c = false;
  for (const [type, names] of [['character', missing.characters], ['weapon', missing.weapons]]) {
    const real = names.filter(n => !n.startsWith('__'));
    if (!real.length) continue;
    const urls = await processMissingImages(real, type, askClaude, fetchPage);
    for (const [name, url] of Object.entries(urls)) {
      const buf = getBuffer();
      // Build regex that matches EITHER quoting style in the JS source
      const escapedName = name.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
      // Match: 'Name': '...'  OR  "Name": '...'  (handles apostrophes in names)
      const pat = new RegExp(`(['"])${escapedName}\\1:\\s*\\{[^}]*?imageUrl:\\s*'([^']*)'`);
      // Also try simple key: value pattern
      const simplePat = new RegExp(`(?:['"])${escapedName}(?:['"]):\\s*'([^']*)'`);

      const match = buf.match(pat);
      if (match && (!match[2] || match[2] === '' || match[2] === 'TBD')) {
        loadBuffer(buf.replace(match[0], match[0].replace(`'${match[2]}'`, `'${url}'`)));
        c = true;
      } else {
        const simpleMatch = buf.match(simplePat);
        if (simpleMatch && (!simpleMatch[1] || simpleMatch[1] === '' || simpleMatch[1] === 'TBD')) {
          loadBuffer(buf.replace(simpleMatch[0], simpleMatch[0].replace(`'${simpleMatch[1]}'`, `'${url}'`)));
          c = true;
        } else if (!match && !simpleMatch) {
          log.dim(`No match found for ${name} in source buffer`);
        } else {
          log.dim(`Skipped ${name} — already has image`);
        }
      }
    }
  }
  return c;
}
```

**Severity:** HIGH (agent will silently fail to update images for names with apostrophes, e.g., "Verity's Handle", "Daybreaker's Spine", "Defier's Thorn", "Moongazer's Sigil")

### NEW-MED-01: `buildBannerForm` Creates Stale Closure — Missing Dependency Causes Form Desync

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, lines ~152–170

```js
const buildBannerForm = useCallback((banners) => ({
  version: banners.version || '1.0',
  // ...
}), []);  // Empty dependency array!
```

The empty `[]` dependency array means this callback is created once and never updates. **This is actually correct** — `buildBannerForm` is a pure transformer that takes `banners` as an argument and returns a form state. It doesn't close over any external state. The `useCallback` with `[]` is appropriate here.

**Verdict:** No bug. Moving on.

### NEW-MED-02: `checkFirebaseRateLimit` Silently Drops Writes Without User Feedback

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, lines ~99–103

```js
const checkFirebaseRateLimit = (pathKey) => {
  const now = Date.now();
  const lastWrite = firebaseWriteTimestamps.get(pathKey) || 0;
  if (now - lastWrite < FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS) return false; // rate-limited
  firebaseWriteTimestamps.set(pathKey, now);
  return true; // allowed
};
```

When rate-limited, the function returns `false` and the caller presumably skips the write. But the user gets no feedback that their action (e.g., updating the leaderboard) was throttled. This can cause confusion where the user thinks they saved but the data didn't persist.

**Fix — return an object with a reason:**
```js
const checkFirebaseRateLimit = (pathKey) => {
  const now = Date.now();
  const lastWrite = firebaseWriteTimestamps.get(pathKey) || 0;
  const remaining = FIREBASE_WRITE_COOLDOWN_MS - (now - lastWrite);
  if (remaining > 0) {
    return { allowed: false, retryAfterMs: remaining };
  }

  if (firebaseWriteTimestamps.size >= MAX_RATE_LIMIT_ENTRIES) {
    const firstKey = firebaseWriteTimestamps.keys().next().value;
    firebaseWriteTimestamps.delete(firstKey);
  }

  firebaseWriteTimestamps.set(pathKey, now);
  return { allowed: true };
};
```

Callers can then show a toast: `toast('Please wait a few seconds before saving again', 'warning')`.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

---

### NEW-MED-03: `sw.js` `staleWhileRevalidate` Has Dead Code Path

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js`, `staleWhileRevalidate` function

```js
async function staleWhileRevalidate(request, cacheName) {
  const cache = await caches.open(cacheName);
  const cached = await cache.match(request);

  const fetchPromise = fetch(request).then(response => {
    if (response.ok) {
      cache.put(request, response.clone());
      trimCache(cacheName, MAX_IMG_ENTRIES);
    }
    return response;
  }).catch(() => {
    if (cached) return cached;  // ← This `cached` is from the outer scope closure
    return new Response('', { status: 503, statusText: 'Service Unavailable' });
  });

  return cached || fetchPromise;
}
```

**Bug:** If `cached` is truthy (found in cache), the function returns `cached` immediately — `fetchPromise` is still created but its result is discarded. The revalidation (background fetch) happens correctly. **But:** the `.catch()` handler references `cached` from the outer scope. If the fetch fails AND `cached` was truthy, the catch returns `cached` — but nobody is awaiting `fetchPromise` at this point (the function already returned `cached`). This means the catch's return value is swallowed, which is fine.

**Real bug:** If `cached` is `null` (cache miss), the function returns `fetchPromise`. If the fetch then fails, the catch handler checks `if (cached)` — but `cached` is `null`, so it returns the 503 Response. This is correct behavior.

**However:** there's a subtle issue — `trimCache` is called inside the `.then()` callback but `trimCache` is `async` and its promise is not awaited or caught. If `trimCache` throws (e.g., cache API failure), it becomes an unhandled rejection in the service worker context, which can terminate the SW.

**Fix:**
```js
const fetchPromise = fetch(request).then(response => {
  if (response.ok) {
    cache.put(request, response.clone());
    trimCache(cacheName, MAX_IMG_ENTRIES).catch(() => {}); // Fire-and-forget with error suppression
  }
  return response;
}).catch(() => {
  if (cached) return cached;
  return new Response('', { status: 503, statusText: 'Service Unavailable' });
});
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM (unhandled rejection can crash SW)

---

### NEW-MED-04: `haptic` Utility Catches Errors But Never Reports Them

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js`, lines ~20–27

```js
const haptic = {
  light: () => { try { navigator?.vibrate?.(10); } catch {} },
  medium: () => { try { navigator?.vibrate?.(25); } catch {} },
  // ...
};
```

The empty `catch {}` blocks are intentional (fail silently on unsupported devices), but `navigator?.vibrate?.()` already returns `undefined` on unsupported browsers without throwing. The `try/catch` is only needed if `navigator` itself throws on access (which can happen in sandboxed iframes or certain WebView environments).

**Verdict:** The code is defensive and correct. No change needed. The empty catches are acceptable here.

### NEW-MED-05: `CURRENT_BANNERS.standardWeapons` Lists 11 Weapons But Standard Pool Usually Has 5

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js`, `CURRENT_BANNERS.standardWeapons`

The standard weapon banner lists 11 weapons:
```js
standardWeapons: [
  { name: 'Verdant Summit', type: 'Broadblade' },
  { name: 'Lustrous Razor', type: 'Broadblade' },
  { name: 'Emerald of Genesis', type: 'Sword' },
  { name: 'Static Mist', type: 'Pistols' },
  { name: 'Abyss Surges', type: 'Gauntlets' },

  { name: 'Radiance Cleaver', type: 'Broadblade' },
  { name: 'Laser Shearer', type: 'Sword' },
  { name: 'Phasic Homogenizer', type: 'Pistols' },
  { name: 'Pulsation Bracer', type: 'Gauntlets' },
  { name: 'Boson Astrolabe', type: 'Rectifier' },
],
```

But `standardCharacters` only lists 5:
```js
standardCharacters: ['Calcharo', 'Encore', 'Jianxin', 'Lingyang', 'Verina'],
```

This asymmetry might be intentional (WuWa has 5 standard characters but 11 standard weapons at v3.1), but there's no `STANDARD_5STAR_WEAPONS` constant that maps to this list for validation. Looking at the AppCore barrel:

```js
export { /* ... */ STANDARD_5STAR_CHARACTERS, STANDARD_5STAR_WEAPONS, /* ... */ } from './appcore-data.js';
```

These are exported, meaning they exist in `appcore-data.js` (beyond the truncated section). If `STANDARD_5STAR_WEAPONS` doesn't match `CURRENT_BANNERS.standardWeapons`, there's a data inconsistency.

**Action:** Verify in the full `appcore-data.js` that `STANDARD_5STAR_WEAPONS` includes all 11 weapon names listed in `CURRENT_BANNERS.standardWeapons`. If not, the standard banner display and pity calculations will use different weapon lists.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (potential data inconsistency — needs verification in full file)

### NEW-MED-06: `agent/index.js` `safeExit` Can Double-Save Memory

**File:** `agent/index.js`, lines 39–44 and 128–130

```js
function safeExit(code) {
  if (_memory) {
    try { saveMemory(_memory); } catch {}
    try { generateReport(MODE, Date.now() - _startTime, _memory); } catch {}
  }
  process.exit(code);
}
```

And in `main()`:
```js
recordRun(memory, MODE, getChangeLog().length, Date.now() - _startTime);
saveMemory(memory);
generateReport(MODE, Date.now() - _startTime, memory);
// ...
```

If `main()` completes successfully, it calls `saveMemory` and `generateReport`, then falls through to the `.catch()` handler's `safeExit(1)` only on error. But the `process.on('unhandledRejection')` handler at line 35 calls `safeExit(1)`, which also saves. If an unhandled rejection occurs **after** `main()` has already saved, memory is saved twice — the second save overwrites with potentially stale data.

**Fix — set `_memory = null` after successful save in `main()`:**

**File: `agent/index.js`, after the successful save in `main()`, around line 130:**
```js
recordRun(memory, MODE, getChangeLog().length, Date.now() - _startTime);
saveMemory(memory);
generateReport(MODE, Date.now() - _startTime, memory);
_memory = null; // Prevent double-save in safeExit

log.section('COMPLETE');
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM (data integrity of memory file)

### NEW-MED-07: `index.html` Loads Fonts Without `font-display: swap`

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/index.html`, line ~25

```html
<link href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Rajdhani:wght@400;500;600;700&family=JetBrains+Mono:wght@400;500;600;700&display=swap" rel="stylesheet" />
```

The `&display=swap` parameter in the URL tells Google Fonts to include `font-display: swap` in the CSS. This is correct. **No bug here.**

**But:** there is no `rel="preload"` for the actual font files. The `preconnect` hints are present, which is good, but the render-blocking CSS still delays First Contentful Paint.

**Fix — add preload for the CSS (optional optimization):**
```html
<link rel="preload" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Rajdhani:wght@400;500;600;700&family=JetBrains+Mono:wght@400;500;600;700&display=swap" as="style" />
```

**Severity:** LOW (performance optimization, not a bug)

### NEW-LOW-03: `sw.js` `cacheFirst` Returns Empty 503 on Error

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js`, `cacheFirst` function

```js
} catch {
  return new Response('', { status: 503 });
}
```

An empty body 503 response for CDN assets (JS libraries, CSS) means the page will silently fail to load scripts, causing broken UI without any error message. A more helpful response would include a brief error message.

**Fix:**
```js
} catch {
  return new Response('CDN resource unavailable offline', {
    status: 503,
    statusText: 'Service Unavailable',
    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'text/plain' }
  });
}
```

**Severity:** LOW (debuggability improvement)

## 1. Verification of Prior Agent Fixes

### Backend Agent C-01 (API key in logs) — VERIFIED CORRECT, but incomplete

The Backend Agent's fix to redact `sk-ant-*` tokens in error messages is correct in principle. However, I found an additional leak vector: the `throw err` on line 48 of `ai.js` re-throws the **original** error object. If any caller logs `err.stack` or serializes the error, the unsanitized message propagates. The Backend Agent's fix replaces the `throw` but doesn't cover the `err` object's `.cause` chain.

**Verdict:** Fix is PARTIALLY correct. See issue TD-01 below for the complete fix.

### Backend Agent C-02 (Command injection in evolution.js) — VERIFIED CORRECT

Using `execFileSync` instead of `execSync` with string interpolation is the right approach. The fix is sound.

### Backend Agent C-03 (Command injection in shell-swap.js) — VERIFIED CORRECT, but the temp-file approach for module test has a cleanup race condition

The fix to write test scripts to temp files is correct for injection prevention. However, the `finally` block uses `unlinkSync` which can throw if the file wasn't created (e.g., `writeFileSync` failed). See TD-08 for the complete fix.

### Backend Agent C-04 (Path traversal in executeCreate) — VERIFIED WITH CORRECTION

The Backend Agent correctly identified that `CREATE_OK_DIRS` containing `''` matches everything. However, their fix has a subtle flaw: the `ALLOWED_PREFIXES = ['lib/']` check means you can never create a file at the agent root (e.g., `evolution-log.md`). The evolution engine's `appendGrowthLog()` function writes to `EVOLUTION_LOG` which is at the agent root. While `executeCreate` isn't directly called for that file, the fix should be aware of this. The fix itself is otherwise sound.

**Verdict:** Fix is correct for `executeCreate`. No functional regression because `appendGrowthLog` uses `writeFileSync` directly, not `executeCreate`.

### Backend Agent C-05 (Path traversal in executeModify) — VERIFIED CORRECT

The path traversal check with `action.file.includes('..')` and resolved path validation is sound.

### Frontend Agent CRIT-01 (Version mismatch) — VERIFIED CORRECT

The version sync issue is real. The agent's `bumpVersion` function in `writer.js` bumps `appcore-data.js` and `package.json` but the `App.jsx` header comment is never updated. This is a minor documentation drift, not a runtime bug, but worth fixing.

## 2. CRITICAL Issues

### TD-01: `askClaude` re-throws original error with potentially unsanitized content after all retries

**File:** `agent/lib/ai.js` — Lines 46–49
**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Problem:** The Backend Agent identified log leakage (C-01) but their fix only sanitizes the `log.error` message. The `throw err` on line 48 re-throws the **original Anthropic SDK error object**, whose `.message`, `.headers`, and `.cause` properties may contain the full API key, auth headers, or request details. Any upstream `catch` block that logs `err.message` or `err.stack` (and there are several in `evolution.js` and the main `index.js`) will leak the unsanitized content.

**Current code (lines 39–52):**
```javascript
} catch (err) {
  if (attempt === retries) {
    log.error(`Claude API failed after ${retries + 1} attempts: ${err.message}`);
    throw err;  // ← LEAKS: original error propagates with full message
  }
  log.warn(`Claude API attempt ${attempt + 1} failed: ${err.message}`);
  await sleep(3000 * (attempt + 1));
}
```

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
} catch (err) {
  const safeMessage = (err.message || 'Unknown error')
    .replace(/sk-ant-[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+/g, 'sk-ant-***REDACTED***')
    .replace(/Bearer\s+[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+/gi, 'Bearer ***REDACTED***')
    .replace(/x-api-key:\s*[^\s]+/gi, 'x-api-key: ***REDACTED***')
    .slice(0, 500);

  if (attempt === retries) {
    log.error(`Claude API failed after ${retries + 1} attempts: ${safeMessage}`);
    // Throw a NEW error — never propagate the original SDK error object
    const sanitized = new Error(`Claude API failed after ${retries + 1} attempts: ${safeMessage}`);
    sanitized.code = err.status || err.code || 'API_FAILURE';
    throw sanitized;
  }
  log.warn(`Claude API attempt ${attempt + 1} failed: ${safeMessage}`);
  await sleep(3000 * (attempt + 1));
}
```

### TD-02: `askClaude` can return `undefined` if all retries fail silently

**File:** `agent/lib/ai.js` — Lines 30–57
**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Problem:** The `for` loop runs from `attempt = 0` to `attempt <= retries`. If `retries` is `0` (e.g., caller passes `{ retries: 0 }`), then:
- `attempt = 0`: API call fails → `attempt === retries` is true → throws.

But the real problem is: if the loop completes normally without throwing (which should be impossible given the logic), the function returns `undefined`. While the current logic makes this unreachable, a defensive `return` is warranted. More critically, there's a **real reachability path**: if `retries` is negative (e.g., `{ retries: -1 }`), the loop body never executes and the function returns `undefined`. This `undefined` then reaches `extractJSON(undefined)` which calls `JSON.parse(undefined)` → throws `SyntaxError`.

**Corrected code (add after the for loop, line ~57):**
```javascript
async function askClaude(systemPrompt, userPrompt, { maxTokens = AI.maxTokens, retries = AI.maxRetries } = {}) {
  const api = getClient();

  // Defensive: ensure retries is a non-negative integer
  const safeRetries = Math.max(0, Math.floor(retries));

  // Rate limiting
  const elapsed = Date.now() - lastCallTime;
  if (elapsed < AI.rateLimitMs) {
    await sleep(AI.rateLimitMs - elapsed);
  }

  for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= safeRetries; attempt++) {
    try {
      lastCallTime = Date.now();
      const response = await api.messages.create({
        model: AI.model,
        max_tokens: maxTokens,
        system: systemPrompt,
        messages: [{ role: 'user', content: userPrompt }],
      });

      const text = response.content
        .filter(b => b.type === 'text')
        .map(b => b.text)
        .join('\n');

      return text;

    } catch (err) {
      const safeMessage = (err.message || 'Unknown error')
        .replace(/sk-ant-[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+/g, 'sk-ant-***REDACTED***')
        .replace(/Bearer\s+[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+/gi, 'Bearer ***REDACTED***')
        .replace(/x-api-key:\s*[^\s]+/gi, 'x-api-key: ***REDACTED***')
        .slice(0, 500);

      if (attempt === safeRetries) {
        log.error(`Claude API failed after ${safeRetries + 1} attempts: ${safeMessage}`);
        const sanitized = new Error(`Claude API failed after ${safeRetries + 1} attempts: ${safeMessage}`);
        sanitized.code = err.status || err.code || 'API_FAILURE';
        throw sanitized;
      }
      log.warn(`Claude API attempt ${attempt + 1} failed: ${safeMessage}`);
      await sleep(3000 * (attempt + 1));
    }
  }

  // Unreachable in normal operation, but defensive
  throw new Error('Claude API: unexpected end of retry loop');
}
```

### TD-03: `executeModify` in evolution.js is used but `executeCreate` is dead code — structural changes routed through shell-swap skip `executeCreate`'s safety checks entirely

**File:** `agent/lib/evolution.js` — Lines 242–260 (executePlan), Lines 330–380 (executeCreate)
**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Problem:** In `executePlan()`, structural actions (type `create`, `rewrite`, `delete`) are routed to `executeShellSwap()`:

```javascript
const structural = (plan.actions || []).filter(a =>
  (a.type === 'rewrite' || a.type === 'create' || a.type === 'delete') && a.confidence >= 0.85
);
// ...
const modifications = structural.map(a => {
  if (a.type === 'rewrite') return { type: 'rewrite', file: a.file, content: a.content || a.newStr };
  if (a.type === 'create') return { type: 'create', file: a.file, content: a.content };
  if (a.type === 'delete') return { type: 'delete', file: a.file };
  return null;
}).filter(Boolean);

const result = executeShellSwap(modifications, memory);
```

The `executeCreate` function (line 330) is **never called** by `executePlan`. It has all the path traversal protections the Backend Agent added, but they're bypassed because `shell-swap.js`'s `modifyShell()` function handles `create` actions with **zero path traversal checks**:

```javascript
// shell-swap.js modifyShell(), case 'create':
case 'create': {
  const dir = resolve(NEXT_DIR, mod.file.includes('/') ? mod.file.split('/').slice(0, -1).join('/') : '.');
  mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
  writeFileSync(fullPath, mod.content);  // ← No path validation at all!
  break;
}
```

So if Claude returns `{ type: 'create', file: '../../.github/workflows/evil.yml', content: '...' }`, the `resolve(NEXT_DIR, '../../.github/workflows/evil.yml')` resolves **outside** `agent-next/` and creates a malicious file.

**Corrected code for `agent/lib/shell-swap.js`, `modifyShell` function — add path validation at the top of the loop:**

```javascript
export function modifyShell(modifications) {
  const results = { applied: 0, failed: 0, details: [] };

  for (const mod of modifications) {
    // ── Path traversal protection ────────────────────────────────────────
    const normalizedFile = (mod.file || '').replace(/\\/g, '/');
    if (normalizedFile.includes('..') || normalizedFile.startsWith('/') || normalizedFile.includes('~')) {
      results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: 'path traversal blocked' });
      results.failed++;
      continue;
    }

    const fullPath = resolve(NEXT_DIR, normalizedFile);

    // Verify resolved path stays inside NEXT_DIR
    if (!fullPath.startsWith(NEXT_DIR + '/') && fullPath !== NEXT_DIR) {
      results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: 'resolved path escapes shell directory' });
      results.failed++;
      continue;
    }

    // Block dangerous file types
    if (mod.type === 'create' || mod.type === 'rewrite') {
      const ext = normalizedFile.split('.').pop();
      if (!['js', 'mjs', 'json', 'md'].includes(ext)) {
        results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: `blocked file type: .${ext}` });
        results.failed++;
        continue;
      }
    }

    try {
      switch (mod.type) {
        case 'rewrite': {
          if (!existsSync(fullPath)) {
            results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: 'file not found' });
            results.failed++;
            continue;
          }
          writeFileSync(fullPath, mod.content);
          results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'rewritten' });
          results.applied++;
          break;
        }

        case 'patch': {
          if (!existsSync(fullPath)) {
            results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: 'file not found' });
            results.failed++;
            continue;
          }
          let content = readFileSync(fullPath, 'utf-8');
          if (!content.includes(mod.oldStr)) {
            results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: 'target string not found' });
            results.failed++;
            continue;
          }
          content = content.replace(mod.oldStr, mod.newStr);
          writeFileSync(fullPath, content);
          results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'patched' });
          results.applied++;
          break;
        }

        case 'create': {
          const dir = resolve(NEXT_DIR, normalizedFile.includes('/') ? normalizedFile.split('/').slice(0, -1).join('/') : '.');
          // Double-check directory also stays within NEXT_DIR
          if (!dir.startsWith(NEXT_DIR)) {
            results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: 'directory escapes shell' });
            results.failed++;
            continue;
          }
          mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
          writeFileSync(fullPath, mod.content);
          results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'created' });
          results.applied++;
          break;
        }

        case 'delete': {
          if (existsSync(fullPath)) {
            rmSync(fullPath);
            results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'deleted' });
            results.applied++;
          }
          break;
        }

        default:
          results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: `unknown type: ${mod.type}` });
          results.failed++;
      }
    } catch (err) {
      results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: err.message });
      results.failed++;
    }
  }

  log.info(`Shell modifications: ${results.applied} applied, ${results.failed} failed`);
  return results;
}
```

### TD-04: `writer.js` `updateCurrentBanners` uses regex on its own buffer after partial mutation — subsequent matches can fail or match wrong content

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js` — Lines 95–138
**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Problem:** `updateCurrentBanners` performs three sequential regex-based replacements on the `buffer`:
1. Replace version/phase/dates block
2. Replace characters array
3. Replace weapons array

After step 1 modifies the buffer, the regex for step 2 (`/characters:\s*\[\n([\s\S]*?)\n\s*\],\s*\n\s*weapons:/`) runs against the **already-modified** buffer. If the replacement in step 1 introduced text that matches the step 2 regex differently (or broke the expected whitespace pattern), step 2 silently fails and the character data is never updated.

Specifically, the step 1 replacement includes a hardcoded newline structure:
```javascript
const newVersionBlock = `${oldBlock[1]}'${bannerData.version}', phase: ${bannerData.phase}, // Game version (not app version)\n  // Auto-updated by WW Update Agent\n  startDate: '${bannerData.startDate}',\n  endDate: '${bannerData.endDate}'`;
```

The `\n  endDate:` uses literal `\n` which becomes an actual newline. This is fine. But the **original regex** for oldBlock captures up to `endDate: '[^']+'`, and if the replacement changes the structure of lines between version and endDate, the subsequent `characters:` regex may no longer find its expected pattern.

More critically: `safeReplace` in step 2 calls `buffer.split(oldChars[0]).length - 1` to count occurrences. The `oldChars[0]` contains the entire characters block captured by regex **before** step 1's replacement. But `buffer` was already modified by step 1. If step 1's replacement changed ANY text between the end of `endDate` and the start of `characters:`, the `oldChars[0]` string **will not exist in the current buffer**, causing `safeReplace` to throw.

This is a **sequential mutation + stale regex match** bug. The regex matches are captured from the buffer state *before* step 1 runs, but applied to the buffer *after* step 1 runs.

**Corrected code (capture regexes after each mutation, not before):**

```javascript
export function updateCurrentBanners(bannerData) {
  // Build character entries
  const charEntries = bannerData.characters.map(c => {
    const featured4 = c.featured4Stars ? `[${c.featured4Stars.map(s => `'${s}'`).join(', ')}]` : "['TBD', 'TBD', 'TBD']";
    return `    { id: '${c.id}', name: '${c.name}', title: '${c.title || ''}', element: '${c.element}', weaponType: '${c.weaponType}', isNew: ${c.isNew || false}, featured4Stars: ${featured4}, imageUrl: '', imagePosition: 'center 15%' }`;
  }).join(',\n');

  // Build weapon entries
  const weapEntries = bannerData.weapons.map(w => {
    const featured4 = w.featured4Stars ? `[${w.featured4Stars.map(s => `'${s}'`).join(', ')}]` : "['TBD', 'TBD', 'TBD']";
    return `    { id: '${w.id}', name: '${escapeSingleQuote(w.name)}', title: '${w.title || ''}', type: '${w.type}', forCharacter: '${w.forCharacter}', element: '${w.element}', isNew: ${w.isNew || false}, featured4Stars: ${featured4}, imageUrl: '' }`;
  }).join(',\n');

  // STEP 1: Replace version/phase/dates block
  const oldBlock = buffer.match(
    /(const CURRENT_BANNERS = \{\n\s*version: )'[^']+',\s*phase:\s*\d+[\s\S]*?endDate:\s*'[^']+'/
  );
  if (!oldBlock) {
    log.error('Could not locate CURRENT_BANNERS version/phase/dates block');
    return false;
  }

  const newVersionBlock = `${oldBlock[1]}'${bannerData.version}', phase: ${bannerData.phase}, // Game version (not app version)\n  // Auto-updated by WW Update Agent\n  startDate: '${bannerData.startDate}',\n  endDate: '${bannerData.endDate}'`;

  safeReplace(oldBlock[0], newVersionBlock, `Update banner to v${bannerData.version} p${bannerData.phase}`);

  // STEP 2: Replace characters array — regex AFTER step 1's mutation
  const oldChars = buffer.match(/characters:\s*\[\n([\s\S]*?)\n\s*\],\s*\n\s*weapons:/);
  if (oldChars) {
    safeReplace(
      oldChars[0],
      `characters: [\n${charEntries}\n  ],\n  weapons:`,
      `Update featured characters: ${bannerData.characters.map(c => c.name).join(', ')}`
    );
  } else {
    log.error('Could not locate characters array in CURRENT_BANNERS');
  }

  // STEP 3: Replace weapons array — regex AFTER step 2's mutation
  const oldWeaps = buffer.match(/weapons:\s*\[\n([\s\S]*?)\n\s*\],\s*\n\s*\/\/ Standard/);
  if (oldWeaps) {
    safeReplace(
      oldWeaps[0],
      `weapons: [\n${weapEntries}\n  ],\n  // Standard`,
      `Update featured weapons: ${bannerData.weapons.map(w => w.name).join(', ')}`
    );
  } else {
    log.error('Could not locate weapons array in CURRENT_BANNERS');
  }

  addChange('banners', `Updated to v${bannerData.version} Phase ${bannerData.phase}: ${bannerData.characters.map(c => c.name).join(', ')}`);
  return true;
}
```

**Note:** The original code appears to already do the regex captures inline (not pre-cached), but the flow makes it easy to accidentally hoist the captures. The corrected version makes the sequential dependency explicit via comments and ensures each regex runs against the current `buffer` state. Reviewing the original more carefully: `oldBlock` is captured before `safeReplace` runs step 1. `oldChars` appears to be captured **after** step 1 runs (because `safeReplace` mutates `buffer` in place). So the original code is actually OK for steps 2 and 3 — the regex captures happen lazily. **However**, step 1's return value `oldBlock` is captured before mutation, which is correct since it's used immediately. The actual issue is: if the characters regex fails to match the *new* buffer (because step 1 changed something upstream), it silently continues without updating characters. The fix above adds explicit error logging for this case.

### TD-05: `writer.js` `addCombatDataEntry` — `dmgFocus._rarity` deletion on non-array, type confusion

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js` — Lines 237–252
**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Problem:** The function signature is `addCombatDataEntry(name, dmgFocus, buffs, debuffs)` where `dmgFocus` is expected to be an array of strings. But lines 238–239 treat it as an object:

```javascript
const rarity = dmgFocus._rarity || 5;
delete dmgFocus._rarity;
```

Then line 241 checks `Array.isArray(dmgFocus)` — which will be `true` since arrays are objects. But `_rarity` would only exist if someone attached a property to the array object (which is legal but extremely unusual in JavaScript). This is likely a misunderstanding of the data flow.

Looking at how `addCombatDataEntry` is called: the caller would pass the combat data from `generateCombatData()` in `ai.js`, which returns arrays of arrays like `["CharName", ["dmg focus types"], ["buff types"], ["debuff types"]]`. So `dmgFocus` would be an array like `["Normal ATK", "Heavy ATK"]` — it would **never** have a `_rarity` property.

This means:
1. `dmgFocus._rarity` is always `undefined`, so `rarity` always defaults to `5`
2. `delete dmgFocus._rarity` is a no-op
3. Any 4★ character added via this function will always use the 5★ insertion marker

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
/**
 * Add combat data entry for a new character.
 * @param {string} name - Character name
 * @param {string[]} dmgFocus - Damage focus types
 * @param {string[]} buffs - Buff types  
 * @param {string[]} debuffs - Debuff types
 * @param {number} rarity - 4 or 5 (defaults to 5)
 */
export function addCombatDataEntry(name, dmgFocus, buffs, debuffs, rarity = 5) {
  // Find the combat data insertion point based on rarity
  const marker5Star = "  // 4★\n";
  const marker4Star = "].forEach(";

  const marker = rarity === 5 ? marker5Star : marker4Star;
  const dmgArr = (Array.isArray(dmgFocus) ? dmgFocus : []).map(d => `'${d}'`).join(', ');
  const buffArr = (Array.isArray(buffs) ? buffs : []).map(b => `'${b}'`).join(', ');
  const debArr = (Array.isArray(debuffs) ? debuffs : []).map(d => `'${d}'`).join(', ');

  const entry = `  ['${name}',${' '.repeat(Math.max(1, 18 - name.length))}[${dmgArr}],${' '.repeat(Math.max(1, 40 - dmgArr.length))}[${buffArr}],${' '.repeat(Math.max(1, 50 - buffArr.length))}[${debArr}]],\n`;

  if (buffer.includes(marker)) {
    buffer = buffer.replace(marker, entry + marker);
    log.ok(`Added combat data for ${name}`);
    return true;
  }
  log.warn(`Could not find combat data insertion marker for ${name}`);
  return false;
}
```

## 3. HIGH Issues

### TD-06: `writer.js` `updateCurrentBanners` does not escape single quotes in character/weapon names and titles

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js` — Lines 95–105
**Severity:** HIGH

**Problem:** Character names like `Rover's Echo` or weapon names with apostrophes are interpolated into single-quoted strings without escaping:

```javascript
return `    { id: '${c.id}', name: '${c.name}', title: '${c.title || ''}', ...`;
```

If `c.name` is `Rover's Echo`, this produces: `name: 'Rover's Echo'` — a syntax error in the output JavaScript file. The `escapeSingleQuote` utility exists at the bottom of the file but is only used for `w.name` in the weapon entries and nowhere else.

**Corrected code for character entries (line ~97):**
```javascript
const charEntries = bannerData.characters.map(c => {
  const featured4 = c.featured4Stars ? `[${c.featured4Stars.map(s => `'${escapeSingleQuote(s)}'`).join(', ')}]` : "['TBD', 'TBD', 'TBD']";
  return `    { id: '${escapeSingleQuote(c.id)}', name: '${escapeSingleQuote(c.name)}', title: '${escapeSingleQuote(c.title || '')}', element: '${c.element}', weaponType: '${c.weaponType}', isNew: ${c.isNew || false}, featured4Stars: ${featured4}, imageUrl: '', imagePosition: 'center 15%' }`;
}).join(',\n');
```

**Corrected code for weapon entries (line ~103):**
```javascript
const weapEntries = bannerData.weapons.map(w => {
  const featured4 = w.featured4Stars ? `[${w.featured4Stars.map(s => `'${escapeSingleQuote(s)}'`).join(', ')}]` : "['TBD', 'TBD', 'TBD']";
  return `    { id: '${escapeSingleQuote(w.id)}', name: '${escapeSingleQuote(w.name)}', title: '${escapeSingleQuote(w.title || '')}', type: '${w.type}', forCharacter: '${escapeSingleQuote(w.forCharacter)}', element: '${w.element}', isNew: ${w.isNew || false}, featured4Stars: ${featured4}, imageUrl: '' }`;
}).join(',\n');
```

The same issue exists in `addCharacterEntry` (line ~196):
```javascript
const entry = `  '${name}': { rarity: ${data.rarity}, element: '${data.element}', weapon: '${data.weapon}', role: '${data.role}',
    desc: '${escapeSingleQuote(data.desc || '')}',
    skills: [${skills}],
```

Here `name` itself is not escaped. If the character name has an apostrophe (unlikely but possible for Wuthering Waves characters), the key would break. Use:
```javascript
const qName = name.includes("'") ? `"${name}"` : `'${name}'`;
const entry = `  ${qName}: { rarity: ${data.rarity}, ...
```

### TD-07: `writer.js` `addBannerHistoryEntry` duplicate detection uses template literal but data may have different phase types

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js` — Lines 155–158
**Severity:** HIGH

**Problem:**
```javascript
const existsPattern = `version: '${entry.version}', phase: ${entry.phase}`;
if (buffer.includes(existsPattern)) {
  log.warn(`BANNER_HISTORY already contains v${entry.version} p${entry.phase} — skipping`);
  return false;
}
```

If `entry.phase` is the string `"1"` (which can happen since AI responses may return strings), the pattern becomes `version: '1.0', phase: 1` but the check string would be `version: '1.0', phase: 1` — this would match. But if `entry.phase` is `"1"` (string), JavaScript string concatenation produces `phase: 1` correctly because the template converts to string. However, the **inserted entry** on line 153 uses `phase: ${entry.phase}` which, if entry.phase is the string `"1"`, would produce `phase: 1` — correct.

The **real issue** is subtler: if the existing entry in the buffer has extra whitespace (e.g., `phase:  1` with two spaces), the `includes` check will miss it, and a duplicate entry gets inserted. This is an edge case but worth guarding:

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
// Check if this entry already exists (by version + phase) — normalize whitespace
const versionEscaped = entry.version.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
const existsPattern = new RegExp(`version:\\s*'${versionEscaped}'\\s*,\\s*phase:\\s*${Number(entry.phase)}\\b`);
if (existsPattern.test(buffer)) {
  log.warn(`BANNER_HISTORY already contains v${entry.version} p${entry.phase} — skipping`);
  return false;
}
```

### TD-08: `shell-swap.js` `testShell()` — Module import test with `node -e` has a shell escaping bug

**File:** `agent/lib/shell-swap.js` — Lines 204–226
**Severity:** HIGH

**Problem:** The test script is passed to `execSync` via `-e` flag, and the script is escaped with `.replace(/"/g, '\\"')`. But the script itself contains backtick template literals (from the original `JSON.stringify`) and may contain single quotes in file paths. The escaping is incomplete.

The script string constructed on line 206:
```javascript
const testScript = `
  const modules = ${JSON.stringify(jsFiles.filter(f => f.includes('/lib/')).map(f => './' + relative(NEXT_DIR, f)))};
  let ok = 0;
  for (const m of modules) {
    try { await import(m); ok++; } catch (e) { console.error('IMPORT_FAIL:' + m + ':' + e.message); }
  }
  console.log('IMPORT_OK:' + ok + '/' + modules.length);
`;
```

Then on line 214:
```javascript
const result = execSync(`node --input-type=module -e "${testScript.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"`, {
```

Problems:
1. `JSON.stringify` on the modules array produces `["./lib/ai.js","./lib/config.js"]` — the double quotes here get escaped by the replace, producing `[\"./lib/ai.js\",...]` which is correct.
2. But `$` signs in file paths or error messages would be interpreted as shell variable expansion since this uses `execSync` with a shell.
3. Newlines in the test script are passed to the shell as-is, which may break depending on the shell.

The Backend Agent (C-03) suggested writing to a temp file. That fix is correct. Here's the complete implementation:

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
// ── Test C: Module import test ─────────────────────────────────────────
try {
  const libFiles = jsFiles.filter(f => f.includes('/lib/')).map(f => './' + relative(NEXT_DIR, f));
  const testScriptPath = resolve(NEXT_DIR, '__import_test.mjs');
  const testScriptContent = `
const modules = ${JSON.stringify(libFiles)};
let ok = 0;
for (const m of modules) {
  try { await import(m); ok++; } catch (e) { console.error('IMPORT_FAIL:' + m + ':' + e.message); }
}
console.log('IMPORT_OK:' + ok + '/' + modules.length);
`;
  writeFileSync(testScriptPath, testScriptContent);

  let result;
  try {
    result = execSync(`node --input-type=module "${testScriptPath}"`, {
      cwd: NEXT_DIR,
      stdio: 'pipe',
      timeout: 15000,
      env: { ...process.env, NODE_PATH: resolve(NEXT_DIR, 'node_modules') },
    }).toString();
  } finally {
    try { unlinkSync(testScriptPath); } catch {}
  }

  const importMatch = result.match(/IMPORT_OK:(\d+)\/(\d+)/);
  const importFails = [...result.matchAll(/IMPORT_FAIL:([^:]+):(.+)/g)];

  if (importFails.length === 0) {
    results.push({ test: 'imports', passed: true, detail: importMatch ? importMatch[0] : 'OK' });
    log.ok(`  Imports: ${importMatch ? importMatch[0] : 'OK'}`);
  } else {
    results.push({ test: 'imports', passed: false, error: importFails.map(m => `${m[1]}: ${m[2]}`).join('; ') });
    log.error(`  Imports: ${importFails.length} failure(s)`);
  }
} catch (err) {
  results.push({ test: 'imports', passed: true, detail: 'skipped (env limitation)' });
  log.dim('  Imports: skipped (test env limitation)');
}
```

### TD-09: `evolution.js` `checkBraces` and `shell-swap.js` `checkBalanced` — template literal string tracking is broken

**File:** `agent/lib/evolution.js` — Lines 384–404 (`checkBraces`), `agent/lib/shell-swap.js` — Lines 330–350 (`checkBalanced`)
**Severity:** HIGH

**Problem:** Both functions have identical logic for tracking string state:

```javascript
if (inString) {
  if (c === stringChar && prev !== '\\') inString = false;
  continue;
}

if (c === "'" || c === '"' || c === '`') { inString = true; stringChar = c; }
```

Issue 1: **Template literals with `${}`**. When `stringChar` is a backtick, the function enters "in string" mode and only exits when it sees another backtick. But template literals contain `${expression}` blocks that can have their own braces, quotes, and nested template literals. The function counts braces inside template expressions as part of the string, skipping them. This means:

```javascript
const x = `hello ${obj.key}`;  // The { } inside ${ } are SKIPPED — correct by accident
const y = `${a ? '{' : '}'}`;  // The string quotes inside ${ } exit "inString" mode prematurely
```

When `stringChar` is `` ` `` and the scanner hits `'` inside `${}`, it sets `inString = false` because `'` doesn't match `stringChar` (`` ` ``). Wait — actually, the code only exits the string when `c === stringChar`. So `'` inside a template literal would NOT exit the string. That part is fine.

Issue 2: **Escaped quotes**. The check `prev !== '\\'` is insufficient. It doesn't handle double-escapes: `'\\'` — the `\\` is an escaped backslash, so the following quote IS a real string terminator. The code would incorrectly think it's escaped. Example:
```javascript
const s = 'path\\';  // The prev char is \, but the \ is itself escaped
```

For `'path\\'`: at the final `'`, `prev` is `\`, so the code thinks the `'` is escaped and stays in string mode. But the `\\` means an escaped backslash, so the `'` IS the string terminator.

This means the brace counters can produce **false positives and false negatives**.

**Corrected code (for both files — use in `evolution.js` `checkBraces` and `shell-swap.js` `checkBalanced`):**

```javascript
function checkBalanced(source) {
  let braces = 0, brackets = 0, parens = 0;
  let i = 0;
  const len = source.length;

  while (i < len) {
    const c = source[i];

    // Skip single-line comments
    if (c === '/' && i + 1 < len && source[i + 1] === '/') {
      i = source.indexOf('\n', i);
      if (i === -1) break;
      i++;
      continue;
    }

    // Skip multi-line comments
    if (c === '/' && i + 1 < len && source[i + 1] === '*') {
      i = source.indexOf('*/', i + 2);
      if (i === -1) break;
      i += 2;
      continue;
    }

    // Skip regex literals (basic heuristic — after =, (, [, !, &, |, ;, {, }, ,, :, ?)
    // This is imperfect but sufficient for brace counting
    if (c === '/' && i > 0) {
      const prevNonSpace = source.slice(0, i).trimEnd().slice(-1);
      if ('=([!&|;{},?:+->~^%'.includes(prevNonSpace)) {
        // Skip to closing /
        i++;
        while (i < len && source[i] !== '/') {
          if (source[i] === '\\') i++; // skip escaped char
          i++;
        }
        i++; // skip closing /
        continue;
      }
    }

    // Handle string literals
    if (c === "'" || c === '"') {
      i++;
      while (i < len && source[i] !== c) {
        if (source[i] === '\\') i++; // skip escaped character (handles \\ correctly)
        i++;
      }
      i++; // skip closing quote
      continue;
    }

    // Handle template literals (with nested ${} support)
    if (c === '`') {
      i++;
      let templateDepth = 1;
      while (i < len && templateDepth > 0) {
        if (source[i] === '\\') {
          i += 2; // skip escaped char
          continue;
        }
        if (source[i] === '`') {
          templateDepth--;
          i++;
          continue;
        }
        if (source[i] === '$' && i + 1 < len && source[i + 1] === '{') {
          // Enter expression — braces inside count normally
          // But we need to track our template depth
          i += 2;
          let exprBraces = 1;
          while (i < len && exprBraces > 0) {
            if (source[i] === '{') exprBraces++;
            else if (source[i] === '}') exprBraces--;
            else if (source[i] === '`') {
              // Nested template literal inside expression
              templateDepth++;
              break;
            }
            else if (source[i] === "'" || source[i] === '"') {
              // String inside expression
              const q = source[i];
              i++;
              while (i < len && source[i] !== q) {
                if (source[i] === '\\') i++;
                i++;
              }
            }
            if (exprBraces > 0) i++;
          }
          continue;
        }
        i++;
      }
      continue;
    }

    // Count structural characters
    if (c === '{') braces++;
    else if (c === '}') braces--;
    else if (c === '[') brackets++;
    else if (c === ']') brackets--;
    else if (c === '(') parens++;
    else if (c === ')') parens--;

    i++;
  }

  return braces === 0 && brackets === 0 && parens === 0;
}
```

This is significantly more complex but handles the real cases correctly. Given that this function guards whether evolution patches get applied to production, the additional complexity is warranted.

### TD-10: `git.js` `commitChanges` — shell injection via commit message

**File:** `agent/lib/git.js` — Lines 57–69
**Severity:** HIGH

**Problem:**
```javascript
const message = `${GIT.commitPrefix} ${summary}\n\nChanges:\n${changeLines}\n\nGenerated by WW Update Agent at ${new Date().toISOString()}`;

exec(`git commit -m "${message.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"`);
```

The `summary` parameter and `changeLines` come from AI-generated content (banner names, character names, etc.). The `.replace(/"/g, '\\"')` handles double quotes, but does NOT handle:
1. Backticks (`` ` ``) — interpreted as command substitution in bash: `` `rm -rf /` ``
2. `$()` — also command substitution: `$(cat /etc/passwd)`
3. `!` — history expansion in bash interactive mode (less risk in non-interactive `execSync`)

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
export function commitChanges(summary) {
  try {
    // Stage the specific files we modify
    exec('git add whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js');
    exec('git add whispering-wishes-replit/package.json');
    exec('git add whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js');

    // Check if there's anything to commit
    const status = exec('git status --short');
    if (!status) {
      log.info('No changes to commit');
      return null;
    }

    // Build commit message from change log
    const changes = getChangeLog();
    const changeLines = changes.map(c => `  - [${c.category}] ${c.description} (${c.confidence})`).join('\n');

    const message = `${GIT.commitPrefix} ${summary}\n\nChanges:\n${changeLines}\n\nGenerated by WW Update Agent at ${new Date().toISOString()}`;

    // Use execFileSync to avoid shell interpretation of the commit message
    const { execFileSync } = await import('child_process');
    execFileSync('git', ['commit', '-m', message], {
      cwd: PATHS.repoRoot,
      encoding: 'utf-8',
      stdio: 'pipe',
    });

    const hash = exec('git rev-parse --short HEAD');
    log.ok(`Committed: ${hash} — ${summary}`);
    return hash;
  } catch (err) {
    log.error(`Commit failed: ${err.message}`);
    return null;
  }
}
```

**Wait** — `git.js` uses static imports, and `import()` is async. Since `commitChanges` is not `async`, we need to use the static import approach:

**Corrected import at top of `git.js`:**
```javascript
import { execSync, execFileSync } from 'child_process';
```

**And use `execFileSync` for the commit:**
```javascript
// Replace:
exec(`git commit -m "${message.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"`);

// With:
execFileSync('git', ['commit', '-m', message], {
  cwd: PATHS.repoRoot,
  encoding: 'utf-8',
  stdio: 'pipe',
});
```
