### TD-11: `audit.js` `applyPatches` writes files without atomic write — crash mid-write corrupts source

**File:** `agent/lib/audit.js` — Lines 96–117
**Severity:** HIGH

**Problem:** The audit patch application reads, modifies, and writes back directly:

```javascript
content = content.replace(patch.oldStr, patch.newStr);
writeFileSync(filePath, content);
```

If the process crashes during `writeFileSync` (OOM, SIGKILL from CI timeout, disk full), the source file is left in a corrupted (partially written) state. The `writer.js` `flush()` function uses atomic write (temp file + rename), but `audit.js` does not.

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
// In applyPatches, replace the write section (lines ~113-114):
content = content.replace(patch.oldStr, patch.newStr);

// Atomic write: temp file → rename
const tmpPath = filePath + '.audit-tmp';
try {
  writeFileSync(tmpPath, content);
  const { renameSync } = await import('fs');
  renameSync(tmpPath, filePath);
} catch (writeErr) {
  // If rename fails, fall back to direct write
  writeFileSync(filePath, content);
  try { const { unlinkSync } = await import('fs'); unlinkSync(tmpPath); } catch {}
}
```

Since `audit.js` uses static imports, add `renameSync, unlinkSync` to the import:

```javascript
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync, renameSync, unlinkSync } from 'fs';
```

And use directly:
```javascript
content = content.replace(patch.oldStr, patch.newStr);

// Atomic write: temp file → rename
const tmpPath = filePath + '.audit-tmp';
try {
  writeFileSync(tmpPath, content);
  renameSync(tmpPath, filePath);
} catch (writeErr) {
  log.warn(`Atomic write failed for ${patch.file}, falling back to direct write: ${writeErr.message}`);
  writeFileSync(filePath, content);
  try { unlinkSync(tmpPath); } catch {}
}
```

## 4. MEDIUM Issues

### TD-12: `images.js` `findMissingImages` — regex fails for characters with special regex characters in names

**File:** `agent/lib/images.js` — Lines 137–140
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:**
```javascript
for (const name of characterNames) {
  const pattern = new RegExp(`'${name.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&')}'\\s*:\\s*'([^']*)'`);
  const match = source.match(pattern);
```

The escaping here is correct — `name` is properly regex-escaped. However, the pattern `'Name'\\s*:\\s*'([^']*)'` only matches the **first** key-value pair format. If the name is used as a key with double quotes (e.g., `"Shorekeeper's Grace": '...'`), the pattern won't match. The writer explicitly handles this case (`addWeaponEntry` uses double quotes for names with apostrophes), so some entries WILL use double quotes.

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
for (const name of characterNames) {
  const escaped = name.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
  // Match both single and double-quoted keys
  const pattern = new RegExp(`['"]${escaped}['"]\\s*:\\s*['"]([^'"]*)['"]`);
  const match = source.match(pattern);
  if (!match) {
    missing.characters.push(name);
  } else if (!match[1] || match[1] === '' || match[1] === 'TBD') {
    missing.characters.push(name);
  }
}
```

### TD-13: `banner-images.js` `applyBannerImages` — race condition between `getBufferFn()` and `loadBufferFn()`

**File:** `agent/lib/banner-images.js` — Lines 138–170
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:** The function iterates over found images and for each one:
1. Gets the current buffer via `getBufferFn()`
2. Runs a regex match on it
3. Calls `loadBufferFn(buf.replace(...))` to update the buffer

If two iterations run (they're sequential, so no async race), but between step 1 and step 3, if `getBufferFn` returns a reference to a mutable string and `loadBufferFn` replaces it, the second iteration's `getBufferFn()` should return the updated buffer. Since strings are immutable in JavaScript, this is actually safe — each call to `getBufferFn()` returns the current string reference, and `loadBufferFn` sets a new string.

**However**, there's a real issue: the regex match captures text from `buf`, but `loadBufferFn` is called with `buf.replace(match[0], ...)`. If the previous iteration changed the buffer, the current `buf` is stale (it was captured at line 140 `const buf = getBufferFn()`). But since we call `getBufferFn()` at the top of each iteration, this is actually fine.

Wait — there's a subtler issue. The `await uploadToImgbb(sourceUrl, imgName)` on line 157 is async. Between `getBufferFn()` and the `loadBufferFn()` call, other code could theoretically modify the buffer. In practice, this function is likely the only modifier at this point in the pipeline, but it's a fragile assumption.

More importantly: the function checks `if (existingMatch[1].startsWith('http'))` to skip already-filled slots. But if two banner images share the same regex pattern structure (unlikely but possible), the first replacement could alter the buffer in a way that the second regex no longer matches.

This is a defensive concern. The corrected approach is to re-fetch the buffer after each upload:

**Corrected code (lines 138–170):**
```javascript
export async function applyBannerImages(foundImages, getBufferFn, loadBufferFn) {
  if (!Object.keys(foundImages).length) return false;
  if (!process.env.IMGBB_API_KEY) {
    log.warn('IMGBB_API_KEY not set — cannot upload banner images');
    return false;
  }

  let changed = false;

  for (const [slot, sourceUrl] of Object.entries(foundImages)) {
    // Always get FRESH buffer at the start of each iteration
    const buf = getBufferFn();

    // PROTECTION: Check if this slot already has a real URL
    if (slot.startsWith('character:') || slot.startsWith('weapon:')) {
      const itemName = slot.split(':')[1];
      const escapedName = itemName.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
      const existingMatch = buf.match(new RegExp(`name:\\s*['"]${escapedName}['"][\\s\\S]*?imageUrl:\\s*'([^']*)'`));
      if (existingMatch && existingMatch[1] && existingMatch[1].startsWith('http')) {
        log.dim(`Skipped ${slot} — already has image`);
        continue;
      }
    } else {
      if (isProtectedImageField(slot)) {
        const existingMatch = buf.match(new RegExp(`${slot}:\\s*'([^']*)'`));
        if (existingMatch && existingMatch[1] && existingMatch[1].startsWith('http')) {
          log.dim(`Skipped protected field ${slot} — already has custom image`);
          continue;
        }
      }
      const existingMatch = buf.match(new RegExp(`${slot}:\\s*'([^']*)'`));
      if (existingMatch && existingMatch[1] && existingMatch[1].startsWith('http')) {
        log.dim(`Skipped ${slot} — already has image`);
        continue;
      }
    }

    // Upload to imgbb (async operation)
    const imgName = slot.replace(/[:/]/g, '-').replace(/\s+/g, '-');
    const imgbbUrl = await uploadToImgbb(sourceUrl, imgName);
    if (!imgbbUrl) continue;

    // Re-fetch buffer AFTER async upload in case it changed
    const freshBuf = getBufferFn();

    if (slot.startsWith('character:') || slot.startsWith('weapon:')) {
      const itemName = slot.split(':')[1];
      const escapedName = itemName.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
      const pattern = new RegExp(`(name:\\s*['"]${escapedName}['"][\\s\\S]*?imageUrl:\\s*)'([^']*)'`);
      const match = freshBuf.match(pattern);
      if (match) {
        loadBufferFn(freshBuf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}'${imgbbUrl}'`));
        log.ok(`Banner image: ${slot} → ${imgbbUrl}`);
        addChange('banner-images', `Filled ${slot} image`);
        changed = true;
      }
    } else {
      const pattern = new RegExp(`(${slot}:\\s*)'([^']*)'`);
      const match = freshBuf.match(pattern);
      if (match) {
        loadBufferFn(freshBuf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}'${imgbbUrl}'`));
        log.ok(`Banner image: ${slot} → ${imgbbUrl}`);
        addChange('banner-images', `Filled ${slot}`);
        changed = true;
      }
    }
  }

  return changed;
}
```

### TD-15: `evolution.js` `analyzeFeedback` — division by zero when calculating success rate

**File:** `agent/lib/evolution.js` — Lines 122–128
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:**
```javascript
const strong = Object.entries(categories)
  .filter(([, v]) => v.success > 3 && v.success / (v.success + v.fail) > 0.8)
  .map(([k]) => k);
```

If both `v.success` and `v.fail` are `0` (which shouldn't happen given the loop above, but could if the memory data is corrupted), this produces `0 / 0 = NaN`, and `NaN > 0.8` is `false`, so no crash. But for the `toxic` calculation:

```javascript
const toxic = Object.entries(categories)
  .filter(([, v]) => v.fail > v.success && (v.fail + v.success) >= 2)
  .map(([k]) => k);
```

This is safe (no division). So this is actually a non-issue for division. However, the real bug is: categories can have `v.success = 0` and `v.fail = 0` but `v.reverted > 0`, meaning reverted patches don't count toward success or failure. This could mask toxic patterns.

**Corrected code:**
```javascript
// Identify toxic patterns — categories where >50% of patches fail OR have high revert rate
const toxic = Object.entries(categories)
  .filter(([, v]) => {
    const total = v.fail + v.success;
    if (total < 2) return false;
    return v.fail > v.success || (v.reverted > 0 && v.reverted / total > 0.3);
  })
  .map(([k]) => k);

// Identify strong patterns — categories where >80% succeed with enough samples
const strong = Object.entries(categories)
  .filter(([, v]) => {
    const total = v.success + v.fail;
    return total > 3 && v.success / total > 0.8 && v.reverted === 0;
  })
  .map(([k]) => k);
```

### TD-16: `writer.js` `addToList` — regex is greedy across multiple lists

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js` — Lines 210–226
**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Problem:**
```javascript
const pattern = new RegExp(`(const ${listName}\\s*=\\s*\\[[\\s\\S]*?)(\\];)`);
```

The `[\s\S]*?` is non-greedy but still matches across line boundaries. If there are two constants with similar patterns (e.g., `const ALL_5STAR_RESONATORS = [...]` and `const ALL_5STAR_WEAPONS = [...]`), and the `listName` matches the first one, the regex could capture content spanning into the second array if the `];` at the end of the first array isn't found before the second.

Actually, `*?` is non-greedy, so it matches the FIRST `];` it finds, which should be the closing of the target array. But if the array contains nested arrays (e.g., `['a', ['b', 'c']]`), the first `];` would be inside a nested array, causing premature match termination.

In practice, these lists are flat arrays of strings, so nested arrays are unlikely. But `];` at the end of a line inside a comment could also match.

**Corrected code (more precise regex):**
```javascript
export function addToList(listName, name) {
  // Escape list name for regex
  const escapedListName = listName.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
  
  // Match the array opening and content, ensuring we find the correct closing bracket
  const startPattern = new RegExp(`const ${escapedListName}\\s*=\\s*\\[`);
  const startMatch = buffer.match(startPattern);
  if (!startMatch) {
    log.warn(`Could not find list: ${listName}`);
    return false;
  }

  const startIdx = startMatch.index

### 1.2 — Frontend Agent CRIT-02 (Firebase Hardcoded Fallback): ⚠️ FIX IS INCOMPLETE

The Frontend Agent proposed setting `FIREBASE_DB` and `FIREBASE_API_KEY` to `null` and guarding with `FIREBASE_AVAILABLE`. The Reviewer Agent independently flagged this as ISSUE 2.1 and proposed a different fix (empty string + `console.error` in prod).

**Problem with both fixes:** Neither agent traced downstream usage. The `fetchWithTimeout` calls to Firebase URLs will produce a `TypeError: Failed to construct 'URL'` if `FIREBASE_DB` is `null`, because string concatenation like `` `${null}/leaderboard.json` `` produces `"null/leaderboard.json"` — a valid but wrong URL that will fire a real network request to a nonexistent host, not a clean failure.

**Correct combined fix:**

```javascript
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — replace Firebase config block
const FIREBASE_DB = import.meta.env.VITE_FIREBASE_DB || '';
const FIREBASE_API_KEY = import.meta.env.VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY || '';
const FIREBASE_AVAILABLE = !!(FIREBASE_DB && FIREBASE_API_KEY);

if (!FIREBASE_AVAILABLE && import.meta.env.PROD) {
  console.error('[WW] CRITICAL: Firebase env vars missing in production. Online features disabled.');
}

// Guard function — use before EVERY Firebase call
const guardFirebase = (operation) => {
  if (!FIREBASE_AVAILABLE) {
    // Return a resolved promise with null to prevent callers from crashing
    return Promise.resolve(null);
  }
  return operation();
};
```

## 2. CRITICAL Issues — New Findings

### CRIT-01: `applyMetaUpdates` — Stale Buffer Bug (Read-After-Write Inconsistency)

**File:** `agent/lib/meta-refresh.js` — Lines 96–131 (`applyMetaUpdates`)

**Problem:** The function calls `getBufferFn()` inside the loop for **each** update, performs a `buf.replace()`, then calls `loadBufferFn(newBuf)` to save it. But on the **next iteration**, it calls `getBufferFn()` again — which may or may not reflect the changes from the previous iteration, depending on how `getBufferFn`/`loadBufferFn` are implemented.

If `getBufferFn` returns a cached snapshot (which is common), then the second `loadBufferFn` call will **overwrite** the first update's changes with a version that doesn't include them.

This is a classic read-modify-write race within a synchronous loop. Three updates to the same character (e.g., changing weapon, echoes, AND teams) will result in only the **last** update being applied.

**Trace:**
```
Iteration 1: buf = getBufferFn()  →  buf has original
              buf.replace(weapon) → new buf with weapon change
              loadBufferFn(new buf)  → saved

Iteration 2: buf = getBufferFn()  →  IF this returns original (cached), weapon change is LOST
              buf.replace(echoes) → new buf with echoes change only
              loadBufferFn(new buf)  → overwrites weapon change
```

**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/meta-refresh.js — replace entire applyMetaUpdates function
export function applyMetaUpdates(updates, getBufferFn, loadBufferFn, memory, minConfidence = 0.85) {
  let changed = false;
  let buf = getBufferFn(); // Read buffer ONCE at the start

  for (const update of updates) {
    if (update.confidence < minConfidence) continue;

    const escaped = update.name.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');

    if (update.field === 'bestWeapon' && typeof update.newValue === 'string') {
      const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?bestWeapon:\\s*)'[^']*'`);
      const match = buf.match(pattern);
      if (match) {
        buf = buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}'${update.newValue}'`);
        addChange('meta-refresh', `${update.name} bestWeapon → ${update.newValue}`);
        changed = true;
      }
    }

    if (update.field === 'bestEchoes' && Array.isArray(update.newValue)) {
      const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?bestEchoes:\\s*)\\[[^\\]]*\\]`);
      const match = buf.match(pattern);
      if (match) {
        const newArr = `[${update.newValue.map(e => `'${e}'`).join(', ')}]`;
        buf = buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}${newArr}`);
        addChange('meta-refresh', `${update.name} bestEchoes updated`);
        changed = true;
      }
    }

    if (update.field === 'teams' && Array.isArray(update.newValue)) {
      const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?teams:\\s*)\\[[^\\]]*\\]`);
      const match = buf.match(pattern);
      if (match) {
        const newArr = `[${update.newValue.map(t => `'${t}'`).join(', ')}]`;
        buf = buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}${newArr}`);
        addChange('meta-refresh', `${update.name} teams updated`);
        changed = true;
      }
    }
  }

  // Write buffer ONCE at the end, with ALL accumulated changes
  if (changed) loadBufferFn(buf);
  return changed;
}
```

### CRIT-02: `applyStandardPoolUpdates` — Regex Matches First Occurrence, Not Target Array

**File:** `agent/lib/standard-pool.js` — Lines 74–78

**Problem:** The regex `/(standardCharacters:\s*\[[\s\S]*?)(])/)` uses a **non-greedy** `[\s\S]*?` quantifier, which means it matches the **shortest** possible content between `[` and `]`. But `standardCharacters` may contain nested brackets in comments or adjacent code. More critically, this regex is **not anchored to `CURRENT_BANNERS`** — it matches the FIRST occurrence of `standardCharacters:` anywhere in the file.

If there's a comment or other object with a `standardCharacters` key before `CURRENT_BANNERS`, the replacement would corrupt the wrong location.

Same issue on line ~86 with `standardWeapons:` — the regex `/(standardWeapons:\s*\[[\s\S]*?)(],)/` will match the first `standardWeapons:` in the file.

**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/standard-pool.js — in applyStandardPoolUpdates, replace standardCharacters handling

// Add to CURRENT_BANNERS.standardCharacters — scope regex to CURRENT_BANNERS block
const bannersBlock = buf.match(/CURRENT_BANNERS\s*=\s*\{[\s\S]*?\n\};/);
if (bannersBlock) {
  const bannersContent = bannersBlock[0];
  const stdCharsMatch = bannersContent.match(/(standardCharacters:\s*\[[\s\S]*?)(])/);
  if (stdCharsMatch && !stdCharsMatch[1].includes(`'${name}'`)) {
    const updatedBanners = bannersContent.replace(
      stdCharsMatch[0],
      `${stdCharsMatch[1]}, '${name}'${stdCharsMatch[2]}`
    );
    buf = buf.replace(bannersContent, updatedBanners);
    changed = true;
  }
}
```

Apply the same scoping pattern for `standardWeapons`.

### CRIT-03: `addNewEvent` — Broken Regex Fallback Logic for Finding EVENTS Object End

**File:** `agent/lib/new-events.js` — Lines 80–93

**Problem:** The function first tries to match the end of the EVENTS object:
```javascript
const eventsEnd = buf.match(/(\n\};\s*\n\s*\/\/ \[SECTION:)/);
```
If that fails, it tries an alternative:
```javascript
const alt = buf.match(/(\n\};\s*\n\s*const (?:BANNER_HISTORY|SUBSCRIPTION))/);
```
If that also fails, it returns false. But then on line 91:
```javascript
const marker = (eventsEnd || buf.match(/(\n\};\s*\n\s*const (?:BANNER_HISTORY|SUBSCRIPTION))/))?.[0];
```
This **re-runs the alt regex** even though `alt` already captured the result. More critically, if `eventsEnd` was null but `alt` matched, the code enters the block that logs a warning and returns `false` on line 87, so line 91 is **unreachable in that case**.

Let's trace:
1. `eventsEnd = null` → enters `if (!eventsEnd)` block
2. `alt = null` → enters inner `if (!alt)` → returns false ✅
3. `alt = [match]` → does **NOT** enter inner `if (!alt)`, falls through
4. Code reaches line 91: `const marker = (eventsEnd || ...)?.[0]` — but `eventsEnd` is null, so it re-evaluates the alt regex. This works but is redundant and confusing.

**The real bug:** If `eventsEnd` fails but `alt` succeeds, the code should use `alt` but never stores it in a way that line 91 can see it. The code technically works by re-running the regex, but if the regex is expensive or the code is refactored, this pattern will break.

**More importantly:** The replacement `buf.replace(marker, entry + marker)` inserts the new event entry **before** the closing `};` of the EVENTS object. But the `entry` string starts with `  ${eventData.key}: {` and ends with `},\n`. If the EVENTS object's last existing entry already has a trailing comma, this works. If it doesn't, the result is **invalid JavaScript** with a missing comma between the last existing entry and the new one.

**Severity:** CRITICAL

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/new-events.js — replace addNewEvent function
export function addNewEvent(eventData, getBufferFn, loadBufferFn) {
  const buf = getBufferFn();

  // Find the closing of the EVENTS object — try multiple patterns
  const patterns = [
    /(\n\};\s*\n\s*\/\/ \[SECTION:)/,
    /(\n\};\s*\n\s*const (?:BANNER_HISTORY|SUBSCRIPTION))/,
    /(\n\};\s*\n\s*const )/,  // Generic fallback
  ];

  let marker = null;
  for (const pattern of patterns) {
    const match = buf.match(pattern);
    if (match) {
      marker = match[0];
      break;
    }
  }

  if (!marker) {
    log.warn(`Could not find EVENTS object end marker for new event "${eventData.name}"`);
    return false;
  }

  // Ensure the last existing event entry has a trailing comma
  // Find the content just before the marker and check for trailing comma
  const markerIndex = buf.indexOf(marker);
  const beforeMarker = buf.slice(Math.max(0, markerIndex - 200), markerIndex);
  const lastNonWhitespace = beforeMarker.trimEnd();
  const needsComma = lastNonWhitespace.length > 0 &&
    !lastNonWhitespace.endsWith(',') &&
    !lastNonWhitespace.endsWith('{');

  const currentEnd = eventData.currentEnd ? `'${eventData.currentEnd}'` : 'null';
  const escapedName = (eventData.name || '').replace(/'/g, "\\'");
  const escapedSubtitle = (eventData.subtitle || '').replace(/'/g, "\\'");
  const escapedDescription = (eventData.description || '').replace(/'/g, "\\'");
  const escapedRewards = (eventData.rewards || '').replace(/'/g, "\\'");

  const commaPrefix = needsComma ? ',' : '';
  const entry = `${commaPrefix}\n  ${eventData.key}: {
    name: '${escapedName}', subtitle: '${escapedSubtitle}',
    description: '${escapedDescription}',
    resetType: '${eventData.resetType}', color: '${eventData.color || 'cyan'}',
    currentEnd: ${currentEnd},
    rewards: '${escapedRewards}',
    imageUrl: '' },\n`;

  const newBuf = buf.replace(marker, entry + marker);
  loadBufferFn(newBuf);

  log.ok(`Added new event: ${eventData.key} ("${eventData.name}")`);
  addChange('new-event', `New event: ${eventData.name}`);
  return true;
}
```

### CRIT-04: `validate.js` Syntax Checker Doesn't Handle Template Literals Correctly

**File:** `agent/lib/validate.js` — Lines 109–135 (`checkSyntax`)

**Problem:** The brace/bracket counter treats backtick `` ` `` as a string delimiter (line 123: `if (c === "'" || c === '"' || c === '\`')`), but template literals in JavaScript can contain **nested expressions** with `${}` which themselves contain braces. The counter enters "in string" mode when it hits a backtick, then ignores all characters (including `{` and `}`) until the next backtick. But `${...}` inside template literals should have their braces counted.

Example:
```javascript
const x = `hello ${obj.name} world`;
```
The parser sees the opening backtick, enters string mode, skips `{`, skips `}`, exits at closing backtick. This is **correct by accident** — the braces inside `${}` are balanced.

But:
```javascript
const x = `value: ${a > b ? '{' : '}'}`;
```
Here the braces inside `${}` are unbalanced (one `{` and one `}`), and the parser would skip them because it's "in a string". This is **also correct by accident** because those are string literals inside the template expression.

**The actual bug:** Template literals can contain **unescaped backticks** inside `${}` expressions:
```javascript
const x = `outer ${`inner`} outer`;
```
The parser will see the first backtick, enter string mode, hit the second backtick (inside `${}`), EXIT string mode prematurely, then count the remaining `}` as an unmatched brace, and then be completely off for the rest of the file.

In `appcore-data.js` (a 1,450-line data file), template literals with nested expressions are unlikely but possible in computed descriptions or URLs.

**More critically:** The parser doesn't handle escaped quotes inside strings:
```javascript
const s = 'it\'s a test';
```
Line 121: `if (c === stringChar && prev !== '\\')` — this checks `prev !== '\\'`. But `prev` is `source[i-1]`, which is the **actual character** at that position. If the source contains `\'`, then `source[i-1]` is `\` (one backslash), and the check `prev !== '\\'` compares a single backslash character against... a single backslash character (the string `'\\'` in JavaScript source code represents a single backslash). So this check **is actually correct**.

Wait — let me re-verify. In JavaScript source code:
- `'\\'` is a string containing one backslash character.
- If `source[i-1]` is a backslash character, then `prev !== '\\\\'` would be wrong, but `prev !== '\\'` is correct.

✅ The escape handling is correct. The template literal nesting is the real issue.

**However**, for `appcore-data.js` which is primarily simple data declarations with single-quoted strings, this is unlikely to trigger in practice. Downgrading to HIGH, but keeping the fix since the validator is supposed to be a safety net.

**Severity:** HIGH (theoretical in current data, but the validator must be reliable)

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/validate.js — replace checkSyntax function
function checkSyntax(source, errors) {
  let braces = 0, brackets = 0, parens = 0;
  let inString = false;
  let stringChar = '';
  let templateDepth = 0; // Track nested template literal depth

  for (let i = 0; i < source.length; i++) {
    const c = source[i];
    const prev = i > 0 ? source[i - 1] : '';

    // Handle template literal expression nesting
    if (!inString && c === '`') {
      inString = true;
      stringChar = '`';
      templateDepth++;
      continue;
    }

    if (inString && stringChar === '`') {
      // Check for ${...} expression start
      if (c === '$' && i + 1 < source.length && source[i + 1] === '{') {
        // Entering template expression — stop string mode, count the brace
        inString = false;
        i++; // skip the '{'
        braces++;
        continue;
      }
      // Check for closing backtick
      if (c === '`' && prev !== '\\') {
        inString = false;
        templateDepth--;
        continue;
      }
      continue; // Skip content inside template literal
    }

    // Handle regular strings
    if (inString) {
      if (c === stringChar && prev !== '\\') inString = false;
      continue;
    }

    // Check if we're exiting a template expression (back into template literal)
    if (c === '}' && templateDepth > 0 && braces > 0) {
      // This closing brace might end a template expression
      // We can't perfectly distinguish, so count it normally
      braces--;
      // If braces go back to the level before the template expression,
      // re-enter string mode
      // This is an approximation — perfect parsing requires a full parser
      continue;
    }

    if (c === "'" || c === '"') {
      inString = true;
      stringChar = c;
    } else if (c === '{') braces++;
    else if (c === '}') braces--;
    else if (c === '[') brackets++;
    else if (c === ']') brackets--;
    else if (c === '(') parens++;
    else if (c === ')') parens--;
  }

  if (braces !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced braces: ${braces > 0 ? braces + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(braces) + ' extra closing'}`);
  if (brackets !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced brackets: ${brackets > 0 ? brackets + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(brackets) + ' extra closing'}`);
  if (parens !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced parentheses: ${parens > 0 ? parens + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(parens) + ' extra closing'}`);
}
```

## 3. HIGH Issues — New Findings

### HIGH-01: `extractEvents` Regex Fails on Multi-Line Event Blocks

**File:** `agent/lib/reader.js` — Lines 108–126 (`extractEvents`)

**Problem:** The regex to match individual events within the EVENTS block:
```javascript
const eventMatches = content.matchAll(/(\w+):\s*\{([\s\S]*?)(?=\n\s*\w+:|$)/g);
```

The lookahead `(?=\n\s*\w+:|$)` expects the next event to start with `\n\s*\w+:`. But if an event's value contains a **nested object** (which `[\s\S]*?` would greedily match into), the regex will terminate at the wrong position.

More critically, consider the event value containing a colon in a string:
```javascript
rewards: 'Tackle the Tower: 800 Astrite',
```
The `(?=\n\s*\w+:)` lookahead will match at `Astrite',\n  nextEvent:`, BUT `[\s\S]*?` is **non-greedy**, so it will actually terminate at the FIRST `\n\s*\w+:` pattern, which could be inside a string value like `resetType: 'Version update'` — where `update` on a new line would look like a new key.

Let me trace more carefully. The pattern `\n\s*\w+:` would match things like:
```
    name: 'Tower of Adversity',
```
Here, `name:` on a newline matches `\w+:`. So the non-greedy `[\s\S]*?` for the first event would stop at the FIRST nested property like `name:` inside the same event, not at the next event key.

**This means every event extracted will only contain the content up to its first property, not the full event block.**

**Severity:** HIGH

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/reader.js — replace extractEvents function
export function extractEvents(source) {
  const events = {};
  const eventsBlock = source.match(/const EVENTS\s*=\s*\{([\s\S]*?)\n\};\s*\n/);
  if (!eventsBlock) return events;

  const content = eventsBlock[1];

  // Match event entries by finding top-level keys followed by { ... }
  // We need to count braces to find the correct closing brace for each event
  const keyPattern = /\n\s+(\w+):\s*\{/g;
  let keyMatch;
  const keyPositions = [];

  while ((keyMatch = keyPattern.exec(content)) !== null) {
    keyPositions.push({
      key: keyMatch[1],
      braceStart: keyMatch.index + keyMatch[0].length - 1, // position of opening {
    });
  }

  for (const { key, braceStart } of keyPositions) {
    // Find matching closing brace by counting
    let depth = 1;
    let pos = braceStart + 1;
    while (pos < content.length && depth > 0) {
      const ch = content[pos];
      if (ch === '{') depth++;
      else if (ch === '}') depth--;
      // Skip string contents
      if (ch === "'" || ch === '"') {
        pos++;
        while (pos < content.length && content[pos] !== ch) {
          if (content[pos] === '\\') pos++; // skip escaped char
          pos++;
        }
      }
      pos++;
    }

    const body = content.slice(braceStart + 1, pos - 1);
    const name = body.match(/name:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
    const currentEnd = body.match(/currentEnd:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || null;
    const resetType = body.match(/resetType:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
    const rewards = body.match(/rewards:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
    events[key] = { name, currentEnd, resetType, rewards };
  }

  return events;
}
```

### HIGH-02: `withCheckpoint` Returns Boolean Wrapper Instead of Actual Result

**File:** `agent/lib/checkpoint.js` — Lines 115–133 (`withCheckpoint`)

**Problem:** The function is supposed to wrap an async step and return its result. When the step is run live, it works. But when the result is restored from cache, it returns the **cacheable** object, not the original result:

```javascript
const cacheable = typeof result === 'boolean' || typeof result === 'number' || result === null
  ? result
  : { completed: true, hadChanges: !!result };

saveCheckpoint(mode, stepName, cacheable);
return result;  // ← First run returns the real result
```

On resume:
```javascript
const cached = getCheckpoint(mode, stepName);
if (cached !== null) {
  return cached;  // ← Returns { completed: true, hadChanges: true } instead of the real result!
}
```

If the caller of `withCheckpoint` expects the actual step result (e.g., an array of fetched sources, or a parsed analysis object), it will receive `{ completed: true, hadChanges: true }` on resume — a completely wrong type.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/checkpoint.js — replace withCheckpoint function
/**
 * Wrap a step function with checkpoint support.
 * 
 * IMPORTANT: This only caches a completion marker, NOT the actual result.
 * On resume, it returns a sentinel value. Callers MUST handle this.
 * 
 * @param {string} mode — 'full' | 'micro'
 * @param {string} stepName — unique step identifier
 * @param {Function} stepFn — async function to run
 * @param {*} [defaultOnResume=null] — value to return when resuming from checkpoint
 *   Callers should pass a safe default that won't crash downstream code.
 * @returns {*} — the step result (from live execution) or defaultOnResume (from cache)
 */
export async function withCheckpoint(mode, stepName, stepFn, defaultOnResume = null) {
  // Check if already completed
  const cached = getCheckpoint(mode, stepName);
  if (cached !== null) {
    log.dim(`  Step "${stepName}" already completed — using default resume value`);
    return defaultOnResume;
  }

  // Run the step
  const result = await stepFn();

  // Save checkpoint marker (we intentionally don't cache the full result)
  saveCheckpoint(mode, stepName, { completed: true, timestamp: Date.now() });
  return result;
}
```

### HIGH-03: `crossVerify` — Normalization Destroys Date Comparison Accuracy

**File:** `agent/lib/cross-verify.js` — Lines 120–126 (`normalize`)

**Problem:** The `normalize` function lowercases all strings:
```javascript
function normalize(value) {
  if (typeof value === 'string') return value.trim().toLowerCase();
```

This is used to compare extracted values across sources. But for **date strings**, lowercasing is correct (ISO dates are all digits). For **character names** and **element names**, lowercasing destroys the ability to detect case mismatches. More importantly, for dates like `2025-06-30T10:00:00Z` vs `2025-06-30T10:00:00.000Z`, the normalization doesn't account for equivalent date representations.

Two sources reporting:
- Source A: `"June 30, 2025"` → normalized to `"june 30, 2025"`
- Source B: `"2025-06-30"` → normalized to `"2025-06-30"`

These are the same date but will count as disagreeing values, reducing confidence.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/cross-verify.js — replace normalize function
function normalize(value) {
  if (typeof value === 'string') {
    const trimmed = value.trim();

    // Try to normalize as a date — if parseable, use ISO date string
    const dateAttempt = new Date(trimmed);
    if (!isNaN(dateAttempt.getTime()) && trimmed.length >= 8) {
      // Only normalize if it looks like a date (contains digits and date separators)
      if (/\d{4}[-/]?\d{2}[-/]?\d{2}/.test(trimmed) || /\w+ \d{1,2},? \d{4}/.test(trimmed)) {
        return dateAttempt.toISOString();
      }
    }

    return trimmed.toLowerCase();
  }
  if (typeof value === 'number') return String(value);
  if (Array.isArray(value)) return value.map(normalize).sort().join(','); // Sort arrays for order-independent comparison
  if (value && typeof value === 'object') return JSON.stringify(value);
  return String(value);
}
```

### HIGH-04: `scraper.js` — `fetchPage` Returns `undefined` if All Retries Fail Without Throwing

**File:** `agent/lib/scraper.js` — Lines 24–60

**Problem:** The `for` loop runs from `attempt = 0` to `attempt <= retries` (inclusive). If `attempt === retries` and the fetch fails, the catch block calls `log.error(...)` and `return { ok: false, ... }` — this is correct.

But there's a subtle path: if `retries = 0` (which callers could pass), then `attempt <= retries` means the loop body runs once with `attempt = 0`. Inside the catch:
```javascript
if (attempt === retries) {  // 0 === 0 → true
  log.error(`Failed to fetch ${url}: ${err.message}`);
  return { ok: false, content: '', url, error: err.message };
}
```
This works correctly.

**HOWEVER**, the function has no `return` statement after the `for` loop. If the loop somehow completes without returning (which shouldn't happen given the logic, but defensive coding requires it), the function returns `undefined`. Callers in `fetchAll` do:
```javascript
results[index] = await fetchPage(url);
```
An `undefined` result would cause downstream `.ok` checks to throw `TypeError: Cannot read property 'ok' of undefined`.

**Severity:** HIGH (defensive — current code is likely safe but the missing return is a maintenance trap)

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/scraper.js — add after the for loop closing brace (after line ~60)
export async function fetchPage(url, { retries = 2, extractText = true } = {}) {
  const domain = getDomain(url);

  // Politeness delay per domain
  const lastFetch = lastFetchByDomain.get(domain) || 0;
  const elapsed = Date.now() - lastFetch;
  if (elapsed < SCRAPER.politenessDelay) {
    await sleep(SCRAPER.politenessDelay - elapsed);
  }

  for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= retries; attempt++) {
    try {
      log.dim(`Fetching ${url}${attempt > 0 ? ` (retry ${attempt})` : ''}`);
      lastFetchByDomain.set(domain, Date.now());

      const controller = new AbortController();
      const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), SCRAPER.timeout);

      const res = await fetch(url, {
        headers: {
          'User-Agent': SCRAPER.userAgent,
          'Accept': 'text/html,application/xhtml+xml,application/xml;q=0.9,*/*;q=0.8',
          'Accept-Language': 'en-US,en;q=0.5',
        },
        signal: controller.signal,
      });

      clearTimeout(timeout);

      if (!res.ok) {
        throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status} ${res.statusText}`);
      }

      let content = await res.text();

      if (extractText) {
        content = htmlToText(content);
      }

      if (content.length > SCRAPER.maxContentLength) {
        content = content.slice(0, SCRAPER.maxContentLength) + '\n\n[...TRUNCATED...]';
      }

      log.ok(`Fetched ${domain} — ${(content.length / 1024).toFixed(1)}KB`);
      return { ok: true, content, url };

    } catch (err) {
      if (attempt === retries) {
        log.error(`Failed to fetch ${url}: ${err.message}`);
        return { ok: false, content: '', url, error: err.message };
      }
      log.warn(`Fetch attempt ${attempt + 1} failed for ${domain}: ${err.message}`);
      await sleep(2000 * (attempt + 1));
    }
  }

  // Defensive fallback — should never be reached, but prevents undefined return
  return { ok: false, content: '', url, error: 'Exhausted retries without result' };
}
```

### HIGH-05: `meta-refresh.js` — Regex Injection via Character Names Containing Special Characters

**File:** `agent/lib/meta-refresh.js` — Lines 52–57

**Problem:** While character names are escaped for regex special chars, the `bestEchoesMatch` and `teamsMatch` parsing uses:
```javascript
const bestEchoesMatch = match[1].match(/bestEchoes:\s*\[([^\]]+)\]/)?.[1] || '';
```

The `[^\]]+` pattern matches everything except `]`. But if an echo set name contains a `]` character (unlikely for game data, but possible in user-modified data), this regex would break. More practically, if the array is empty `[]`, the regex `[^\]]+` requires at least one character, so `match` returns null and the fallback `|| ''` kicks in — which is fine.

**The real issue** is in `refreshCharacterBuilds`: the character name escape only protects against regex special chars in the name used for the outer pattern. But the **inner** `match[1]` content (the body of the character's data block) is extracted and then searched for `bestWeapon`, `bestEchoes`, and `teams`. The regex `'${escaped}':\\s*\\{([\\s\\S]*?)\\n\\s*\\}` matches the FIRST `}` after a newline-whitespace, but character data blocks contain NESTED objects (like `ascension: { boss: '...', common: '...' }`). The non-greedy `[\\s\\S]*?` combined with `\\n\\s*\\}` will match the first closing brace of a nested object, not the character's closing brace.

This means `match[1]` will only contain the content up to the first nested object's closing `}`, cutting off `bestEchoes`, `teams`, and other fields that come after the first nested object.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/meta-refresh.js — replace the currentData extraction in refreshCharacterBuilds
const currentData = {};
for (const name of characters) {
  const escaped = name.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
  // Use brace counting instead of non-greedy regex to find the full character block
  const startPattern = new RegExp(`'${escaped}':\\s*\\{`);
  const startMatch = startPattern.exec(currentSource);
  if (!startMatch) continue;

  // Find the matching closing brace by counting
  const blockStart = startMatch.index + startMatch[0].length;
  let depth = 1;
  let pos = blockStart;
  while (pos < currentSource.length && depth > 0) {
    const ch = currentSource[pos];
    if (ch === '{') depth++;
    else if (ch === '}') depth--;
    // Skip string contents
    if (ch === "'" || ch === '"') {
      const quote = ch;
      pos++;
      while (pos < currentSource.length && currentSource[pos] !== quote) {
        if (currentSource[pos] === '\\') pos++; // skip escaped
        pos++;
      }
    }
    pos++;
  }

  const body = currentSource.slice(blockStart, pos - 1);
  const bestWeapon = body.match(/bestWeapon:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
  const bestEchoesStr = body.match(/bestEchoes:\s*\[([^\]]*)\]/)?.[1] || '';
  const bestEchoes = [...bestEchoesStr.matchAll(/'([^']+)'/g)].map(m => m[1]);
  const teamsStr = body.match(/teams:\s*\[([^\]]*)\]/)?.[1] || '';
  const teams = [...teamsStr.matchAll(/'([^']+)'/g)].map(m => m[1]);
  currentData[name] = { bestWeapon, bestEchoes, teams };
}
```

### HIGH-06: `changelog.js` — `appendToChangelog` Section Insertion Can Corrupt File

**File:** `agent/lib/changelog.js` — Lines 70–87

**Problem:** When appending to an existing date+version section, the code finds `sectionEnd`:
```javascript
const sectionEnd = existing.indexOf('\n## ', existing.indexOf(`## v${appVersion} — ${date}`) + 1);
```
Then it inserts new lines at `insertPoint`. But it inserts with `'\n' + newLines` — if `insertPoint` is at the end of the file (because there's no next section), this appends after the last character. If the file ends with `\n`, the result is `\n\n- **Category**: description` which is correct. But if the file doesn't end with `\n`, the result is `...last line- **Category**: description` — no separator.

More critically, when creating a new section, the code finds `headerEnd`:
```javascript
const headerEnd = existing.indexOf('\n\n', existing.indexOf('# '));
```
If the file contains `# Whispering Wishes — Changelog\n\nAuto-generated...`, `headerEnd` finds the `\n\n` after the title. The new section is inserted at `headerEnd + 2`, which is right after the double newline — this puts it BETWEEN the title and the description paragraph, not after it.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/changelog.js — replace appendToChangelog function
export function appendToChangelog(entries, appVersion) {
  if (!entries.length) return;

  const date = entries[0].date;

  let existing = '';
  if (existsSync(CHANGELOG_FILE)) {
    existing = readFileSync(CHANGELOG_FILE, 'utf-8');
  } else {
    existing = `# Whispering Wishes — Changelog\n\nAuto-generated by the WW Update Agent.\n\n`;
  }

  const sectionHeader = `## v${appVersion} — ${date}`;

  if (existing.includes(sectionHeader)) {
    // Append to existing section — find the end of this section
    const sectionStart = existing.indexOf(sectionHeader);
    const sectionEnd = existing.indexOf('\n## ', sectionStart + sectionHeader.length);
    const insertPoint = sectionEnd === -1 ? existing.length : sectionEnd;

    const newLines = entries.map(e => `- **${e.category}**: ${e.description}`).join('\n');
    // Ensure proper spacing
    const prefix = existing[insertPoint - 1] === '\n' ? '' : '\n';
    existing = existing.slice(0, insertPoint) + prefix + newLines + '\n' + existing.slice(insertPoint);
  } else {
    // New section — insert after the preamble (after "Auto-generated..." paragraph)
    const section = `${sectionHeader}\n\n${entries.map(e => `- **${e.category}**: ${e.description}`).join('\n')}\n\n`;

    // Find first existing ## section to insert before it, or append at end
    const firstSection = existing.indexOf('\n## ');
    if (firstSection !== -1) {
      // Insert before the first existing section
      existing = existing.slice(0, firstSection + 1) + section + existing.slice(firstSection + 1);
    } else {
      // No existing sections — append after preamble
      existing = existing.trimEnd() + '\n\n' + section;
    }
  }

  writeFileSync(CHANGELOG_FILE, existing);
  log.ok(`Changelog updated: ${entries.length} entries`);
}
```

### HIGH-07: `changelog.js` — `updateAppChangelog` Parses Entries Incorrectly

**File:** `agent/lib/changelog.js` — Lines 102–122

**Problem:** When trimming to `MAX_APP_ENTRIES`, the code splits by newlines and filters lines starting with `{`:
```javascript
const trimmed = allEntries.split('\n')
  .filter(l => l.trim().startsWith('{'))
  .slice(0, MAX_APP_ENTRIES)
  .join(',\n');
```

But the entries look like:
```javascript
  { date: '2025-06-30', category: 'Banners', text: 'Updated banner dates' }
```
The `l.trim().startsWith('{')` check works. BUT the entries already have commas between them (from the `join(',\n')`), and the filter drops the comma lines, then re-joins with `,\n`. This causes **double commas** in some cases, or entries that already have trailing commas getting another one.

Also, if `existingEntries` is empty (first run), the line:
```javascript
const allEntries = newEntries.join(',\n') + (existingEntries ? ',\n' + existingEntries : '');
```
correctly skips adding the existing. But `existingEntries` is `existingMatch[1].trim()` — if the existing CHANGELOG array is `[\n]` (empty with whitespace), `trim()` returns `''`, and the falsy check works. Fine.

The real problem is that `existingEntries` might contain entries with **multi-line formatting** (e.g., if someone manually edited the changelog). The `split('\n').filter(l => l.trim().startsWith('{'))` would then lose the closing `}` of entries that span multiple lines.

**Severity:** HIGH (data corruption in changelog array)

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/changelog.js — replace the trimming logic in updateAppChangelog
export function updateAppChangelog(entries, getBufferFn, loadBufferFn) {
  if (!entries.length) return false;

  let buf = getBufferFn();

  // Build new entries as single-line strings
  const newEntries = entries.map(e =>
    `  { date: '${e.date}', category: '${e.category}', text: '${e.description.replace(/'/g, "\\'")}' }`
  );

  const existingMatch = buf.match(/const CHANGELOG\s*=\s*\[([\s\S]*?)\];\s*\n/);

  if (existingMatch) {
    // Parse existing entries properly — match individual { ... } blocks
    const existingContent = existingMatch[1];
    const existingEntryPattern = /\{[^}]+\}/g;
    const existingEntryStrings = [];
    let entryMatch;
    while ((entryMatch = existingEntryPattern.exec(existingContent)) !== null) {
      existingEntryStrings.push('  ' + entryMatch[0].trim());
    }

    // Combine: new entries first, then existing, trimmed to max
    const allEntries = [...newEntries, ...existingEntryStrings].slice(0, MAX_APP_ENTRIES);

    const newBlock = `const CHANGELOG = [\n${allEntries.join(',\n')}\n];\n`;
    buf = buf.replace(existingMatch[0], newBlock);
  } else {
    // Create CHANGELOG constant before the exports
    const exportMarker = '\n// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n// EXPORTS';

    if (buf.includes(exportMarker)) {
      const changelogBlock = `\n// [SECTION:CHANGELOG]\nconst CHANGELOG = [\n${newEntries.join(',\n')}\n];\n`;
      buf = buf.replace(exportMarker, changelogBlock + exportMarker);

      const exportMatch = buf.match(/(export \{[\s\S]*?)(};)/);
      if (exportMatch && !exportMatch[1].includes('CHANGELOG')) {
        buf = buf.replace(exportMatch[0], `${exportMatch[1]}  CHANGELOG,\n${exportMatch[2]}`);
      }
    }
  }

  loadBufferFn(buf);
  return true;
}
```

### HIGH-08: `adaptive-schedule.js` — `calculateUrgency` Overlapping Conditions Create Dead Code

**File:** `agent/lib/adaptive-schedule.js` — Lines 31–49

**Problem:** The conditions overlap and create unreachable branches:
```javascript
// Critical: within 6 hours of banner end (transition imminent)
if (hoursLeft <= 6 && hoursLeft > -6) {
  return { urgency: 'critical', hoursLeft, shouldRun: true };
}

// Elevated: within 48 hours of banner end
if (hoursLeft <= 48 && hoursLeft > 0) {
  return { urgency: 'elevated', hoursLeft, shouldRun: true };
}

// Post-transition: banner just ended (within last 6 hours)
if (hoursLeft <= 0 && hoursLeft > -6) {
  return { urgency: 'critical', hoursLeft, shouldRun: true };
}
```

The third condition (`hoursLeft <= 0 && hoursLeft > -6`) is **completely covered** by the first condition (`hoursLeft <= 6 && hoursLeft > -6`). If `hoursLeft` is between -6 and 0, it matches condition 1 first and returns. Condition 3 is **dead code**.

Also, the second condition checks `hoursLeft > 0` but condition 1 already handles `hoursLeft <= 6`, so condition 2 only fires when `6 < hoursLeft <= 48`. This is logically correct but confusing.

**Severity:** HIGH (dead code in scheduling logic — the intent may differ from the implementation)

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/adaptive-schedule.js — replace calculateUrgency
export function calculateUrgency(bannerEndDate, memory) {
  const endTime = new Date(bannerEndDate).getTime();
  
  // Guard against invalid dates
  if (isNaN(endTime)) {
    log.warn(`Invalid banner end date: ${bannerEndDate}`);
    return { urgency: 'normal', hoursLeft: Infinity, shouldRun: true };
  }
  
  const hoursLeft = (endTime - Date.now()) / 3600000;

  // Post-transition: banner ended within the last 6 hours
  // New banner data should be available — critical to catch it
  if (hoursLeft <= 0 && hoursLeft > -6) {
    return { urgency: 'critical', hoursLeft, shouldRun: true };
  }

  // Pre-transition: within 6 hours of banner end
  if (hoursLeft > 0 && hoursLeft <= 6) {
    return { urgency: 'critical', hoursLeft, shouldRun: true };
  }

  // Elevated: within 48 hours of banner end
  if (hoursLeft > 6 && hoursLeft <= 48) {
    return { urgency: 'elevated', hoursLeft, shouldRun: true };
  }

  // Banner ended more than 6 hours ago — stale, but should still run
  if (hoursLeft <= -6) {
    return { urgency: 'normal', hoursLeft, shouldRun: true };
  }

  // Normal: more than 48 hours until banner end
  return { urgency: 'normal', hoursLeft, shouldRun: true };
}
```

## 4. MEDIUM Issues — New Findings

### MED-01: `memory.js` — `wasRecentlyPatched` Uses Strict Equality on Description Strings

**File:** `agent/lib/memory.js` — Line 74

**Problem:**
```javascript
return memory.patches.some(p =>
  p.description === description && new Date(p.timestamp).getTime() > cutoff
);
```

This uses **strict string equality**. If the description differs by a single character (e.g., trailing whitespace, different Unicode normalization, or an extra period), the match fails and the agent will re-apply a patch it already applied. This is a fragile deduplication mechanism.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/memory.js — replace wasRecentlyPatched
export function wasRecentlyPatched(memory, description) {
  const cutoff = Date.now() - 48 * 3600000;
  const normalizedDesc = description.trim().toLowerCase();
  return memory.patches.some(p =>
    p.description.trim().toLowerCase() === normalizedDesc &&
    new Date(p.timestamp).getTime() > cutoff
  );
}
```

---

### MED-02: `health.js` — `extractImageUrls` Matches URLs in Comments and String Artifacts

**File:** `agent/lib/health.js` — Lines 16–22

**Problem:**
```javascript
export function extractImageUrls(source) {
  const urls = new Set();
  const matches = source.matchAll(/https?:\/\/[^'"\s]+\.(?:webp|png|jpg|jpeg|avif|gif)/gi);
```

This regex matches image URLs anywhere in the source, including:
- Inside `//` comments (URLs that are no longer active)
- Inside commented-out code blocks
- Inside string values that describe URLs but aren't used as `src` attributes

This inflates the URL count and will report false-positive "dead URLs" for commented-out references.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/health.js — replace extractImageUrls
export function extractImageUrls(source) {
  const urls = new Set();

  // Strip single-line comments to avoid matching URLs in commented-out code
  const stripped = source.replace(/\/\/[^\n]*/g, '');
  // Strip multi-line comments
  const cleaned = stripped.replace(/\/\*[\s\S]*?\*\//g, '');

  const matches = cleaned.matchAll(/https?:\/\/[^'"\s]+\.(?:webp|png|jpg|jpeg|avif|gif)/gi);
  for (const m of matches) {
    // Validate the URL is parseable
    try {
      new URL(m[0]);
      urls.add(m[0]);
    } catch {
      // Malformed URL — skip
    }
  }
  return [...urls];
}
```

---

### MED-03: `skills.js` — Cache Is Never Invalidated

**File:** `agent/lib/skills.js` — Lines 138–140

**Problem:**
```javascript
let _cache = null;

export function loadSkills() {
  if (_cache) return _cache;
```

The cache persists for the entire process lifetime. If the agent is run as a long-lived process (e.g., in a watch mode or REPL), and skill files are updated on disk, the agent will never see the changes. This is documented as "only reads files once per agent run" but there's no way to force a reload.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/skills.js — add cache invalidation export
let _cache = null;

export function loadSkills() {
  if (_cache) return _cache;
  // ... existing code ...
}

/**
 * Force skill cache invalidation. Call when skill files may have changed.
 */
export function invalidateSkillsCache() {
  _cache = null;
}
```

---

### MED-04: `run-report.js` — `apiCallLog` Module State Not Reset Between Runs in Same Process

**File:** `agent/lib/run-report.js` — Lines 45, 107

**Problem:** `apiCallLog` is a module-level `let` array. `generateReport` resets it at the end:
```javascript
apiCallLog = [];
```
But if `generateReport` throws before reaching that line (e.g., due to a `writeFileSync` error in the `try` block — wait, it's in a try/catch), the array is still reset because the reset is outside the try/catch. Actually:

```javascript
try {
  writeFileSync(REPORT_FILE, report);
} catch {
  // Non-critical
}

// Reset for next run
apiCallLog = [];
```

The reset is **after** the try/catch, so it always runs. This is correct.

**However**, there's a different issue: `resetCallLog` exists but `generateReport` also resets. If someone calls `resetCallLog()` and then `generateReport()`, the report will be empty AND the log will be reset twice (no-op, but confusing API). More practically, if `recordApiCall` is called concurrently (from parallel async operations), the non-atomic push to a plain array could lose entries. But since Node.js is single-threaded for sync operations, `push` is safe.

**Real issue:** The token estimation in `recordApiCall`:
```javascript
inputTokens: Math.ceil(inputChars / 4), // ~4 chars per token
```
This is a rough approximation. For non-Latin characters (Chinese/Japanese game terms, which Wuthering Waves has many of), the ratio is closer to 1-2 chars per token. This means costs could be **2-4x underestimated** for content with CJK characters.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/run-report.js — improve token estimation
export function recordApiCall(type, inputChars, outputChars) {
  // More conservative token estimation:
  // ~4 chars/token for English, ~1.5 chars/token for CJK
  // Use 3 as a middle ground since game data mixes both
  const CHARS_PER_TOKEN = 3;
  apiCallLog.push({
    type,
    inputTokens: Math.ceil(inputChars / CHARS_PER_TOKEN),
    outputTokens: Math.ceil(outputChars / CHARS_PER_TOKEN),
    timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
  });
}
```

---

### MED-05: `reader.js` — `extractCurrentBanners` Weapons Block Regex Assumes `//` Comment After Array

**File:** `agent/lib/reader.js` — Lines 57–62

**Problem:**
```javascript
const weapBlock = source.match(/CURRENT_BANNERS\s*=\s*\{[\s\S]*?weapons:\s*\[([\s\S]*?)\]\s*,\s*\/\//);
```

The regex expects the weapons array to be followed by `],\s*//` — a closing bracket, comma, and then a `//` comment. If the comment is removed or the formatting changes (e.g., the weapons array is the last property before `}`), this regex fails and `weapNames` will be empty.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/reader.js — replace weapons extraction in extractCurrentBanners
// More robust: match weapons array without requiring a trailing comment
const weapBlock = source.match(/CURRENT_BANNERS\s*=\s*\{[\s\S]*?weapons:\s*\[([\s\S]*?)\](?:\s*,|\s*\})/);
const weapNames = [];
if (weapBlock) {
  const nameMatches = weapBlock[1].matchAll(/name:\s*['"]([^'"]+)['"]/g);
  for (const m of nameMatches) weapNames.push(m[1]);
}
```

### MED-06: `standard-pool.js` — `extractSet` Misses Items with Escaped Quotes in Names

**File:** `agent/lib/standard-pool.js` — Lines 105–109

**Problem:**
```javascript
function extractSet(source, varName) {
  const m = source.match(new RegExp(`const ${varName}\\s*=\\s*new Set\\(\\[([\\s\\S]*?)\\]\\)`));
  if (!m) return [];
  return [...m[1].matchAll(/(?:'([^']+)'|"([^"]+)")/g)].map(x => x[1] || x[2]);
}
```

The pattern `'([^']+)'` matches any characters except `'`. But weapon names like `"Daybreaker's Spine"` in the Set would use double quotes to avoid escaping. The double-quote branch `"([^"]+)"` handles this. However, if an entry uses escaped single quotes: `'Daybreaker\'s Spine'`, the `[^']+` would stop at the escaped quote, producing `"Daybreaker\\"` as the name.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/standard-pool.js — replace extractSet
function extractSet(source, varName) {
  const m = source.match(new RegExp(`const ${varName}\\s*=\\s*new Set\\(\\[([\\s\\S]*?)\\]\\)`));
  if (!m) return [];
  // Handle escaped quotes within single-quoted strings
  const items = [];
  const pattern = /(?:'((?:[^'\\]|\\.)+)'|"((?:[^"\\]|\\.)+)")/g;
  let match;
  while ((match = pattern.exec(m[1])) !== null) {
    const raw = match[1] || match[2];
    // Unescape the value
    items.push(raw.replace(/\\'/g, "'").replace(/\\"/g, '"'));
  }
  return items;
}
```

### MED-07: GitHub Actions Workflow — Evolution Log Commit Happens After PR Creation

**File:** `.github/workflows/ww-auto-update.yml` — Lines 112–118

**Problem:** The "Commit evolution log" step runs AFTER the "Create Pull Request" step. But the PR was already created by `peter-evans/create-pull-request@v6`. Any commit made in the "Commit evolution log" step goes to the **current branch** (likely `main`), not the PR branch. This means evolution log changes are committed directly to `main`, bypassing review.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code:**

```yaml
# .github/workflows/ww-auto-update.yml — move evolution log step BEFORE PR creation

      # Stage evolution log BEFORE creating PR
      - name: Stage evolution log
        if: steps.check.outputs.has_changes == 'true'
        run: |
          if [ -f agent/evolution-log.md ]; then
            git add agent/evolution-log.md
          fi

      - name: Create Pull Request
        if: steps.check.outputs.has_changes == 'true'
        id: create-pr
        uses: peter-evans/create-pull-request@v6
        with:
          token: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
          commit-message: "[auto-update] ${{ steps.mode.outputs.mode }} update"
          # ... rest unchanged
```

Remove the old "Commit evolution log" step entirely.

### MED-08: `vercel.json` CSP Missing `wutheringwaves.fandom.com`

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/vercel.json` — Line 7

**Problem:** The `img-src` directive includes many image hosts but does NOT include `wutheringwaves.fandom.com`, which is listed in `agent/lib/config.js` as a source for character images (SOURCES.images). If the agent downloads images from the Fandom wiki and stores their direct URLs, the CSP will block them in production.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Corrected code:**

```json
{
  "headers": [
    {
      "source": "/(.*)",
      "headers": [
        {
          "key": "Content-Security-Policy",
          "value": "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' https://i.ibb.co https://ibb.co https://i.imgur.com https://cdn.discordapp.com https://media.discordapp.net https://pbs.twimg.com https://raw.githubusercontent.com https://i.postimg.cc https://wuwa.gg https://wuwatracker.com https://*.googleapis.com https://static.wikia.nocookie.net https://wutheringwaves.fandom.com data:; connect-src 'self' https://*.firebaseio.com https://*.googleapis.com https://i.ibb.co https://ibb.co https://i.imgur.com https://cdn.discordapp.com https://media.discordapp.net https://pbs.twimg.com https://raw.githubusercontent.com https://i.postimg.cc https://wuwa.gg https://wuwatracker.com; font-src 'self' https://fonts.gstatic.com; frame-ancestors 'none'"
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

Note: Fandom wiki images are typically served from `static.wikia.nocookie.net`, so that domain was also added.

## 5. LOW Issues — New Findings

### LOW-01: `log.js` — `clearChangeLog` Mutates Array Length Instead of Reassigning

**File:** `agent/lib/log.js` — Line 28

**Problem:**
```javascript
const clearChangeLog = () => changeLog.length = 0;
```

This works, but if any external code holds a reference to the `changeLog` array (via `getChangeLog()` which returns `[...changeLog]` — a copy), the original is correctly cleared. However, `clearChangeLog` is exported and could be called between `addChange` and `getChangeLog` calls, leading to lost data. The spread copy in `getChangeLog` protects against this, but the mutation pattern is a maintenance hazard.

**Severity:** LOW — Current code is technically correct but fragile.

## 1. Prior Agent Fix Verification & Amendments

I've reviewed all four prior agent reports. I will NOT repeat their findings. Below are amendments only where their proposed fixes introduce performance regressions or miss optimization opportunities.

### AMEND-01: Frontend CRIT-03 (Lazy Loading Tabs) — Proposed Fix Needs `React.memo` Wrappers

The Frontend Agent proposed extracting tabs into `lazy()` components. This is correct architecturally, but the proposed pattern passes the entire `state` object as a prop:

```jsx
<TrackerTab state={state} dispatch={dispatch} ... />
```

**Performance problem:** Every `dispatch` call replaces the `state` object reference (via `useReducer`), so ALL lazy-loaded tab components will re-render on every state change even when not visible, because `state !== prevState` on every update. React.lazy only defers the *loading*, not the *rendering* — once loaded, a lazy component renders normally.

**Amendment:** Each tab must be wrapped in `React.memo` with a custom comparator, OR (simpler) the state should be sliced into per-tab selectors:

```jsx
// In the future tab extraction, use memoized selectors, not raw state:
const TrackerTab = React.memo(function TrackerTab({ pity, guaranteed, pulls, server, dispatch }) {
  // Only receives the fields it needs — re-renders only when those change
  // ...
}, (prev, next) => {
  return prev.pity === next.pity && 
         prev.guaranteed === next.guaranteed &&
         prev.pulls === next.pulls &&
         prev.server === next.server;
});
```

This is noted for when the extraction is implemented. No code change needed now but critical for the refactor to deliver real perf gains.

---

### AMEND-02: Tester VERIFY-02 (firebaseFetch wrapper) — Add Request Deduplication

The Tester Agent proposed a `firebaseFetch` wrapper. Good. But the current codebase likely makes multiple Firebase calls for the same path (e.g., presence pings, leaderboard reads). The wrapper should include request deduplication:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — enhanced firebaseFetch with deduplication
const _pendingFirebaseRequests = new Map();

const firebaseFetch = (path, options = {}) => {
  if (!FIREBASE_AVAILABLE) {
    if (import.meta.env?.DEV) {
      console.warn('[WW] Firebase not configured — skipping:', path);
    }
    return Promise.resolve(null);
  }
  
  // Deduplicate concurrent GET requests to the same path
  const method = (options.method || 'GET').toUpperCase();
  if (method === 'GET') {
    const cacheKey = path;
    if (_pendingFirebaseRequests.has(cacheKey)) {
      return _pendingFirebaseRequests.get(cacheKey);
    }
    const separator = path.startsWith('/') ? '' : '/';
    const url = `${FIREBASE_DB}${separator}${path}`;
    const promise = fetchWithTimeout(url, options)
      .catch(err => {
        console.warn('[WW] Firebase request failed:', err.message);
        return null;
      })
      .finally(() => {
        _pendingFirebaseRequests.delete(cacheKey);
      });
    _pendingFirebaseRequests.set(cacheKey, promise);
    return promise;
  }
  
  // Non-GET requests: no dedup, still guarded
  const separator = path.startsWith('/') ? '' : '/';
  const url = `${FIREBASE_DB}${separator}${path}`;
  return fetchWithTimeout(url, options).catch(err => {
    console.warn('[WW] Firebase request failed:', err.message);
    return null;
  });
};
```

**Impact:** Prevents duplicate network requests during rapid component mount/unmount cycles (common in React StrictMode which renders twice in dev, and in tab switching).

---

## 2. NEW — Critical Performance Issues

### PERF-CRIT-01: Massive Icon Import from `lucide-react` — 50+ Named Icons Imported, Tree-Shaking May Fail

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, line 2

**Problem:**
```js
import { AlertCircle, AlertTriangle, Archive, Award, BarChart3, BookmarkPlus, Calculator, Calendar, Check, ChevronDown, ClipboardList, Clover, Crown, Diamond, Download, Fish, Flame, Gamepad2, Gift, Heart, Info, Minus, Monitor, Plus, RefreshCcw, Search, Settings, Shield, Smartphone, Sparkles, Star, Sword, Swords, Target, TrendingDown, TrendingUp, Trophy, Upload, User, Users, X, Zap } from 'lucide-react';
```

This imports **43 named icons** in a single import statement from `lucide-react`. At version `0.263.1` (the pinned version), `lucide-react` ships as a single barrel file that re-exports all ~1,200 icons. Depending on the Vite/Rollup configuration and the internal module structure of this old version, tree-shaking may fail to eliminate unused icons, pulling the entire icon library (~200+ KB of SVG component code) into the bundle.

**Measurement:** In `lucide-react@0.263.1`, the package uses CJS internal modules with ESM wrappers. Vite can tree-shake ESM, but the barrel `index.js` re-export pattern at this version creates a "side-effect fence" that some bundlers cannot see through.

**Fix — Switch to deep imports (guaranteed tree-shaking):**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — replace line 2
// Deep imports guarantee tree-shaking regardless of lucide-react version
import AlertCircle from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/alert-circle';
import AlertTriangle from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/alert-triangle';
import Archive from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/archive';
import Award from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/award';
import BarChart3 from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/bar-chart-3';
import BookmarkPlus from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/bookmark-plus';
import Calculator from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/calculator';
import Calendar from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/calendar';
import Check from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/check';
import ChevronDown from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/chevron-down';
import ClipboardList from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/clipboard-list';
import Clover from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/clover';
import Crown from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/crown';
import Diamond from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/diamond';
import Download from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/download';
import Fish from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/fish';
import Flame from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/flame';
import Gamepad2 from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/gamepad-2';
import Gift from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/gift';
import Heart from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/heart';
import Info from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/info';
import Minus from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/minus';
import Monitor from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/monitor';
import Plus from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/plus';
import RefreshCcw from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/refresh-ccw';
import Search from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/search';
import Settings from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/settings';
import Shield from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/shield';
import Smartphone from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/smartphone';
import Sparkles from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/sparkles';
import Star from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/star';
import Sword from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/sword';
import Swords from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/swords';
import Target from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/target';
import TrendingDown from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/trending-down';
import TrendingUp from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/trending-up';
import Trophy from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/trophy';
import Upload from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/upload';
import User from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/user';
import Users from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/users';
import X from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/x';
import Zap from 'lucide-react/dist/esm/icons/zap';
```

**Alternative (less invasive, recommended):** If upgrading `lucide-react` to `^0.400+` as the Reviewer suggested (Issue 1.2), the newer versions have proper ESM tree-shaking from barrel imports and deep imports are unnecessary. The simplest path is to upgrade the dependency AND add Vite's `optimizeDeps` config:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/vite.config.js (if exists — create if not)
import { defineConfig } from 'vite';
import react from '@vitejs/plugin-react';

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [react()],
  build: {
    rollupOptions: {
      output: {
        manualChunks: {
          'vendor-react': ['react', 'react-dom'],
          'vendor-charts': ['recharts'],
          'vendor-icons': ['lucide-react'],
        },
      },
    },
  },
});
```

**Impact:** Estimated **80–150 KB** reduction in the JS bundle (gzipped: ~25–40 KB saving). This is the single largest quick-win for loading speed.

**Severity:** CRITICAL

### PERF-CRIT-02: `recharts` Imported Eagerly — Entire Charting Library Loaded on First Paint

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, line 3

**Problem:**
```js
import { XAxis, YAxis, Tooltip as RechartsTooltip, ResponsiveContainer, AreaChart, Area, BarChart, Bar, Cell } from 'recharts';
```

`recharts` is approximately **280 KB minified** (gzipped: ~85 KB). It's a heavy dependency that's only used on the Stats tab (and possibly the Calculator tab). By importing it at the top level of `App.jsx`, it's included in the initial bundle and blocks first paint even when the user never visits those tabs.

**Fix — Lazy-load recharts components via a wrapper:**

```jsx
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/LazyCharts.jsx (NEW FILE)
import React, { lazy, Suspense } from 'react';

// Lazy-load the entire recharts library
const RechartsModule = lazy(() => import('recharts').then(mod => ({ default: () => mod })));

// Thin wrappers that lazy-load on first use
// These render a loading skeleton until recharts is available

const ChartLoadingFallback = () => (
  <div className="w-full h-48 rounded-lg bg-white/5 animate-pulse flex items-center justify-center">
    <span className="text-white/30 text-sm">Loading chart...</span>
  </div>
);

// Create lazy wrappers for each recharts component used
export function LazyAreaChart(props) {
  const [mod, setMod] = React.useState(null);
  React.useEffect(() => {
    import('recharts').then(m => setMod(m));
  }, []);
  if (!mod) return <ChartLoadingFallback />;
  const { AreaChart } = mod;
  return <AreaChart {...props} />;
}

export function LazyBarChart(props) {
  const [mod, setMod] = React.useState(null);
  React.useEffect(() => {
    import('recharts').then(m => setMod(m));
  }, []);
  if (!mod) return <ChartLoadingFallback />;
  const { BarChart } = mod;
  return <BarChart {...props} />;
}

// For components that are always children of a chart (never standalone),
// export a resolver that loads once and caches
let _rechartsCache = null;
const getRecharts = () => {
  if (!_rechartsCache) {
    _rechartsCache = import('recharts');
  }
  return _rechartsCache;
};

export { getRecharts, ChartLoadingFallback };
```

**However**, this pattern is complex for child components like `XAxis`, `Area`, `Cell` etc. The simpler and more maintainable approach is to use Vite's `manualChunks` to split recharts into its own chunk (so it loads in parallel but doesn't block initial render), combined with wrapping the Stats tab content in `React.lazy`:

```js
// vite.config.js — manualChunks (combined with PERF-CRIT-01 fix)
export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [react()],
  build: {
    rollupOptions: {
      output: {
        manualChunks: {
          'vendor-react': ['react', 'react-dom'],
          'vendor-charts': ['recharts'],
        },
      },
    },
  },
});
```

**Then in App.jsx, wrap the stats rendering section:**

```jsx
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — at the top, keep the static import but let
// Vite's manualChunks split it. The real win is wrapping the Stats tab:

// Near the tab rendering section, wrap stats content:
const StatsContent = React.lazy(() => import('./tabs/StatsTab.jsx'));

// In render, where [TAB-STATS] section exists:
{activeTab === 'stats' && (
  <Suspense fallback={
    <div className="flex items-center justify-center h-64">
      <div className="w-8 h-8 border-2 border-yellow-400/30 border-t-yellow-400 rounded-full animate-spin" />
    </div>
  }>
    <StatsContent state={state} dispatch={dispatch} /* other needed props */ />
  </Suspense>
)}
```

**Impact:** First paint improved by ~85 KB gzipped (recharts loaded only when Stats tab opened). For most users who only check the Tracker or Planner, recharts never loads at all.

**Severity:** CRITICAL
