### NEW-MED-02: `deploy-check.js` `checkDeploymentStatus` Leaks Fetch Timeout Handle on Success

**File:** `agent/lib/deploy-check.js`, lines 40–58

**Problem:** The `AbortController` pattern:

```javascript
const controller = new AbortController();
const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 10000);

const res = await fetch(url, {
  method: 'HEAD',
  signal: controller.signal,
  headers: { 'User-Agent': 'WhisperingWishes-DeployCheck/1.0' },
});

clearTimeout(timeout);
```

If the `fetch` rejects (network error), the `clearTimeout` is never called because execution jumps to the `catch` block. This leaves the `setTimeout` callback pending, which holds a reference to the `AbortController` and prevents GC until the timer fires.

**Fix — `agent/lib/deploy-check.js`, replace lines 38–60:**

```javascript
  for (const url of [productionUrl, fallbackUrl]) {
    const controller = new AbortController();
    const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 10000);

    try {
      const res = await fetch(url, {
        method: 'HEAD',
        signal: controller.signal,
        headers: { 'User-Agent': 'WhisperingWishes-DeployCheck/1.0' },
      });

      if (res.ok) {
        log.ok(`Deployment healthy: ${url} (${res.status})`);
        return { status: 'success', url, statusCode: res.status };
      } else {
        log.warn(`Deployment issue: ${url} returned ${res.status}`);
        return { status: 'failure', url, statusCode: res.status };
      }
    } catch (err) {
      log.dim(`Could not reach ${url}: ${err.message}`);
      continue;
    } finally {
      clearTimeout(timeout);
    }
  }
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — minor resource leak, accumulates over many runs

---

### NEW-MED-03: `ai.js` `extractJSON` Attempts Multiple Expensive Parses Without Short-Circuiting

**File:** `agent/lib/ai.js`, lines 62–80

**Problem:** The function tries up to 4 `JSON.parse` calls on potentially large strings. Each failed parse is expensive. The function can be optimized by detecting the most common case first (code fence) and using a cheaper pre-check.

```javascript
function extractJSON(text) {
  try { return JSON.parse(text); } catch {}
  // Tries 3 more parse attempts...
}
```

If `text` starts with prose like "Here's the JSON:" (very common from Claude), the first `JSON.parse` will fail on the first character. We can skip it cheaply.

**Fix — `agent/lib/ai.js`, replace lines 62–80:**

```javascript
function extractJSON(text) {
  if (!text || typeof text !== 'string') {
    throw new Error('Cannot extract JSON from empty or non-string input');
  }

  // Cap input size to prevent DoS from enormous AI responses
  const MAX_JSON_INPUT = 512 * 1024; // 512KB
  if (text.length > MAX_JSON_INPUT) {
    throw new Error(`AI response too large for JSON extraction (${(text.length / 1024).toFixed(0)}KB > ${MAX_JSON_INPUT / 1024}KB limit)`);
  }

  const trimmed = text.trim();

  // Fast path: response starts with { or [ — try direct parse
  if (trimmed.startsWith('{') || trimmed.startsWith('[')) {
    try { return JSON.parse(trimmed); } catch {}
  }

  // Common path: extract from code fence
  const fenceMatch = trimmed.match(/```(?:json)?\s*([\s\S]*?)```/);
  if (fenceMatch) {
    try { return JSON.parse(fenceMatch[1].trim()); } catch {}
  }

  // Fallback: find first { or [ to last matching } or ]
  const jsonStart = trimmed.search(/[\[{]/);
  if (jsonStart >= 0) {
    const startChar = trimmed[jsonStart];
    const endChar = startChar === '{' ? '}' : ']';
    const jsonEnd = trimmed.lastIndexOf(endChar);
    if (jsonEnd > jsonStart) {
      try { return JSON.parse(trimmed.slice(jsonStart, jsonEnd + 1)); } catch {}
    }
  }

  throw new Error('Could not extract valid JSON from AI response');
}
```

**Key improvements:**
- Input size cap (Backend Agent flagged this in H-01 but didn't provide the fix in `extractJSON` itself)
- Null/type guard
- Skip first `JSON.parse` when text clearly doesn't start with JSON
- Smarter matching of start/end characters (only look for matching closing bracket)

**Severity:** MEDIUM — wasted CPU on every AI call + DoS vector

---

### NEW-MED-04: `writer.js` `bumpVersion` Reads and Writes `sw.js` Without Checking if the Version String Actually Exists

**File:** `agent/lib/writer.js`, lines 66–76

**Problem:**
```javascript
try {
  let sw = readFileSync(PATHS.swFile, 'utf-8');
  const swOldVersion = sw.match(/const APP_VERSION = '([^']+)'/)?.[1];
  if (swOldVersion) {
    sw = sw.replace(`const APP_VERSION = '${swOldVersion}'`, `const APP_VERSION = '${newVersion}'`);
    writeFileSync(PATHS.swFile, sw);
    log.ok(`Updated sw.js version to ${newVersion}`);
  }
} catch (err) {
  log.warn(`Could not update sw.js: ${err.message}`);
}
```

If the sw.js file has the version string appearing multiple times (e.g., in a comment and as a constant), `String.replace` only replaces the first occurrence. This leaves a stale version in the file. Unlikely today, but fragile.

More importantly: `bumpVersion` modifies THREE external files (`appcore-data.js` via buffer, `package.json`, `sw.js`) but only the buffer write is atomic (via the flush mechanism). If `sw.js` write succeeds but the buffer flush later fails, the files are out of sync. This is the version mismatch bug that Frontend CRIT-01 already found — and this code is the likely cause.

**Fix — `agent/lib/writer.js`, make external file writes deferred, replace lines 46–80:**

```javascript
// Track deferred file writes (applied during flush)
const deferredWrites = [];

export function bumpVersion(currentVersion) {
  const parts = currentVersion.split('.');
  parts[2] = String(parseInt(parts[2] || '0') + 1);
  const newVersion = parts.join('.');

  safeReplace(
    `const APP_VERSION = '${currentVersion}';`,
    `const APP_VERSION = '${newVersion}';`,
    `Bump version ${currentVersion} → ${newVersion}`
  );

  // Defer package.json update to flush time
  deferredWrites.push(() => {
    try {
      const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(PATHS.packageJson, 'utf-8'));
      pkg.version = newVersion;
      writeFileSync(PATHS.packageJson, JSON.stringify(pkg, null, 2) + '\n');
      log.ok(`Updated package.json version to ${newVersion}`);
    } catch (err) {
      log.warn(`Could not update package.json: ${err.message}`);
    }
  });

  // Defer sw.js update to flush time
  deferredWrites.push(() => {
    try {
      let sw = readFileSync(PATHS.swFile, 'utf-8');
      const swOldVersion = sw.match(/const APP_VERSION = '([^']+)'/)?.[1];
      if (swOldVersion) {
        // Use replaceAll to catch all occurrences
        sw = sw.replaceAll(`const APP_VERSION = '${swOldVersion}'`, `const APP_VERSION = '${newVersion}'`);
        writeFileSync(PATHS.swFile, sw);
        log.ok(`Updated sw.js version to ${newVersion}`);
      }
    } catch (err) {
      log.warn(`Could not update sw.js: ${err.message}`);
    }
  });

  addChange('version', `${currentVersion} → ${newVersion}`);
  return newVersion;
}
```

**Then update `flush` to execute deferred writes (replace lines 266–280):**

```javascript
export function flush() {
  if (!buffer) {
    log.warn('No buffer to flush');
    return false;
  }

  const tmpPath = PATHS.dataFile + '.tmp';
  try {
    writeFileSync(tmpPath, buffer);
    renameSync(tmpPath, PATHS.dataFile);
  } catch (err) {
    log.warn(`Atomic write failed, falling back to direct: ${err.message}`);
    writeFileSync(PATHS.dataFile, buffer);
  }

  log.ok(`Wrote updated appcore-data.js (${(buffer.length / 1024).toFixed(1)}KB)`);

  // Execute deferred file writes (package.json, sw.js, etc.)
  for (const write of deferredWrites) {
    try {
      write();
    } catch (err) {
      log.warn(`Deferred write failed: ${err.message}`);
    }
  }
  deferredWrites.length = 0; // Clear after execution

  return true;
}
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — race condition causing the version mismatch that Frontend Agent identified as CRIT-01

### NEW-MED-05: `evolution.js` `generateEvolutionPlan` Sends Entire Memory Object to Claude Prompt

**File:** `agent/lib/evolution.js`, lines 175–230

**Problem:** The function receives `memory` in its context and accesses `memory.runs` and `memory.failures`. While only summaries are sent to Claude, the full `memory` object is passed into `generateEvolutionPlan` and is accessible in the closure. This is not a leak per se, but the function also sends `memory.runs` entries which could contain file paths, commit hashes, and other sensitive operational data in their properties.

More practically, `memory.runs` and `memory.failures` are filtered but the filtered results are iterated fully:

```javascript
const runsLast7d = (memory.runs || [])
  .filter(r => Date.now() - new Date(r.timestamp).getTime() < 7 * 86400000);
```

If `memory.runs` has thousands of entries (agent running every 6 hours for years), this creates thousands of `Date` objects for each evolution call.

**Fix — limit memory access and add early size cap. `agent/lib/evolution.js`, lines 208–215:**

```javascript
  // Only look at the last 100 runs to bound computation
  const recentRuns = (memory.runs || []).slice(-100);
  const runsLast7d = recentRuns
    .filter(r => Date.now() - new Date(r.timestamp).getTime() < 7 * 86400000);

  const recentFailures = (memory.failures || []).slice(-100);
  const failuresLast7d = recentFailures
    .filter(f => Date.now() - new Date(f.timestamp).getTime() < 7 * 86400000);
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM — unbounded memory iteration, minor performance degradation over time

## 2. CRITICAL New Issues

### CRIT-P3-01: `getStaleUrls` Memory Leak — `checkedUrls` Map Grows Unbounded

**File:** `agent/lib/memory.js`, lines 95–101 and 40–45

**Problem:** The `memory.checkedUrls` object is never trimmed. Unlike `runs`, `patches`, and `failures` which are capped at `MAX_ENTRIES = 200` in `saveMemory()`, `checkedUrls` grows indefinitely. Over months of operation, if image URLs rotate (old images deleted, new ones added), stale entries accumulate. With ~267 images currently and churn over time, this could grow to thousands of entries — each with a URL key and an object value.

More critically, this means the memory JSON file grows without bound, eventually causing `readFileSync` and `JSON.parse` to slow the agent startup. On a GitHub Actions runner with limited memory, a large `.memory.json` could cause OOM.

**Severity:** CRITICAL (unbounded resource growth in autonomous system)

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/memory.js — replace saveMemory function (lines 40–50)

const MAX_ENTRIES = 200;
const MAX_URL_ENTRIES = 500; // Cap checked URLs

/**
 * Save memory to disk.
 */
export function saveMemory(memory) {
  // Trim old entries
  if (memory.runs.length > MAX_ENTRIES) memory.runs = memory.runs.slice(-MAX_ENTRIES);
  if (memory.patches.length > MAX_ENTRIES) memory.patches = memory.patches.slice(-MAX_ENTRIES);
  if (memory.failures.length > MAX_ENTRIES) memory.failures = memory.failures.slice(-MAX_ENTRIES);

  // Trim stale URL checks — keep only the most recently checked URLs
  if (memory.checkedUrls && Object.keys(memory.checkedUrls).length > MAX_URL_ENTRIES) {
    const entries = Object.entries(memory.checkedUrls);
    entries.sort((a, b) => {
      const aTime = new Date(a[1].checkedAt).getTime() || 0;
      const bTime = new Date(b[1].checkedAt).getTime() || 0;
      return bTime - aTime; // newest first
    });
    memory.checkedUrls = Object.fromEntries(entries.slice(0, MAX_URL_ENTRIES));
  }

  // Trim metaRefresh entries (from meta-refresh.js)
  if (memory.metaRefresh && Object.keys(memory.metaRefresh).length > MAX_URL_ENTRIES) {
    const entries = Object.entries(memory.metaRefresh);
    entries.sort((a, b) => (b[1] || 0) - (a[1] || 0));
    memory.metaRefresh = Object.fromEntries(entries.slice(0, MAX_URL_ENTRIES));
  }

  try {
    writeFileSync(MEMORY_FILE, JSON.stringify(memory, null, 2));
    log.dim('Memory saved');
  } catch (err) {
    log.warn(`Could not save memory: ${err.message}`);
  }
}
```

---

### CRIT-P3-02: `applyMetaUpdates` Re-reads Buffer Per Update But Applies Stale Data

**File:** `agent/lib/meta-refresh.js`, lines 97–131

**Problem:** Inside the `for` loop over `updates`, each iteration calls `getBufferFn()` to get the current buffer, then calls `loadBufferFn(buf.replace(...))` to write back. However, `buf` is retrieved from `getBufferFn()` at the start of each iteration. If `getBufferFn()` returns a **copy** (not a live reference), successive iterations would each operate on the original buffer, and only the LAST update would persist — all prior updates within the same loop would be silently discarded.

This is the same class of bug across three if-blocks within the same iteration — `bestWeapon`, `bestEchoes`, and `teams` can only all apply if they're on different characters. If two updates target the same character (e.g., both `bestWeapon` and `bestEchoes` for "Jiyan"), only the second one survives because `buf` is re-fetched after `loadBufferFn` stores the first.

Wait — actually re-reading: `getBufferFn()` is called once per update at line 101 `const buf = getBufferFn();`, and then IF the first if-block (bestWeapon) matches and calls `loadBufferFn`, the NEXT if-block (bestEchoes) at line 110 does NOT re-fetch `buf` — it still uses the old `buf` from line 101. So if both `bestWeapon` and `bestEchoes` match for the same update entry, the `bestEchoes` replacement operates on the PRE-weapon-update buffer, and `loadBufferFn` overwrites the weapon update.

**Severity:** CRITICAL (silent data loss — updates clobber each other)

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/meta-refresh.js — replace applyMetaUpdates function (lines 95–133)

/**
 * Apply meta refresh updates to the write buffer.
 */
export function applyMetaUpdates(updates, getBufferFn, loadBufferFn, memory, minConfidence = 0.85) {
  let changed = false;

  for (const update of updates) {
    if (update.confidence < minConfidence) continue;

    // CRITICAL: Re-fetch buffer at start of EACH field update and after EACH write
    // to prevent successive replacements from clobbering each other.
    const escaped = update.name.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');

    if (update.field === 'bestWeapon' && typeof update.newValue === 'string') {
      const buf = getBufferFn(); // Fresh read
      const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?bestWeapon:\\s*)'[^']*'`);
      const match = buf.match(pattern);
      if (match) {
        loadBufferFn(buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}'${update.newValue}'`));
        addChange('meta-refresh', `${update.name} bestWeapon → ${update.newValue}`);
        changed = true;
      }
    }

    if (update.field === 'bestEchoes' && Array.isArray(update.newValue)) {
      const buf = getBufferFn(); // Fresh read after potential prior write
      const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?bestEchoes:\\s*)\\[[^\\]]*\\]`);
      const match = buf.match(pattern);
      if (match) {
        const newArr = `[${update.newValue.map(e => `'${e}'`).join(', ')}]`;
        loadBufferFn(buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}${newArr}`));
        addChange('meta-refresh', `${update.name} bestEchoes updated`);
        changed = true;
      }
    }

    if (update.field === 'teams' && Array.isArray(update.newValue)) {
      const buf = getBufferFn(); // Fresh read after potential prior write
      const pattern = new RegExp(`('${escaped}':[\\s\\S]*?teams:\\s*)\\[[^\\]]*\\]`);
      const match = buf.match(pattern);
      if (match) {
        const newArr = `[${update.newValue.map(t => `'${t}'`).join(', ')}]`;
        loadBufferFn(buf.replace(match[0], `${match[1]}${newArr}`));
        addChange('meta-refresh', `${update.name} teams updated`);
        changed = true;
      }
    }
  }

  return changed;
}
```

---

### CRIT-P3-03: `addNewEvent` Pattern Matches Wrong Insertion Point — Can Corrupt File

**File:** `agent/lib/new-events.js`, lines 80–97

**Problem:** The function tries to find the end of the EVENTS object with two patterns:
1. `buf.match(/(\n\};\s*\n\s*\/\/ \[SECTION:)/)`
2. `buf.match(/(\n\};\s*\n\s*const (?:BANNER_HISTORY|SUBSCRIPTION))/)`

If NEITHER matches, the function silently returns false. But if pattern 1 fails and pattern 2 succeeds, or vice versa, the logic at line 92 re-runs the match that already failed:

```javascript
const marker = (eventsEnd || buf.match(/(\n\};\s*\n\s*const (?:BANNER_HISTORY|SUBSCRIPTION))/))?.[0];
```

This is redundant but not the real bug. The real bug is: if the EVENTS object is followed by something ELSE (like a comment or a different constant name not in the pattern), the regex will match the WRONG `};` closing brace — potentially the closing brace of `CHARACTER_DATA` or `WEAPON_DATA` — and insert event data inside the wrong object, corrupting the entire file.

**Severity:** CRITICAL (file corruption potential)

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/new-events.js — replace addNewEvent function (lines 75–109)

/**
 * Add a new event to the EVENTS object in appcore-data.js.
 */
export function addNewEvent(eventData, getBufferFn, loadBufferFn) {
  const buf = getBufferFn();

  // Strategy: Find the EVENTS object specifically by matching its declaration,
  // then find the LAST entry inside it, and insert after that entry.
  // This is safer than trying to find the closing brace.

  const eventsStart = buf.indexOf('const EVENTS = {');
  if (eventsStart === -1) {
    log.warn(`Could not find EVENTS object declaration for new event "${eventData.name}"`);
    return false;
  }

  // Find the closing }; of the EVENTS object by counting braces from the opening
  let braceDepth = 0;
  let eventsEndIdx = -1;
  let foundOpen = false;

  for (let i = eventsStart; i < buf.length; i++) {
    const ch = buf[i];
    // Skip strings to avoid counting braces inside them
    if (ch === "'" || ch === '"') {
      const quote = ch;
      i++;
      while (i < buf.length && buf[i] !== quote) {
        if (buf[i] === '\\') i++; // skip escaped quotes
        i++;
      }
      continue;
    }
    if (ch === '{') {
      braceDepth++;
      foundOpen = true;
    } else if (ch === '}') {
      braceDepth--;
      if (foundOpen && braceDepth === 0) {
        eventsEndIdx = i;
        break;
      }
    }
  }

  if (eventsEndIdx === -1) {
    log.warn(`Could not find closing brace of EVENTS object for new event "${eventData.name}"`);
    return false;
  }

  // Insert new event entry just before the closing brace
  const currentEnd = eventData.currentEnd ? `'${eventData.currentEnd}'` : 'null';
  const entry = `  ${eventData.key}: {
    name: '${eventData.name}', subtitle: '${(eventData.subtitle || '').replace(/'/g, "\\'")}',
    description: '${(eventData.description || '').replace(/'/g, "\\'")}',
    resetType: '${eventData.resetType}', color: '${eventData.color || 'cyan'}',
    currentEnd: ${currentEnd},
    rewards: '${(eventData.rewards || '').replace(/'/g, "\\'")}',
    imageUrl: '' },\n`;

  const before = buf.slice(0, eventsEndIdx);
  const after = buf.slice(eventsEndIdx);
  const newBuf = before + entry + after;

  loadBufferFn(newBuf);

  log.ok(`Added new event: ${eventData.key} ("${eventData.name}")`);
  addChange('new-event', `New event: ${eventData.name}`);
  return true;
}
```

## 3. HIGH New Issues

### HIGH-P3-01: `fetchAll` Doesn't Handle Individual Failures — Holes in Results Array

**File:** `agent/lib/scraper.js`, lines 65–80

**Problem:** `fetchAll` creates a `results` array of size `urls.length` and fills it via index. If `fetchPage` throws an unhandled error (unlikely given its try/catch, but possible with AbortController edge cases on Node 18), that index is never set, leaving `undefined` in the results array. Downstream consumers (like `detectNewEvents`, `checkStandardPool`) iterate results and access `.content` without null checks — causing `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined`.

More practically: even when `fetchPage` returns `{ ok: false, content: '' }`, the callers pass `results.filter(r => r.ok).map(r => r.content)` — but some callers DON'T filter:

- `agent/lib/new-events.js` line 25: `sourceContents` is passed directly (no `.ok` filtering visible)
- `agent/lib/standard-pool.js` line 20: `sourceContents` is passed directly
- `agent/lib/meta-refresh.js` line 67: `buildSources` is passed directly

If any source fails, these modules send empty content to Claude, wasting API calls.

**Severity:** HIGH

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/scraper.js — replace fetchAll function (lines 60–80)

/**
 * Fetch multiple URLs concurrently (respecting per-domain politeness).
 * Returns array of SUCCESSFUL results only (failed URLs are logged and excluded).
 * Each result has: { ok: true, content: string, url: string }
 */
export async function fetchAll(urls, { includeFailures = false } = {}) {
  // Group by domain so we can fetch different domains in parallel
  // but same-domain sequentially
  const byDomain = new Map();
  urls.forEach((url, i) => {
    const d = getDomain(url);
    if (!byDomain.has(d)) byDomain.set(d, []);
    byDomain.get(d).push({ url, index: i });
  });

  const results = new Array(urls.length);

  // Fetch each domain group in parallel, but URLs within a domain sequentially
  await Promise.all(
    [...byDomain.values()].map(async (group) => {
      for (const { url, index } of group) {
        try {
          results[index] = await fetchPage(url);
        } catch (err) {
          log.error(`Unexpected error fetching ${url}: ${err.message}`);
          results[index] = { ok: false, content: '', url, error: err.message };
        }
      }
    })
  );

  if (includeFailures) {
    return results;
  }

  // Filter out failures and undefined entries
  return results.filter(r => r && r.ok && r.content.length > 0);
}
```

---

### HIGH-P3-02: `extractPerSource` Calls Claude Sequentially — Wastes Time

**File:** `agent/lib/cross-verify.js`, lines 83–105

**Problem:** The `extractPerSource` function iterates sources in a sequential `for` loop, calling `askClaudeFn` one at a time. With 3 sources, this means 3 sequential API calls. Each call takes ~2-5 seconds. Since each call is independent (different source content, no data dependency), they should be parallelized. The AI rate limiter in `ai.js` already handles concurrency — parallel calls will be serialized there if needed.

**Severity:** HIGH (3x slower than necessary for cross-verification, which is the whole point of this module)

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/cross-verify.js — replace extractPerSource function (lines 83–105)

/**
 * Extract data from multiple sources independently using Claude.
 * Returns per-source extraction results.
 * Runs extractions in PARALLEL since each source is independent.
 *
 * @param {Object[]} sources — [{ url, content }]
 * @param {string} extractionPrompt — The prompt template. Use {SOURCE_CONTENT} as placeholder.
 * @param {Function} askClaudeFn
 * @returns {Object[]} — [{ source: 'game8.co', data: {...} }]
 */
export async function extractPerSource(sources, extractionPrompt, askClaudeFn) {
  const extractOne = async (source) => {
    const domain = getDomain(source.url);
    const prompt = extractionPrompt.replace('{SOURCE_CONTENT}', source.content.slice(0, 20000));

    try {
      const response = await askClaudeFn(
        'Extract game data from this single source. Return only valid JSON. If data is unclear or not found, use null.',
        prompt,
        { maxTokens: 2048 }
      );

      const data = JSON.parse(response.replace(/```json?\n?/g, '').replace(/```/g, '').trim());
      return { source: domain, data, url: source.url };
    } catch (err) {
      log.dim(`Extraction failed for ${domain}: ${err.message}`);
      return { source: domain, data: {}, url: source.url };
    }
  };

  // Run all extractions in parallel — AI rate limiter handles serialization if needed
  const results = await Promise.all(sources.map(extractOne));
  return results;
}
```

---

### HIGH-P3-03: `checkSyntax` Validator Doesn't Handle Template Literals Correctly

**File:** `agent/lib/validate.js`, lines 104–130

**Problem:** The `checkSyntax` function tracks string state with `inString` and `stringChar`, handling `'`, `"`, and `` ` ``. However, template literals (backtick strings) can contain `${...}` expressions that themselves contain braces, brackets, and even nested template literals. The current parser treats the entire backtick string as opaque text (skipping to the closing backtick), which means `${}` expressions inside template literals don't contribute to brace counting. This is actually the OPPOSITE problem — it would MISS unbalanced braces inside template expressions.

More critically: `appcore-data.js` uses backtick strings for multi-line descriptions. If a description contains an unescaped `}` or `{`, the brace counter would skip it (correct). But if it contains `${variable}`, the `$` and `{` before `variable` would be skipped because we're "in a string" — but then the `}` that closes the expression would also be skipped, so the count stays balanced. This is actually working by accident.

**The REAL bug:** The escape detection `prev !== '\\'` is wrong for double escapes. Consider the string `'test\\\''` — the `\\` is an escaped backslash, and the `\'` is an escaped quote. With the current check: at the final `'`, `prev = '\\'` which is `\`, so it thinks the quote is escaped — but it's NOT (the backslash before it is the second character of `\\`). This causes the parser to think the string is still open, and all subsequent braces are skipped.

**Severity:** HIGH (validator can greenlight broken code)

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/validate.js — replace checkSyntax function (lines 97–133)

/**
 * Basic syntax checks — look for common JS problems.
 */
function checkSyntax(source, errors) {
  // Check balanced braces/brackets
  let braces = 0, brackets = 0, parens = 0;
  let inString = false, stringChar = '';
  let inTemplateDepth = 0; // Track template literal expression nesting
  let inLineComment = false;
  let inBlockComment = false;

  for (let i = 0; i < source.length; i++) {
    const c = source[i];
    const next = i < source.length - 1 ? source[i + 1] : '';

    // Handle line comments
    if (inLineComment) {
      if (c === '\n') inLineComment = false;
      continue;
    }

    // Handle block comments
    if (inBlockComment) {
      if (c === '*' && next === '/') {
        inBlockComment = false;
        i++; // skip '/'
      }
      continue;
    }

    // Detect comment starts (only outside strings)
    if (!inString) {
      if (c === '/' && next === '/') {
        inLineComment = true;
        i++;
        continue;
      }
      if (c === '/' && next === '*') {
        inBlockComment = true;
        i++;
        continue;
      }
    }

    if (inString) {
      if (stringChar === '`') {
        // Template literal: handle ${...} expressions
        if (c === '$' && next === '{') {
          inTemplateDepth++;
          inString = false; // Exit string mode to count braces in the expression
          i++; // skip '{'
          braces++; // The ${ adds an opening brace
          continue;
        }
      }

      // Count consecutive backslashes before this character
      let backslashCount = 0;
      let j = i - 1;
      while (j >= 0 && source[j] === '\\') {
        backslashCount++;
        j--;
      }

      // Quote is escaped only if preceded by ODD number of backslashes
      if (c === stringChar && backslashCount % 2 === 0) {
        inString = false;
      }
      continue;
    }

    // We're outside strings — check if inside a template expression
    // and if closing brace returns us to the template literal
    if (inTemplateDepth > 0 && c === '}') {
      braces--;
      // This might close a template expression OR a regular block inside it
      // We track depth: the outermost } returns to template string mode
      // But we can't easily distinguish — accept the approximation
    }

    if (c === "'" || c === '"' || c === '`') {
      inString = true;
      stringChar = c;
    } else if (c === '{') braces++;
    else if (c === '}') braces--;
    else if (c === '[') brackets++;
    else if (c === ']') brackets--;
    else if (c === '(') parens++;
    else if (c === ')') parens--;
  }

  if (braces !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced braces: ${braces > 0 ? braces + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(braces) + ' extra closing'}`);
  if (brackets !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced brackets: ${brackets > 0 ? brackets + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(brackets) + ' extra closing'}`);
  if (parens !== 0) errors.push(`Unbalanced parentheses: ${parens > 0 ? parens + ' unclosed' : Math.abs(parens) + ' extra closing'}`);
}
```

---

### HIGH-P3-04: `htmlToText` Regex Chain is Pathological on Large HTML — Performance Hazard

**File:** `agent/lib/scraper.js`, lines 91–123

**Problem:** The `htmlToText` function applies 15+ sequential regex replacements on the full HTML string (which can be 500KB+). Several of these regexes are `[\s\S]*?` patterns — lazy quantifiers on dotall classes — which are O(n²) in pathological cases on V8's regex engine. Specifically:

- `/<script[\s\S]*?<\/script>/gi` — quadratic on deeply nested scripts
- `/<style[\s\S]*?<\/style>/gi` — same
- `/<!--[\s\S]*?-->/g` — same
- `/<a[^>]*href="([^"]*)"[^>]*>([\s\S]*?)<\/a>/gi` — nested groups with dotall

On a 500KB page with many scripts, this can take 200-500ms per page. With 6+ pages per run, this adds 1-3 seconds of pure CPU time.

**Severity:** HIGH (measurable performance impact on every run)

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/scraper.js — replace htmlToText function (lines 86–123)

/**
 * Extract readable text from HTML — strips tags, scripts, styles,
 * and excessive whitespace. Optimized for large documents.
 */
function htmlToText(html) {
  // Phase 1: Remove script/style/noscript blocks using indexOf-based iteration
  // This avoids pathological regex behavior on large documents
  let text = removeTagBlocks(html, 'script');
  text = removeTagBlocks(text, 'style');
  text = removeTagBlocks(text, 'noscript');

  // Phase 2: Remove HTML comments (use non-greedy match on smaller remaining text)
  text = text.replace(/<!--.*?-->/gs, '');

  // Phase 3: Convert structural tags to newlines
  text = text.replace(/<\/?(?:div|p|br|hr|h[1-6]|li|tr|td|th|table|section|article|header|footer|nav|main|blockquote|pre)[^>]*>/gi, '\n');

  // Phase 4: Extract link text (drop href)
  text = text.replace(/<a[^>]*>(.*?)<\/a>/gi, '$1');

  // Phase 5: Remove all remaining tags
  text = text.replace(/<[^>]+>/g, ' ');

  // Phase 6: Decode HTML entities
  text = decodeEntities(text);

  // Phase 7: Clean whitespace
  text = text.replace(/[ \t]+/g, ' ');
  text = text.replace(/\n[ \t]+/g, '\n');
  text = text.replace(/\n{3,}/g, '\n\n');
  text = text.trim();

  return text;
}

/**
 * Remove all occurrences of a block-level tag and its content using
 * indexOf-based search. O(n) instead of regex's potential O(n²).
 */
function removeTagBlocks(html, tagName) {
  const openPattern = `<${tagName}`;
  const closePattern = `</${tagName}>`;
  let result = '';
  let searchFrom = 0;

  while (searchFrom < html.length) {
    const openIdx = html.toLowerCase().indexOf(openPattern, searchFrom);
    if (openIdx === -1) {
      result += html.slice(searchFrom);
      break;
    }

    // Include text before the tag
    result += html.slice(searchFrom, openIdx);

    // Find the closing tag
    const closeIdx = html.toLowerCase().indexOf(closePattern, openIdx);
    if (closeIdx === -1) {
      // Unclosed tag — skip to end
      break;
    }

    searchFrom = closeIdx + closePattern.length;
  }

  return result;
}

/**
 * Decode common HTML entities efficiently.
 */
function decodeEntities(text) {
  // Use a single pass with a map for common entities
  const entityMap = {
    '&amp;': '&', '&lt;': '<', '&gt;': '>', '&quot;': '"',
    '&#039;': "'", '&apos;': "'", '&nbsp;': ' ',
  };

  return text.replace(/&(?:#\d+|#x[0-9a-f]+|\w+);/gi, (entity) => {
    // Check named entities
    const lower = entity.toLowerCase();
    if (entityMap[lower]) return entityMap[lower];

    // Numeric character references
    if (entity.startsWith('&#x') || entity.startsWith('&#X')) {
      const code = parseInt(entity.slice(3, -1), 16);
      return code ? String.fromCharCode(code) : '';
    }
    if (entity.startsWith('&#')) {
      const code = parseInt(entity.slice(2, -1), 10);
      return code ? String.fromCharCode(code) : '';
    }

    return ''; // Unknown entity — strip
  });
}
```

### HIGH-P3-05: `loadSkills` Reads Same File Twice (Wasted I/O)

**File:** `agent/lib/skills.js`, lines 127–130

**Problem:**

```javascript
const scopeRaw = loadSkillFile(SKILL_PATHS.scopeContext);
const aestheticAuditRaw = loadSkillFile(SKILL_PATHS.scopeContext); // AUDIT-AESTHETIC.md
```

`scopeRaw` and `aestheticAuditRaw` are loaded from the exact same path list (`SKILL_PATHS.scopeContext`). This means the same file is read from disk twice. Given skill files can be 100KB+, this is wasted I/O and memory (two copies of the same string).

**Severity:** HIGH (wasted resources, code clarity)

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/skills.js — replace lines 127–130 in loadSkills()

export function loadSkills() {
  if (_cache) return _cache;

  log.info('Loading audit skills...');

  const appAuditRaw = loadSkillFile(SKILL_PATHS.appAudit);
  const designRaw = loadSkillFile(SKILL_PATHS.designAesthetic);
  const scopeRaw = loadSkillFile(SKILL_PATHS.scopeContext);
  // scopeContext path points to AUDIT-AESTHETIC.md which contains the executed audit
  // Reuse the same loaded content — no need to read twice
  const aestheticAuditRaw = scopeRaw;

  _cache = {
    available: {
      appAudit: !!appAuditRaw,
      designAesthetic: !!designRaw,
      scopeContext: !!scopeRaw,
    },

    // Condensed extracts for Claude prompts
    appAuditRules: extractAuditRules(appAuditRaw),
    designBrief: extractDesignBrief(aestheticAuditRaw || designRaw),
    scopeRules: extractScopeRules(scopeRaw),

    // Full content (for deep audits that need the complete framework)
    full: {
      appAudit: appAuditRaw,
      designAesthetic: designRaw,
      scopeContext: scopeRaw,
    },
  };

  const loaded = Object.entries(_cache.available).filter(([, v]) => v).map(([k]) => k);
  log.ok(`Skills loaded: ${loaded.length ? loaded.join(', ') : 'none found'}`);

  return _cache;
}
```

### HIGH-P3-06: `run-report.js` Module-Level Mutable State Leaks Between Runs

**File:** `agent/lib/run-report.js`, lines 43–44, 75

**Problem:** `apiCallLog` is a module-level `let` variable:

```javascript
let apiCallLog = [];
```

The `generateReport` function resets it at line 104: `apiCallLog = [];`. But if the agent crashes BEFORE `generateReport` is called, and the Node.js process is reused (e.g., in a test runner or a persistent process), the old call log persists into the next run, inflating cost reporting.

Additionally, `resetCallLog()` at line 109 creates a NEW empty array, but `recordApiCall` pushes to whatever `apiCallLog` points to. This is technically correct (both reference the module binding), but the pattern is fragile — if anyone imports `apiCallLog` directly (not through the exported functions), they'd hold a stale reference.

**Severity:** HIGH (incorrect cost reporting if process reused)

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/run-report.js — replace module-level state and functions (lines 43–44, 49–55, 107–110)

// Track API calls during the run — use array mutation (not reassignment) for safety
const apiCallLog = [];

/**
 * Record an API call for the report.
 */
export function recordApiCall(type, inputChars, outputChars) {
  apiCallLog.push({
    type,
    inputTokens: Math.ceil(inputChars / 4), // ~4 chars per token
    outputTokens: Math.ceil(outputChars / 4),
    timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
  });
}

/**
 * Reset the call log (called at start of each run and after report generation).
 */
export function resetCallLog() {
  apiCallLog.length = 0; // Mutate in place — safer than reassignment
}

// In generateReport, replace line 104:
// OLD: apiCallLog = [];
// NEW: apiCallLog.length = 0;
```

Then at the end of `generateReport`:

```javascript
  // Reset for next run
  apiCallLog.length = 0;

  return { totalCost, totalInputTokens, totalOutputTokens };
}
```

### MEDIUM-01: Inconsistent Error Message Logging Across Agent Modules

**File:** Multiple files (see list below)

**Problem:** Error messages from `catch` blocks are logged with varying formats across the agent. Some include the function context, some don't. None apply the sanitization pattern the Backend Agent recommended for `ai.js`. While these aren't API-key-leaking risks, consistency matters for debugging in GitHub Actions logs.

Affected locations:
- `agent/lib/scraper.js:52` — `log.error(\`Failed to fetch ${url}: ${err.message}\`)`
- `agent/lib/meta-refresh.js:90` — `log.warn(\`Meta refresh analysis failed: ${err.message}\`)`
- `agent/lib/new-events.js:68` — `log.warn(\`New event detection failed: ${err.message}\`)`
- `agent/lib/standard-pool.js:57` — `log.warn(\`Standard pool check failed: ${err.message}\`)`
- `agent/lib/cross-verify.js:99` — `log.dim(\`Extraction failed for ${domain}: ${err.message}\`)`
- `agent/lib/memory.js:32` — silent `catch {}` (no logging at all)
- `agent/lib/checkpoint.js:46,85,97` — silent `catch {}` blocks
- `agent/lib/changelog.js:89` — no catch at all around file operations

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix (centralized error helper):**

```javascript
// agent/lib/log.js — add at the end, before export

/**
 * Create a safe error message string, capped in length and with
 * potential secrets redacted.
 */
const safeErrorMessage = (err, maxLen = 300) => {
  const msg = (err?.message || String(err) || 'Unknown error')
    .replace(/sk-ant-[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+/g, 'sk-***')
    .replace(/Bearer\s+[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+/gi, 'Bearer ***')
    .replace(/AIzaSy[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+/g, 'AIza***');
  return msg.length > maxLen ? msg.slice(0, maxLen) + '...' : msg;
};

export { log, addChange, getChangeLog, clearChangeLog, safeErrorMessage };
```

Then in each file, replace raw `err.message` with `safeErrorMessage(err)`:

```javascript
// Example: agent/lib/meta-refresh.js line 90
import { log, addChange, safeErrorMessage } from './log.js'; // updated import

// In catch block:
log.warn(`Meta refresh analysis failed: ${safeErrorMessage(err)}`);
```

Apply the same pattern to all 6 affected files listed above.

### MEDIUM-02: `extractEvents` Regex Over-Matches — Picks Up Non-Event Properties

**File:** `agent/lib/reader.js`, lines 95–113

**Problem:** The regex `content.matchAll(/(\w+):\s*\{([\s\S]*?)(?=\n\s*\w+:|$)/g)` matches any `word: {` pattern inside the EVENTS block. If an event has a nested property like `details: { ... }`, it would be incorrectly parsed as a separate event key. Additionally, the non-greedy `[\s\S]*?` with the lookahead `(?=\n\s*\w+:|$)` can produce incorrect captures when event blocks have varying indentation.

More practically: the regex captures `rewards` and `imageUrl` fields correctly because they're simple strings. But `name`, `currentEnd`, and `resetType` use `.match()` on the captured `body`, which could match properties from a DIFFERENT event if the main regex boundary is wrong.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (data extraction can silently grab wrong values)

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/reader.js — replace extractEvents function (lines 88–115)

/**
 * Extract EVENTS object — returns { key: { name, currentEnd, resetType } }
 */
export function extractEvents(source) {
  const events = {};
  const eventsBlock = source.match(/const EVENTS\s*=\s*\{([\s\S]*?)\n\};\s*\n/);
  if (!eventsBlock) return events;

  const content = eventsBlock[1];

  // Match each top-level event key by finding 'key: {' at consistent indentation (2 spaces)
  // Then capture everything until the next top-level key or end of block
  const eventPattern = /^  (\w+):\s*\{([\s\S]*?)\},?\s*$/gm;
  let match;

  while ((match = eventPattern.exec(content)) !== null) {
    const key = match[1];
    const body = match[2];

    // Extract properties from THIS event's body only
    const name = body.match(/name:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
    const currentEnd = body.match(/currentEnd:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || null;
    const resetType = body.match(/resetType:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
    const rewards = body.match(/rewards:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';

    events[key] = { name, currentEnd, resetType, rewards };
  }

  // Fallback: if the regex above matched nothing (different indentation), use original approach
  if (Object.keys(events).length === 0) {
    const fallbackMatches = content.matchAll(/(\w+):\s*\{\s*\n\s*name:\s*'([^']+)'/g);
    for (const m of fallbackMatches) {
      const key = m[1];
      const name = m[2];
      // Find this specific event's block
      const blockStart = content.indexOf(m[0]);
      const nextKey = content.indexOf('\n  ', blockStart + m[0].length + 50);
      const body = content.slice(blockStart, nextKey > -1 ? nextKey : content.length);

      const currentEnd = body.match(/currentEnd:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || null;
      const resetType = body.match(/resetType:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';
      const rewards = body.match(/rewards:\s*'([^']+)'/)?.[1] || '';

      events[key] = { name, currentEnd, resetType, rewards };
    }
  }

  return events;
}
```

### MEDIUM-03: `withCheckpoint` Loses Detailed Results — Only Caches `{ completed: true }`

**File:** `agent/lib/checkpoint.js`, lines 112–123

**Problem:**

```javascript
const cacheable = typeof result === 'boolean' || typeof result === 'number' || result === null
    ? result
    : { completed: true, hadChanges: !!result };
```

When `stepFn` returns a complex object (e.g., fetched source content, analysis results), the checkpoint reduces it to `{ completed: true, hadChanges: true }`. On resume, the cached value has no useful data — the caller receives `{ completed: true }` instead of the actual step output. This means that while the checkpoint correctly prevents re-running the step, the step's consumers can't use the cached result and may silently proceed with empty/undefined data.

**Severity:** MEDIUM (resume after crash produces incorrect behavior)

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/checkpoint.js — replace withCheckpoint function (lines 100–125)

/**
 * Wrap a step function with checkpoint support.
 * If the step was already completed (from a crashed run), returns cached result.
 * Otherwise runs the step and saves a checkpoint.
 *
 * @param {string} mode — 'full' | 'micro'
 * @param {string} stepName — unique step identifier
 * @param {Function} stepFn — async function to run (returns any result)
 * @param {Object} [options]
 * @param {Function} [options.serialize] — custom serializer for checkpoint data
 * @returns {*} — the step result (from cache or live execution)
 */
export async function withCheckpoint(mode, stepName, stepFn, options = {}) {
  // Check if already completed
  const cached = getCheckpoint(mode, stepName);
  if (cached !== null) {
    // If the cached result is a simple marker, return it as-is
    // Callers should handle this gracefully
    return cached;
  }

  // Run the step
  const result = await stepFn();

  // Serialize result for checkpoint storage
  let cacheable;
  if (options.serialize) {
    cacheable = options.serialize(result);
  } else if (result === null || result === undefined) {
    cacheable = null;
  } else if (typeof result === 'boolean' || typeof result === 'number' || typeof result === 'string') {
    cacheable = result;
  } else if (Array.isArray(result)) {
    // For arrays (like fetched sources), store a summary
    cacheable = { completed: true, hadChanges: result.length > 0, count: result.length };
  } else if (typeof result === 'object') {
    // For objects, try to store a reasonable subset (under 10KB)
    const json = JSON.stringify(result);
    if (json.length < 10240) {
      cacheable = result; // Small enough to cache fully
    } else {
      cacheable = { completed: true, hadChanges: true, keys: Object.keys(result) };
    }
  } else {
    cacheable = { completed: true };
  }

  saveCheckpoint(mode, stepName, cacheable);
  return result;
}
```

### MEDIUM-04: `changeLog` Array in `log.js` is Never Reset Between Runs

**File:** `agent/lib/log.js`, lines 26–29

**Problem:** `clearChangeLog` exists but is exported and never called by default. If the agent's `index.js` doesn't call `clearChangeLog()` at the start of a run, and the process is reused (testing, development, or if Node.js module caching persists), the change log accumulates across runs.

Tracing the code: `changelog.js` line 15 calls `getChangeLog()` which returns whatever has accumulated. `run-report.js` line 60 also calls `getChangeLog()`. Neither calls `clearChangeLog()` first.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/log.js — enhance clearChangeLog to be automatically callable

const changeLog = [];
const addChange = (category, description, confidence = 'HIGH') => {
  changeLog.push({ category, description, confidence, time: timestamp() });
};
const getChangeLog = () => [...changeLog];
const clearChangeLog = () => { changeLog.length = 0; };

export { log, addChange, getChangeLog, clearChangeLog };
```

The `index.js` entry point should call `clearChangeLog()` at startup. Since `index.js` isn't in this chunk, document the requirement:

```javascript
// agent/index.js — add near the top of the main function
import { clearChangeLog } from './lib/log.js';
// At the start of each run:
clearChangeLog();
```

### MEDIUM-05: GitHub Actions Workflow Doesn't Cache `node_modules` — Slow CI

**File:** `.github/workflows/ww-auto-update.yml`, lines 30–35

**Problem:** The workflow installs agent dependencies every run with `npm install` but never caches `node_modules`. For a weekly job this is fine, but for manual dispatch (which could be frequent during active development), each run spends 15-30 seconds installing the Anthropic SDK and its transitive deps.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```yaml
# .github/workflows/ww-auto-update.yml — add after "Setup Node.js" step (line ~35)

      - name: Cache agent dependencies
        uses: actions/cache@v4
        with:
          path: agent/node_modules
          key: agent-deps-${{ hashFiles('agent/package-lock.json') }}
          restore-keys: |
            agent-deps-

      - name: Install agent dependencies
        working-directory: agent
        run: npm ci  # Use `ci` instead of `install` for deterministic installs
```

### MEDIUM-06: `adjustConfidence` Returns > 1.0 Despite `Math.min` — Floating Point Edge Case

**File:** `agent/lib/cross-verify.js`, lines 108–113

**Problem:**

```javascript
export function adjustConfidence(baseConfidence, agreement, sourceCount) {
  if (sourceCount <= 1) return baseConfidence * 0.8;
  if (agreement >= 1.0) return Math.min(1.0, baseConfidence * 1.1);
  if (agreement >= 0.66) return baseConfidence;
  return baseConfidence * 0.7;
}
```

If `baseConfidence` is already 1.0, then `1.0 * 1.1 = 1.1`, and `Math.min(1.0, 1.1) = 1.0` — fine. But the function doesn't clamp the other branches. If `baseConfidence` is 1.5 (malformed input from Claude), the `agreement >= 0.66` branch returns `1.5` — above 1.0. Downstream, confidence values > 1.0 bypass all threshold checks, potentially auto-applying changes that should be reviewed.

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/cross-verify.js — replace adjustConfidence (lines 108–113)

/**
 * Boost or penalize confidence based on cross-verification agreement.
 * Always returns a value clamped to [0.0, 1.0].
 */
export function adjustConfidence(baseConfidence, agreement, sourceCount) {
  // Clamp input to valid range
  const base = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, baseConfidence));

  let adjusted;
  if (sourceCount <= 1) adjusted = base * 0.8;           // Single source penalty
  else if (agreement >= 1.0) adjusted = base * 1.1;      // Full agreement bonus
  else if (agreement >= 0.66) adjusted = base;            // 2/3 agree — neutral
  else adjusted = base * 0.7;                              // Majority disagree — penalty

  return Math.max(0, Math.min(1, adjusted));
}
```

### MEDIUM-07: `applyStandardPoolUpdates` Uses Greedy Regex That Can Match Wrong Array

**File:** `agent/lib/standard-pool.js`, lines 74–79

**Problem:**

```javascript
const stdCharsMatch = buf.match(/(standardCharacters:\s*\[[\s\S]*?)(])/);
```

The `[\s\S]*?` is lazy, but `buf.match()` returns the FIRST match. If there are multiple `standardCharacters:` occurrences (e.g., in comments, BANNER_HISTORY entries, or old code), this could match the wrong one. Since this is modifying the source file, matching the wrong array would silently corrupt data.

Similarly:
```javascript
const stdWeapMatch = buf.match(/(standardWeapons:\s*\[[\s\S]*?)(],)/);
```

**Severity:** MEDIUM

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/standard-pool.js — replace the standardCharacters matching section (lines 72–82)

      // Add to CURRENT_BANNERS.standardCharacters — match within CURRENT_BANNERS block
      const currentBannersStart = buf.indexOf('const CURRENT_BANNERS');
      if (currentBannersStart !== -1) {
        // Search only within the CURRENT_BANNERS block
        const bannersBlock = buf.slice(currentBannersStart, currentBannersStart + 5000);
        const stdCharsMatch = bannersBlock.match(/(standardCharacters:\s*\[[\s\S]*?)(])/);
        if (stdCharsMatch && !stdCharsMatch[1].includes(`'${name}'`)) {
          const replacement = `${stdCharsMatch[1]}, '${name}'${stdCharsMatch[2]}`;
          buf = buf.slice(0, currentBannersStart) +
                bannersBlock.replace(stdCharsMatch[0], replacement) +
                buf.slice(currentBannersStart + bannersBlock.length);
          changed = true;
        }
      }
```

Apply the same scoping pattern for `standardWeapons`:

```javascript
      // Add to CURRENT_BANNERS.standardWeapons array — scoped to CURRENT_BANNERS block
      if (currentBannersStart !== -1) {
        const bannersBlock = buf.slice(currentBannersStart, currentBannersStart + 8000);
        const stdWeapMatch = bannersBlock.match(/(standardWeapons:\s*\[[\s\S]*?)(],)/);
        if (stdWeapMatch && !stdWeapMatch[1].includes(`'${weapon.name}'`)) {
          const entry = `\n    { name: '${weapon.name}', type: '${weapon.type}' },`;
          const replacement = `${stdWeapMatch[1]}${entry}\n  ${stdWeapMatch[2]}`;
          buf = buf.slice(0, currentBannersStart) +
                bannersBlock.replace(stdWeapMatch[0], replacement) +
                buf.slice(currentBannersStart + bannersBlock.length);
          changed = true;
        }
      }
```

## 5. LOW New Issues

### LOW-01: Duplicate `getDomain` Function Across Multiple Files

**Files:** `agent/lib/scraper.js` line 11, `agent/lib/cross-verify.js` line 126

**Problem:** Two identical implementations of `getDomain`:

```javascript
// scraper.js:11
const getDomain = (url) => {
  try { return new URL(url).hostname; } catch { return 'unknown'; }
};

// cross-verify.js:126
function getDomain(url) {
  try { return new URL(url).hostname.replace('www.', ''); } catch { return url; }
}
```

They're NOT identical — `scraper.js` returns `'unknown'` on error, `cross-verify.js` returns the raw URL. And `cross-verify.js` strips `www.` while `scraper.js` doesn't. This inconsistency means cross-verification logs show `game8.co` while scraper logs show `game8.co` (happens to match) but for `www.prydwen.gg` they'd diverge.

**Severity:** LOW (cosmetic inconsistency + code duplication)

**Fix:** Extract to a shared utility:

```javascript
// agent/lib/utils.js (new file)

/**
 * Extract the domain name from a URL, stripping 'www.' prefix.
 * Returns 'unknown' if the URL is malformed.
 */
export function getDomain(url) {
  try {
    return new URL(url).hostname.replace(/^www\./, '');
  } catch {
    return 'unknown';
  }
}

/**
 * Sleep for the specified milliseconds.
 */
export const sleep = (ms) => new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms));
```

Then update imports in `scraper.js` and `cross-verify.js`:

```javascript
// agent/lib/scraper.js — replace lines 11–12
import { getDomain, sleep } from './utils.js';
// Remove local getDomain and sleep declarations

// agent/lib/cross-verify.js — replace line 126
import { getDomain } from './utils.js';
// Remove local getDomain function
```

```javascript
    'standard-pool': 'Standard Pool',

    // Data enrichment (both forms accepted — agent modules use different prefixes)
    'enrichment': 'Data',
    'data-enrichment': 'Data',
    'meta-refresh': 'Builds',

    // Agent self-improvement
    'evolution': 'Agent',
    'evolution-create': 'Agent',
    'shell-swap': 'Agent',

    // App changes
    'ui-polish': 'UI',
    'bugfix': 'Fixes',
    'code-quality': 'Code',
    'feature': 'Features',
    'a11y': 'Accessibility',
    'new-event': 'Events',

    // Operational
    'images': 'Images',
    'health': 'Maintenance',
    'version': 'Version',
    'rollback-warning': 'Warning',
  };
  return map[cat] || cat.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + cat.slice(1);
}
```

---

### LOW-05: `readScheduleHint` Returns Raw Parsed JSON Without Validation

**File:** `agent/lib/adaptive-schedule.js`, lines 100–105

**Problem:**

```javascript
export function readScheduleHint() {
  if (!existsSync(SCHEDULE_FILE)) return null;
  try {
    return JSON.parse(readFileSync(SCHEDULE_FILE, 'utf-8'));
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}
```

If the `.schedule-hint.json` is corrupted or contains unexpected keys (e.g., from a different version of the agent), the caller receives an unvalidated object. While the file is agent-internal, schema validation prevents subtle bugs when the hint format evolves.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:**

```javascript
// agent/lib/adaptive-schedule.js — replace readScheduleHint (lines 100–105)

/**
 * Read the last schedule hint with basic schema validation.
 */
export function readScheduleHint() {
  if (!existsSync(SCHEDULE_FILE)) return null;
  try {
    const hint = JSON.parse(readFileSync(SCHEDULE_FILE, 'utf-8'));
    // Basic schema check
    if (!hint || typeof hint.urgency !== 'string' || typeof hint.hoursLeft !== 'number') {
      return null;
    }
    return hint;
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}
```

---

### LOW-06: Vercel CSP Header is Missing `worker-src` Directive

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/vercel.json`, Content-Security-Policy header

**Problem:** The app registers a service worker (`sw.js`), but the CSP header has no `worker-src` directive. While browsers typically fall back to `script-src` for workers (and `script-src 'self'` is present), explicit is better than implicit. Some browsers (older Safari) don't fall back correctly.

**Severity:** LOW

**Fix:**

```json
{
  "key": "Content-Security-Policy",
  "value": "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; worker-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' https://i.ibb.co https://ibb.co https://i.imgur.com https://cdn.discordapp.com https://media.discordapp.net https://pbs.twimg.com https://raw.githubusercontent.com https://i.postimg.cc https://wuwa.gg https://wuwatracker.com https://*.googleapis.com data:; connect-src 'self' https://*.firebaseio.com https://*.googleapis.com https://i.ibb.co https://ibb.co https://i.imgur.com https://cdn.discordapp.com https://media.discordapp.net https://pbs.twimg.com https://raw.githubusercontent.com https://i.postimg.cc https://wuwa.gg https://wuwatracker.com; font-src 'self' https://fonts.gstatic.com; frame-ancestors 'none'"
}
```

---

### LOW-07: Firebase Rules Allow `totalPulls` Up to 999,999 — Unrealistically High

**File:** `whispering-wishes-replit/database.rules.json`, line 14

**Problem:**

```json
"&& newData.child('totalPulls').val() <= 999999"
```

At 160 Astrite per pull and ~999,999 pulls, that's ~160 million Astrite. Even whale accounts don't approach this. A more realistic cap (e.g., 50,000) would prevent spoofed leaderboard data while still accommodating extreme players.

**Severity:** LOW (data quality, not security)

**Fix:**

```json
"&& newData.child('totalPulls').isNumber()
&& newData.child('totalPulls').val() >= 0
&& newData.child('totalPulls').val() <= 50000"
```

## 6. Performance Optimization Catalog

### PERF-01: Pre-compile Frequently Used Regexes

**File:** `agent/lib/reader.js`, multiple locations

**Problem:** Functions like `extractCharacterNames`, `extractWeaponNames`, `extractLists`, and `extractAllCharacters` all compile new `RegExp` objects from string patterns on every call. In `readCurrentState()` (line 154), these are all called in sequence on the same 1,400-line source. While regex compilation isn't expensive for simple patterns, the `extractLists` function creates 7 regexes (one per `extract()` call).

**Impact:** ~1-2ms per call,
